# Internal Package Layout

Internal architecture reference for agents and contributors. This document captures per-package responsibilities, invariants, and cross-package contracts that are not derivable from the code alone (or that would be expensive to re-derive every time). User-facing schema / behavior docs live under [`docs/reference/`](../reference/) — this file is purely about *internal code structure*.

The executable entrypoint lives in `cmd/dwe`; most code is under `internal/`. Tests sit next to code as `*_test.go`; fixtures live in package-local `testdata/`.

## Layout

`internal/` is organized in three layers — see [Dependency Rules](#dependency-rules) for the intended contracts (depguard activation is pending):

```
internal/
  cli/                   cobra command tree, no domain logic
  core/                  domain logic, subclustered
    project/             "what is a DWE project" (config, services, stack)
    execution/           pipeline engine + collaborators
    workflow/            deploy, lifecycle, reset, snapshot, setup
    usercommands/        declarative user-command system
    validate/            static validation framework + subdomains
    docs/                embedded docs subsystem
    ui/                  domain-aware renderers (sink — imported only by cli/)
    notify/              desktop notifications
    bridge/              host bridge for dev containers (daemon, compose overlay, shim assets)
  shared/                leaf infrastructure (docker, git, lock, render, i18n, …)
```

## Dependency Rules

Three layering rules are intended contracts. Activation in `depguard` is deferred until the pre-existing cross-layer imports surfaced during the restructure are resolved (see Task 10 notes in `docs/plans/completed/2026-05-28-internal-restructure-layered.md`). Until then they are enforced by convention and code review only:

- **`shared-no-domain`** — `internal/shared/**` must not import `internal/core/**` or `internal/cli/**`. Shared is leaf infrastructure.
- **`core-no-cli`** — `internal/core/**` must not import `internal/cli/**`. Domain code is ignorant of cobra.
- **`ui-is-sink`** — `internal/core/ui/**` is a sink: no other `internal/core/**` package may import it. Only `internal/cli/**` may pull it in. The `internal/core/ui/tui/` TUI framework follows this rule strictly — it is importable only from `core/ui/*` + `cli/` and no new importers were added through Stage 2. The docs browser has been relocated to `internal/core/ui/docstui/` (Stage 4), keeping `core/docs` free of `core/ui` imports and placing the framework dependency on the `core/ui` side of the boundary. A concrete corollary: `internal/core/docs/` must stay free of `internal/core/ui/` imports, which is the whole reason the interactive browser lives in `internal/core/ui/docstui/` rather than under `core/docs/`. Mechanically checkable with `grep -rn "core/ui" internal/core/docs/ --include='*.go'` — which must stay empty; the `--include` filter is required, since without it the generated `embedded/` docs mirror matches its own prose.

Soft (non-enforced) intra-`core/` ordering: `project ← execution ← workflow`. Anything depending on a named user operation lives in `workflow/`; anything that runs a pipeline lives in `execution/`; anything that defines "the project" lives in `project/`.

## CLI (`internal/cli/`)

The cli layer is a thin cobra composition root plus 20 per-domain subpackages. Each subpackage owns one command subtree and exports a single `NewCmd(groupID string, flags *cmdctx.RootFlags) *cobra.Command` entry point that the root composes via `root.AddCommand(<pkg>.NewCmd(group, flags))`; this replaces the old inline `newXxxCmd` declarations and the `addCmd` helper. Three pattern variants exist: (1) **standard** — single-export `NewCmd(groupID, flags)`; (2) **multi-export Go-domain grouping** — `lifecycle/` exposes `NewRunCmd` / `NewStopCmd` / `NewRestartCmd` / `NewResetCmd` because the four commands share a `preflightRun` test seam and the preflight+lock+journal pattern, even though they are independent top-level cobra commands; (3) **special** — `completion/` exposes `AttachInstallUninstall(parent *cobra.Command, flags *cmdctx.RootFlags)` because its subcommands attach to cobra's auto-generated `completion` command (`root.InitDefaultCompletionCmd()` cannot be replaced). `version.NewCmd(groupID string)` is the lone standard-variant that omits `flags` because it needs none.

**Cross-cutting CLI behaviors anchored on `cmdctx.RootFlags`** (details under each subpackage):

- **JSON output mode (`--output json` + `--pretty`)**: Read-only commands emit naked DTOs on stdout; errors go through `cmdctx.WriteError` as a `{"error":{code,message,hint,details}}` envelope on stderr. Use `cmdctx.WriteData[T](flags, cmd, data, textFn)` to dispatch text vs JSON. Construct typed errors with `cmdctx.Err(code, msg)` / `cmdctx.ErrWrap(code, err)` and chain `.WithHint(...)` / `.WithDetail(key, val)`. JSON mode side-effects (set in `PersistentPreRunE`): `NO_COLOR=1`, `SilenceErrors=true`, `SilenceUsage=true`. `runRoot` (no subcommand) routes to `runRootJSON` when JSON is on — never emits brand header or pending banner in that mode. DTO convention: snake_case fields, RFC3339 timestamps, durations in seconds and sizes in bytes as numbers; wrap top-level arrays in a keyed object (`{"apps":[...]}`); `omitempty` on optional inner fields but never on the wrapper key. Stderr in JSON mode is reserved for the error envelope — gate any `warning:` writes behind `flags.Output != "json"`. **Diagnostic commands (`validate` and subdomains) are the exception** to the envelope rule: they always emit diagnostics-as-data on stdout, even at severity=error; exit code conveys severity (0 ok/info, 1 error or warning+`--strict`), and `validationFailedError` carries `ExitCode()` so `main.go` short-circuits the envelope path. TUI-capable commands (`status`, future `cmdbrowser`) force TUI off when `flags.Output == "json"`. Exit codes via `cmdctx.ExitCodeFor(err)`: 0 success, 1 runtime, 2 usage (e.g. `invalid_output`, `--pretty` without `--output json`). Use `cli.NewRootCmdWithFlags()` (not `NewRootCmd()`) when you need the `*RootFlags` pointer; the no-flags constructor exists for the 14+ legacy test call sites only.
- **Display-string localization**: Every call site that renders user-command descriptions, parameter descriptions, group names, or doc-generator section headers to stdout must use the `i18n.Translator` interface (via `rflags.I18n`). Use typed helpers: `store.CommandDescription(locale, id, fallback)`, `store.ParamDescription(locale, id, paramName, fallback)`, `store.GroupTitle/Description(locale, id, fallback)`, `store.T(locale, uiKey, fallback)`. Never read `def.Description` directly in display code. Completion-path safety: `__complete` paths bypass `PersistentPreRunE`, so wrap via `i18n.TranslatorOrNop(rflags.I18n)`. Storage/hashing sites (journal, deploy steps, phase definitions) stay English — never introduce locale to `internal/core/workflow/deploy/journal/hash.go` or any persistence layer.

- **Container command policy (host bridge)**: when `DWE_INVOKED_FROM=container` (force-set by the bridge daemon — design D7; client-sent values are stripped, so not spoofable from a container), only an **allowlist, default-deny** subset of the CLI is reachable: the `commands` subtree, `status`, `info`, `logs`, `docs`, `prompt`, `version`, `help`, `vars`, bare `dwe`, cobra's `__complete`/`__completeNoDesc`, plus the nested exceptions `bridge status` and `render config` (the other `render` subcommands — env/ide/ai/git — stay host-only). `vars`'s read subcommands (`get`/`list`/`inspect`) are freely reachable; `vars set` is reachable but **deny-by-default RUNTIME-gated** by `bridge.vars_writable` inside `cli/vars/set.go` (the allowlist is prefix-wide and cannot see the var arg). `render config` rejects `--harvest` in a container (host-only state mutation). `validate` and the user-facing `completion` generator are deliberately host-only (the hidden `__complete` machinery stays allowed so a completion script baked into an image degrades silently instead of injecting a policy error into the shell). Everything else — including every NEW top-level command, until explicitly added to `bridgeAllowedTopLevel` in `internal/cli/bridgepolicy.go` — fails with typed `cmdctx.Err("bridge_command_blocked")` (run-on-host hint; stop/restart/reset additionally carry the suicide explanation). The gate runs inside the EXISTING root `PersistentPreRunE` (step 2b — after output-mode validation, before project resolution); `applyBridgeContainerVisibility` at the end of `NewRootCmdWithFlags` hides blocked commands from help and completion (a parent stays visible when any descendant is allowed — the `bridge` → `bridge status` shape). The policy is inherited by nested invocations (`type: dwe` user commands keep the env). Related fallback: bare `dwe commands`/`dwe cmd` (non-tty stdin) and bare `dwe docs` (non-tty stdout) OR truthy `DWE_NONINTERACTIVE` (the shared `cmdctx.NonInteractiveEnv()` `{"1","true"}` set) print their `list` output instead of the TUI browser. **Inside the allowlisted `commands` subtree there is a second, per-command gate**: user commands are container-reachable only when opted in via a `bridge:` block (`model.BridgeDef` — command-level or file `group:` header, field-wise merge, opt-in default), resolved into `CommandDef.BridgeHidden` by `registry.ApplyVisibility` and enforced by `registry.list` (all listing/completion variants — including the inspect escape hatch), the tree walker in `cli/command/list.go`, and `bridgeGuard` in `cli/command/bridgegate.go` (typed `command_not_bridged` on direct invocation AND inspect). The workflow runner and pipeline engine deliberately never consult `BridgeHidden` — a bridged workflow executes non-bridged sub-commands host-side. See § Core — User Commands. User-visible policy table: `docs/reference/concepts/bridge.md` § Command policy inside containers.

**Naming rationale**: `docker/` and `compose/` are kept as **separate packages** rather than collapsed into one `docker/` package exporting `NewDockerCmd`/`NewComposeCmd`; the latter would force the stuttering `docker.NewDockerCmd` (textbook Go anti-pattern of the `http.HTTPClient` shape). The file hosting `NewCmd` inside each subpackage is named after the package (`lifecycle/lifecycle.go`, `snapshot/snapshot.go`, `deploy/deploy.go`, `render/render.go`, …) to avoid overloading `root.go` — `internal/cli/root.go` is THE root. Where a subpackage name collides with an import (`internal/shared/version`, `internal/shared/prompt`, `internal/shared/docker`, `internal/core/workflow/snapshot`, `internal/core/workflow/lifecycle`, `internal/core/validate`, `internal/core/docs`), callers and the colliding import alias as `versioninfo` / `promptpkg` / `dockerpkg` / `snapshotpkg` / `lifecyclepkg` / aliased `cmdValidate` / `cmdDocs` etc.

**Cross-sibling import policy**: siblings communicate via `cli/cmdctx/` and never cross-import each other. Two pre-existing exceptions are documented and out of scope to fix here: `cli/service/` → `cli/deploy.RunHelper` (service-toggle drives deploys after enable/disable; resolved by lifting `RunHelper` into `core/workflow/deploy/` in a follow-up), and `cli/lifecycle/{run,restart}.go` → `cli/info.Run` (the `ShowInfo` lifecycle callback; the import became a sibling import once `lifecycle/` was extracted).

**Section preamble — cross-cutting behaviors anchored in `root.go`**: **Validate schema-bypass pattern** — `isValidateCommand(cmd)` in `PersistentPreRunE` switches from `project.Resolve` (schema-validating) to `project.Locate` (locate only) so schema errors surface as diagnostics rather than aborting before `RunE`; apply this pattern to any future diagnostic-mode command. **Exit-code interface** — errors implementing `ExitCode() int` are translated to custom process exit codes in `cmd/dwe/main.go` via `errors.As`; the fang error handler suppresses the "Error:" line for them. `validationFailedError` in `cli/validate/validate.go` is the canonical example.

- `internal/cli/` — composition root (`root.go`, ~620 LoC): `NewRootCmd`, `initRootCmd`, `PersistentPreRunE`, `runRoot`, `applyStyles`, `resolveLocalization`, `allowedWithoutProject`, `isValidateCommand`. No inline command builders; no `addCmd` helper. Companions: `root_test.go`, `root_resolver_test.go`, `fang_integration_test.go`.
- `internal/cli/cmdctx/` — typed `RootFlags` bundle threaded into every RunE callback (cfg path, language, accent overrides, project-root resolver, etc.) plus flag-helper utilities (e.g. the exported `CompletionConfigPath(flags *RootFlags, cmd *cobra.Command) (configPath, projectRoot string, err error)`, used by every `ValidArgsFunction` callback to safely resolve project state under cobra's hidden `__complete` path, which bypasses `PersistentPreRunE`). This is the single seam through which siblings exchange context. Shared cross-sibling helpers exported here: `WriteData[T]` / `WriteJSON[T]` (text-vs-JSON dispatch; `WriteJSON` is `WriteData` with an empty text renderer for JSON-only payloads), `WriteError` / `Err` / `ErrWrap` (typed-error envelope), `AcquireProjectLocksOrReport` (the project-lock acquire-and-report wrapper — see § snapshot Lock acquisition), `AcquireProjectLocksSilent` (the **no-print** variant with the *identical* error contract — `*lock.ProjectLockHeldError` returned unchanged with ExitCode 2, any other error wrapped `acquiring project locks: %w` — but writing NOTHING; the sanctioned wrapper for a **live alt-screen** call site (an in-TUI form overlay) where stderr diagnostics mid-frame would corrupt the TUI, so the caller surfaces the returned lock-held error itself as a status flash — see § cli/vars in-TUI edit), and `WarnSilentLog` (the shared pipeline-`ErrSilent` "Full output saved to" log-tail hint used by `deploy run` / `reset run` — the one cmdctx helper that imports `core/execution/pipeline`). Calling `CompletionConfigPath` before any config-dependent work in a `ValidArgsFunction` is a MUST, not a convention: on ANY error (from it or from the subsequent `config.LoadConfig`) the callback must return `nil, cobra.ShellCompDirectiveNoFileComp` and print **nothing**, because the `__complete` protocol reads the callback's stdout as the completion list and a diagnostic line would be offered to the user as a candidate. Neither lock wrapper may be re-rolled inline at a call site: the acquire-and-report body is ~9 lines and every hand-copy is a chance to drop the `*lock.ProjectLockHeldError` passthrough that carries `ExitCode() == 2`, degrading a "someone else is deploying" into a generic exit 1. Also exported here: `EmitDefaultNotice(cmd, flags, pipeline, file)` — the single consumer of the `defaulted bool` returned by every `Ensure*Config` constructor (`EnsureDeployConfig` / `EnsureResetConfig` / `EnsureRunConfig` / `EnsureStopConfig`). It writes ONE `render.Writer.Info` line ("Using built-in default <pipeline> pipeline (override with workspace/<file>.yml).") to **stderr** and is a **no-op under `--output json`** so stdout stays the parseable surface; the same decision is unconditionally mirrored to the Debug firehose via `trace.Debugf`, which is why suppressing the user-facing line in JSON mode loses nothing diagnostically. `cli/validate`'s `emitFilterHint` is modelled on it.
- `internal/cli/info/` — `dwe info` renderer. `NewCmd(groupID, flags)` plus `Run(ctx, …)` (exported because `cli/lifecycle/{run,restart}.go` consume it for the `ShowInfo` lifecycle callback).
- `internal/cli/version/` — `dwe version`. `NewCmd(groupID string)` — the lone flagless variant.
- `internal/cli/prompt/` — `dwe prompt` cobra command for `--help` discoverability only. The fast path bypasses cobra entirely via `cmd/dwe/main.go`'s `isPromptInvocation` dispatch into `shared/prompt.Run` (lipgloss would auto-downgrade to no-color when stdout is piped). `NewCmd` `RunE` is unreachable in normal startup. The command is registered on root with `Hidden: true`: it stays in `root.Commands()` (findable programmatically) and `dwe prompt --help` still renders its own help block — it is only dropped from the `dwe --help` listing (cobra also excludes `Hidden` commands from completion suggestions, so `dwe pro<TAB>` does not offer it). All three halves are test-pinned (`TestPromptCmd_UseAndFlags` asserts `Hidden`, `TestPromptCmd_HiddenFromRootHelp` asserts the listing omission, `TestPromptRegisteredAtRoot` asserts it is still registered), so unhiding it is a deliberate contract change, not a cosmetic one.
- `internal/cli/shell/` — `dwe shell` plus the misnamed-from-prior-refactor `exec.go` (was `service_cli.go`) carrying `resolveShellOptions`, `serviceContainerState`, `dockerExecCLI`, `composeRunCLI`, `runInteractive`, `errContainerNotFound`. Watch for the `os/user` import — package name `shell` does not collide. `NewCmd(groupID, flags)`. **Container detection is label-based, not name-guessed:** `serviceContainerState(projectName, service, processEnv, dockerBin)` delegates to the shared `docker.ServiceContainerName` (the one home for label-based container lookup; label-key consts `docker.ComposeProjectLabel` / `ComposeServiceLabel` / `ComposeOneoffLabel` are also reused by `stack.ServiceRunning` and `env` port-conflict). `service` is **svc.Container** — the value compose stamps into `com.docker.compose.service` and the service key dwe uses everywhere (topology, stop, logs); it defaults to the folder key but may be overridden in `service.yml`, so `resolveShellTarget` returns it and the probe/exec/run paths target it (NOT the user-facing service name). `serviceContainerState` runs two passes (running, then `--all`) to distinguish "stopped" from "absent"; each delegates to `docker.ServiceContainerName`, which probes `docker ps … --format '{{.Names}}'` (a single portable field — avoids `.State` and `--format json`, whose shapes differ between docker and podman) and **prefers a long-lived service container over an ephemeral one-off** by first filtering `com.docker.compose.oneoff=False`, falling back to the unfiltered query only when that finds nothing (so a one-off `compose run` container — e.g. a concurrent `dwe shell --mode run` session — is never exec'd into when a real service container exists, while compose backends that omit the one-off label still resolve via the fallback). Empty stdout from a zero-exit `docker ps` unambiguously means "no match" and a real Docker error exits non-zero and is surfaced, so a probe hiccup can never be mistaken for absence (which would start a duplicate). The state probe is the injectable `containerProbeFunc` seam (`func(service) (name, status, err)`); `errContainerNotFound` (no labelled container) drives auto-mode's fall-through to `docker compose run`. **One-shot mode** (`-c, --command "<cmd>"`): parallel dispatch path via `dispatchShell` (called from `RunE`) → branches on `shellCLIFlags.command`; non-empty routes to `runOneShotCommand` → `dockerExecOneShot` / `composeRunOneShot` (no info banner, no compose-run hardcoded `-it`); empty routes to existing interactive `runServicesCLI`. TTY auto-detect via package-level `ttyDetector` seam (defaults to `widgets.IsInteractiveFn`, swapped in tests). **TTY resolution is a tri-state `ttyMode` (auto / on / off), not a bool**: `--tty`/`-t` and `--no-tty` map to on/off via `resolveTTYMode` in `RunE`, which rejects the two together; absent flags leave auto. `oneShotTTYMode` makes auto mean **off** for `-c` (unlike the interactive path, which auto-detects) so a piped `-c` stays byte-clean — at the cost of the child block-buffering, which is exactly what `--tty` exists to override. `dockerExecTTYFlags(mode)` returns `["-i","-t"]` / `["-i"]` and **drops `-i` under a forced TTY with non-terminal stdin** (docker refuses `-i -t` there); `composeRunTTYFlags(mode)` returns `["-i"]` or `["-i","-T"]` (compose run allocates a TTY by default — `-T` disables). Compose cannot honour a forced TTY with non-terminal stdin at all: `composeTTYUnavailable` detects it and `warnComposeTTYUnavailable` degrades to the non-TTY vector with a warning written to **stderr only** — stdout may be piped into a consumer that must not see it. Child exit codes flow through cobra/fang via `*shellCommandExitError{code, underlying}` (extracts from `*exec.ExitError` in shared `wrapExitError`); `cmd/dwe/main.go:75-81` extracts `interface{ ExitCode() int }` and calls `os.Exit`, and `errHandler` at main.go:40-44 suppresses Fang's `Error:` banner for `ExitCode`-bearing errors so the user sees only the child's stdout/stderr. The global root `-c` shorthand was dropped (only `--config` long form remains) to free `-c` for `shell`.
- `internal/cli/logs/` — `dwe logs <service>` for streaming container logs by service name (canonical entry point — use it instead of `docker logs <container>` directly, since it handles container-name resolution via `daemon.ResolveContainerName`). Files: `logs.go` (`NewCmd` + `ResolveLogsTarget` exported helper + `runLogs` orchestrator), `parse.go` (NDJSON line parser for JSON mode). Flags: `--tail N` (default 50, 0 = all), `--since DUR|TS`, `-f/--follow`, plus the global `--output text|json` (NDJSON in JSON mode — one `{"ts","stream","msg"}` object per line). Read-only: no lock, no preflight. `ValidArgsFunction` uses `cmdctx.ServiceNameCompletion(flags)` so completion lists current project services. `NewCmd(groupID, flags)`.
- `internal/cli/status/` — `dwe status` group. `NewCmd(groupID, flags)` composes a default `RunE` rendering health + apps + tools + infra + deploy + topology + git + daemons in order; each section also addressable via subcommand (`status apps` / `tools` / `infra` / `deploy [svc]` / `topology` / `git` / `daemons`, one file per subcommand). Default-only `--no-apps` / `--no-tools` / `--no-infra` / `--no-deploy` / `--no-topology` / `--no-git` / `--no-daemons` flags suppress sections; a typed `section` enum + ordered list is the single source of truth for both the default orchestrator order and the `--no-*` flag set. The group does **not** use `PersistentPreRunE` (would replace the root project-resolution hook); instead `loadStatusContext(flags)` runs from each subcommand's `RunE` to load cfg/journal/tracked/topology on demand. `status deploy <svc>` uses `cobra.MaximumNArgs(1)` and `ValidArgsFunction` routed through `cmdctx.CompletionConfigPath` (`trackedServiceCompletion` lives in `deploy.go` since it is the sole caller). All stdout via `cmd.OutOrStdout()`; warnings via `cmd.ErrOrStderr()`. Test seams: `isTerminalFn`, `runStatusTUIFn`. The `PersistentPreRunE` avoidance generalizes: **cobra never chains the hook** — a child's `PersistentPreRunE` *replaces* the nearest ancestor's rather than running after it — so any grouped subtree that declares one silently loses the root's project resolution, style application, and locale setup. The sanctioned pattern for shared per-subtree setup is a plain loader helper invoked from each subcommand's own `RunE` (`loadStatusContext` is the reference implementation); apply it to every future command group, not just `status`.
- `internal/cli/snapshot/` — `dwe snapshot` tree (`list`, `current`, `inspect`, `create`, `restore`, `rollback`, `remove`, `pack`, `unpack`), one file per subcommand. `NewCmd(groupID, flags)` plus shared helpers in `snapshot.go` and `liveui.go` (the `newSnapshotLiveObserver` factory wiring `liveui.LiveLine` into the workflow runner's `StepObserverFactory`). `internal/core/workflow/snapshot` is imported as `snapshotpkg` to avoid collision with the cli package name.
- `internal/cli/validate/` — `dwe validate`. Single-file subtree. `NewCmd(groupID, flags)` plus internal helpers `loadForValidate`, `errPartialLoad`, `validationFailedError` (the canonical `ExitCode()`-bearing error). `docs generate --scope cli` works without a project, but `validate` requires either `Resolve` or `Locate` depending on `isValidateCommand` routing. **Filter hint (agent-discoverability milestone)**: `shouldEmitFilterHint(rows, errors, quiet, levelRaw) bool` + `emitFilterHint(...)` write ONE stderr info line after a long human-mode table, naming `--level` / `--quiet`. Threshold `filterHintThreshold = 20` rendered rows; suppressed when `--quiet` or `--level` is already set, when `rows == errors` (neither flag would remove anything), and in JSON mode. Same shape and constraints as `cmdctx.EmitDefaultNotice` — stderr only, stdout stays the parseable surface. The predicate is split from the emitter so it is unit-testable without a cobra command.
- `internal/cli/command/` — `dwe commands [id]` (alias `cmd`), the single user-command surface. No positional id → cmdbrowser selector; exact id → direct run; `-i/--inspect <id>` prints the resolved definition (mutually exclusive with `--set` / `--yes`); `--set k=v` (repeatable, `StringArrayVar`) prefills the param form. Files: `command.go` (NewCmd), `runbyid.go` (orchestrator — see below), `list.go` (browser/tree rendering, `selectorTitle`), `inspect.go` (`printInspect`/`printInspectAt`/`inspectStepDescription`), `completion.go` (`registryIDCompletion`), `daemonset.go` (`daemonSetCompletion` for `command --set` flag completion). Orchestrator `runCommandByID(ctx, stdin/stdout/stderr, cfg, reg, projectRoot, id, runOpts)` owns the run pipeline: `reg.Get` → private guard → `prepareParams` (= `resolve.ParamDefaults` raw-string prefill, distinct from `resolve.Params` at `BuildRunContext`, + `resolve.Options` membership validation for `--set`/default values, extracted so the browser closures can reuse the exact rules) → `buildAskFields + ask.Run` (huh form for interactive prompting; skipped when all required params pre-satisfied or non-interactive) → `BuildRunContext` → attach I/O channels → render confirmation title against `rctx.Render` → `widgets.ConfirmRun` with `stringifyParams(rctx.Params)` summary → set `rctx.SkipConfirm` to prevent re-prompt → `usercommands.RunCommand`. **In-TUI param-form short-circuit (commands-param follow-up)** — `runOpts.PrefilledParams map[string]string` (nil on `dwe commands <id>` direct / `--set` / non-interactive / JSON paths — untouched) carries the params harvested by the browser's in-TUI overlay: `prepareParams` still runs UNCONDITIONALLY (its membership validation is the idempotent safety net), but when `PrefilledParams != nil` `showForm` is forced false and `values = PrefilledParams`, so `buildAskFields`/`runAsk` are skipped and the harvested map flows straight through; `BuildRunContext`'s `resolve.Params` is still the final validate. **Confirm stays post-exit** — `def.Confirmation` shows its huh yes/no after teardown (right before the run banner + streamed output), never in the overlay. The browser wiring lives in `list.go`: `makeBrowserSelector` takes the raw `setFlags []string` (parsed LAZILY inside the closures via `parseSetFlags` — eager parse would break the non-interactive `writeCommandsList` fallback which returns before `runCommandByID`) + a `prefilledOut *map[string]string` out-param (set from `Result.Values`, mirroring `skipConfirmOut`/`forceFormOut`); `makeRunFormSpec` builds the `cmdbrowser.RunFormSpec` (nil outside ModeRun) whose `BuildForm` recomputes the same `showForm` predicate then `ask.Build(..., RunOptions{ShowHelp:&false})` (returns `(nil,nil)` for no-form / zero-field empty-options commands → quit-and-run) and `Harvest` maps `ask.Result` → the param map via `mergeAnswers`. Test seams: package-level vars `runAsk` / `confirmRun` / `runUserCommand` / `notifyContext` (`signal.NotifyContext`) — subtests overriding any of these MUST NOT call `t.Parallel()`. The TUI `y` toggle is folded into `runOpts.Yes` at the call site as `yesFlag || skipFromTUI`. `runbyid.go` (not `run.go`) disambiguates from `lifecycle/run.go`.
- `internal/cli/docs/` — `dwe docs` tree. Files: `docs.go` (NewCmd + TUI launch + `docsSelectorTitle`), `generate.go` (the huge `newDocsGenerateCmd` block: `runDocsGenerate`, `validateDocsFlags`, `resolveFormats`, `resolveScopes`, `genCLIDocs`, `genHiddenCLI*`, `walkAllCommands`, `genCLIIndex`, `writeCLIIndexEntries`, `genRegistryDocs`, `genRegistryMarkdown`, `writeCommandMarkdown`, `genCommandsIndex`, `genTopLevelIndex`, `mmdcAvailable`), `cache.go`, `export.go`, `list.go`, `search.go`, `show.go`, `llmstxt.go` (newDocsLlmsTxtCmd + runDocsLlmsTxt for `dwe docs llms-txt`), `llmstxt_collectors.go` (project-data collectors — `collectServiceSummaries`, `collectCommandSummaries`, `collectInfoSummary` — kept in cli layer so `internal/core/docs/llmstxt/` stays pure-function and config-import-free). `internal/core/docs` aliased as `coredocs` in files that need both names. `docs generate --scope cli` works without a project; `--scope all` / `--scope commands` requires a project. Docs subsystem is read-only — never calls `lock.AcquireProjectLocks` and never runs preflight; resolves locale **directly** via `i18n.ResolveLocale(flagLang, cfgLang, sysLang)` (NOT `rflags.Locale` — clamped to YAML store, wrong namespace for markdown). `llmstxt_collectors.go` also carries `collectBuiltinSummaries` / `collectConditionSummaries` (agent-discoverability milestone) — `cli/docs` is the ONLY layer that may import `internal/core/execution/...`; the data reaches the generator through `Opts.Builtins` / `Opts.Conditions`, never through an import in `core/docs`. `search.go` carries the `--literal` flag and renders the append-only 4th TSV column (snippet); `--literal` is a FLAG, not shell quoting. **`docs search` ranking** (`search.go`): tokens are AND-ed and ranked by the **MIN per-token match count**, never a sum — a sum lets the commonest word carry a document that barely matches the rare one. The per-document tier is a tie-break strictly BELOW that count: ordering by tier outright buried the strongest answer, and invisibly so at a narrowed `--limit`. Matching is **substring**, deliberately (a `service_dirs_ensure` fragment must find the builtin), and the snippet is sanitized (non-printables dropped, whitespace collapsed) and capped **including** its ellipsis, so the append-only 4th TSV column can never split into a fifth field.
- `internal/cli/completion/` — install/uninstall subcommands attached to cobra's auto-generated `completion` parent via `AttachInstallUninstall(parent, flags)`. Files: `completion.go` (Attach + shared `completionExitError`, `isSupportedShell`, `supportedShells`, `ErrUnsupportedShell`), `install.go` (`newInstallCompletionCmd` + path/profile/atomic-write helpers + seam vars `resolvePowerShellProfile`/`completionHomeDir`/`completionReadFile`), `uninstall.go` (`newUninstallCompletionCmd` + `runUninstall` + `emitZshFpathHint`). In `root.go` the wiring is `root.InitDefaultCompletionCmd()` → `root.Find(["completion"])` → set `GroupID = groupAdvanced` → `completion.AttachInstallUninstall(completionCmd, flags)`.
- `internal/cli/docker/` — `dwe docker` passthrough (351 LoC of subprocess + arg-policy plumbing). `NewCmd(groupID, flags)`. `internal/shared/docker` aliased as `dockerpkg` inside the file because the package itself is now named `docker`. **Build-base prepull hook** (`build.prepull_bases`, gated on `p.dockerCfg.Build.PrepullBases`): both the `build` and `up` `RunE`s call the same-package `prepullBases(errOut io.Writer, compose *dockerpkg.Compose, services []string, force bool)` helper AFTER `newDockerPipeline` (`.env` must already be regenerated — `compose config` depends on it) and BEFORE `compose.Exec`. `prepullBases` is the one CLI-side place that prints user-facing text for this feature (`fmt.Fprintf(errOut, "warning: ...\n")`, `errOut = cmd.ErrOrStderr()`); it never returns an error — `DeriveBuildBases` failing, an individual `PullImage` failing on an already-present base, etc. all degrade to a warning and execution always proceeds to the normal `compose build`/`up` (trap: enabling the flag must never make a build worse than leaving it off). The one loud warning is a **missing** base that also failed to pull — it names the ref and states the build will likely fail. `resolveBuildInvocation` gained a `prepull bool` param: `force && prepull` emits only `--no-cache` for compose (daemon-side prepull already re-pulled the bases unconditionally); every other `(force, prepull)` combination is byte-identical to pre-flag behavior. `build`'s service filter narrows prepull derivation to the named args; `up` always derives against every service (nil filter) since `up` builds transitive dependencies too.
- `internal/cli/compose/` — `dwe compose` passthrough. `NewCmd(groupID, flags)`. Split from `docker/` to avoid the stuttering `docker.NewComposeCmd` (see Naming rationale).
- `internal/cli/deploy/` — `dwe deploy` tree. `NewCmd(groupID, flags)` exposed; `deploy.go` hosts the entry point (renamed from `root.go` for symmetry). Bare `dwe deploy` (no subcommand) in a TTY opens an interactive menu via huh's `NewSelect` with options Wizard (first, only on fresh projects) / Run (all) / Run service… / Plan (all) / Plan service… / Exit. Wizard runs only on fresh projects (missing `local.yml` or empty) when setup questions are present OR port conflicts exist; orchestration in `internal/core/workflow/setup/`. Non-TTY `dwe deploy` prints help and exits 2. **The age-identity gate sits at `runDeployMenu`'s entry** — after the TTY check, BEFORE the menu's single `LoadConfigOrWrap` — in the `ensureIdentity(cmd, flags, baseDir)` helper over the `keygateEnsureFn` seam: one site, no reload, and every menu action beyond `plan` needs plaintext anyway (`plan` is gated too, deliberately — a plan built without the key would print commands derived from `<encrypted>`). The raw layers are loaded with the error dropped, so a broken `workspace.yml` reaches the gate as nil layers and `LoadConfigOrWrap` below still reports today's `loading config: …` message; `keygate`'s three sentinels all map to the typed `secrets_no_identity` with `secrets.IdentityHint`, and the recipient detail and hint are attached only when `config.RecipientFromLayers` actually yields one (`IdentityHint("")` would print a path with an empty stem). `runPreWizardPreflight` is untouched: it remains the non-interactive wall behind the offer. Subcommands `plan`, `run`, `state` are non-interactive CLI. `deploy step` is removed entirely — no alias or shim. `cli/deploy/menu.go` uses package-private `runDeployRunFn`/`runDeployPlanFn` rather than dispatching into `cli/lifecycle/`. `RunHelper` is consumed by `cli/service/` (documented pre-existing cross-import).
- `internal/cli/lifecycle/` — multi-export Go-domain grouping for `run`/`stop`/`restart`/`reset`. Files: `lifecycle.go` (package preamble + `var preflightRun = preflight.Run` test seam — **only `reset.go` uses the seam**; `stop.go` calls `preflight.Run` directly and `run.go`/`restart.go` route through `deploy.RunHelper`), `run.go` / `stop.go` / `restart.go` / `reset.go`. Exports: `NewRunCmd`, `NewStopCmd`, `NewRestartCmd`, `NewResetCmd` (each `(groupID, flags)`). `stop.go` also defines `StopService(ctx, StopServiceDeps, name)` / `stopServiceLocked(ctx, StopServiceDeps, name)` (per-service docker-stop bypass for `dwe stop <name>` — compose overlay is filtered out for disabled services, so direct `docker stop` is required; locked variant for callers that already hold project locks). Per-service reset does NOT use `stopServiceLocked` — it drives stop+rm through the synthetic-pipeline `docker_stop_remove_container` builtin instead. `restart.go` also defines `RestartService(ctx context.Context, baseDir string, cfg *config.DweConfig, name string, out io.Writer) error` (per-service docker-restart bypass for `dwe restart <name>` — no preflight, no project locks; `docker.ErrNoSuchContainer` maps to a user-actionable "run `dwe deploy run` first" error; `var restartContainerFn = docker.RestartContainer` seam for test injection). Lifecycle commands acquire project locks **after** preflight succeeds so `type: command` checks do not run under operation locks. `internal/core/workflow/lifecycle` aliased as `lifecyclepkg` in `run.go`/`restart.go`/`stop.go`. `info.Run` consumed via the sibling import documented above. **`reset --clear-generated`**: clears the `generatedstore` (scoped by `--service` via `ClearService` / all via `ClearAll` + `Save`) **only after the FULL reset succeeds — including the post-pipeline journal cleanup** (`journal.Remove` for full reset / `journal.ReplaceServiceWithPending` per-service), never merely after `RunWithOptions` returns; a pipeline OR journal-mutation failure leaves the store untouched (else a deployed-journal + empty-store mismatch would make the run gate trust a service with no secrets). Full reset has NO command-level confirm to hook (confirmation is a pipeline step), so the interactive "also clear N generated values?" prompt is decided at the command level up front (default No); per-service reuses the existing `resetConfirmFn` seam. Default behavior unchanged — store preserved. The per-service restart bypass is deliberately *lighter* than argument-less `dwe restart` (which runs the full stop lifecycle then the full run lifecycle): it skips compose, lifecycle hooks, preflight and project locks entirely and issues a single `docker restart`, so do not "restore" preflight/locks there. Per-service reset likewise never removes volumes — that stays opt-in per service by adding `docker_remove_project_volumes` to `workspace/services/<name>/reset.yml`.
- `internal/cli/render/` — `dwe render` tree (env / ide / ai / git / **config**). `NewCmd(groupID, flags)`; entry point in `render.go` (renamed from `root.go` for symmetry). `config.go` adds `dwe render config [service]` (optional positional → one service; none → all eligible **app** services via `DeployOrder(cfg, []string{"app"})` — config rendering is app-only, only apps declare `dir`/`render`/`generated`) driving `templates/config.RenderConfigs`; `--harvest` switches to a **harvest-only** pass (`HarvestGenerated`, NO render) for bootstrapping an already-committed value into the store. The default render path is read-only wrt locks (no preflight, no project locks); `--harvest` mutates the shared store, so it acquires project locks first (via `cmdctx.AcquireProjectLocksOrReport`, mirroring the deploy harvest builtin and `reset --clear-generated`) before loading the store. Resolves the store path from baseDir + `generatedstore.DefaultRelPath`; cli is the single stdout writer. Opt-in — no `dwe init` scaffold wiring (matches the user-authored copy mechanism it replaces). **Container reachability**: `render config` is a nested bridge exception (so a container can regenerate config after `vars set`); the other render subcommands (env/ide/ai/git) stay host-only. Because `--harvest` MUTATES host `.dwe/generated.yml` (a write path that bypasses `bridge.vars_writable`), `config.go` rejects `--harvest` with typed `render_harvest_host_only` when `bridgeclient.InContainer()` — only the read-only render is container-reachable.
- `internal/cli/vars/` — `dwe vars` tree (registered under `groupConfiguration` alongside `service`/`render`/`validate`/`scaffold`). `NewCmd(groupID, flags)` plus `get.go` / `list.go` / `inspect.go` / `set.go` / `browser.go`. `get <var>` (effective value — scalar or YAML subtree), `list [namespace]` (flat `vars.*` leaves, namespace-filterable, mirrors `commands list`), `inspect <var>` (per-layer `Default`/`Local`/`Current` via `config.ResolveLayeredPath` + usages via `varsusage.ScanUsages` → `uirender.VarInspectView`), `set <var> [value]` (writes `local.yml` via the comment-preserving node path; `vars.*` path-confined; coerces via `varsusage.CoerceScalar`; acquires project locks via `cmdctx.AcquireProjectLocksOrReport` (symmetry with the `services` toggle that shares the writer/file — no preflight, not a lifecycle mutation); pre-state-bytes rollback; reloads config; no `value` + interactive → huh form via the `runAsk = ask.Run` seam, JSON/non-interactive with no value → `vars_value_required`). **Container-write gate** lives here (runtime, since `bridgeCommandAllowed` is prefix-wide): when invoked from a container (`bridgeclient.InContainer()`), the target must match `config.VarsWritableAllows(cfg.Bridge.VarsWritable, target)` else typed `vars_not_container_writable`; from the host `set` is unrestricted. Bare `dwe vars` → TUI browser (`browser.go`, `cmdbrowser` in `ModeEdit`); `!IsInteractive || cmdctx.NonInteractiveEnv()` or a namespace arg or JSON mode → `vars list`. Completion via `cmdctx.CompletionConfigPath`. **In-TUI edit-and-stay** (TUI Stage 7): the ≥80-col frame path wires `cmdbrowser.Options.Edit` via `newVarsEditSpec(flags, leaves, inspectCache)` — `BuildForm(idx)` opens the shared `set` form (`ask.Build`, `RunOptions.ShowHelp:false`, fields from `buildVarSetFields` carrying the layered `varSetFormDescription` + a `varsusage.CoerceScalar` `Validate` probe — invalid scalars/maps/sequences rejected **in-form**, an improvement over the old post-submit error, though the post-submit coercion stays authoritative), and `Commit(idx, res)` coerces → `writeVarOverrideSilent` (`cmdctx.AcquireProjectLocksSilent` → the shared `writeVarOverrideCore` — capture / apply overlay / atomic write / reload / restore-on-failure, extracted from `writeVarOverride`) → `delete(inspectCache, path)` → rebuilds the one `Item` (fresh value + `layerBadge` + `Inspect` closure via `buildVarBrowserItem`) → `CommitOutcome{Item, Flash}`. A held lock is **returned as the error flash, never printed** (alt-screen is live). `writeVarOverride` keeps the printing `cmdctx.AcquireProjectLocksOrReport` wrapper over the same core for the CLI path. **Behaviour change:** after an in-TUI edit the browser stays open with the row refreshed and a `✓ …` status flash — the old `✓ set …` stdout line is gone (nothing is printed on exit); edit-and-stay applies **only** to the frame path — the <80-col fallback keeps the flat exit→`runVarsSet`→reopen loop. Standalone `vars set <path>` (no value) and the JSON / non-interactive paths are unchanged (plus the new inline field validation on the interactive form). The container check at the `vars set` call site is `bridgeclient.InContainer()`, the *equivalent but separate* detector to `bridgeInvokedFromContainer()` in `internal/cli/bridgepolicy.go` used by the prefix-wide command allowlist — two call sites reading the same `DWE_INVOKED_FROM` contract, so a change to one must be mirrored in the other or the read gate and the write gate disagree about what "in a container" means. **Encrypted state (`secrets.go`)**: the `Encrypted` flag is derived from the leaf's **origin** layer — `layerBadge`'s highest-precedence-non-nil scan, then a `cfg.SecretsState.UnresolvedAt(layer, path)` lookup — never from "any layer holds a marker", so a `defaults.yml` marker shadowed by a plaintext `local.yml` value renders plaintext while a `local.yml` marker over a plaintext default renders `<encrypted>`. `UnresolvedAt` matches through `config.PathCovers` (equality **or** a dot-boundary prefix), not equality: `varsusage.EnumerateVars` stops at a SEQUENCE and reports the leaf as `vars.tokens` while the decrypt walk records each element as `vars.tokens.0`, so an exact match reported a masked value as `encrypted: false` in `vars list` while `vars inspect` (whose flag comes from `MaskSecretValue`) said true. `cli/vars`' `secretNote` uses the same helper for the same reason. `list` / `inspect` / `get` / the browser all mask through `uirender.MaskSecretValue`; `get` recurses into a namespace subtree, and the no-value `set` form's `inlineFormValue` description masks too (the form must never offer a marker as an editable-looking current value). The `secret:` note wording lives in one `unresolvedNote` / `identitySourceDisplay` pair keyed off the stable `config.Reason*` constants, so an unknown future reason degrades to a generic line rather than an empty note. `vars set` gains a stderr-only (never JSON) shadow warning when the path it overrides holds a marker in a lower layer. ⚠️ `vars inspect` performs one extra `LoadLayersWithSecrets` read (`secretsStateFor`) purely for that note, because `ResolveLayeredPath` returns no state; the browser threads `cfg.SecretsState` through instead, so its per-leaf overlays add no reads.
- `internal/cli/secrets/` — `dwe secrets` tree (registered under `groupConfiguration` next to `vars`). `NewCmd(groupID, flags)` plus `init.go` / `status.go` / `set.go` / `get.go` / `files.go` (`encrypt`/`decrypt`) / `key.go` (`key export` / `import` / `list` / `remove`) / `rekey.go`, over a shared `secrets.go` holding `loadRawLayers`, the local aliases for the moved inventory (`markerRow` / `fileRow` / `inventory`, `var relToRoot = keygate.RelToRoot`) and a four-line `collectInventory` wrapper. **The scanner and the classification live in `internal/core/workflow/keygate/`** — `identitySet` moved there as the exported `IdentitySet`, so the gate, `key import`'s readability report and `status` cannot disagree; the aliases are what kept ~15 unrelated call sites in `init`/`set`/`rekey`/`files` untouched by the move. **Not in `bridgeAllowedTopLevel`** — no container can mint, rekey, export, import, list or remove a key, or add a secret (pinned by `bridgepolicy_test.go` cases, `key list`/`key remove` included: the policy matches the top-level name only). `key list` and `key remove` are the two entries in `allowedWithoutProject` (the keys directory is machine-wide, not project-scoped; pinned positively AND negatively by `TestAllowedWithoutProject_KeyHousekeeping`, since `key import`/`key export`/`status` must still require a project). Writers (`init`, `set`, `encrypt`, `key import`, `key remove`, `rekey`) take `cmdctx.AcquireProjectLocksOrReport` — `key remove` only when a project is resolved, because outside one there is nothing to race — and run **no preflight** (config edits, not stack mutations); `status`, `get`, `decrypt`, `key export`, `key list` are read-only. **`keygate.IdentitySet` is the single classifier**: the configured identity first, then every straggler keyfile from `secrets.LoadAnyIdentity`, so a half-rekeyed tree is reported and read per value instead of failing wholesale; `CheckMarker` runs FIRST so a damaged payload reads as `corrupt` even on a machine that holds the key. A row only a STRAGGLER opened keeps its readable state but carries `reasonStaleKey` (`stale_key`), and `statusView` derives the renderer's green/amber `OK` as `state-is-readable && Reason == ""` — WITHOUT that qualifier `status` printed a half-rekeyed tree entirely green while `LoadConfig` (which tries the configured identity alone) recorded `wrong_identity` and `secrets.unresolved` blocked every lifecycle command, i.e. it said "nothing to do" in the one recovery scenario `rekeyResumeHint` sends the user to it for. `keygate.Inventory` (behind the `collectInventory` wrapper) reads markers through `config.CollectMarkers` (never a private walk, so it cannot disagree with the loader about sequence indices or key order) and discovers `*.age` under `workspace/templates/config/**` with `pathsafe.ContainedRel` + `CheckNoSymlinks` + regular-file `Lstat` — a candidate that fails the discipline is REPORTED as `not decryptable` with the refusal as its reason rather than skipped, since a symlinked "secret" is exactly what a status report must not swallow (`filepath.WalkDir` never follows symlinks, so the scan cannot leave the tree). **`key import` has three input branches and one verdict**: `resolveIdentity` picks `--file` → a piped stdin (`readIdentityText`) → the hidden form (`promptIdentityFn`, defaulting to `secretsprompt.PromptIdentity` over the cobra streams), and all three end in the shared `identityMismatchError`, so "valid key, wrong project" is worded once. The branch point is the RESOLVER, not the reader, because the form yields a `secrets.Identity` and not text. Only the interactive branch pre-checks `Stat(KeyfilePath)`: the write is `O_EXCL`, and opening a form whose write is already doomed would make a developer hand over a private key for nothing — `--file`/stdin keep the historical parse → recipient → `O_EXCL` order (`TestKeyImport_RejectsMismatch` pins it). `--output json` at a TTY and `DWE_NONINTERACTIVE` keep refusing with `secrets_identity_source_required`; a cancelled form is `secrets_import_cancelled`. The raw layers are loaded ONCE (`requireRecipient` → `loadRawLayers` + `recipientOrErr`) and reused for the post-import `Inventory`, so the `markers_readable` / `files_readable` counters describe the same tree the recipient check ran against; a failed scan degrades to zeroes rather than turning a written keyfile into a command failure. **`key list` / `key remove` are keyfile housekeeping, not project state**: `list` renders `secrets.ListKeyfiles` through `render.SecretsKeyList` with the `current project` relation folded INTO the state cell (a row has both a state and a project relation, and a fourth column would be empty on every machine-wide key), the FILE column carrying the base name under a `Directory` header line while JSON keeps the absolute path; `currentRecipient` calls `config.LoadRawLayers` **directly**, not `loadRawLayers`, because a listing must not fail over `ValidateLayerRoots` on a config it only consults to mark one row. `remove` resolves `secrets.KeyfilePath(recipient)` only — a `misnamed` file is never targeted by a command aimed at the recipient it happens to hold — refuses the current project's identity without `--force` (`secrets_key_in_use`), refuses without a way to ask and without `--yes` (`secrets_confirmation_required`), and treats a declined `runConfirm` as a finished command (`kept <path>`, exit 0) rather than a failure. **Ordering invariants**: `init` writes the keyfile as the FIRST mutation (a committed recipient with no readable identity locks the project out of its own secrets) and removes it again if the `workspace.yml` write fails, so a re-run is not blocked by `WriteKeyfile`'s no-clobber guard; `set` resolves the value BEFORE taking the locks (a hidden `ask.FieldPassword` prompt can sit open indefinitely) and re-reads the recipient UNDER the lock (so a concurrent `rekey` cannot retire it mid-command); `rekey` follows the decision-11 sequence — read-only decrypt+validate of everything → new keyfile as the first mutation (old kept) → re-encrypt files then layers via `local.Splicer.ReplaceScalars` + `Splicer.Write` with the per-file policy → `secrets.recipient` LAST, so an interrupted run converges on a re-run and its failures carry `rekeyResumeHint` while read-only refusals carry `written: false`. The read-only pass also runs `dryRunSplices`, rehearsing every phase-4/5 edit on throwaway `Splicer`s (each marker re-encrypted to the CURRENT recipient — age ciphertext length is a function of the plaintext, not of which X25519 recipient it is sealed to, so the probe splices at the real width): decryptability alone does not imply splice-ability, and a block-scalar or flow-mapping marker decrypts fine and would refuse only in phase 4 — after the new keyfile exists and every `.age` source is already sealed to a key pair that was never exported, which a commit of that tree makes permanent for everyone else. **All three writers (`init`, `set`, `rekey`) go through the `local` SPLICE writer, not the node writer** — a secrets edit's diff must be reviewable on a hand-annotated layer file. Its three refusal sentinels map onto one typed code through the shared `spliceUnsupportedError` / `spliceWriteError` pair in `secrets.go`: both branch on `errors.Is` BEFORE the command's own `cmdctx.ErrWrap`, because `ErrWrap` builds a NEW outer `CodedError` and the JSON serializer reports the OUTERMOST code — wrapping first buries `secrets_write_unsupported` under `secrets_recipient_write_failed` / `secrets_rekey_failed`. `layerWritePolicy(path, layers)` is the shared "which layer file is this" derivation returning BOTH the writer label and the mode policy (workspace.yml by position since `--config` may point anywhere; the two overlays by base name). The file helpers spell the output flag `--out`, not `-o` (the root owns `-o` for `--output`), apply the path discipline to both ends but containment only inside the project, refuse output == input, and chmod **tighten-only** (a decrypted plaintext target is forced to 0600 even when it already existed at 0644; an overwritten ciphertext file keeps its repository mode). Every command has a typed DTO through `cmdctx.WriteData` and returns typed `cmdctx.Err`/`ErrWrap`; `status` renders its TEXT report from the same `statusJSON` the JSON mode encodes, so the two surfaces cannot report different rows. User-visible schema: `docs/reference/config/secrets.md`.
- `internal/cli/service/` — `dwe services` tree. `NewCmd(groupID, flags)`. Bare `dwe services` opens a single multi-select toggle in TTY text mode across every optional service of any type with mandatory items pre-checked/disabled and a `Type` column visible in the table; non-TTY stdin OR `--output json` dispatches to a read-only list renderer (`service/service_list.go`): text mode emits Apps / Tools / Infra sections reusing `stack.RenderApps`/`RenderTools`/`RenderInfra`, JSON mode emits a unified `{"services":[{name,type,container_name,mandatory,enabled,running,status,ports?,hosts?}, …]}` array. All-mandatory short-circuit before opening the form; `services enable <name>` / `services disable <name>` look up the service by name across all types (no `--type` flag); `enable <mandatory>` warns and exits 0; `disable <mandatory>` errors and exits non-zero; completion via `completeDisabledOptional` / `completeEnabledOptional` filters span the union across all service types. **`dwe tools` is dropped** — no alias, no shim; cobra emits the standard "unknown command" error. `service/service_plan.go` contains `buildTogglePlan(cfg, registry, svcDeploys, toggles) (TogglePlan, error)` (pure plan builder, no filesystem access), `executeTogglePlan(ctx, deps ExecuteDeps, plan TogglePlan, opts ExecuteOptions) error` (orchestrates before/apply/after steps via stubbed runtimes `RunDeploy`/`RunRestart`/`RunUserCmd`), `runDeployHelper(ctx, cmd, flags, opts DeployOpts) error` (extracted from `deployRunCmd` for reuse by the toggle executor; `DeployOpts.Services []string` supports nil=full-project, one=single-service, N=multi-service batch). `TogglePlan{BeforeSteps, ApplySteps []ApplyStep, AfterSteps, Notes}`. `ApplyStep{Kind PendingKind, Services []string}`. `mutateAndPlan`/`mutateAndPlanBatch` now write `local.yml` via `local.ServiceTogglesOverlay` → `local.ApplyOverlayToNode` → `local.WriteLocalYAMLNode` (the comment-preserving node path shared with `vars set`/setup), replacing the load-map → mutate-map → `WriteLocalYAML` flow; validation (`ValidateServiceToggle`) and pre-state-bytes rollback are preserved. The pre-existing `cli/service/` → `cli/deploy.RunHelper` cross-import is documented and out of scope to fix here. **Pending-state gate.** A toggle without `--apply` writes `local.yml` and regenerates `.env` (`envfile.Write`) immediately, then records the deferred work as journal pending — but only through the `journalSnapshot.everDeployed` gate (`journalSnapshot` / `loadJournalSnapshot` in `service_toggle.go`). Note the shape: pending is **project-level** `journal.ProjectState.Pending *PendingApply`, with the affected services carried inside `PendingOp.Services []string` — there is no per-service pending field. `everDeployed` is true when ANY service state is non-empty and not `StatusNotDeployed` (so `StatusDeployed` / `StatusFailed` / `StatusInProgress` / `StatusPartial` all count), OR `state.Project.LastRun != nil`, OR `state.Project.Status` is non-empty and not `StatusNotDeployed` (the project-only-deploy case, where `state.Services` is empty). A journal **load error** also yields `everDeployed = true`: silently skipping the pending write on a corrupt file would drop the toggle's intent invisibly, whereas letting `AddPendingOps` run means the file is either repaired or the command hard-fails with the corruption in the message. The gate is therefore closed only when there has been NO deploy attempt at all — pending means "config drifted since deploy" and is meaningless before the first one. With the gate closed the flow both skips the pending write AND suppresses the stack probe, the "stack is not running; hooks and pending state were skipped" banner and the apply prompt, printing instead a single "stack has not been deployed yet; run `dwe deploy` to apply." line after the rendered plan — emitted regardless of plan shape, because even a `RequiresNone` toggle takes effect only at the first deploy. `--apply` is the exception: it still runs the docker probe on a never-deployed stack so a broken docker setup surfaces as an early warning instead of a deeper executor error, and it always executes (the apply step is itself what brings the stack up). After a successful apply, `executeTogglePlan` clears pending with `journal.ClearPendingOps` under a fresh `lock.AcquireProjectLocks`, passing the `[]journal.PendingClear` built by `buildPendingClears(contributors)` — never `ClearPending`, which would erase pending recorded by other sessions.
- `internal/cli/scaffold/` — `dwe init` (the bootstrap command — creates a project rather than acting inside one). `NewCmd(groupID, flags)`. **Package name `scaffold`, command `Use: "init"`** — `init` is a reserved Go function name and an illegal package name, so both the cli and core packages are named `scaffold`. Owns flag parsing, mode selection, the huh form, and result reporting; all disk work is delegated to `internal/core/workflow/scaffold` (this package is the only writer to stdout/stderr). **Mode selection**: interactive iff `widgets.IsInteractiveFn(os.Stdin) && !useDefaults && flags.Output != "json"` (the `-d/--default` flag takes all defaults; `-f/--force` recreates/overwrites). Flags pre-fill the form's field defaults and the **entire form is collected before any disk write**, so a mid-form `Ctrl-C` (surfaced as `widgets.ErrCancelled`) leaves the disk untouched and exits 0. The form is behind a package-level `runFormFn` seam (mirrors `cli/command/runbyid.go`'s `runAsk`) so the abort path and value-mapping are testable without a real TTY; flag→`core.Options` mapping and output shaping stay in plain functions outside the seam. **Existing-project gate**: before any write, if `core.HasProjectConfig(core.ResolveTarget(targetDir))` reports a pre-existing `workspace.yml` in the *target itself* (distinct from the ancestor `NestedWarning` check) and `--force` is not set, the run requires explicit consent — interactive confirmation via the `confirmRecreateFn` seam (returns `widgets.ErrCancelled` on Esc/Ctrl-C → clean exit), or a `scaffold_project_exists` error in non-interactive mode telling the user to pass `--force`. On confirmation it sets `force=true` and recreates everything; declining is a clean no-op. **Field validation**: `validateName`/`validatePrefix`/`validateAccent`/`brandTextValidator` run both as per-field huh validators AND, via `validateInput`, on the final resolved values before scaffolding — so flag-driven (non-interactive) inputs are checked too (accent must be 6-digit `#RRGGBB`; prefix lowercase `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*`; branding text single-line, no control chars). Name resolution precedence: `--name` → positional `[name]` → cwd basename; a positional `[name]` also sets the target dir to `./<name>/`. JSON output shape: `{target, created, skipped, symlink_fallback, nested_warning}` via `cmdctx.WriteData`; errors via typed `cmdctx.Err`/`ErrWrap`. **No preflight, no project locks** (there is no project yet) — `init` is in `allowedWithoutProject` keyed on the full path `"dwe init"` (not bare `"init"`, which `cmd.CommandPath()` never yields).
- `internal/cli/bridge/` — `dwe bridge` subtree (Advanced group): `start.go` / `stop.go` / `status.go` / `logs.go` plus the hidden `bridge daemon` foreground entry (`daemon.go`) that the lifecycle ensure spawns. `NewCmd(groupID, flags)`; every subcommand is `cobra.NoArgs`. `bridge daemon --project-root <abs>` is in `allowedWithoutProject` (it takes everything from the flag; cwd discovery must not gate a detached spawn) and redirects its own stderr/stdout to `.dwe/bridge/daemon.log` via the spawner. **Every daemon-touching call goes through a same-package seam** (`ensureDaemonFn` / `stopDaemonFn` / `probeDaemonFn` + an `init()` stub for the whole test binary, mirroring `cli/status`) — the production functions spawn/probe via `os.Executable()`, which from a test recursively re-executes the test binary (the documented recursion hazard). `start` refuses with typed `bridge_not_enabled` when no enabled service has bridge enabled (a daemon for zero bridged services is useless); `stop` stays an idempotent no-op and appends the not-enabled explanation to its not-running message. `status` reports liveness via `bridge.ProbeDaemon` (pidfile-flock probe; uptime = pidfile mtime, written exactly once by the daemon's own startup acquire), socket/port from the bridge-dir files (a stopped daemon shows neither), and shim state via `shimassets.Status`; everything through `cmdctx.WriteData` (JSON contract). `logs --tail N` defaults to 50, `0` = all, negative → `invalid_tail` (exit 2, same code as `dwe logs`); missing file → stderr notice in text mode, `{"lines":[]}` in JSON. From containers only `bridge status` is reachable (see Container command policy above). User-visible behavior: `docs/reference/concepts/bridge.md`.
- `internal/cli/test/` — `dwe test` tree (Pipelines group, integration-tests milestone — stages 1b through 3b): `run.go` (`newTestRunCmd`, `runTestRun`) and `list.go`, registered via `NewCmd(groupID, flags)` in `test.go`. `test` requires a project — absent from `allowedWithoutProject`, and absent from `bridgeAllowedTopLevel` (`bridgepolicy.go`), so it is blocked in container context with zero production code (pinned in `bridgepolicy_test.go`). `runTestRun` calls `envtest.ScrubComposeEnv()` as its first statement — before the run-level `signal.NotifyContext`, the `scenarioRunner` seam, or any subprocess — per the spec §3 ordering. `ScrubComposeEnv` unsets every `COMPOSE_*` variable in the process and deliberately leaves `DOCKER_*` untouched (daemon/context selection must still apply), so the disposable copy's compose invocations can never inherit an ambient `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME`/`COMPOSE_FILE` from the developer's shell; it is environment hygiene, unrelated to the bridge daemon's dangerous-env strip set (no trust boundary here). The canonical ordering rule is "before the per-scenario flock, any goroutine, UI, or subprocess" — which is why stage 3b's parallel entry point `runTest` scrubs again at its top (after only the `--parallel < 1` guard) before fanning out; the unset is idempotent, so the sequential path's second call in `runTestRun` is harmless. `scenarioRunner` is a one-method interface (`RunScenario(ctx, envtest.RunRequest) (*envtest.ScenarioResult, error)`) satisfied by `envtest.NewRunner()`; the package-level `newRunner` var is the test seam (a real `Runner` would spawn `dwe validate`/`dwe deploy run` subprocesses and recurse into the test binary). No args → every scenario from `envtest.ListScenarios` (sorted); explicit names are validated against that same list up front so an unknown name fails via a typed `cmdctx.Err("unknown_scenario", …)` before any scenario runs — `"unknown_scenario"` was added to `cmdctx.ExitCodeFor`'s usage-class switch (mirroring the existing `logs` package's `"invalid_since"` entry) rather than inventing a parallel exit-code mechanism. A scenario `RunScenario` refuses to even attempt (lock held, kept run, load failure — a bare `error`, no `*ScenarioResult`) is folded into the same result set as a synthetic `StatusError` outcome, so the batch keeps going and still reports every requested scenario. Exit codes are carried by a private `testRunOutcomeError{code}` whose `Error()` renders no text (same pattern as `deploy`'s `deployCancelledError`) — any `error`-status outcome → 2, else any `failed` → 1, else 0; the run's JSON/text payload is written via `cmdctx.WriteData` BEFORE this sentinel is returned, so main.go's `ExitCode()` path suppresses further stderr output and the already-written payload stays the sole stdout content. **JSON mode never leaks live pipeline output**: `flags.Output == "json"` swaps in `silentReporterFactory` (screen writer = `sharedrender.NewWriter(io.Discard)`, termOut = `io.Discard`, but the file log via `pipeline.OpenPipelineLog` still records) as the `envtest.RunRequest.ReporterFactory`, rather than hardwiring the runner to `os.Stdout` (`silentReporterFactory` is renamed from the old `jsonReporterFactory` because stage 3b's parallel text path reuses it too — see the stage-3b bullet below). JSON payload shape: `{scenarios: [{name, status, failed_step, duration_seconds, report_dir}], summary}` (`failed_step`/`report_dir` are `omitempty` — omitted for a passing scenario); text mode renders one line per scenario (status, failed step or prep-error message, duration, and — under `--keep` — the kept compose project name + copy path) followed by the same `buildSummary` line used by both modes (`"N passed, M failed (name: step \"…\"; …)"`). `flags.I18n`/`flags.Locale` thread into `envtest.RunRequest` so a scenario's own `type: command` steps keep the display-string contract; no new `ui.*` i18n keys were needed since `dwe test` introduces no TUI surface (verified against `internal/shared/i18n/translator.go` — that table is scoped to TUI/docs-browser strings, not a general per-command string table, matching every other plain CLI subcommand). `list.go` renders scenario name + verbatim `description:` (spec §8, never localized) via `envtest.LoadScenario` per discovered name; an absent `workspace/tests/` directory renders an empty list, not an error. **Stage 2 — `clean.go`** (`newTestCleanCmd`, `dwe test clean [scenario...]`, `--dry-run`): mirrors `run`'s composition — `envtest.ScrubComposeEnv()` FIRST (clean drives `compose down` via `Teardown`, same ordering rule as `run`), then the package-level `cleanFn = envtest.Clean` seam var (mirrors `newRunner`) builds and runs a `CleanRequest` from args/flags, rendered via `cmdctx.WriteData` — JSON `{dry_run, swept, skipped, failed, orphans}`, text = one summary line (`"swept N, skipped M (live), P failed, K orphan(s)"`, "would sweep" wording under `--dry-run`). Exit code uses the same write-payload-then-return-a-no-text-sentinel pattern as `testRunOutcomeError`: any `Failed` entry → 1 (the payload is still emitted so the caller can see which manifest failed); `Skipped`(live) and `Orphans` are NOT failures (exit 0); a hard `Clean` error (unreadable manifests dir) surfaces as a normal `cmdctx.Err`. `clean` is registered in `test.go` alongside `run`/`list` and inherits the whole `test` subtree's container-block (absent from `bridgeAllowedTopLevel`) with zero extra production code — `bridgepolicy_test.go` pins `dwe test clean` explicitly in addition to the top-level `test` entry. `run.go`'s `--keep` hint (`renderScenarioLine`) now points at `dwe test clean <scenario>` instead of "clean up manually"; `renderScenarioLine` also gained a `report: <dir>` line whenever `o.ReportDir != ""` (a failed, non-kept scenario), so the failure-report path is discoverable in the default text output the same way `report_dir` already was in JSON. Both are best-effort: `Runner.finish` sets `ReportDir` only when `CollectReport` returns without error (i.e. the report directory was created) — a failed scenario whose report dir could not even be created renders no `report:` line and an empty `report_dir`, and the artifacts inside a created report may themselves be partial/blank when a capture fails. **Stage 3b — `--parallel N` orchestration** (`run.go` + `livestatus.go`): the `--parallel` int flag (default 1) fans scenarios out; `N < 1` → typed `cmdctx.Err("invalid_parallel", …)` (exit 2) before anything runs. **Orchestration lives entirely in the CLI** (`envtest` stays UI-free — it only gained the coarse `Progress` seam) — `runTest` computes `effective = min(parallel, len(names))`, and `effective <= 1` takes the **byte-identical** sequential path (`runTestRun`, the pre-stage-3b loop). Only `effective > 1` enters `runTestParallel`: a plain `errgroup.Group` (NOT `errgroup.WithContext` — sibling scenarios must never cancel each other) with `SetLimit(effective)`, one goroutine per scenario in original order writing its outcome into `slots[i]` by index. **Goroutines ALWAYS return nil** — a `RunScenario` error becomes a per-scenario `StatusError` outcome exactly as the sequential path folds it, never a group cancellation; each goroutine's first statement is `if ctx.Err() != nil { return nil }` (a cancelled ctx before dispatch leaves the slot nil, matching the sequential `break`). After `Wait`, nil slots (scenarios never started due to Ctrl+C) are compacted out, so both text and JSON output stay deterministic and in name order regardless of completion order. The parallel path uses `silentReporterFactory` (the same silent factory JSON mode uses — per-scenario deploy/pipeline output lands in the copy's own run log only) and installs the aggregated display + a per-scenario `Progress` closure (`req.Progress = func(p) { display.Phase(i, p) }`) **only in text mode** (`flags.Output != "json"`); JSON mode at N>1 passes a nil display and the same silent factory with suppressed warnings — enforced by construction. **`livestatus.go`** — `runLiveStatus` wraps one `liveui.LiveLine` in block mode: constructor seeds every visible row via `SetBlockRowPending(i, name)` where `visibleRows = max(1, min(len(names), termHeight−3))` (via an injectable `termSize` seam; overflow scenarios beyond `visibleRows` report start/finish via framed `Println` lines instead of a row). `Started`/`Phase`/`Finished`/`Warn`/`Close` map to the block-row methods (running label `name  <phase>…`, final `SetBlockRowFinal` `name  passed` / `name  failed — step "X"`, footer `running k/n scenarios…`); `Warn` routes through `PrintlnDiag` (bytes land on the stderr diag writer, block stays intact). **Non-TTY degradation is explicit**: `Started`/`Finished` branch on `!s.enabled` and emit flat `scenario <name>: started` / `scenario <name>: <status>` `Println` lines for EVERY scenario (not just overflow) — the block-row methods are silent no-ops when the LiveLine is disabled, so relying on them would leave a piped/CI run mute until the final report. `runLiveStatus` holds its own mutex for the full body of every method: in disabled mode `LiveLine.Println`/`PrintlnDiag` write to the shared writer without the LiveLine mutex, so the display mutex serializes those cross-goroutine writes (nesting `s.mu` → `live.mu` is deadlock-free — LiveLine never calls back). `phaseLabels` maps each `envtest.ProgressPhase` to a plain-English label (`preparing…`, `validating…`, …) — the test CLI surface is intentionally non-localized. `fakeRunner`'s call recording is mutex-guarded so the parallel tests (and `make test-race`) can drive it from multiple goroutines. **Output styling (`style.go`)** — the run report, `list`, `clean`, and per-scenario warnings render through the `internal/core/ui/styles` (lipgloss) palette: accent scenario names, severity-colored status words (success/danger/warning), muted secondary text, and ✓/✗/• glyphs matching the aggregated live view. Styling is gated per output surface by `writerIsTTY(w io.Writer)` (true only for a `*os.File` TTY) — the color-off branch of every render helper is **byte-identical** to the historical plain form, so a pipe/redirect/CI run (and every exact-match golden, which writes to a `*bytes.Buffer`) stays ANSI- and glyph-free; only interactive terminals get color. The JSON `payload.Summary` is built with `color=false` so ANSI never leaks into the JSON envelope; the parallel display's `Warn` gates on the block's own `s.enabled` (stdout-TTY) flag. Color-path tests force the profile via `lipgloss.SetColorProfile(termenv.TrueColor)` (mirroring `styles_test.go`) and assert on `stripANSI`ed visible text + glyph presence. **Cost profile (`profile.go`, agent-discoverability milestone)**: `newCostProfiler(baseDir, configPath) *costProfiler` + nil-receiver-safe `(*costProfiler).profile(scn) *testCostProfileJSON` back the `cost_profile` object on each `dwe test list --output json` row. Three invariants: (a) the profiler is built **only** in JSON mode, so the text path keeps `list`'s original no-config-load shape byte-for-byte; (b) EVERY failure path in `newCostProfiler` returns nil (config, `docker.yml`, project/service deploy pipelines) and `profile` is nil-receiver-safe, so an unloadable config degrades to "no profile", never to an error — `list` must keep working while the config is mid-edit; (c) the command registry is loaded best-effort (nil on failure) and only feeds `host_steps`. `scenarioServices` applies the scenario's `env.services` overlay (enable before disable; a `required: true` service stays enabled) onto a shallow config view, so the compose chain is scenario-specific too. **`host_steps` is the gate field and is deliberately wider than `type: shell`** — see `countHostSteps`: shell steps, the `shell` builtin, a `type: command` resolving to a non-container command (recursing through `workflow`s; an UNRESOLVABLE id counts as host — the safe direction for a field that gates an unattended run), a `type: dwe` whose subcommand re-enters project code (`cmd`/`run`/`restart`/`deploy`/`reset`/`test`), and shell `when:`/`check:` including the `check: auto` sentinel; a step counts once however many channels it uses, and a phase-level shell `when:` counts as one. dwe's own subcommands are NOT counted — the built-in default pipeline is all `type: dwe`, so counting them would leave every project permanently gated and the field would report nothing. `isolation_findings` carries only the **non-blocking** kinds (the blocking ones abort the run before deploy anyway). Facts only — no cheap/expensive verdict; that decision rule lives in `skills/dwe/SKILL.md` so it can change without a release. Invariants (a) and (b) together protect `list`'s **full** read-only contract, not just the config load: `dwe test list` takes no locks and touches no Docker, so every fact in `cost_profile` is derived by statically parsing config/compose/pipeline files — never by probing a daemon — and a future field that needs a probe belongs somewhere else. The `effective <= 1` byte-identity is not aspirational either: `TestRunTest_ParallelExceedsScenarioCount_SequentialPath` runs `--parallel 8` over a single scenario against `--parallel 1` and asserts exact stdout AND stderr equality (plus that the sequential path installs no `ReporterFactory`). Parallel per-scenario warnings render as `[<scenario>] warning: <msg>` (`scenarioWarnLine`, `style.go`) before `PrintlnDiag`, so an interleaved warning from N goroutines is always attributable to its scenario.

## Core — Foundation (`internal/core/project/`)

- `internal/core/project/project/` — project discovery: `Locate(flag)` (upward walk from cwd; explicit mode when flag non-empty), `Resolve(flag)` (Locate + validates required project config fields); `ErrNotFound` sentinel; constant `ConfigFilename`.
- `internal/core/project/config/` — `DweConfig` and all YAML loaders (`LoadConfig`, `LoadServices`, `LoadServiceFolder`, `LoadProjectDeployConfig`, `LoadServiceDeployConfig`, `LoadDockerConfig`, `LoadLifecycleConfig`, `LoadInfoConfig`, `LoadStylesConfig`); merged-config types (services, render policy, parallel groups, actions, file gates, `DeployStep.SubStepOverrides` / `SubStepOverride`, `StatusColumn` for custom per-row columns). **Unified services schema.** All service-shaped entities (apps, tools, infra) live under `workspace/services/`, one folder per service. `service.yml` is the per-folder declaration file; folder name = map key (no `name:` field inside the file). Services are discriminated by `type:` (`app` | `tool` | `infra`). `ServiceType` is a named string type with typed constants `ServiceTypeApp` / `ServiceTypeTool` / `ServiceTypeInfra`, predicate methods `IsApp()` / `IsTool()` / `IsInfra()` on both `ServiceType` and `ServiceConfig`, sentinels `ErrServiceTypeMissing` / `ErrServiceTypeUnknown` / `ErrServiceFieldNotAllowed` / `ErrServiceExtendsCrossType` / `ErrServicePortsShape` / `ErrServiceHostsShape` / `ErrServicePortOutOfRange` / `ErrDependsOnTool`, and an unexported `allowedFieldsFor(t)` field allowlist consumed by the loader's strict decode and hand-mirrored by the validators (see the allowlist-bookkeeping paragraph below — nothing cross-checks the two tables) (updated to include `on_enable`, `on_disable`, `notes` for all toggleable service types). Each `ServiceConfig` owns `Ports map[string]ServicePortSpec` and `Hosts map[string]string` (always maps — no scalar shorthand). `ServicePortSpec{Port int, Scheme string}` accepts two YAML shapes via a custom `UnmarshalYAML`: bare-int shorthand (`http: 5173` → `{Port: 5173, Scheme: ""}`) or rich form (`http: {port: 5173, scheme: https}`). The mapping branch enforces its own strictness (unknown fields rejected, `port:` required at service.yml decode, null `port:` rejected) because `yaml.Node.Decode` does NOT inherit a parent decoder's `KnownFields(true)`. Helpers: `Port(name) int` returns `Ports[name].Port` (zero for missing); `PortScheme(name) string` returns the per-port override (empty when not set); `EffectiveScheme(portName, runtimeUseHTTPS) string` walks the precedence chain (per-port `Scheme` → `Info.Scheme` → runtime fallback → `"http"`/`"https"`) and is the canonical way to resolve a URL scheme for a service port; `PortNumbers() map[string]int` flattens to a scheme-less view for downstream consumers that don't carry scheme metadata (status rows, conflict probes). `ServiceInfoBlock.Scheme string` is the service-wide scheme override consulted by `EffectiveScheme`. For reverse-proxy URLs (`auto-urls` `port_via`), the **routed** service's `Info.Scheme` participates — it both wins the URL scheme and pins which proxy listener key (`http` vs `https`) gets looked up, via `resolveProxyTarget` in `internal/core/ui/render/info_auto_urls.go` / `resolveProxyTargetData` in `internal/cli/info/info_data.go`. The **proxy's own** `Info.Scheme` is deliberately ignored (it would otherwise leak the proxy's dashboard scheme onto every routed service); only a per-port override on the proxy's chosen listener plus `runtime.use_https` participate as fallbacks. `Host(name) string` returns the hostname for missing keys. `ServiceConfig` also has `OnEnable *ServiceToggleHooks`, `OnDisable *ServiceToggleHooks`, `Notes *ServiceNotes` fields. `ServiceToggleHooks{Requires ToggleRequires, Before []string, After []string}`; `ToggleRequires` is a string enum with zero-value `RequiresUnspecified`, values `RequiresNone`/`RequiresRestart`/`RequiresDeploy`. The dot-path namespace is `services.<name>.ports.<port-name>` (always a bare int, even when the typed `ServicePortSpec` carries a scheme override — preserves the contract for env-export rules like `from: services.main.ports.http`) and `services.<name>.hosts.<host-name>`.
