Per-port scheme overrides are surfaced as a sibling dot-path under `services.<name>.port_schemes.<port-name>` (string), emitted by `injectServicesIntoRaw` only for services that actually use the feature. **`LoadConfig` sequencing**: load services via `LoadServices(baseDir)` → `validateServicesOverlay` per layer (rejects any key other than `enabled:` / `ports:` / `hosts:` under `services.<name>` with per-layer source attribution; per-port value is either a bare int in 1..65535 or a rich-form mapping `{port?, scheme?}` — at the overlay layer, `port` is OPTIONAL so a developer can override scheme alone for an inherited port, but at least one of `port`/`scheme` must be present; hosts map → string) → merge 3-layer raw YAML (workspace.yml → defaults.yml → local.yml) → resolve `Enabled` and **deep-merge** per-developer port/host overrides into `cfg.Services[name].Ports` / `.Hosts` by entry name (`applyOverlayPorts` / `applyOverlayHosts` — declared entries the overlay does not mention are preserved; new entries are added) → `injectServicesIntoRaw` (mirrors every resolved field including post-overlay `ports` / `hosts` so `${services.X.ports.Y}` resolves through `Raw`). `LoadServiceFolder(baseDir, name)` reads `workspace/services/<name>/service.yml` with strict decode; `LoadServices(baseDir)` enumerates `workspace/services/*/`, calls `LoadServiceFolder` per folder, applies cross-service `extends:` toposort + inheritance merge, and collects all folder errors via `errors.Join`. Missing `workspace/services/` directory → empty map (no error). **Per-developer port/host overrides are a load-bearing DWE feature** — developers must be able to remap a clashing port or change a `*.local` hostname in `local.yml` without touching the shared service folders, and the resolved values must stay visible in `dwe status`. Do not narrow the overlay allowlist back to `enabled:` only, and do not remove the HOSTS / PORTS columns from `render.ServicesTable`. `RuntimeConfig` is now `{ UseHTTPS, SPX }`. `extends:` resolution uses `slices.Clone` / `maps.Clone` defensive copies so child mutation never corrupts parent. Per-type accessors `(c *DweConfig).AppServices()` / `ToolServices()` / `InfraServices()` (each filters by type); `ComposeFiles()` / `ComposeFilesAll()` emit `base → tools (sorted) → infra (sorted) → apps (sorted) → c.Compose.Extra` — order is part of the public surface, locked. Per-service `compose.extra` overlays from `workspace/local.yml` (`ServiceConfig.LocalComposeExtra`, `yaml:"-"`) are emitted **inside the same `all || svc.Enabled` gate** as `svc.Compose`, immediately after the service's own compose files; project-wide `compose.extra` (`ComposeConfig.Extra`, `yaml:"-"`) is appended **strictly last** to `composeFiles()` output and is unconditional. Both fields are populated by post-decode injection from the local.yml raw map only — never decoded directly from any YAML; injection runs BEFORE `ResolveServiceExtends`, which copies `LocalComposeExtra` parent→child when the child has none. Pre-merge validators `validateOverlayCompose` / `validateLocalCompose` / `validateNonLocalCompose` gate the schema to local.yml only (non-local layers see a hard error pointing at `workspace/local.yml`). Path safety after injection runs four ordered checks via `validateComposeExtraPathSafety` / `validateComposeExtraPath`: (1) reject `filepath.IsAbs(p)` — absolute paths bypass containment since `filepath.Join(baseDir, "/x")` returns `/"/x"`; (2) reject `filepath.Clean(p) != p` — unclean paths such as `link/../evil.yml` erase symlink components before `filepath.Join`, defeating the symlink check in step 4; (3) `pathsafe.ContainedRel(baseDir, abs)` — rejects `..`-escapes; (4) `pathsafe.CheckNoSymlinks(baseDir, abs, "compose overlay")` — rejects symlink components in the resolved path. Existence (`os.Stat`) is step 5 but is skipped for disabled-service overlays (`validateComposeExtraPathSafety` without Stat) because disabled services are excluded from `ComposeFiles()` while structural checks 1–4 still run since `ComposeFilesAll()` includes their paths. Paths are stored as-written (relative); Docker Compose resolves them via `cmd.Dir = BaseDir`. User-facing details: `docs/reference/config/workspace.md` § Compose overlays. **Always** access binaries via `config.DweBin`/`DockerBin`/`ShellBin`/`GitBin` (nil-safe), never `cfg.Binaries.*`. **Deploy config loaders** were split by context: `LoadProjectDeployConfig(path)` (project-wide `workspace/deploy.yml` — `after:` is not a field of `ProjectDeployConfig`, so strict `KnownFields(true)` decode rejects it); `LoadServiceDeployConfig(path)` (per-service deploy — permits `after:`); `LoadServiceDeployConfigs(baseDir, services)` (bulk loader for all services); `ParseDeployConfigForValidation(path)` (parse-only for validators, permits `after:` so validators can inspect and emit per-file diagnostics). `LoadResetConfig` (project-wide) rejects `after:`. New `LoadServiceResetConfig(baseDir, name)` and `LoadServiceResetConfigs(baseDir)` for per-service `reset.yml` (also reject `after:`). Additional loaders: `LoadSnapshotConfig(baseDir)` (strict-decode, `KnownFields(true)`; returns `(nil, nil)` cleanly when `workspace/snapshot.yml` is absent — caller decides whether absence is fatal per subcommand); `LoadValidateConfig(baseDir)` (strict-decode; single parse point — callers thread the result into `validate.Context` rather than re-parsing); `UIConfig` (the `ui:` block, decoded inline into `DweConfig` via `LoadConfig`; pointer fields distinguish absent from explicit zero; read via the nil-safe accessors `UICommandsDefaultDepth` / `UICommandsAutoCollapseEmpty` / `UICommandsShowTypeBadges`). **Strict root + `vars:` sandbox.** The merged 3-layer root is strict: `LoadConfig` enforces a single source-of-truth allowlist `allowedRootKeys` (declared in `internal/core/project/config/workspace.go`; enforced per layer by `validateLayerRoots` in `layers.go`) (`{schema_version, project, runtime, state, exports, compose, ui, docs, services, vars, update, bridge, stop}`) — any other top-level key is a **hard load-time error** with a `vars:`-hint message naming the offending layer file. The check iterates **per layer** (so the error attributes the source file) and runs **after** the dedicated `binaries:`/`tools:` legacy-key rejections (which keep their own migration messages) and **before** the `__configPath` / `injectServicesIntoRaw` injections. Arbitrary free-form project values have exactly one legal home: the `Vars map[string]any` (`yaml:"vars"`) block — strict at the root (it's an allowed key) but **unvalidated and arbitrarily nestable inside**; references resolve through `Raw` dot-paths unchanged (`${vars.db.password}`, `from: vars.db.user`). `schema_version` is tolerated-but-uninterpreted forward-compat metadata. **Formalized top-level `update:` block.** `Update *UpdateConfig{ Mode string }` (`yaml:"update"`) is the project self-update policy — 3-layer merged (scalar `mode` last-layer-wins), so the author sets policy in `workspace.yml` and a dev overrides in `local.yml`. Pointer so absent (nil → off) is distinct from present-empty (→ on). `UpdateConfig.EffectiveMode()`: nil → `"off"`; empty mode → `"on"`; else the value. `LoadConfig` value-validates `mode ∈ {on, off}` at load time (the allowlist only checks key *names*), mirrored as a `dwe validate` diagnostic. This **replaced** the old `lifecycle.yml` `run.update` flag (removed — enabling update no longer blanks the lifecycle `run` phases); the run consumer reads `cfg.Update.EffectiveMode()`. **Top-level `bridge:` block (`BridgeConfig{VarsWritable []string}`, `yaml:"bridge"`).** `"bridge"` is in `allowedRootKeys`; 3-layer merged; the deny-by-default container-write allowlist behind `dwe vars set`. Nil-safe accessor `BridgeVarsWritable(cfg) []string` (nil/empty → no container writes). The matcher `VarsWritableAllows(patterns, target) bool` uses **dot-boundary** semantics (NOT `strings.HasPrefix`): an exact pattern matches only the identical path; a `vars.x.*` wildcard matches only when `target` begins with `base + "."` — so `vars.db.*` allows `vars.db.host` but DENIES `vars.db`, `vars.dbx.host`, `vars.database.host`. **Distinct scope** from per-service `services.<name>.bridge:` in `service.yml` (`ServiceBridgeConfig`, enablement) — this top-level block is project-wide container-write POLICY. **Shared layer helper (`layers.go`)**: `LoadLayers(workspacePath) ([]Layer, error)` + `ResolveLayeredPath(workspacePath, path) (LayeredValue, error)` factor the 3-layer load/validation out of `LoadConfig` so `dwe vars inspect`'s per-layer (`default`/`local`/`current` + origin; `LayeredValue.Default`/`Local`/`Current`) resolution and `LoadConfig` share one source of truth for path selection, optional-layer handling, and strict-root/legacy attribution — they cannot drift. **`check: auto` sentinel (`action.go` + `validateStepShape` in `workspace.go`)**: `*Action` is used ONLY for `DeployStep.Check`, so its `UnmarshalYAML` accepts one scalar — exactly `auto` (`Auto` / `AUTO` / `"auto "` keep the pre-existing "action must be a mapping" message; a null `check:` never reaches `UnmarshalYAML` at all — yaml.v3 resolves the null tag first and leaves the pointer nil) — decoding it into `Action{Type: config.AutoCheckType}`. Consumers MUST ask through `config.IsAutoCheck(*Action)`, never string-compare `Type`; `IsAutoCheck` additionally requires an EMPTY payload, so the mapping form `{type: auto, cmd: …}` is an unknown action type rather than a sentinel (a cmd written there is rejected, not silently dropped by the rewrite). The sentinel exists from load time onward specifically so `step.Check != nil` holds for `pipeline.StepForcesRun`, `journal.deployStepToMap` and `dwe reset step` — a resolve-time-only rewrite would silently stop an auto-check step from forcing re-runs. `Action.Validate()` exempts it (its shape rules need the enclosing step); `validateStepShape` owns the three load-time rejections, each with its own reasoned message: `auto` without `when:` (nothing to invert), with `when: {type: builtin}` (the predicate and check-builtin registries are disjoint), and with `when: {type: template}` (a false template `when:` already dropped the step, so the inverse would always fail). Note the hash consequence: `journal.StepHash` does not hash `Check`, but `deployStepToMap` feeds `Service/ProjectConfigHash`, so migrating a step from an explicit inverse to `auto` shifts that hash and costs a one-time re-run of the service's steps. **Nil-safe binary accessors.** The set is `config.DweBin` / `DockerBin` / `ShellBin` / `GitBin` / `MmdcBin` (the last defaults to `"mmdc"` and is read by `internal/cli/docs/` when constructing the mermaid `MmdcRenderer`); the one deliberate non-accessor is `internal/core/execution/condition`'s hardcoded `sh -c` for step `when:` predicates, chosen for POSIX portability rather than the project's overridable `ShellBin` (`internal/core/execution/builtin/shell.go` carries a comment citing that convention, so it is not free to "fix"). **Loader strictness is a per-surface contract, and a new loader must match the surface it joins.** Strict (`yaml.NewDecoder` + `KnownFields(true)`, unknown field ⇒ hard load error): the pipeline loaders here (`LoadLifecycleConfig`, and every deploy/reset entry point via `loadProjectDeployConfigDecode` / `loadServiceDeployConfigDecode` / `loadDeployConfigDecode`), `LoadServiceFolder`, `LoadSnapshotConfig`, `LoadValidateConfig`, the user-command file loader (`usercommands/model`), the template-pack `manifest.LoadManifest`, `envtest.LoadScenario`, `workflow/setup`'s loader, and `shared/i18n`'s. Lenient (`yaml.Unmarshal`, unknown keys silently ignored): `LoadInfoConfig`, `LoadStylesConfig`, `LoadDockerConfig`, the `local.yml` toggle reader (`project/local`), and the topology reader (`ui/render/topology.go`). Mixing the two is a silent-failure generator in both directions — a lenient decode on a strict surface swallows the typo the strict decode exists to catch, and a strict decode on a lenient surface hard-fails a file authors treat as free-form. **`io.EOF` carve-out — exactly four strict *pipeline* loaders.** `LoadLifecycleConfig`, `loadProjectDeployConfigDecode`, `loadServiceDeployConfigDecode` and `loadDeployConfigDecode` (the last three back `LoadProjectDeployConfig` / `LoadServiceDeployConfig` / `LoadResetConfig` / `LoadServiceResetConfig` / `ParseDeployConfigForValidation`, so the tolerance is edited in three unexported places, not eight public ones) treat an `io.EOF` from an all-comment or empty document as an ABSENT file rather than a parse error, leaving the built-in default pipeline in force unchanged. It exists for `dwe init`'s inert scaffold overrides (§ Core — Workflow, `scaffold/` § Inert overrides) — mirror it in any new strict pipeline loader, or a freshly-scaffolded project fails to load its own commented-out `deploy.yml`. The carve-out is deliberately NOT universal: `manifest.LoadManifest` maps `io.EOF` to `manifest is empty` and `envtest.LoadScenario` to `scenario file is empty or invalid`, because neither has a built-in default to fall back to (`shared/i18n`'s `parseBundle` also tolerates `io.EOF`, but on its own reasoning — an empty translation bundle is a valid bundle — not as part of this carve-out). `LoadInfoConfig` reaches the same outcome by another route — `yaml.Unmarshal` never returns `io.EOF`, so it runs a separate `map[string]any` probe decode and treats zero top-level keys as absent (deliberately not `len(cfg.Sections) == 0`, which would also override a deliberate `sections: []` or a `footer:`-only file). **Config-load wrappers.** `LoadConfigOrWrap(workspacePath) (*DweConfig, error)` is the single home of the canonical `loading config: %w` prefix (it replaced ~29 identical hand-rolled load-and-wrap blocks, which is the only reason the prefix reads the same in every command); it lives here rather than in `cli/cmdctx/` precisely so `core/workflow/` can reach it without violating `core-no-cli`. Never re-wrap `LoadConfig` by hand. The typed `cmdctx.ErrWrap("project_invalid_config", err)` envelope (see § CLI cross-cutting behaviors, JSON output mode) is a **separate, non-overlapping** path used by JSON-capable read-only commands — do not fold it into `LoadConfigOrWrap`, or every config failure in `dwe status --output json` / `dwe logs` / `dwe vars` loses its machine-readable `{"error":{code}}` envelope. The docker sibling is `LoadDockerConfigOrEmpty(baseDir, cfg) (*DockerConfig, error)`: `os.ErrNotExist` → `(&DockerConfig{}, nil)` because a project with no `workspace/docker.yml` is legal, every other failure → `loading docker config: %w`. Use it wherever a missing `docker.yml` must not be fatal, instead of re-testing `errors.Is(err, os.ErrNotExist)` at the call site. **The `service.yml` field allowlist is hand-mirrored, not shared.** `allowedFieldsFor` stays unexported per project Go conventions, so `internal/core/validate/config/workspace.go` keeps a duplicate table `servicesAllowedFields`. Adding ANY new top-level `service.yml` field — a new toggle, a `bridge:` sub-field, `generated:`, anything — therefore requires updating **BOTH**: the decode is `KnownFields(true)`, so a field missing from the loader table is a hard load error (`ErrServiceFieldNotAllowed`) rather than an ignored key, and a field missing from the validator table makes `dwe validate` report a valid file as invalid. The loader side is guarded by the `services_loader` tests, the validator side by the `servicesValidator` tests; nothing cross-checks the two tables. The two local-compose fields are the deliberate inverse: neither `ComposeConfig.Extra` nor `ServiceConfig.LocalComposeExtra` may EVER be added to either table — that single edit silently undoes the local.yml-only gate above, because the strict `service.yml` decode would then accept `compose.extra` as a structural, git-tracked service field. `render.config` needs neither entry, because `render` is already allowlisted and its `config:` sub-block is struct-enforced by `ServiceRenderConfig.Config *RenderConfigSection`. **Service-folder file set (symmetry).** A service folder holds at most three files and `service.yml` is the only REQUIRED one — an absent `service.yml` fails `LoadServiceFolder` with `loading service %q definition: …` (and `dwe validate`'s `config.services-folder` validator raises a `SeverityError` for the folder), so an empty folder under `workspace/services/` is never silently ignored. `deploy.yml` and `reset.yml` are OPTIONAL and **type-agnostic**: `LoadServiceDeployConfigs` / `LoadServiceResetConfigs` iterate every service regardless of `type:` and `continue` past `os.ErrNotExist`, so a `tool` or `infra` service may declare a pipeline exactly like an `app`. Adding a fourth per-service file type also means extending `knownServiceFiles` in `internal/core/validate/config/services_folder.go` (see § Core — Validation). **Adding a new formalized top-level config field means adding its key to `allowedRootKeys`** — the strict root is a hard error, so until the key is listed every fixture and every real project declaring the field fails to load. The `binaries:`/`tools:` legacy rejections are themselves iterated **per layer** and name the offending layer file, and that per-layer placement is load-bearing rather than symmetric-for-neatness: `deepMerge` drops nil values, so a layer carrying ONLY a `binaries:`/`tools:` key never reaches the merged map and `validateLayerRoots` (`layers.go`) is the only pass that can see it — without it such a layer would load silently. Within one layer the legacy checks run FIRST so their migration messages win over the generic "unknown top-level key" message. `schema_version` needs no special-case branch anywhere: it is a plain `allowedRootKeys` member that nothing reads, and adding an exception path for it would be the wrong fix. **One documented exception to `tpl`'s leave-it-literal known-head whitelist**: `docker.yml`'s `project_name` is resolved by `resolveVarTemplate` (`docker.go`, reached from `LoadDockerConfig` and `ResolveComposeProjectName`), an older lightweight resolver with NO namespace whitelist — any dot-path into `Raw` resolves via `ResolvePath` — that **errors** on an unresolved path (`unresolved path %q in template %q`, plus `unclosed ${` and a 10-iteration circular-reference guard) instead of leaving the reference literal the way `CompileVarSyntax` does. That inversion is deliberate: a broken compose project name must fail loudly rather than reach `docker compose -p ${...}`. User-visible schema details: `docs/reference/config/` (and `docs/reference/config/vars.md` for the `vars`/`bridge.vars_writable` surface). **Encrypted secrets (`secrets.go` + the layer API in `layers.go`).** `LoadConfig` is split into `LoadRawLayers(path)` (today's old `LoadLayers` body — ciphertext as written, used by the `secrets` CLI and by the decrypt pass itself), `LoadLayersWithSecrets(path) ([]Layer, SecretsState, error)` (the primitive: raw layers → `ValidateLayerRoots` → recipient from the `workspace.yml` layer → `secrets.LoadIdentity` → `decryptLayers` on a **deep copy**, so the raw slice is never mutated), and the state-discarding `LoadLayers(path)` wrapper that keeps its old signature — `ResolveLayeredPath` and the three `cli/vars` callers keep using it, so the two loaders cannot drift. Assembly is one private `assembleConfig(layers, state)` shared by `LoadConfig` (decrypted layers, sets `cfg.SecretsState`, **registers every decrypted plaintext with `trace.RegisterRedaction`** — harvested by `collectDecryptedValues` BEFORE `assembleConfig` runs, because `deepMerge` stores the first layer's nested maps into the merged map *by reference* and then mutates them in place while merging the higher layers, so a post-merge re-walk of `workspace.yml` reads back whatever `local.yml` shadowed the marker with: the override got globally redacted and the secret did not) and `LoadConfigSanitized` / `LoadConfigSanitizedOrWrap` (the SAME assembly over the RAW layers, empty state, registers nothing) — which is what the ide/ai/git render commands and the `validate/templates` dry-run builders load, so every field a tracked-output template can reach carries the marker where the real config carries plaintext. **Decryption lives in the loader, not in `tpl`** (decision 1): every consumer of the merged config — `${vars.*}`, `exports.env` via `ResolvePath`, `vars inspect`, the journal hash — stays crypto-unaware; a lazy resolver-level decrypt would have left `envfile.BuildContent`, `vars get`, the JSON dumps and the hash as four separate crypto-aware sites. **The hash sees plaintext on purpose** (decision 6): age output is non-deterministic, so hashing ciphertext would invalidate `already up-to-date` on every re-encrypt; both `ServiceConfigHash` and `ProjectConfigHash` have a test pinning that the same plaintext under two different ciphertexts hashes equal. `walkScalars` (sorted-key recursion with dot-paths and `.N` sequence indices) is the single traversal shared by the decrypt pass, the has-a-marker probe and the redaction collector; it must recurse into **both** map shapes yaml.v3 emits — one non-string key (`vars: {8080: …}`, legal in the free-form `vars:` sandbox) demotes an entire mapping to `map[any]any`, and `deepCopyValue` clones that shape for the same reason (the decrypt pass writes into it, and the raw layers must stay ciphertext). Skipping it does not merely hide a secret from `SecretsState` and both validators: `local.ReplaceScalars` walks nodes and does not care about key kind, so `dwe secrets rekey` and the marker inventory disagree — with a sibling string-keyed marker in the same file the rewrite aborts at "an encrypted value appeared after the read-only pass" on every retry (the new keyfile is already on disk), and with none it reports success while rotating `secrets.recipient` and leaving the value encrypted to the retired key; `LoadLayersWithSecrets` skips the identity lookup entirely when no layer carries a marker, so a project without secrets never touches `~/.config`. `CollectMarkers(layers)` + `RecipientFromLayers(layers)` are the exported raw-layer inventory (`secrets status`, `rekey` and the validators read the tree through them; the traversal stays unexported). **`secrets:` is legal in the FIRST layer only**, enforced in the now-exported `ValidateLayerRoots` alongside the non-mapping and malformed-recipient refusals, each naming the file — so `LoadConfig`, `ResolveLayeredPath` and the `secrets set` staging check reject the same inputs (`compose.extra` is the precedent for a one-layer-only key). `allowedRootKeys`, `tpl.KnownVarHeads` and `validate/config.formalBlockStructs` all gained `"secrets"` in the same commit (the subset test pins the first two). ⚠️ Full `validateLayerRoots` now runs INSIDE `LoadLayersWithSecrets`, i.e. earlier than before (it used to run after the services-overlay validation and the merge) — required so the layer restriction is enforced before the recipient is read; it changes which error wins when a layer is invalid two ways at once.
- `internal/core/project/config/compose_scan.go` (integration-tests stage 3a) — `ScanComposeIsolation(cfg *DweConfig, projectRoot string) []IsolationFinding`, a leaf function (no `diag`/`validate` import) that parses `cfg.ComposeFiles()` with a narrow YAML struct (`services.<name>.{container_name, ports}`, top-level `volumes`/`networks` `{external, name}`) looking for constructs that bypass Docker-Compose project-name scoping. Each `IsolationFinding{Kind, Resource, HostPort, Blocking, Message, File}` carries an **intrinsic** `Blocking bool` — `true` for `KindContainerName` / `KindRawHostPort` (real collisions with the working environment), `false` for `KindNamedVolume` / `KindNamedNetwork` / `KindExternalVolume` / `KindExternalNetwork` (shared-resource hazards) — severity policy is entirely **caller-owned** (the `envtest` runner treats `Blocking` as fatal unless `--skip-isolation-check`; `internal/core/validate/tests/` maps everything to `SeverityWarning`), keeping the scanner itself policy-light. **every construct is collapsed across the `-f` chain, not reported per file** — a base declaration a later overlay corrects would otherwise be flagged for a resource the merged stack never creates. Two collapse shapes, both owned by `ScanComposeIsolation`: (a) the **scalar** fields (`container_name`, a volume/network's `name:` / `external:`) are last-declaration-wins, tracked per `declKey{kind, resource}` — a file that declares one non-effectively (`external: false`, a `!reset`) CLEARS the key instead of adding a finding, an absent key leaves the earlier winner standing; (b) **`ports:`** is a sequence, which compose merges by APPENDING, so every file's literal host port really is published and stands as its own finding — until a file replaces the whole list with `!reset` (key dropped, its own entries never reach the merged stack either) or `!override` (this file's entries replace every earlier one), which cancels the earlier files' port findings for that service. That difference is load-bearing in both directions: collapsing ports last-wins would drop a genuinely published port, and reporting them per file blocks `dwe test run` over a port an `!override []` overlay — the exact fix an author would write — already removed. Every field of the narrow shape is therefore decoded as a `yaml.Node`, never its natural Go type: yaml.v3 leaves an unknown tag unresolved, so `container_name: !reset null` decoded into a `string` reads as a service literally named `"null"`, while `ports: !reset null` into a `[]yaml.Node` is a `TypeError`. `mergeTagOf` is the single classifier (`mergeNone` / `mergeReset` / `mergeOverride`, alias-resolved first); `classifyContainerName` maps its node to `absent` (missing key, or a plain explicit null — `!reset` is the only documented clear) / `cleared` (`!reset`) / `set` (anything else, including `!override`, whose value is an ordinary name). A `*yaml.TypeError` from the file-level decode is deliberately TOLERATED (everything yaml.v3 could decode is kept) — dropping a whole file over one field this narrow shape cannot read would silently blind the scanner to that file's blocking findings; any other unmarshal error still skips the file. Callers must NOT re-derive this collapse — `internal/core/validate/config/container_name.go` consumes the scanner's findings directly. Port detection handles both short (`"8080:80"`, `"127.0.0.1:8080:80"`, host ranges `"8080-8090:80-90"`) and long (`{target, published}`) compose syntax, strips a `/tcp`|`/udp` suffix first, flags a token matching `^\d+(-\d+)?$` (single literal or literal range — range stores the low end in `HostPort`, full range in `Message`), and ignores `${...}`-interpolated tokens and container-port-only entries; IPv6 bracketed hosts (`[::1]:8080:80`) are explicitly out of scope (not flagged). An unreadable/malformed compose file is skipped silently — the scanner is advisory; real parse errors surface through the copy's `dwe validate` subprocess / `docker compose` itself. Two callers: `internal/core/workflow/envtest/` (runner prepare-phase gate, before the `dwe validate` subprocess spawns) and `internal/core/validate/tests/` (all findings as warnings). **`ScanComposeCost(cfg, projectRoot) ComposeCostFacts`** (agent-discoverability milestone) is the sibling scanner feeding `dwe test list`'s `cost_profile`: `BuildServices` / `ExternalImages` / `MaxStartPeriod`. It shares `parseComposeFiles` with `ScanComposeIsolation` — **do not add a third compose parser**. Per-service facts merge across the `-f` chain the way compose resolves it (later `image:` wins, `build:` anywhere marks the service as building), a building service is EXCLUDED from `ExternalImages` (its `image:` is a local tag, not something to pull), and `start_period` is **max, not sum** (`up --wait` waits in parallel). Every merged fact must also CLEAR, not just accumulate: a later file's `healthcheck.disable` drops an earlier `start_period`, and a `!reset` on `image:` / `build:` / `healthcheck:` drops that fact outright (`mergeTagOf`, shared with `ScanComposeIsolation`) — a sticky `build:` would report a build the merged stack never runs AND suppress the image it actually pulls. `!override` is an ordinary declaration here: these are all scalar/mapping fields, which already merge last-one-wins.
- `internal/core/project/config/docker.go` (compose identity) — **compose project name: one resolver, lowercased.** `docker compose -p` and every compose-BYPASS lookup must agree on ONE string, so the name has exactly **two** resolution entry points and no third. `ResolveComposeProjectName(baseDir, cfg)` reads `project_name` from disk through the narrow `readDockerProjectName`, which deep-merges `workspace/docker.yml` with `workspace/docker.local.yml` (local wins, same two-file precedence as the prompt's mirror) — never a full `DockerConfig` round-trip, so the lightweight per-service paths (`dwe restart|stop|logs <svc>`, `reset --service`) stay immune to an unrelated `args:`/`volumes:` schema error elsewhere in the file; `os.ErrNotExist` or an empty value falls back to `cfg.Project.FullName()`, an empty `baseDir` skips the read entirely, and a template-resolution error on `project_name` itself IS propagated (a broken `-p` must fail loudly). `ComposeProjectName(dockerCfg, cfg)` is its in-memory sibling for callers already holding a resolved `DockerConfig` (pipeline builtins via `spec.ExecContext.DockerConfig`, status collectors, completion paths): both arguments nil-safe, no template expansion. Both route through `normalizeComposeProjectName` (`strings.ToLower`) — Compose requires `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*` and rejects uppercase, while `project.name` / `project.prefix` / `docker.yml project_name` are free-form user text, making this the one Compose constraint dwe cannot construct around. **Never re-derive the `dockerCfg.ProjectName ?: cfg.Project.FullName()` precedence inline.** That is exactly how `docker.buildCompose` (the `-p` value itself), `dwe docker project-name`, the reset `docker_stop_remove_container` builtin, `spec.RunContext.Compose`, and the `ports_free` preflight each grew their own copy, and how the `-p` value diverged from the lowercased name that per-service stop/restart/logs, status probes, the bridge overlay, and the `config.container_name` validator all build `<project>-<container>` from — compose then scopes its own containers, networks, and volumes under a different project than every label lookup that goes looking for them. `buildCompose` also ALWAYS passes `-p`: without the `FullName()` fallback an absent/empty `docker.yml` left `ProjectName` empty, `BuildArgs` omitted `-p`, and compose v2 silently scoped by directory basename. The single deliberate exception is `internal/shared/prompt.readComposeProjectName`, a standalone hot-path reader that must not load the full config and therefore repeats the lowercasing by hand. Lowercasing is a **behavior change for existing projects with uppercase names** — compose treats their pre-existing containers and volumes as belonging to a different project. `ComposeProjectNameCandidates(dockerCfg, cfg)` is the migration bridge for compose-BYPASS label lookups (daemon builtins, `docker_stop_remove_container`, stop/reap/status/completion): canonical normalized name first, then the PRE-normalization spelling of `dockerCfg.ProjectName`, then the legacy `cfg.Project.FullName()` scope, empties and duplicates dropped — a container created before normalization still carries the original-cased `com.docker.compose.project` label, so probing only the lowercased name reports "nothing to stop" while the container keeps running.
- `internal/core/project/user/` (package `user`, imported as `userpkg`) — user-level config loader (`Load(projectRoot)`): defaults → `~/.config/dwe/config` (identical path on every OS — no platform-native location, no XDG fallback) → `<projectRoot>/.dwe/config` → env (`DWE_NOTIFY_*` and `DWE_LANGUAGE`). Flat `key = value` parser (full-line `#` only, inline-`#` rejected, dotted keys rejected, unknown keys warn for forward-compat). `Config.NotifyEnabledFor(kind string) bool` is the master+per-op gate keyed on `"deploy"` / `"run"` / `"command"` (string-keyed so `notify` doesn't import `user` cyclically). `Config.Language string` field holds the resolved locale (empty = unset, resolved at startup). **Alias contract**: all callers import as `userpkg "github.com/semsemyonoff/dwe/internal/core/project/user"` because bare `user` collides with `os/user`. Dropping the alias where unambiguous is a follow-up. User-visible details: `docs/reference/config/notifications.md` and `docs/reference/config/i18n.md`.
- `internal/core/project/local/` (package `local`, imported as `localpkg`) — local YAML toggle helpers: `LoadLocalYAML`, `WriteLocalYAML`, `SetLocalEntryEnabled`, `ValidateServiceToggle`, `ServiceTogglesOverlay`, plus the comment-preserving node round-trip in `local_node.go`. **Single `local.yml` write path**: `LoadLocalYAMLNode(path) (*yaml.Node, error)` (missing file → fresh empty mapping doc) → `ApplyOverlayToNode(doc, overlay map[string]any) error` (recursive patch: scalar → replace the matched value node's `Value` and set `Tag`/`Style` **from the coerced new value, NOT the old node** — preserving only Head/Line/FootComments, so a coerced `true`/`42` over a previously quoted string emits a BARE scalar that reloads typed; nested map → recurse, create child mapping if absent; absent key → append; never delete) → `WriteLocalYAMLNode(path, doc)` (shared atomic temp+rename + `MkdirAll(0o755)` + `Chmod(0o600)`). The map-based `WriteLocalYAML` is now a **thin wrapper** over this path (map → overlay onto a fresh doc); the old `MergeIntoLocal`/`deepMerge`/`validateMergeable`/`deepCopyMapInternal` were removed. ALL three writers route through it — `vars set` (`cli/vars/`), `services enable/disable` (`cli/service/service_toggle.go`, via `ServiceTogglesOverlay`), and the setup wizard (`workflow/setup/wizard.go`) — so `local.yml` comments/formatting survive every mutation. The node-aware **mergeable guard** rejects descending a map overlay through an existing scalar / sequence / alias / explicit-null / merge-key (`<<`) node by default (avoids silently discarding developer data), with one documented exception ported from the old `setup/merge.go`: a legacy bare-int port leaf upgrades to a `{port: N}` map. Multi-doc files operate on the first document (error otherwise); flow-style and comment-only/empty docs handled explicitly. **Alias contract**: callers import as `localpkg "github.com/semsemyonoff/dwe/internal/core/project/local"` because `local` is a common local-variable name (confirmed collision in `cli/service/service_toggle.go`). The style-derivation rule is a correctness control, not cosmetics: blindly preserving the OLD node's `DoubleQuoted` style would break the `set` coercion contract outright — the value would reload as a string no matter what `varsusage.CoerceScalar` decided, so `vars set x true` would silently keep writing a quoted `"true"` forever. The same round-trip is the **structural writer for all three layer files**, not just `local.yml`: `LoadYAMLNode(path, label)` / `ApplyOverlay(doc, overlay, label)` / `EncodeYAMLNode(doc, label)` / `WriteYAMLNode(path, doc, label, policy)`, with `LoadLocalYAMLNode` / `WriteLocalYAMLNode` / `WriteLocalYAML` kept as wrappers passing `LabelLocal` + `ForceMode(0o600)` so `local.yml` behaviour is byte-identical. `label` names the file in the errors that carry no path (`documentRoot`, the merge-key refusal, marshal); read/parse errors name the path instead. The write policy is explicit and load-bearing: `ForceMode(0o600)` for the gitignored `local.yml`, `PreserveOrDefault(0o644)` for the tracked `workspace.yml` / `workspace/defaults.yml` — forcing 0600 on a tracked file would surprise git and editors, and preserving `local.yml`'s mode would leave a credential file readable. The merge-key (`<<`) rule is a **refusal, never a rewrite**: appending a key that may be merge-inherited is an error, while a mapping that already holds the key explicitly (and every mapping elsewhere in the document) is edited in place with the anchor, the merge and the comments intact. `EncodeYAMLNode` clears the `!!merge` `Tag` on every `<<` key node before `yaml.Marshal` (reusing `mappingHasMergeKey`'s predicate — a structural fix, never a textual post-process): yaml.v3 round-trips `<<: *svc` as `!!merge <<: *svc`, valid YAML that no author wrote. The atomic write's temp file is `.dwe-yaml-*.tmp`, **not** `*.yml`: a leftover from a crashed write must not be picked up by a `*.yml` config glob.

**Two writers, and which edit uses which.** The node writer above re-encodes the whole document, so it reformats indentation to yaml.v3's default and **does NOT preserve blank lines** (yaml.v3 keeps them only inside comment blocks) — acceptable for `local.yml`-shaped structural edits (`vars set`, the services toggle, the setup wizard), fatal for a tracked, hand-annotated layer file: one `secrets set` into a 759-line `defaults.yml` produced a 373+/386− diff. `local_splice.go`'s **`Splicer`** is the second writer, used by `secrets init` / `set` / `rekey` only: `NewSplicer(path, label)` → `SetScalar(path, value)` / `ReplaceScalars(fn func(string) (string, bool, error))` → `Bytes()` → `Write(path, policy)`. It changes only the bytes of the nodes it edits; indentation, blank lines, comments, anchors, merge keys, quoting and line endings survive verbatim. Contracts, each load-bearing: (1) yaml.v3's `(Line, Column)` is a **locator, not a byte offset** — `Column` counts RUNES, so it is converted through a per-parse line-start table by advancing runes, and it points at the node's **properties** (`&anchor`, `!tag`), which are skipped and preserved before the value token starts; (2) the multi-line **detector is an equality check** — the candidate span (to end of line, minus `\r`, minus a trailing ` #…` comment, right-trimmed; style-aware unquoting for quoted scalars) must equal `node.Value`, because yaml.v3 exposes no end position and `Style == 0` cannot distinguish a wrapped plain scalar from a single-line one; (3) an insertion's **mapping end comes from the RAW lines** (blank / comment / deeper-indented lines belong to the mapping, deeper-indent tested BEFORE comment so a literal scalar's `#`-leading content line is continuation), never from a decoded `Node.Value`, which cannot see block-scalar continuation lines — that scan is for a NESTED key only, since a new top-level key (and any insertion into a document-less file) is appended at EOF instead, gaining one blank line when the file already separates top-level blocks that way. Both placements then answer to `endsInKeptBlockScalar(node)`: when the node's LAST value scalar is a **keep-chomped** block scalar (`|+` / `>+`), the line breaks trailing it are part of its VALUE, so the EOF append drops the cosmetic separator (which would silently extend the value) and the nested scan moves its insertion point PAST those blank lines (splicing before them would silently shorten it). Both are edits `verify()` cannot see — it re-reads only the paths this Splicer set — so the marker is located at the scalar node's own `Column` (properties skipped) rather than searched for on the header line: a `|`/`>` inside a header comment (`notes: |+ # see a > b`) would otherwise decide the answer; (4) `Write` **verifies before persisting** — re-parse, then every recorded `SetScalar` path and every `ReplaceScalars`-accepted scalar (keyed by dotted node path, sequence elements by index) must read back as requested, else `ErrVerify` and the file is untouched; (5) refused shapes are `ErrMultilineScalar` (`|`/`>`, wrapped plain or quoted) and `ErrUnsplicable` (any flow-style ancestor — in replacement AND insertion, an alias on the path, a `null`/sequence/non-mapping parent, a key absent from a `<<`-carrying mapping) — note the asymmetry: only a null PARENT is refused, while an implicit-null **leaf** (`key:` with nothing after the colon) is a normal replacement target whose span is empty and whose rendered value supplies its own separating space. `ReplaceScalars` calls `fn` FIRST and checks splice-ability only for scalars `fn` accepted — a declined `|` scalar (every real annotated layer has one) is left alone, never a silent skip of an accepted one — and applies spans bottom-up so earlier offsets stay valid. The CLI maps all three sentinels onto `secrets_write_unsupported`, but not onto one hint — `ErrVerify` is not a shape the author can reshape at the target (the write DID splice, then failed to read back), so its hint points at a duplicate or merge-inherited key elsewhere in the file; and a refusal reaching `rekey`'s phase 4/5 additionally carries `rekeyResumeHint` + `written: true`, since by then the new keyfile exists and part of the tree is re-encoded.
- `internal/core/project/varsusage/` — pure (stdout-free) helpers behind `dwe vars`. `resolve.go`: `EnumerateVars(cfg) []string` (sorted dot-paths of leaves under merged `cfg.Vars`, nested maps = namespaces), `ResolveVar(cfg, path) (any, bool)` (effective value via `config.ResolvePath(cfg.Raw, path)`; subtree for namespace paths), `CoerceScalar(raw) (any, error)` (the PINNED `vars set` grammar — parse via `yaml.Node`, REQUIRE a `ScalarNode` (reject `{...}`/`[...]`); legacy octal `0755` / leading-zero `01` / `1.2.3` / bare timestamps → string; an EMPTY argument (`raw == ""`, which is what the shell leaves after stripping `set x ""`) → YAML **null**, while only the 2-char literal `""` — written `set x '""'` so the shell cannot strip it — → the empty string, and bare `null`/`~` → YAML null; `yes`/`no`/`on`/`off` → string under interface decode). `scan.go`: the **field-aware** static usage scanner — `ScanUsages(projectRoot, queryPath) (ScanResult, error)` returning sorted/de-duped `Usage{File, Line, Kind, Text}`. It scans ONLY the fields the runtime renders, keyed by file type (NOT a flat file grep — that yields both false positives and false negatives): `${vars.x}` matched with the renderer's OWN exported pattern `tpl.VarPattern` for `${...}`-rendered fields (`templatedKeys` scalars / `templatedMapKeys` mappings / `templatedSeqKeys` sequences — the last covering `argv:` and `compose_args:`, whose elements each render through `tpl.RenderCommand` in `runio.RenderArgvWithArgs` / `service.buildRenderedComposeArgs`) plus **config** render templates under `workspace/templates/config/**` (`scanConfigTemplates`; arbitrary text → per-line raw scan for BOTH `${vars.x}` and `{{ resolve .Raw "vars.x" }}`). The YAML walk skips each service's `src/` hub (`workspace/services/<name>/src`) — that is the application's own git checkout, which dwe only ever writes RENDERED output into, so walking it costs a full `node_modules`/`vendor` traversal and can only produce hits in files dwe does not own; that matters because `config.template_refs` runs this scan on every `dwe validate`, not just on an explicit `dwe vars inspect`. The template-pack walk skips ONLY the pack `manifest.yml`, matched by **basename** — NOT by `.yml`/`.yaml` extension. A config pack can declare a YAML-bodied render output (`render: [{from: config.yaml, to: ...}]`), and `RenderConfigs` feeds that body through `tpl.RenderCommand` like any other template, so a `host: ${vars.db.host}` inside it IS a real usage; an extension-based skip silently misses those references (a regression caught in review). Only the `config` template-pack kind is scanned — it is the one rendered through the `${...}` substrate (`RenderConfigs`); the sibling ide/ai/git packs use the raw `{{ }}`/`packcommon.TemplateData` substrate (no `${...}` resolution) so a `${vars.x}` there is inert and is NOT scanned. Templated fields are ALSO scanned for the Go-template `{{ resolve .Raw "vars.x" }}` form (bare `vars.x` token inside a `{{ }}` block) — info `text`/`value`/`title` and custom-column `value` render through `tpl.Render`, where a `${...}` literal is inert, so the `{{ }}` form is the real reference there. Structural `from:`/`default_from:` + typed `when.expr` are walked via a `yaml.Node` walk. Matches by exact path OR namespace prefix (dot-boundary). **Documented caveat**: Go-template `.Vars.x` field access (capital-V sprig field form) and dynamically-built paths are NOT tracked. **`templatedKeys` completeness is an invariant, not a convenience list**: it must name EVERY scalar field the pipeline resolver renders — currently `cmd` / `text` / `value` / `title` / `project_name` / `confirm` / `when`, plus `timeout`, `command` (`files_gate.command`) and `argv_append_from`. Omitting one is silent in both directions: `pipeline.renderStepFields` substitutes `step.Timeout` and `files_gate.command` alongside `cmd`, so a `${vars.typo}` there renders to `""` — a step that quietly becomes unbounded, or a gate that quietly falls back to probing the step's own `cmd` — and because `config.template_refs` reports only what this scanner enumerates, an un-enumerated field is invisible to `dwe validate` as well as to `dwe vars inspect`.
- `internal/core/project/services/` — per-service-folder loading helpers and shared types layered over `config/`. Imports `config/` directly.
- `internal/core/project/stack/` — Docker Compose topology + health, plus status section collectors / renderers: `AggregateHealth`, `HealthFromStatusInput(in StatusInput) Health` (extracted topology-vs-rows selection — topo with runtime statuses wins over rows fallback; consumed by `cli/status/` for the opportunistic `promptcache.Write` call so JSON and TUI paths get accurate state too, not just the plain branch), `HealthState(h Health) string` (pure mapping `HealthRunning→"running"` / `HealthPartial→"partial"` / `HealthStopped→"stopped"`; default `"stopped"`; the three literals are the on-disk schema of `.dwe/prompt-cache.yml` — duplicated rather than importing `shared/promptcache` so `core/` stays independent of `shared/promptcache`), `FetchComposeTopology`, `ParseTopologyFromFiles`; `RenderHealth` / `HealthIndicator` / `RenderApps` / `RenderTools` / `RenderInfra` / `RenderDeployStatus` / `RenderTopology` are pure string-returning section renderers; `HealthIndicator(in StatusInput) string` returns just the indicator glyph + state (e.g. `● running`); `RenderHealth` is `"DWE: " + HealthIndicator(in)` composed by the `status` group — one per service `type:` (apps / tools / infra are three distinct sections, each filtered by `IsApp()` / `IsTool()` / `IsInfra()`). **Collect/render split** (responsive-table Task 9): each section's probe half and render half are separate functions — `CollectApps`/`CollectTools`/`CollectInfra(in) (ServiceSection, []error)` never call `IsRunning` and never format a table; `RenderAppsRows`/`RenderToolsRows`/`RenderInfraRows(sec ServiceSection, width int) string` never call `IsRunning` and are pure formatting at the given width (the collect/render boundary is spy-tested directly: `IsRunning` must be observed on the collect side only). `RenderApps`/`RenderTools`/`RenderInfra(in) (string, []error)` are unchanged convenience wrappers kept for the pre-existing non-TUI callers: they `Collect*`, then **branch** — `in.Width > 0` → `render.ServicesTableAt(..., in.Width)`, otherwise the sink-probing `render.ServicesTable(...)`. **The branch is load-bearing, not redundant**: `Render*Rows(sec, 0)` means *unbounded*, NOT "probe stdout", so collapsing the zero-width path into `Render*Rows` would silently opt `dwe status` / `dwe services` out of responsive tables. Same shape for `DeployStatus(in)` (`render.DeployStatusAt` vs `render.DeployStatus`) and for `RenderDaemons(rows)`, which routes through the sink-probing `render.DaemonTable` while `RenderDaemonsAt(rows, width)` is the explicit-width sibling (0 = unbounded). `StatusInput.Width int` (`0` = resolve from the sink) is the override channel for those wrappers, but has **no production caller today** — `cli/status` leaves it `0`, and the status TUI does not use it at all: the TUI goes through the collect/render split (`CollectApps` + `RenderAppsRows(sec, width)`), which takes the width as a parameter. The same split pattern extends to `CollectDeployStatus(in) []render.DeployStatusRow` + `RenderDeployStatusRows(rows, width) string`, to `RenderDaemonsAt(rows, width)`, and to `render.GitWorkspaceAt(rows, width)` (`render.GitWorkspace(rows)` is its sink-probing sibling) — `CollectGitWorkspace`/`CollectDaemons` were already probe-only, so only their render side gained a width parameter. `RenderTopology` has no width parameter and needed no split — it renders a tree diagram, not a table, with no Docker probe of its own. `RenderApps` / `RenderTools` / `RenderInfra` return `(string, []error)` so the command layer aggregates one stderr warning per section; `BuildCustomColumns(cfg, t ServiceType)` + `RenderCustomCells(defs, data)` power the per-service `status:` block (hermetic `tpl.Render` only — never `RenderCommand`); `CollectGitWorkspace(ctx, cfg, projectRoot)` runs `<config.GitBin(cfg)> status -b --porcelain=v2` per service under `errgroup.WithContext` + `SetLimit(8)` with `exec.CommandContext` for cancellation; probes `<svc.Dir>/src` (mirrors `internal/core/execution/templates/git`, which writes hooks to `<svc.Dir>/src/.git/hooks/`) — services whose `<dir>/src` is missing or not a directory are silently omitted; pre-checks `<dir>/src/.git` to avoid walking to a parent repo (blank cells when absent, nil row.Err); dedups services that extend a parent and share its `dir` by keeping the extends-chain root (shallowest depth, alphabetic tiebreak); reports per-row errors on `GitWorkspaceRow.Err` (goroutines always return nil so siblings aren't cancelled on first error); `CollectDaemons(ctx, cfg, dockerCfg)` shells out `docker ps --format=json` filtered on `dwe.project` + `dwe.daemon.id` labels, parses NDJSON per line, returns `([]statusview.DaemonRow, []error)`; `RenderDaemons(rows)` returns `(string, []error)` (hidden when empty — same treatment as topology). **Service running-state is label-based**: `ServiceRunning(projectName, composeService, dockerBin, processEnv) bool` delegates to `docker.ServiceContainerName(..., runningOnly=true)` and treats a non-empty name as running. It matches on compose labels rather than a guessed `<project>-<container>` name (the old `docker inspect <project>-<container>` guess produced false "stopped" readings), excludes one-off `compose run` containers (so a transient `dwe shell --mode run` container alone does not flip status to running), and stays correct under any `container_name` override and compose's default `<project>-<service>-<index>` index suffix. `processEnv` carries `docker.yml` `process_env` (DOCKER_HOST / DOCKER_CONTEXT) so status probes the same daemon as lifecycle commands and `dwe shell` — `cli/status/` and `cli/service/` build it via `docker.MergeEnv(dockerCfg.ProcessEnv)`. The `ContainerCheckFn` seam's argument is the **compose service name = `svc.Container`** (the `com.docker.compose.service` value; defaults to the folder key but may be overridden), NOT the folder map key — both callers close over the project name + processEnv and call `ServiceRunning`; `collectRowsByType` passes `svc.Container`. **Signature contract**: nothing in this package (nor in `core/ui/render/`) takes an `io.Writer` for section output — collectors and renderers return strings plus per-row errors, and `internal/cli/` is the **single** writer to stdout/stderr. Threading a writer down would move sink policy (`--output json` suppression, stdout-vs-stderr routing, `cmd.OutOrStdout()` test capture) out of the CLI layer and make the byte-compared goldens impossible. The one grandfathered exception here is `RenderServiceDeployDetail(w, …)`, kept for the `dwe status deploy <svc>` detail view; do not add a second. Passing a width on does not stop at the table body either: `wrapSection(title, body, width)` (`status.go`) hands the *same* budget to `render.SectionTitleAt`, because the plain `render.SectionTitle` always sizes its `── Title ──…` bar from the ambient terminal — a panel-width table under a terminal-width title is precisely the overflow the responsive-table work exists to prevent, so a new section renderer must thread its width into `wrapSection` as well as into the table call. `RenderTopology` is the deliberate width-`0` exception: it calls `wrapSection(..., 0)` on purpose, since its body is a `render.Topology` dependency tree rather than a `tableView`, so no panel budget is honored by the body and there is none for the title to match.

## Core — Execution (`internal/core/execution/`)

- `internal/core/execution/pipeline/` — pipeline executor and `PlainReporter`: `Reporter` interface (concurrent-safe; implementations must tolerate sub-step events from N goroutines); `SuspendForExec` / `ResumeAfterExec` wrap every sequential step body so the child writes directly to the host terminal. Action dispatch: `ExecAction` (pure dispatch — no `when:`, no reporter, no check). `RunWithOptions` owns `when:` eval, reporter calls, `check:` orchestration, and `continue_on_error`. Plan-time helpers: `ResolvePhaseSteps`, `PrintPlanTable`, `FormatCondition` / `FormatAction` / `FormatFilesGate`. **`StepCommand`, `FormatAction` and `FormatCondition` are display-only and REDACT**: each runs `trace.Redact` over the string leaves it is about to format (`step.Cmd` plus every string leaf of `With`, in a **deep copy** — nested maps, `map[any]any`, `[]any`, `[]string`) and then over the assembled line. Leaf-first is not belt-and-braces ordering, it is the only order that works: `builtin.Describe` renders `confirm`'s value with `%q` and the `type: command` branch builds `--set k=v`, so a secret carrying a quote, backslash or newline no longer equals the registered string by the time a final redact could match it. The deep copy is equally load-bearing — `DeployStep.With` is the same map `DeployStep.Action()` hands to `ExecAction`, and `cli/lifecycle/reset.go` calls `StepCommand` unconditionally BEFORE its `--dry-run` branch, so an in-place redaction would make a real `dwe reset step <addr>` execute with `***` as every secret param (pinned by a test asserting the caller's `With` is unchanged). This is what redacts every plan surface at once — the plan table, `--format shell` (a redacted PREVIEW, not an executable script), `buildPlanStepJSON`, both workflow shell printers and `reset step --dry-run` — and any future print surface inherits it. `UnresolvedTemplateRefs` is deliberately computed on the REDACTED display string, so a secret containing `${…}` cannot resurface through the `unresolved` field. The one non-display consumer, `FormatCondition` feeding `Recorder.OnStepSkip`'s `reason`, never persists it (`file_recorder.go` uses it only for an `== "state"` early return), so journal bytes and the deployment hash are unaffected. Values shorter than `secrets.MinRedactRunes` (4) are not redacted anywhere, by design. **Resolve-time rendering (`render.go`)**: `RenderStep(cfg, step)` / `RenderWhen(cfg, when)` are the exported entry points (`dwe reset step` bypasses `ResolvePhaseSteps` entirely and must call them before any raw-field read); internally `renderStepFields` renders `cmd`, the string leaves of `with` (recursively), `check`, `files_gate`, and `timeout` **into a deep copy** — `With`/`Check`/`FilesGate` are reference types shared with the loaded config, so rendering in place would make `journal.ProjectConfigHash` depend on deploy scope and double-render a second resolve. The gate is `hasKnownVarRef`, **not** a bare `tpl.VarPattern` match: a string enters `tpl.RenderCommand` only when it carries a `${...}` that passes `tpl.IsVarNamespaceRef` (known head AND a dot-path tail, `${args}` excepted — the single membership rule; never re-index `tpl.KnownVarHeads` or re-derive the tail test locally), so a bare Go-template idiom, a shell-style `${VAR}`, or a head-only `${host}` shell variable never reaches the engine. The `with:` widening for an inheriting `files_gate` skips a `type: builtin` host step (`gateInheritsWith`) — a builtin's parameter map must never be dragged into this template space, even when the gate borrows it. A field mixing a known head WITH a literal `{{ }}` **does** reach it and fails — an authoring constraint documented in `docs/reference/config/deploy/index.md`, NOT a bug to be fixed by weakening the gate. The narrow-vs-wide switch is the `wide bool` threaded through `renderWith` / `renderValue` / `renderWithLeaf`; a `type: builtin` `with:` map is never widened, because builtins render it in a *different* template space — `message`'s `text` is a documented Go template over `DweConfig` (`{{ .Project.Name }}`) and the `shell` predicate takes `docker inspect -f "{{.State.Status}}"` format strings, so widening there hard-fails the resolve of every project using the documented form. The known-head gate is necessary but not sufficient: `renderContextFor` populates only `Raw` and `Host`, so `renderIfKnown`/`renderWithLeaf` call `tpl.ValidateRawScope` FIRST — on every non-empty string, gate or no gate — to reject `${param.*}` / `${context.*}` / `${files.*}` / `${generated.*}` / `${args}`, whose lenient resolvers would otherwise substitute `""` for a namespace that has no pipeline source at all (`${snapshot.*}` already failed here via `validateSnapshotScope`).  `UnresolvedTemplateRefs(s)` (`step.go`) is the inverse scan used to annotate plan output; `ResolvedStep.DisplayPhaseWhen()` is what plan renderers must read for the phase header (the rendered `PhaseWhen`, falling back to the raw `Phase.When` only for a template condition) — reading `rs.Phase.When` directly reintroduces the "plan lies about what will run" divergence. Plan-time sentinels: `ErrNestedParallel`, `ErrUnnamedSubStep`, `ErrInteractiveInParallel`, `ErrEmptyParallelSteps`, `ErrDuplicateStepName`, `ErrSubStepOverridesInvalid`. State: `Recorder` / `FileRecorder` (mutex-protected). Log files: `OpenPipelineLog` / `OpenSubStepLog`. **Executor step ordering**: `phase-when → step-when → files_gate probe → (journal-skip only when FilesGate == nil) → execute` (gated steps always re-probe). **Always-run helper (`forcesrun.go`)**: `StepForcesRun(rs ResolvedStep) bool` is the single "always re-run" lever — true for `check:` steps and predicate-body builtin steps (`step.Type == "builtin" && builtin.KindOf(step.Cmd) == KindPredicate` — a predicate used as a step body is an assertion and must re-assert every deploy), recursing **one level** into `rs.Parallel.Steps` (deeper nesting is schema-rejected). Predicate-body detection MUST gate on `Type == "builtin"` — a `type: shell` step whose command text is `shell` must not force execution. `files_gate` and `when:` are deliberately excluded (files_gate keeps its own journal-bypass at the call sites; `when:` stays a pure conditional). Consumed by both deploy skip sites (`internal/cli/deploy/deploy.go`: the `hasAlwaysRunSteps` early up-to-date gate and the `makeSkipDecider` per-step decider that feeds `journal.Decide`'s `hasCheck → Run` lever). User-facing parallel / `files_gate:` semantics: `docs/reference/config/deploy/`. `Run` and `ExecStep` are deprecated thin wrappers. **Per-step timeout (integration-tests stage 3a)**: a general, opt-in `ResolvedStep.Timeout time.Duration` (parsed from `config.DeployStep.Timeout` string at resolve time in `resolveLeafStep`/`resolveParallelStep`; `< 0` is a resolve error, `0`/absent leaves the step unbounded — byte-identical to today) is enforced in `executeStepBody` by wrapping ONLY the step **body** `ExecAction` call in `context.WithTimeout(ctx, rs.Timeout)` — the `check:` action deliberately stays on the shared, unwrapped `ctx` (a fast skip-predicate, not the work being bounded). A non-nil body error is relabelled `step %q timed out after %s` only when `ctx.Err() == nil && errors.Is(stepCtx.Err(), context.DeadlineExceeded)`, so an outer Ctrl+C cancellation is never mislabelled a timeout. The contract is honestly scoped to **ctx-honoring bodies**: `shell`/`dwe` subprocesses terminate via the existing `bindCancelTerm` SIGTERM+`WaitDelay` path, ctx-aware builtins (`http_check`, `docker_wait_healthy`, `tcp_reachable`, …) return on deadline, but a body blocked on interactive input (e.g. a `confirm` builtin awaiting stdin) is NOT force-interrupted — the deadline fires but the goroutine stays blocked until input arrives (accepted, documented limitation; making every interactive prompt ctx-aware is out of scope). Parallel substeps need no extra plumbing — each substep is its own `ResolvedStep` and flows through the same `executeStepBody` path via `runParallelSubStep`. No group-level timeout exists — only per-leaf. **`check: auto` (`autocheck.go`)**: `ResolveAutoCheck(*condition.Condition) (*config.Action, error)` + `InvertShellCommand(cmd)` are the SINGLE derivation of a `check: auto` step's real check — `resolveLeafStep` and `dwe reset step` (`internal/cli/lifecycle/reset.go`, which bypasses `ResolvePhaseSteps`) both call them, never a copy. **Ordering inside `resolveLeafStep` is load-bearing**: whole-step render → `resolveStepWhen` → sentinel rewrite → `builtin.Validate` of the check. Rewriting before the render would invert an unrendered `when.Cmd` while the runtime evaluates the rendered one; rewriting after the validate would send the literal `auto` into the builtin registry or skip validating the derived check. The rewrite assigns a **fresh pointer** (`step.Check = derived`) onto the local copy — mutating through the shared pointer would make a second resolve produce `! ( ! ( … ) )` (silently restoring the original logic) and shift `Service/ProjectConfigHash` depending on deploy scope. The derived form is `{type: builtin, cmd: shell, with: {cmd, timeout: "0"}}`: the builtin gets a hard `sh -c` and `ectx.ProjectRoot` as cwd (matching `condition.EvalCmd`), whereas a `type: shell` action would use the project's overridable `ShellBin`; `timeout: "0"` is unbounded (`builtin.Shell.Run` treats 0 as no deadline — NOT an already-expired `WithTimeout(ctx, 0)`), matching the `when:` it inverts. `InvertShellCommand` wraps across newlines (`! (\n<cmd>\n)`) — an inline `! ( … )` turns a trailing `# comment` / `&` / unterminated heredoc into a syntax error instead of an inversion. `ResolvedStep.AutoCheck` records that the rewrite happened and `ResolvedStep.DisplayCheck()` is what every plan renderer must call (human table, `--output json`, `--format shell` comment, reset plan) so plan output reports `auto (inverse of when)` rather than the derived machinery. Load-time shape rules (sentinel decode, the three rejections) live in `internal/core/project/config/`. A runtime `when:` renders at three scopes — leaf step (`resolveStepWhen`), phase (`ResolvePhaseSteps`), and parallel-group parent — through `renderWhen`; a parallel group's own body is NOT re-rendered, so `rs.Step.Parallel.Steps[i]` stays raw while `rs.Parallel.Steps[i].Step` is the rendered copy, a known divergence that is harmless only because `Parallel` is never hashed. Rendering at **exec** time instead would desync the journal hash from the command actually run, leave `dwe deploy plan` printing stale `${...}` literals, and skip `with:` entirely — which is why a `RenderCommand` error here fails the step outright rather than letting the literal through to reproduce as a worse-worded `Bad substitution` at run time. Rendering happens ONCE and is **not idempotent**, so every downstream consumer of a resolved `with:` must enter through `usercommands.BuildPreRenderedRunContext`, never `BuildRunContext`; both pipeline sites (`execCommandAction` and the `evalFilesGate` probe in `executor.go`) do. A test that hand-builds a `ResolvedStep` with an unrendered `With` / `FilesGate.With` is exercising a state production cannot produce — build fixtures through `ResolvePhaseSteps`. The gate lives ONLY in this resolve helper and must never move into `tpl.RenderCommand`: user commands legitimately and documentedly mix `${...}` with raw `{{ }}` in one field, so a gate there would break them. The **wide** gate for `with:` leaves admits a known head **OR** a literal `{{`, and applies at exactly the two sites where `usercommands.BuildRunContext` used to render the map at exec time and no longer does: a `type: command` step/action, and a `files_gate` probe — its own `with:` (always wide, regardless of the host step's type), or the host step's map when the gate declares none (`len(files_gate.with) == 0`); a gate carrying its own `with:` never reads the host map, which therefore keeps its native narrow gate. Two concrete points behind the `ValidateRawScope` pre-scan: without it `cmd: "git checkout ${param.branch}"` renders down to `git checkout` plus a trailing space — a plausible, silently wrong command rather than a visible blank — and the erasure is invisible to both downstream detectors, since `UnresolvedTemplateRefs` skips known heads by construction and `config.template_refs` resolves root keys only, so no post-hoc report can catch it. `UnresolvedTemplateRefs` scans the **rendered** command and reports head-only tokens (`${host}`) alongside unknown heads; it feeds the human plan table (`print.go`) and the `unresolved` field of `--output json`, but never `--format shell`, which stays one clean line per step (a redacted preview, not an executable script — see above). Because every known-head reference has by then either been substituted or already failed the resolve, a surviving match is a genuine leftover — which is what licenses annotating it at all. On `DisplayCheck()`: the call it replaces is `FormatAction(rs.Step.Check)` — the neighbouring plan helper, and the natural habit — which on an auto-check step prints the derived `shell` builtin's inverted command instead of `auto (inverse of when)`, so the plan advertises machinery the author never wrote.
- `internal/core/execution/condition/` — deploy step `when:` evaluator. Two APIs coexist: legacy string-based (`Classify`, `IsRuntime(string)`, `EvalRuntime`, `Kind*` constants) for workflow string `when:`, and typed (`Condition` struct, `EvalRuntimeTyped`) for pipeline `when:`. Template conditions evaluate at plan time in `pipeline/resolve`, not here. Uses hardcoded `sh -c` (POSIX-portable, intentionally not `ShellBin`). **Known wart**: `type: builtin` in `when:` resolves the *predicate* registry here; `type: builtin` in a step body or `check:` resolves the *engine builtin* registry in `internal/core/execution/builtin/` — same keyword, two registries, disambiguated by YAML position. **`generated-missing <svc> <field>` predicate**: gates a service's generate step (true when the field is absent from `.dwe/generated.yml` or the store is missing). ⚠️ `EvalBuiltin` does `SplitN(predicate," ",2)`, so `parts[1]` is the WHOLE remaining string — the case re-splits it on whitespace into `<svc>`/`<field>` via the exported `ParseGeneratedMissing(args)` (the single two-arg parser, reused by the `config` validator's cross-check), NOT the single-path `path`/`rel` variable. User-facing semantics: `docs/reference/config/conditions.md`. **`Predicates() []PredicateEntry`** (agent-discoverability milestone) is the enumeration surface for `dwe docs llms-txt`, **disjoint** from `builtin.Inventory()` despite the shared word: a verb listed here is rejected in a `check:`/step body and vice versa. `TestPredicates_MatchEvalBuiltinSwitch` guards BOTH directions — forward by evaluating each listed verb, reverse by parsing `EvalBuiltin`'s `switch verb` case literals out of `condition.go` with `go/ast`. A count pinned to `len(Predicates())` would be tautological (a new switch case alone leaves it green), so keep the AST walk. Author-facing consequence of that two-direction guard: adding a `when:` verb means updating **both** `Predicates()` and `EvalBuiltin`'s `switch` — either half alone fails the drift test.
- `internal/core/execution/filesgate/` — deploy step file-existence gate (parser-types): `FilesGate`, `State` (`readable` / `missing`), `RequireSpec`, `StepRef` (minimal step-shaped value so validators avoid importing `config.DeployStep`). Subpackage `spec/` does plan-time validation (`Validate`, `ResolveRequireIDs` against command `files:` block). Root package stays import-light. User-facing semantics: `docs/reference/config/deploy/conditions.md` § `files_gate:`.
- `internal/core/execution/builtin/` — engine builtins usable as step bodies or `check:` actions. **Package layout**: a leaf `spec/` subpackage (cycle-breaker — `Builtin` interface, `Kind`, `CallerContext`, `ExecContext`, `Entry` struct, exported helpers `GetStringParam`/`GetBoolParam`/`GetStringSlice`/`GetStringMap`/`GetMapAny`/`GetDurationParam`/`SortedKeys`, plus shared env-file parsers `ParseEnvEntries`/`ParseEnvKeys`/`EnvLineKey` used by both `services/` and `env/`) + six domain subpackages each exposing `func Builtins() map[string]spec.Entry`: `containers/` (8 entries — `docker_daemon_start` / `docker_daemon_logs` / `docker_daemon_stop` / `daemons_reap` / `containers_running` / `docker_stop_remove_container` / `docker_wait_healthy` / `docker_remove_project_volumes`), `services/` (6 — legacy copy mechanism `service_configs_copy` / `service_configs_check` (deprecation-warned) + `service_dirs_ensure`, plus the render mechanism `service_configs_render` / `service_configs_render_check` / `service_generated_harvest`), `fs/` (2 — `file_exists` / `remove_paths`), `env/` (2 — `env_keys_present` / `executable_in_path`), `interaction/` (2 — `confirm` / `message`), `source/` (1 — `source_clone`). Root `builtin/` holds the public API (`Get` / `Run` / `Validate` / `Describe` / `IsInteractive` / `KindOf`), 4 cross-cutting predicates (`shell`, `tcp_reachable`, `http_check`, `config_keys_present`) that have no natural domain home, and the registry assembly: `buildRegistry()` merges the 6 subpackage `Builtins()` maps with the 3 root entries and panics on duplicate names (the panic is paired with `TestNoDuplicateRegistryNames` for compile-time-equivalent coverage). `config_keys_present` (`KindPredicate`, `with: {keys: [<dot-path>...]}`) asserts each dot-path resolves to a non-empty value in the merged `cfg.Raw` — the config-aware counterpart of `env_keys_present` (reads in-memory merged config, not an on-disk `.env`); addressing matches the setup wizard's `writes:` dot-paths, so it pairs naturally with the `post-setup` check stage. `http_check` (`KindPredicate`, `with: {url, status?=200, contains?, retries?=0, interval?=1s, timeout?=5s}`, modeled on `tcp_reachable.go`) asserts an HTTP GET returns the expected status (and optional body substring); the retry loop runs `retries+1` attempts, waiting `interval` (ctx-cancellable) between and applying `timeout` per attempt via `context.WithTimeout`. **Predicate-as-body / kindAllowed relaxation (integration-tests stage 1a)**: `kindAllowed` now permits `KindPredicate` in the step-body context (`CtxUserYAML`), not just `check:`/validate — a predicate used as a step body is an **assertion** (`false` fails the step with the predicate's message; `true` succeeds). Predicates already return `error` on `false`, so the executor/resolve needed no change — only the kind gate opened. Because `CtxUserYAML` is shared, user-command `type: builtin` definitions accept predicate builtins too (intentional — commands and pipelines share the registry). `KindInternal` stays engine-only. The exported classifier `KindOf(name) (Kind, bool)` (mirrors `IsInteractive`) is what `pipeline.StepForcesRun` uses to detect predicate bodies — `Get(cmd, CtxPredicate)` cannot distinguish predicates from actions since both are valid there. The validate domain mirror is test-only: `dwe validate` routes body-kind checks through the same `kindAllowed`; the only `validate/checks/loader.go` `builtin.Validate` calls are `CtxPredicate` (unaffected). `http_check` is also in that loader's hardcoded `workspace/validate.yml` checks allowlist (now seven builtins). Root re-exports `spec.Builtin`/`spec.Kind`/`spec.CallerContext`/`spec.ExecContext` and the const groups via type/const aliases so external callers (`pipeline/`, `validate/checks/`, `workflow/lifecycle/`) continue using `builtin.Builtin` / `builtin.ExecContext` / `builtin.CtxUserYAML` unchanged. Behavior contracts (unchanged by the refactor): `Builtin` interface threads `ctx` for cancellation; `spec.ExecContext.DockerConfig` (loaded once per top-level invocation, normalised `os.ErrNotExist` → `&DockerConfig{}`) is the single source of docker policy — builtins never call `config.LoadDockerConfig` themselves; `IsInteractive(name)` is the centralised predicate (`confirm`, `docker_daemon_logs`) consumed by both pipeline plan-time (`pipeline/resolve.go` `checkInteractive`) and workflow runtime (`runners/builtin/builtin.go` parallel guard); `docker_stop_remove_container` backs the per-service reset synthetic `container` phase (stop+rm one named container, idempotent on missing, skips rm if stop fails); `containers_running` is the fast "is running" check (no polling, no timeout, no healthcheck — the right partner for `check:` on `docker up` steps); `daemons_reap` is auto-injected into `dwe stop` via `lifecycle.EnsureStopConfig` as the synthetic `_auto_reap_daemons` phase; the `containers/` daemon trio (`docker_daemon_start` / `docker_daemon_logs` / `docker_daemon_stop`) backs registry expansion of `type: daemon` user commands (see `internal/core/usercommands/`). **Config-render trio** (all `KindAction`, thin wrappers over `internal/core/execution/templates/config/`): `service_configs_render` (`with: {service, mode?}`, default `replace`) → `RenderConfigs`; `service_configs_render_check` exists to be paired as the render step's `check:` so its PRESENCE forces re-run every deploy via the `hasCheck → Run` lever in `journal/decision.go` (mirrors `service_configs_copy`+`service_configs_check`); `service_generated_harvest` (`with: {service}`) → `HarvestGenerated`. The copy pair (`service_configs_copy` / `service_configs_check`) is deprecated — `ConfigsCopy.Run` emits a single `ectx.Output.Warning` notice (NOT also from `ConfigsCheck.Run`, which would double-warn as the copy step's check). **`source/source_clone`** (`KindAction`, `with: {repo, dir, branch?}`): clones a git repo into a project-relative dir with the idempotency gate built in (destination carries a `.git` entry — matched by `Lstat` of ANY type, since a worktree/submodule `.git` is a file — → skip+success, branch-blind on purpose; absent/empty → clone; non-empty non-git → error naming the path). The destination goes through `pathsafe.ContainedRel` + `CheckNoSymlinks` BEFORE touching the filesystem and `EnsureRealUnder` on the symlink-resolved parent AFTER `MkdirAll` (which follows symlinks) — both halves are required. Git binary via the nil-safe `config.GitBin`, never `cfg.Binaries.*`. Non-interactive posture is load-bearing (an unattended deploy must not hang on a credential prompt): `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` plus **empty** `GIT_ASKPASS`/`SSH_ASKPASS` (git only runs an askpass helper when the value is non-empty, so emptying defeats an inherited GUI helper and falls through to the terminal prompt that `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` turns into an immediate error — do NOT "fix" this to a dummy program), `GIT_SSH_COMMAND=ssh -o BatchMode=yes` only when unset **or empty** (empty is meaningful for the askpass pair but not here — git would take an empty ssh command literally instead of falling back to its default, so nothing can be expressed by clearing it), and `cmd.Stdin = nil`. It is an ACTION, so `kindAllowed` makes it reachable from `check:` (see the general relaxation above) — deliberately not "fixed", since narrowing `kindAllowed` would affect every action builtin; it is blocked from `workspace/validate.yml` by that loader's hardcoded seven-name allowlist. **`Inventory() []InventoryEntry`** (agent-discoverability milestone) is the enumeration surface for `dwe docs llms-txt`. The human summary lives on **`spec.Entry.Summary`**, NOT as a method on the `spec.Builtin` interface — so a new builtin needs its summary added in the owning subpackage's `Builtins()` map, and a test (not the compiler) asserts every entry has a non-empty one. The package doc-comment deliberately no longer lists the builtins (it drifted) — it points at `Inventory()`. Disjoint from `condition.Predicates()`. The render check-pairing exists because the render step's real inputs are invisible to the journal hash — a template-pack edit and a cleared `generatedstore` both leave `ProjectConfigHash`/`ServiceConfigHash` unchanged — so without the paired `check:` a `dwe deploy run` would report `already up-to-date` over stale rendered configs; the pairing is what makes template edits and store clears always apply. Because the `source_clone` gate is built in, it **replaces** the `when:`/`check:` pair a workspace would otherwise wrap around a hand-rolled `git clone` step; re-adding one is redundant at best, and a `when:` that disagrees with the `.git` probe is wrong — the builtin, not the caller, owns the "already materialised" decision. Both `Inventory()` and `InventoryEntry` live in `builtin.go`. The disjointness from `condition.Predicates()` is a **documentation** rule as well as a runtime one: never write (in prose, in a doc-comment, or in generated output) that `check:` accepts `when:` predicates or that a step builtin is usable in `when:` — the shared `builtin` spelling invites exactly that sentence, and `llmstxt`'s `writeBuiltinsSection` exists to say the opposite.
- `internal/core/execution/templates/` — template pack helpers shared between render commands and validators (`manifest/`, `packroot/`, `ide/`, `ai/`, `git/`). Manifest schema, `<pack>.local/` overrides, and collision policies (IDE / git: deepest extends wins; AI: shallowest) are documented in `docs/reference/render/index.md`. Each kind exposes `SelectServices`, `ResolveTemplatePack` (implicit-missing returns `found=false, err=nil`; explicit-missing or invalid-name errors), `LoadManifest`, `ValidateManifest`, `DryRunRender`, `ExtendsRoot`. Manifest is **mandatory** — packs without `manifest.yml` return `ErrManifestMissing`. **`ResolveTemplatePack` implicit chain** (when `render.<kind>.template` is unset): service name → walk `extends:` chain ancestor-by-ancestor → `default`. First existing pack wins. Invalid pack names anywhere in the chain are skipped silently. **`DryRunRender`** parses + executes every render template in-memory against `TemplateData` without writing — used by the `templates` validator domain to surface missing-key / parse / execution errors at `dwe validate` time. **`AIRenderEnabledExplicit`** defaults to `s.IsApp()` (matches IDE/Git): only app services render AI docs by default; tools/infra opt in explicitly via `render.ai.enabled: true`. **`TemplateData.Service` vs `Resolved`**: `Service` is the canonical config identity (extends-chain root, used to key raw-config lookups); `Resolved` is the rendering identity (collision winner); `ServiceCfg` is the merged block of `Resolved` (so e.g. `.ServiceCfg.Container` reflects the extender's overlay).
- `internal/core/execution/templates/config/` — the **config** render kind: materializes service config files from template packs + a durable value store, replacing the legacy copy mechanism. **Deliberately does NOT use `packcommon`/`TemplateData`/raw-`{{ }}`** like ide/ai/git — config templates use the `${...}` shorthand substrate via `tpl.CompileVarSyntax`+`RenderContext` (user/devbox ergonomics, `${APP_*}`/`${DB_*}` parity).
