Two core functions: `RenderConfigs(projectRoot, cfg, serviceName, store)` resolves the pack (convention service-name → `extends` chain → `default`, plus `render.config.template` pin and `<pack>.local/` override via `packroot.Resolve`), loads + validates the manifest (`manifest.File`, `render: from→to`), builds the per-service `RenderContext` (merged config + `Generated = store.Service(serviceName)`), and renders each entry into `<svc.Dir>/<to>` (mode `replace`; pathsafe destination guard — authors write `to: src/...`, the `src/` join is convention not hardcoded). `RenderConfigs` is single-service; callers that process the whole project (the `dwe render config` no-arg path and `renderConfigsForRun`) iterate `config.DeployOrder(cfg, []string{"app"})` (deterministic golden output — never `range cfg.Services`). **Config rendering is app-only** — only app services may declare `dir` / `render` / `generated` (see `allowedFieldsFor`), so the auto-iteration loops pass just `"app"`; sweeping tool/infra would resolve the implicit `default` pack for a dir-less service and error. `HarvestGenerated(projectRoot, cfg, serviceName, store)` iterates the service's `generated:` fields, reads `<svc.Dir>/<file>` (pathsafe), applies `pattern` (regex, **capture group 1**), `SetIfAbsent` into the store, then `Save` — errors (missing file / no match / no capture group) surface, never silently skipped (**harvest, not mint**: the engine is hermetic, so the service mints the secret and DWE only reads a string back). **A field the store already holds is skipped before the read** (`store.Has` → record `Wrote:false` with the stored value, `continue`), so those strict errors apply only to a not-yet-stored field — precisely when a bad read could pollute the store. That skip is load-bearing rather than an optimisation: the documented pipeline gates only the mint step, and `dwe reset run` (without `--clear-generated`) keeps the store while wiping the service hub, so on the next deploy the minted file is gone while its value is still authoritative; an unconditional re-read failed the whole deploy over a value it already had. Both `HarvestResult` consumers (the builtin and `dwe render config --harvest`) read only `Field`/`Wrote`, which is why reporting the stored value for a skipped field is free. `CheckRendered(projectRoot, cfg, serviceName)` centralizes pack/manifest resolution + target-existence for the `service_configs_render_check` builtin. Consumed by the three `services/` builtins, the `dwe render config` CLI command, and `renderConfigsForRun`. The mint side is always the service's own tooling (`php artisan key:generate`, `bin/magento setup:install`, …) — DWE never runs a secret generator itself. There is a fourth strict error alongside missing file / no match / no capture group: an **empty capture** — a pattern that matches a line but whose group 1 is empty is rejected (`pattern %q matched but captured an empty value`), since a half-minted secret must not enter the store. The unconditional-re-read regression was found by a control run doing a plain `dwe reset run` → `dwe deploy run`, not hypothesised. Do NOT "fix" a harvest failure by bolting a second `generated-missing` gate onto the harvest step: the skip belongs inside `HarvestGenerated` so every project inherits it, whereas a gate pushes the workaround into every project's `deploy.yml`. Allowlist bookkeeping for this feature: `generated:` is a top-level `service.yml` key, so it needs entries in BOTH `config.allowedFieldsFor` and `validate/config.servicesAllowedFields` (see § Core — Foundation); `render.config` needs neither, because `render` is already allowlisted and its `config:` sub-block is struct-enforced. **Encrypted sources and the marker guard.** `RenderConfigs` loads the identity ONCE and only when the manifest carries an `.age` `from:` (`loadPackIdentity`), so a pack without encrypted sources never touches `~/.config`; the load stays lazy about its failure — the error is carried on `packIdentity` and raised by `renderTemplateFile`, so the message can name the source file that actually needed the key (an empty recipient points at `secrets:` / `dwe secrets init`, a failed lookup at `dwe secrets key import`). `renderTemplateFile` decrypts an `.age` source after `os.ReadFile` and before `tpl.RenderCommand`, writes `.age`-sourced outputs 0600 with an **explicit `os.Chmod`** (a pre-existing 0644 target must be tightened; `os.WriteFile` alone would not), and — for every source — refuses to write output for which `secrets.ContainsMarker` is true, naming the entry's `to:`. That last guard is what catches a `${vars.*}` substitution of an *unresolved* marker without tracking which one, and it lives here because `render config` runs no preflight. **`to:` is never auto-stripped** — authors write `from: creds.json.age`, `to: src/creds.json`. `ValidateManifest` and `check.go` are unchanged: "`.age` source but no recipient" is the `secrets.recipient` validator's diagnostic at `dwe validate` time and `RenderConfigs`' error at render time.
- `internal/core/execution/templates/packcommon/` — shared leaf for the ai/ide/git template-pack infrastructure they have in common (the per-kind *validators* and collision resolvers deliberately stay separate — AI is shallowest-wins, IDE/git deepest-wins). Owns the extends-walk trio `ImplicitPackCandidates` / `ExtendsDepth` / `ExtendsRoot` plus the single `const maxDepth = 32` (previously duplicated 9×); the shared `TemplateData` struct with `AppServices` / `ToolServices` / `InfraServices` accessors; and `DryRunRender(kind, …)` / `executeTemplateInMemory(kind, …)` (the kind literal is the only per-pack difference). `ai`, `ide`, and `git` keep backward-compatible `type TemplateData = packcommon.TemplateData` aliases and `var` aliases for the walk funcs so external callers in `cli/render` and `validate/templates` compile unchanged. Cycle-free: `config` does not import `templates`. `packcommon` itself is untouched by the secrets work — the sanitization happens one level up, at the **loader**: `cli/render/ide.go`, `cli/render/ai.go`, `templates/git/git.go`'s CLI caller and the three `validate/templates` dry-run builders load `config.LoadConfigSanitizedOrWrap` / `LoadConfigSanitized` instead of `LoadConfig`, so every `TemplateData` field (`Project`, `ServiceCfg`, `Runtime`, `Services`, `Cfg`) carries the committed marker rather than the plaintext. Their outputs are usually git-tracked, so this is a structural guarantee rather than a path allowlist; the `validate/templates` builders share one `sanitizedCfg(ctx)` helper (`overrides.go`) that falls back to `ctx.Cfg` when there is no config path or the load fails — a validator's job is to report template problems, not to re-report a config load the caller already handled.
- `internal/core/execution/preflight/` — assembler for the preflight hook invoked from lifecycle commands. `Run(ctx, cfg, cmdRegistry, baseDir, stage, skip, errOut) error`: when `skip == true` prints `"preflight skipped (--skip-preflight)"` to `errOut` and returns nil — NO validate.yml load, NO validator assembly, NO execution (the flag is a true bypass because `type: command` checks invoke arbitrary user scripts). Otherwise performs the single `LoadValidateConfig` parse, populates `validate.Context` with cfg + load result + warnings + load error, assembles `env.All(cfg)` + `checks.AllForStages(...)` + the single `config.validate` validator (so a malformed validate.yml surfaces inline as part of preflight, not silently) + the **second** cherry-pick, `valsecrets.UnresolvedValidator()` (readiness, not content: an encrypted value this machine cannot decrypt makes the deploy render ciphertext or fail mid-pipeline, so it belongs at the gate — while every other `secrets.*` diagnostic stays validate-only). `cli/deploy/menu.go::runPreWizardPreflight` mirrors the same cherry-pick, so the wizard gate and the final gate agree, runs sequentially, renders diagnostics via `render.FormatDiagnostics` + `render.DiagnosticsTable` to `errOut` (always stderr at the command boundary), returns `*preflight.Error` (with `ExitCode() int = 1`) when any error-severity diagnostic surfaces. **Ordering contract**: every caller MUST invoke preflight BEFORE any side effect on Docker, git, or the filesystem — including `lock.Acquire` (deploy creates `.dwe/deploy/deploy.lock`), git probe/pull (run), and container stops (stop). cfg load and `loadCommandRegistry` are hoisted above preflight; registry-load failure is tolerated (nil registry yields unknown-command diagnostics). **Deploy two-gate / `post-setup`**: the check stage set comes from `stagesForPreflight(stage)` — for `"deploy"` it returns `{deploy, post-setup}`, every other stage maps to itself, and `""` → nil (match all). This is the ONLY place `post-setup` checks run: the interactive deploy menu's early pre-wizard gate (`cli/deploy/menu.go::runPreWizardPreflight`) queries only `"deploy"`, so `post-setup` checks are skipped there and execute at this final preflight after the setup wizard has populated `local.yml` (or, on `dwe deploy run`, immediately before the pipeline). User-visible schema: `docs/reference/config/validate.md` § Stages.

## Core — Workflow (`internal/core/workflow/`)

- `internal/core/workflow/deploy/` — deploy plan resolution: `ResolvePlan`, `ResolveServicePlan`, `ResolveServicesPlan`, `ResolveServicesPlanSubset(cfg, reg, deploys, names)` (subset deploy: validates every name has deploy.yml, topo-sorts by `after:`, strips duplicate ImplicitEnvStep, prepends one env step), `FindStep` (updated to read `workspace/services/<name>/deploy.yml`), `TrackedServices`, `LoadTrackedServices` (canonical tracked-service set from the resolved plan; nil registry tolerated — gate validation skipped). `TopoSortByAfter(deploys, services)` performs deploy-time ordering by the `after:` field (distinct from runtime `depends_on:` sort); returns names in dependency-first order with alphabetical tie-break; sentinel errors `ErrDeployCycle`, `ErrDeploySelfReference`, `ErrDeployUnknownAfterRef`. **Pipeline defaults**: `DefaultDeployConfig()` returns the built-in deploy pipeline (services → docker up --wait → post-deploy info+success); `EnsureDeployConfig(loaded *config.ProjectDeployConfig) (*config.ProjectDeployConfig, bool)` returns user config (false) or default (true). The CLI deploy entrypoint overwrites `cfg.Deploy` with the Ensure result so downstream resolvers always see a non-nil config. Subpackage `journal/` owns the state-file types and I/O (`ProjectState`, `ServiceState`, `PhaseState`, `StepState`, `Status`, `Load` / `Save` / `Remove` / `RemoveService`, hashing: `ActionHash` / `StepHash` / `ServiceConfigHash` / `ProjectConfigHash`, `Decide` for skip logic, `Recompute` for aggregates). `ProjectState` carries `Pending *PendingApply` (yaml omitempty). `PendingApply{Operations []PendingOp, ConfigHash string, CreatedAt time.Time}`; `PendingOp{Kind PendingKind, Services []string}`. `PendingKind` string enum: `PendingKindUnspecified` (zero), `PendingRestart`, `PendingDeploy`. Helper methods: `(*PendingApply).Find(kind)`, `(*PendingOp).ServiceNames()` (defensive copy). Package-level atomic helpers: `AddPendingOp`, `AddPendingOps` (atomic batch), `ClearPending`, `ClearPendingForKind`, `ClearPendingForServices`, `ClearPendingOps` (atomic batch clear), `ReplaceServiceWithPending` (atomic combine of RemoveService + AddPendingOp). State file: `.dwe/deploy/state.yml` (gitignored). The `defaulted bool` returned by every `Ensure*Config` has exactly one consumer across all four pipelines — `cmdctx.EmitDefaultNotice` (stderr-only, no-op in JSON mode; see § `internal/cli/cmdctx/`); `lifecycle` additionally routes it through the `OnDefaultUsed` callback so it can stay cobra-free. Pick between the clear helpers by ownership, not convenience: a caller that recorded specific ops must clear with `ClearPendingOps(statePath, clears)` passing exactly the `[]PendingClear` its own contributors produced — `ClearPending` wipes the whole `PendingApply` block and would therefore erase pending recorded by an unrelated concurrent session. On the write side, `AddPendingOps` on a corrupt state file either rewrites it cleanly or hard-fails; callers deliberately rely on that (rather than pre-validating) so corruption surfaces instead of silently dropping a pending intent. **`vars:` participates in both config hashes.** `ProjectConfigHash` mixes in `cfg.Vars` and `ServiceConfigHash` takes the same block as its third input, because resolve-time rendering substitutes `${vars.*}` into a step's `cmd`/`with`/`check`: omitting it from either would let `already up-to-date` skip a step whose rendered command actually changed. Both are needed, not one: `internal/cli/deploy/deploy.go`'s `makeSkipDecider` compares a service-scoped step against `ServiceConfigHash` and a project-scope step against `ProjectConfigHash` — and a `--service` deploy still carries project-scope steps, because `ResolveServicePlan` prepends the same `ImplicitEnvStep` (`Service: ""`) that the full plan does, so both hashes are live on every scope. Adding `vars` shifted every existing project's hash once on upgrade; the resulting one-time re-run is safe because steps are idempotent/gated. User-facing journal semantics: `docs/reference/config/state/index.md`.
- `internal/core/workflow/envtest/` — integration-test scenario schema + loader (stage 1a) plus the runner/isolation implementation (stages 1b–3b: reports, per-step timeouts, compose-isolation gate, parallel scenarios with the `Progress` seam and process-wide port leases; CLI lives in `internal/cli/test/` below). `Scenario{Description, Env ScenarioEnv{Services ScenarioServices{Enable, Disable []string}, Vars map[string]any}, Timeout string, Steps []config.DeployStep}` — `Steps` reuses `config.DeployStep` verbatim (the full field set: `files_gate`, `parallel`, `continue_on_error`, …; no test-only step allowlist). `Timeout` stays a raw string (parsed in 1b). `LoadScenario(path)` does a **strict** `KnownFields(true)` decode then calls `config.ValidateDeploySteps` for step-shape validation. **Loader strictness divergence**: unlike the strict pipeline loaders, an empty / all-comment file is an **error** ("scenario file is empty or invalid") — `io.EOF` is NOT tolerated (a scenario has no meaningful default; spec §8 carve-out). Registry-dependent validation (command existence, `sub_step_overrides`) stays with `ResolvePhaseSteps`/the 1b runner. `ListScenarios(baseDir)` enumerates `workspace/tests/*.yml` (`.yaml` accepted; absent dir → empty list, no error); the scenario name = basename without extension and must already match `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*$` — reject otherwise (`ValidateScenarioName`), **no** case-folding or sanitising (the name feeds the compose project name in 1b). Only `AutoPortSentinel = "auto"` ships for var values — a raw-string sentinel meaning "free host port" (allocation logic is 1b; no typed field). **`${...}` rendering is `ResolvePhaseSteps`' job, and there is no scenario-local pre-pass.** `envtest` deliberately owns NO renderer: `resolveLeafStep` renders `cmd:`/`with:`/`check:`/`files_gate`/`timeout` (and `when.cmd` at every scope) into a copy at the top of the function, before `builtin.Validate`/`spec.Validate` read the step, so the order contract that once justified a loader-side pre-pass is already satisfied — and a scenario's context is the same Raw + Host one, so `${param.*}` / `${context.*}` / `${files.*}` / `${generated.*}` / `${args}` are hard errors there via `tpl.ValidateRawScope` exactly as they were here. **Adding a pre-pass back would render twice, and rendering is not idempotent**: a var whose value is itself a `${...}` reference expands twice, and a var holding a literal `{{` is re-parsed as a Go template and hard-fails the resolve — in both cases the scenario would exercise a command the deploy pipeline never runs. The step-shape validator is reached via the new `config.ValidateDeploySteps(steps []DeployStep, context string) error` — a thin exported wrapper over the unexported `validateStepShape`/`validatePhaseSteps` in `project/config/workspace.go`, since full step-shape validation was otherwise unreachable outside `project/config`. **Stage 1b runner** — `Runner{execDwe, allocatePorts, newTeardownDeps, clock}` (`runner.go`), every external effect a seam so tests never touch real Docker/dwe; `NewRunner()` wires production implementations. `RunScenario(ctx, RunRequest) (*ScenarioResult, error)` runs spec §6's flow: `lock.Acquire(LockPath(baseDir, scenario))` (never `lock.AcquireProjectLocks` — the copy's own `.dwe/` locks are the deploy subprocess's job) — the flock path is `.dwe/tests/locks/<scenario>.lock` (`LockPath`) → `LoadScenario` → resolve timeout (flag > scenario `timeout:` > `defaultScenarioTimeout=30m`) → **kept-run guard** (`existingManifestPaths` finds any `<scenario>-<runid>.yml` under `ManifestsDir`; a hit returns `*KeptRunError` with nothing touched — a `--keep` environment or half-dead run owns the copy) → `CopyTree` → seed `local.yml` load + `writeCopyLocalYAML` (host-port remap + `AllocatePorts` + `BuildLocalOverlay` + `ApplyHostPortOverrides` + `WriteGeneratedLocalYAML`) → `WriteDockerIdentity` → `WriteManifest` (before any Docker interaction) — so a half-dead run is always sweepable from the manifest alone; symmetrically, every prep failure BEFORE the manifest exists (`prepFail` — seed `local.yml` load, `writeCopyLocalYAML`, `WriteDockerIdentity`, and `WriteManifest` itself) best-effort `os.RemoveAll`s the copy and warns, because manifest-driven `Teardown` has nothing to find yet and stage-2 `dwe test clean` would otherwise never see the leftover; from `WriteManifest` onward the inverse holds and cleanup is manifest-driven only, never a raw `os.RemoveAll` → subprocess `dwe validate` then `dwe deploy run --silent` (one retry via `retryDeployWithFreshPorts` when the copy allocated any port and deploy fails — trigger is a deploy failure that BOTH has allocated ports (`hasAllocatedPorts` — a remapped host port for an enabled service, OR an `env.vars: auto` port) AND looks like a host-port bind conflict (`isPortBindConflict` string-matches the combined deploy tail + error; a TOCTOU loss can surface as either the preflight text or a later compose bind error, so both are matched — a non-port failure with allocated ports does NOT retry, pinned by `TestRunScenario_NonPortConflictFailure_NoRetry`); the retry re-allocates EVERY port via `writeCopyLocalYAML`) → `runSteps` in-process → deferred `Teardown` (fresh `context.Background()`, skipped entirely under `Keep`). **Error-vs-result split**: a bare `(nil, error)` return means nothing was created to report against (lock held, kept run, scenario/timeout load failure) — from `CopyTree` onward every failure is instead captured in a non-nil `*ScenarioResult` (`StatusError` for copy/config/manifest/validate failures, `StatusFailed` for deploy/step/timeout failures) with a nil error, so a multi-scenario CLI run keeps going and still reports every scenario. A timeout firing during any phase forces `StatusFailed` regardless of which phase was in flight. `runSteps` loads the copy's own post-deploy `config.LoadConfig` + `usercommands.LoadRegistryFromConfigPath`, calls `reg.ApplyVisibility(copyCfg, copyRoot)` BEFORE `ResolvePhaseSteps` (mirrors deploy — `executor.go`'s Hidden-target skip reads `CommandDef.Hidden`, zero-value false until `ApplyVisibility` runs), then a synthetic `config.DeployPhase{Name:"tests"}` → `ResolvePhaseSteps(copyCfg, reg, phase, "")` → `pipeline.RunWithOptions` with `SkipConfirm:true`, `SkipDecider:nil` (every step runs), a `failStepCapture` reporter wrapper recording the first failing step's resolved address for `ScenarioResult.FailedStep`. `ReporterFactory func(workDir, name string) (rep pipeline.Reporter, logWriter, subprocOut io.Writer, cleanup func(), err error)` is CLI-injected via `RunRequest` (nil → `defaultReporterFactory` = `OpenPipelineLog`+`NewPlainReporter`, always `os.Stdout`-backed) so `internal/cli/test/` can substitute an `io.Discard`-screen variant in JSON mode without touching the runner. **Subprocess color** — the `dwe validate`/`dwe deploy run` subprocess writes to a pipe (`subprocOut`), so lipgloss would downgrade its output to no-color even when the runner streams it to a real terminal; `RunRequest.ForceColor` adds `CLICOLOR_FORCE=1` to the subprocess env (`TERM`/`COLORTERM` inherited, so the profile matches the parent's own rendering) and the CLI sets it ONLY in interactive, sequential text mode on a TTY — never JSON or `--parallel` (>1), where nothing is streamed to a terminal. The run log stays plain regardless: `defaultReporterFactory` tees `subprocOut` to `termOut` raw but through `stripANSI(logFile)` on the log side (a stateful streaming ANSI stripper in `ansi.go` that holds back an escape sequence split across two writes), so the run log — and, on failure, the report's `pipeline.log` — never gains color codes. The `dwe validate`/`dwe deploy run` subprocess spawn is the injectable `execDweFunc` seam (`execDweProcess`, resolving `os.Executable()` — the same pattern as the bridge daemon's `SpawnFunc`); tests MUST stub it, since letting it resolve to the real binary would recursively re-exec the test binary. **Copy** (`copy.go`) — `CopyTree(srcRoot, dstRoot, gitBin, warn)`: `os.RemoveAll(dstRoot)` first (the copy path is fixed per scenario; kept/live runs are protected upstream by the kept-run guard, not by skipping the wipe), then `git -C srcRoot ls-files -co --exclude-standard -z` selects files (worktree-absent entries — uncommitted deletions — are skipped; `.dwe/`, `.env`, `.git/` always excluded top-level; symlinks recreated as symlinks, permissions preserved); a non-git tree or git failure falls back to a full `filepath.WalkDir` copy with the same exclusions plus one `warn` call (gitignored artifacts included). **Generated `local.yml`** (`localyaml.go`) — `BuildLocalOverlay(seed, scn, projectName, ports, warn)` composes low→high: seed = original `local.yml` (`compose.extra` + every `services.<name>.compose.extra` stripped, warned) → scenario `env.vars` dot-paths under `vars:` (`AutoPortSentinel` values replaced from the `ports` map) + `env.services.enable/disable` → `services.<name>.enabled` → identity `project.prefix = projectName` + `update.mode = "off"` (explicit — an empty/absent mode means ON per `EffectiveMode`). `ports.go`'s `AllocatePorts(n)` opens `n` `net.Listen("tcp", ":0")` listeners and only closes them after harvesting every port, guaranteeing intra-batch uniqueness (TOCTOU across the allocate→write→deploy gap is accepted per spec §9 — the one retry covers it). **Automatic host-port isolation** (`hostports.go`): `enabledHostPortKeys(cfg, scn)` enumerates every host port declared under `services.<name>.ports` by a service that will be ENABLED in the copy (original merged `Enabled` overridden by the scenario's `env.services.enable/disable`; ports outside 1..65535 skipped, mirroring `collectDeclaredPorts`), `buildHostPortOverrides` pairs each with a freshly allocated port (scheme preserved), and `ApplyHostPortOverrides` merges them into the overlay AFTER `BuildLocalOverlay` as `services.<name>.ports.<x>` (bare int, or `{port, scheme}` when the original carried a scheme) — coexisting with any `services.<name>.enabled` toggle. This is what makes a test run alongside the working environment with NO scenario port config: `ports_free` preflight reads `services.<name>.ports` literally, and a project sourcing its compose host bindings from the same field (directly or via an `exports.env` `from: services.<name>.ports.<x>`) binds from it too, so preflight and the actual bind move together. Host-port isolation is host-wide, not merely project-wide: `AllocatePorts` listens on `:0`, so the run also dodges ports currently held by other projects, not only by the developer's own working environment. `writeCopyLocalYAML` (`runner.go`) drives host-port + `auto`-var allocation as a SINGLE `AllocatePorts` batch (so a host port and a var port never collide) and is re-invoked verbatim by the deploy retry. Deliberately independent of `env.vars: auto`, which only rewrites vars-routed ports and cannot touch a `services.<name>.ports` int (the strict-int preflight reads it literally — `${vars.x}` in a service `ports:` is rejected at load). **Docker identity** (`dockeridentity.go`) — `WriteDockerIdentity(copyRoot, projectName)`: copy has `workspace/docker.yml` → write `workspace/docker.local.yml` with only `project_name:` (overwrites any copied one, last-word layer); copy has none → write `workspace/docker.yml` with `project_name:` PLUS an explicit `[]` for every `DockerArgs` key (semantics-neutral — reproduces `LoadDockerConfigOrEmpty`'s missing-file zero-value exactly, pinned by a `LoadDockerConfig`-equivalence test; a no-`args:` file would wrongly gain the up/logs/run/down defaults) and deletes any stray copied `docker.local.yml`. **Manifest** (`manifest.go`) — `Manifest{Scenario, RunID, ComposeProject, CopyPath, BridgeDir, ReportDir, CreatedAt}`; `WriteManifest`/`LoadManifest`/`DeleteManifest` (atomic write, mkdir -p) at `.dwe/tests/manifests/<scenario>-<runID>.yml` — the field set is chosen to cover stage-2 `dwe test clean` (`ReportDir` is populated but unused until stage 2). **Teardown** (`teardown.go`) — `Teardown(ctx, m, TeardownDeps, warn)` runs, each step best-effort (errors joined, later steps still run): `ComposeDown` (built from `docker.NewCompose(...).BuildInternalArgs("down", "--remove-orphans")` — **`BuildInternalArgs`, never `BuildArgs`**: bypassing the copy's `docker.yml` `args.down` policy is the mechanism that enforces "never `-v`", since a project setting `args.down: ["-v"]` would otherwise turn teardown into `compose down -v` and delete shared named volumes ahead of the prefix-scoped `RemoveVolumes` step, so teardown is always exactly `compose down --remove-orphans` — run via `exec.CommandContext` + `Dir=copy` + `BuildEnv()` — deliberately NOT `Compose.Exec`, which has no context param and hard-wires `os.Stdout`/`Stderr`/`Stdin`; never appends `-v`; a copy that no longer loads reports `skipped=true` via `warn` and every later step still runs) → `ReapContainers` (`docker ps -aq --filter label=com.docker.compose.project=<m.ComposeProject>`, `-a` required since exited/one-off containers still hold volume refs, then `docker.RemoveContainer` each — exact manifest-recorded identity, never a name-pattern guess) → `RemoveVolumes` (delegates to the Task-5-extracted `containers.RemoveVolumesByProjectPrefix`, prefix `<ComposeProject>_`, shared unprefixed volumes survive) → `StopBridge` (`bridge.StopDaemon(m.BridgeDir)`, stale/missing pidfile is a clean no-op) → `RemoveCopy` (`os.RemoveAll`) → `DeleteManifest`. `TeardownDeps` is one seam per step (`NewTeardownDeps(manifestPath, log)` wires the real subprocess/docker/bridge calls; tests construct a recording `TeardownDeps` directly). Callers MUST pass a fresh context — never the scenario's expired deadline, since teardown must still run after a timeout or Ctrl+C (the runner uses its own `teardownTimeout=5m` background context). **Stage 2 — failure reports** (`report.go`): `CollectReport(ctx, m, ReportDeps, warn) (reportDir string, err error)` writes three artifacts into `m.ReportDir` — `os.RemoveAll` then `os.MkdirAll` first (per-scenario overwrite: the latest failure is what you debug), then `pipeline.log` (pure-fs copy of `copyRoot/.dwe/logs/test.log`, missing source = warn+skip, never an error), `compose-ps.txt` (`deps.PS`), `container-logs.txt` (`deps.Logs`, combined, `==== <id> ====` headers) — every step best-effort, a partial `(output, err)` from `PS`/`Logs` still gets written plus a warn. `ReportDeps{PS, Logs func(ctx, *Manifest) (string, error)}` mirrors `TeardownDeps`; `NewReportDeps()` wires `reportPSReal`/`reportLogsReal`, which key the same compose-vs-identity graceful degradation off `loadCopyConfig(m.CopyPath)` as `Teardown`: success → `docker.NewCompose(...).BuildInternalArgs("ps", "--all")` / `BuildInternalArgs("logs", "--no-color", "--tail", "200")` — **`BuildInternalArgs`, never `BuildArgs`**, because `BuildArgs` injects global flags (`--ansi always`/`--progress tty`) AND per-command policy defaults (`dockerCfg.Args.Logs`), so a project's `docker.yml` setting `args.logs: ["-f"]` would make report collection follow logs forever; `ps` MUST carry `--all` since plain `compose ps` is running-only and would drop exactly the crashed service a failure report exists to surface. Config-load failure falls back to identity-label capture (`docker ps -a --filter label=com.docker.compose.project=<proj>` for `PS`; per-container-id `docker logs --tail 200 <id>` for `Logs`, reusing `listContainersFn`). Both real impls route through one generic seam `captureCmdFn(ctx, bin string, args, env []string, dir string) ([]byte, error)` (default `captureCmdReal` = `exec.CommandContext(...).CombinedOutput()`) — **combined** stdout+stderr, not `Output()`: `docker logs` replicates a non-TTY container's stderr to its own stderr, so an stdout-only capture would drop exactly the crash/panic output the report exists to surface, and combining also folds a failing capture command's own diagnostic into the artifact. `reportLogTailLines = 200` const. **Runner wiring** (`runner.go`): `Runner.collectReport func(ctx, *Manifest, warn func(string)) (string, error)`, defaulted by `NewRunner` to `CollectReport(ctx, m, NewReportDeps(), warn)`; `finish` calls it once — **after** the status is finalised (incl. the deadline→`StatusFailed` override) but **before** `teardown()` (containers must still be alive, the pipeline log must still be present) — only `if r.collectReport != nil && !req.Keep && status != StatusPassed`, under a **fresh** `context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), reportTimeout)` (never the scenario's expired deadline, same reasoning as teardown's own fresh context). Success sets `result.ReportDir`; failure only `warn`s — collection is best-effort and never changes the scenario outcome. The `r.collectReport != nil` guard exists purely for `runner_test.go`'s inline `&Runner{...}` literals (no shared constructor) — production `NewRunner` always sets the seam. **`Clean`** (`clean.go`): `ListManifests(baseDir)` generalises the 1b `existingManifestPaths` pattern across every scenario (all `<scenario>-<runid>.yml` under `ManifestsDir`, sorted; absent dir → empty, no error); `projectBaseName(cfg)` was extracted out of `ComposeProjectName` so the orphan-scan prefix cannot drift from the real compose-name derivation. `Clean(ctx, CleanRequest{BaseDir, Scenarios, DryRun, Warn}) (*CleanResult, error)`: enumerates manifests (unreadable dir → hard error; absent → empty result), builds `known` from the **full** unfiltered manifest set (a filtered-out or skipped-live manifest still "explains" its docker project — never counted as orphan), then filters by `Scenarios` for the sweep. Per manifest: `lock.Acquire(LockPath(baseDir, m.Scenario))`, classified via `errors.AsType[*lock.HeldError](err)` (never a bare type assertion, so future error-wrapping can't misclassify) → `Skipped{reason:"live"}`; any other lock error → `Skipped{reason:"lock error"}` + warn (never destroy on uncertainty); free + `DryRun` → `Swept` (would-sweep) without touching anything; free + real run → `cleanTeardownFn` (default: `Teardown(ctx, m, NewTeardownDeps(manifestPath, nil), warn)`) under a fresh `teardownTimeout` context — **nil error ⇒ `Swept`, non-nil error ⇒ `Failed{…, Error}` + warn, NEVER `Swept`** (`Teardown` is best-effort and joins errors after attempting every step, so a partial failure could still have deleted the manifest; reporting it swept would be false success and strand the leftover for the next `dwe test run` to trip over). The flock is released either way. **Orphan scan is report-only and best-effort**: `config.LoadConfigOrWrap` failure on the original project → warn + skip *only* the scan (the sweep itself needs nothing from the original config, since `Teardown` resolves everything from the manifest + copy); `listComposeProjectsFn(ctx, dockerBin)` (default `docker ps -a --format '{{.Label "com.docker.compose.project"}}'`, deduped) error → warn + empty orphans, never aborts `Clean`. Orphans = distinct scanned projects whose name has the `normalize(projectBaseName(origCfg) + "-t-")` prefix (fed through the exact same normaliser `ComposeProjectName` uses) minus `known`; each becomes `Orphans{ComposeProject, Note:"no manifest — remove manually"}` and is **never torn down** — the label scan may only report, matching by name pattern is explicitly not a destroy trigger anywhere in this package. **Stage 3b — coarse `Progress` seam + process-wide port lease set.** `RunRequest.Progress func(phase ProgressPhase)` is a **UI-free** coarse-progress callback (nil → no-op, nil-guarded at the top of `RunScenario` exactly like `Warn`) — envtest fires the typed `ProgressPhase` enum (`PhasePreparing`, `PhaseValidating`, `PhaseDeploying`, `PhaseDeployRetry`, `PhaseRunningSteps`, `PhaseCollectingReport`, `PhaseTearingDown`) at the natural start of each phase, and the CLI (not envtest) maps phase → display label, keeping the layering intact (envtest imports no `ui`). Firing points: `PhasePreparing` after the kept-run guard + config load, before `CopyTree`; `PhaseValidating`/`PhaseDeploying` before their subprocess; `PhaseDeployRetry` only on the one `retryDeployWithFreshPorts`; `PhaseRunningSteps` before `runSteps`; `PhaseCollectingReport` only inside the `collectReport != nil && !Keep && status != StatusPassed` branch; `PhaseTearingDown` only when teardown actually runs (not under `--keep`). **Port lease set** (`ports.go`): a package-level `leaseMu sync.Mutex` + `leasedPorts map[int]struct{}` records every port `AllocatePorts` ever returns in this process; a harvested port already in the set is skipped (that listener closed, another opened) and every returned port is registered, so two concurrent `AllocatePorts` batches in one process can never collide — closing the intra-process TOCTOU that parallel scenarios would otherwise hit. Leases are **never released**: `dwe test` is short-lived and allocates at most dozens of ports; the failed deploy attempt's ports stay leased so the retry automatically gets fresh ones. A bounded per-batch attempt count (`allocateAttemptsPerBatch = 100 + 20*n`) guards a pathological loop and returns `envtest: allocating free port: exhausted N attempts (M ports leased)` on exhaustion. Cross-process races between separate `dwe test run` invocations stay covered by the copy's `ports_free` preflight + the one deploy retry (spec §9). An unexported `resetLeases()` test helper clears the global under the mutex so sibling tests isolate lease state via `t.Cleanup(resetLeases)`. The same `BuildArgs` policy hazard covers the `ps` capture, not only `logs`: `BuildArgs` would apply `dockerCfg.Args.Ps` (`args.ps`) and reshape a machine-readable table, which is why BOTH captures take `BuildInternalArgs`. **Manifest identity guard (`clean.go`, stage 2)** — `sweepManifest` calls `validateManifestIdentity(baseDir, manifestPath, m)` BEFORE the flock and before any destructive step; a mismatch warns `refusing untrusted manifest` and records `Skipped{reason:"invalid manifest"}` — never a teardown. It re-derives every path a sweep touches from `(baseDir, m.Scenario, m.RunID)` and rejects anything that does not match: `copy_path == RunDir(baseDir, scenario)`, `bridge_dir == bridge.DefaultBridgeDir(copy_path)`, the on-disk filename `== "<scenario>-<runID>.yml"` (so one scenario's manifest cannot declare another's identity), and `copy_path` lexically within `.dwe/tests/runs/` AND symlink-free via `pathsafe.CheckNoSymlinks` — the lexical test cannot see a symlinked component, and a planted symlink at `runs/` or any ancestor would make `os.RemoveAll(copy_path)` resolve through it and delete host state outside the test root. The threat is concrete rather than theoretical: manifests live under the project's `.dwe/tests/manifests/`, which DWE mounts into containers, so a lower-trust container can plant one with an arbitrary `copy_path`/`bridge_dir` that a host developer's `dwe test clean` would then obey (the command is itself container-blocked — this guards the host-run case, and plain corruption). `validateComposeProject` pins the destructive Docker identity the same way, since `compose_project` drives container reap-by-label and volume removal-by-prefix: it must ALWAYS carry the normalised `-t-<scenario>-<runID>` test infix the runner unconditionally produces (enforced with `strings.HasSuffix`) — a floor the real dev environment's `<base>` never satisfies, so teardown can never be aimed at it even if the copy's own identity were tampered with — and, when the disposable copy still loads, must EXACTLY equal `copyComposeIdentity(copyPath)` (`config.ComposeProjectName` over the copy's own `docker.yml`/`docker.local.yml`, i.e. the value the copy's own `docker compose down` resolves). That identity is deliberately read from the COPY and NEVER re-derived from the current root config: a run kept (`--keep`) or left by a failure before a `project.name`/`project.prefix` rename still carries the exact identity teardown uses, so re-deriving would reject a legitimate manifest as `invalid manifest` and strand its copy plus its Docker resources — contradicting `dwe test clean`'s manifest+copy recovery contract. A gone/unloadable copy degrades to the infix floor alone. **Isolation gate (stage 3a, `scanComposeIsolationGate` in `runner.go`)** — after the reporter factory is wired but BEFORE the `dwe validate` subprocess spawns, the runner loads the COPY's config and runs `config.ScanComposeIsolation`, warning EVERY finding; one or more `Blocking` findings with `RunRequest.SkipIsolationCheck` false ends the scenario via `finish(StatusFailed, "")` — teardown still runs and NEITHER the validate nor the deploy subprocess is ever spawned — after a warning naming each blocking message plus the `--skip-isolation-check` escape. A copy whose config fails to load skips the scan entirely (the `dwe validate` subprocess surfaces the real config error instead).
- `internal/core/workflow/keygate/` — the shared "this project needs an age identity you do not have — enter it now?" gate, plus the inventory scanner every `dwe secrets` surface reports from. Two files: `keygate.go` (`Options`, `PromptFunc`/`ConfirmFunc`, `Ensure`, `Explanation`, `ErrAborted` / `ErrEnvSourceUnusable` / `ErrKeyfileUnusable`, `NonInteractiveEnv`) and `inventory.go` (`IdentitySet` + `LoadIdentitySet` with `Decrypt` / `DecryptBytes` / `Reason`, `Inventory`, `Result.Readable`, `HasEncryptedSurface`, `CollectAgeFiles`, `RelToRoot`, `MarkerRow`/`FileRow` and the state/reason constants — **moved here from `internal/cli/secrets`** so the gate, `key import` and `status` share ONE scanner and ONE classification; `cli/secrets` keeps its local vocabulary through type aliases and consumes them). **`Ensure` runs on the RAW layers, before `LoadConfig`** — the caller loads the config exactly once, already decrypted: no reload step, and no window in which a wizard proceeds with still-unresolved state. **It skips itself — `(false, nil)` — on anything that is not its own question**: nil layers, layers failing `ValidateLayerRoots` (a misplaced `secrets:` block must surface as today's config error, not as a prompt), an empty or malformed recipient (that is the `secrets.recipient` validator's story, and a prompt whose match can never succeed must not open), no encrypted surface, a usable identity, or a non-interactive context (`!Interactive`, `NonInteractive`, `Yes`, `OutputJSON`, nil `Prompt`, nil `Confirm`) — in every one of those the caller's existing failure path speaks unchanged, so the gate is never the thing that reports a problem. The two refusals that fire in EVERY mode are the ones typing a key could not fix: a present-but-unusable `DWE_AGE_KEY` / `DWE_AGE_KEY_FILE` (`LoadIdentity` is first-present-source-wins with no fall-through, so a freshly written keyfile would not even be consulted) and an existing unusable keyfile (`WriteKeyfile` is `O_EXCL`, so an import could not replace it — the message names `dwe secrets key remove`). Interactive order is Confirm → Prompt → `lock.AcquireProjectLocks` → `WriteKeyfile` → release → **verify through a real `LoadIdentity`** (a keyfile that does not load back is a failure, not a success with a caveat) → re-run `Inventory` for the `N encrypted value(s) and M .age file(s) are now readable` report. `HasEncryptedSurface` is the cheap bool probe (`config.CollectMarkers` or one `*.age` under `workspace/templates/config/`, early-return, walk error counts as "no surface") — that is what keeps a healthy `dwe run` free of a per-invocation decrypt scan, and why the offer is count-free while the report is not. **Zero `core/ui` imports** (§ Dependency Rules, ui-is-sink): the interactive halves arrive as function values wired by the cli layer from `core/ui/secretsprompt`, and ANY error from either becomes `ErrAborted` with DWE-authored text — matching `widgets.ErrCancelled` by name would mean importing `core/ui` or duplicating a sentinel into a third package, and the only two causes (a cancel, which adds nothing, and a form failure, the one error whose text travelled next to the typed key) are both better dropped. Nothing here traces or logs the submitted text or a parse error over it: the gate runs before `LoadConfig`, i.e. before `trace.RegisterRedaction` is installed, so there is no redactor to fall back on. `NonInteractiveEnv()` duplicates `cmdctx.NonInteractiveEnv`'s `{"1","true"}` set for core callers (`cmdctx` is off-limits), pinned equal by a test on the `cmdctx` side so `keygate`'s own test package never imports the cli layer. Consumers: `cli/deploy` via `keygateEnsureFn`, `core/workflow/lifecycle` via `KeygateEnsureFunc`, and `cli/secrets` for `Inventory` / `IdentitySet`. User-visible schema: `docs/reference/config/secrets.md`.
- `internal/core/workflow/lifecycle/` — lifecycle pipeline execution: `RunPhases`, `RunRun` (git update + phases + show-info), `RunRestart` (stop + run), `RunStop`; `RunContext.ShowInfo` is injected to avoid importing cobra renderers; `RunContext.SkipNotify` is set by `RunRestart` on its inner `RunRun` call so a restart fires at most one desktop notification (restart is not a user-invoked run — spec says zero notifications). **The age-identity gate runs through `var KeygateEnsureFunc = keygate.Ensure`** (next to `PreflightFunc`) in the package-local `ensureIdentity(ctx, workDir)`: `RunRun` calls it BEFORE `config.LoadConfigOrWrap` — the `.env` render that refuses a marker happens right after, so a later gate would fire after the failure it exists to prevent — and **`RunRestart` calls it BEFORE `RunStop`**, which is why the stop leg goes through the new `var runStopFn = RunStop` seam: a missing key must not tear the stack down and only then fail. The nested `RunRun` call inside `RunRestart` short-circuits on the now-usable identity. `RunContext` carries `OutputJSON` plus the `KeyPrompt`/`KeyConfirm` hooks, filled only by `cli/lifecycle` (`keygate.go`'s `keyPrompt`/`keyConfirm` over the cobra streams); every other caller — `cli/service/service_plan.go`'s toggle executor, which also passes `Yes: true` — leaves them nil, so the gate is non-interactive there by construction. `isKeygateRefusal` groups the three sentinels so the notifier `defer` treats them like a `*preflight.Error`: a refusal to start is not a failed run. **Pipeline defaults**: `DefaultRunConfig()` / `DefaultStopConfig()` return freshly-allocated built-in pipelines (callers may mutate safely). `EnsureRunConfig(loaded *LifecycleConfig) (*LifecycleRunConfig, bool)` and `EnsureStopConfig(cfg *LifecycleConfig) (*LifecycleStopConfig, bool)` each return the user config (false) or the default (true) — the bool signals whether the CLI should emit the info line. `EnsureStopConfig` always prepends `autoReapPhase()` regardless of defaulting; the auto-reap phase is the sole exception to full-replacement composition. `RunContext.OnDefaultUsed func(DefaultedPipeline)` and `StopContext.OnDefaultUsed func(DefaultedPipeline)` let CLI adapters emit the info line without importing cobra renderers. `DefaultedPipeline` is a typed string enum (`DefaultedRun`, `DefaultedStop`). `RunRestart` propagates `ctx.OnDefaultUsed` into `stopCtx` so restart fires the callback for both legs. Deploy gate is unconditional — no bypass flag (compose errors immediately on undeployed services). **Run-preamble config render**: `renderConfigsForRun` auto-renders service configs (replaying stored `${generated.*}` values) and is called **AFTER the deploy gate AND after the post-pull cfg reload, but BEFORE `RunPhases`** — NOT at the early `.env` render; `run` never runs generate/harvest. Ordering IS the safety argument: a `reset --clear-generated` + `dwe run` fails the gate before render is reached, so secrets are never blanked. `missingGeneratedKeys` makes run-render non-destructive: if any declared `generated:` key is absent from the store, that service's render is SKIPPED with a `dwe deploy run` hint (deploy render stays lenient — first deploy mints; this guard is run-only). `config.SharesExtendsParentHub` skips (silently) an `extends` child whose hub dir is inherited from its parent: such a child is a config-render **alias** (same resolved pack + hub → byte-identical output) whose `${generated.*}` values are minted under the PARENT's store key, because it has no own `deploy.yml`/harvest (e.g. a debug sidecar that bind-mounts the app's source). The parent renders the shared hub; rendering the alias again is redundant and would falsely trip `missingGeneratedKeys` (its keys are absent under the child's own name). This mirrors deploy, which never schedules a render for such a child. A child with its OWN `dir` is a genuine render target and is NOT skipped. The helper is the **exported** `config.SharesExtendsParentHub(svc, services)` in `internal/core/project/config/workspace.go` (there is no unexported `sharesExtendsParentHub` in `lifecycle/` — `renderConfigsForRun` only calls it); its predicate is `svc.Extends != "" && svc.Dir != "" && svc.Dir == parent.Dir`, so "a child with its OWN `dir`" means a dir DIFFERENT from the parent's — re-declaring the same string is still an alias. The other consumer is `dwe render config` (`internal/cli/render/config.go`, both the render loop and the `--harvest` loop), where the skip is silent only in the no-arg sweep: an explicitly named alias gets an Info line naming the parent to render/harvest instead. On that path the consequence is worse than redundancy — there is no `missingGeneratedKeys` guard, so a lenient render would **blank the shared secret the parent just wrote**, and `--harvest` would mint a spurious store entry under the child's own name.
- `internal/core/workflow/reset/` — reset plan resolution (`ResolvePlan`, `FindStep`); mirrors deploy. `DefaultResetConfig()` returns the built-in reset pipeline (confirm → docker down → remove volumes + remove services/); `EnsureResetConfig(loaded *config.ProjectDeployConfig) (*config.ProjectDeployConfig, bool)` returns user config (false) or default (true).
- `internal/core/workflow/scaffold/` — pure domain behind `dwe init`. `Scaffold(Options) (Result, error)`: resolves the target dir, renders the embedded template plan, writes each file atomically (temp-file + `rename`), merges `.gitignore`, and links `CLAUDE.md` → `AGENTS.md`. **No cobra, no `io.Writer`** (honors "core returns data, cli writes"); the cli layer is `internal/cli/scaffold/`. `Options{TargetDir, Name, Prefix, Service, Branding{Title,Tagline,Accent}, Force}`; `Result{Target, Created, Skipped []string, SymlinkFallback, NestedWarning bool}`. **Idempotent at the file level** — fill-gaps, skip pre-existing files unless `Force`; a `Force` run reports everything `Created` (except the always-idempotent `.gitignore` append-merge). Two exported helpers let the cli layer pre-resolve the same path Scaffold writes to: `ResolveTarget(targetDir) (absPath, error)` (empty → cwd, then `Abs`) and `HasProjectConfig(absTarget) (bool, error)` (stats `workspace.yml` in the target *itself* — used for the cli existing-project consent gate, distinct from the ancestor-walking `detectNestedProject`/`NestedWarning`). Load-bearing details: **Starter artefacts (agent-discoverability milestone)**: `serviceScopedOutputs` lists the rendered paths that belong to the chosen service but do NOT live under `workspace/services/<name>/` — the ai render pack and `workspace/tests/smoke.yml`. `applyServicePlan` drops the service folder **plus** every path in that list when `Service == ""`, pinned against the rendered plan so a template rename cannot silently make the rule a no-op. Those two are also the ONLY inert-mirror files that must ship ACTIVE (a strict manifest needs one entry; the scenario loader errors on an empty document); the per-service `deploy.yml` skeleton in particular MUST stay commented — **not** because it fails to resolve (`TestEmbeddedTemplates_ServiceDeployPipelineResolves` uncomments it, loads it through the strict service-deploy loader and runs `ResolvePhaseSteps` green for every phase, which is what proves the skeleton teaches a shape that actually runs, `source_clone` params and `check: auto` derivation included), but because an **active** skeleton makes `dwe deploy run` try to clone its placeholder repo URL (`git@example.com:acme/<service>.git`) on every fresh project. The ai pack's embedded source is `AGENTS.md.tmpl.tmpl` because `mapEmbedPath` strips one `.tmpl`. Any template edit requires regenerating `testdata/golden_default.txt`. The skeleton's `bootstrap` step is `type: dwe` + `docker run …`, never a hand-rolled `docker compose …` in a `type: shell` step: `skills/dwe/SKILL.md` names that exact line (`npm ci` pasted into `deploy.yml`) as the anti-pattern, and a bare `docker compose` runs on the HOST and bypasses dwe's `-p`/`-f` chain — it resolves a *different* compose project and misses the service's overlays. Uncommenting the skeleton also moves the project's `host_steps` (`dwe test list --output json`, see § `internal/cli/test/`) from 0 to 1: its shell `when:` is real host execution, and the `check: auto` it demonstrates is only valid alongside a shell `when:` — so a project adopting the skeleton has to weigh that against the unattended-`dwe test run` gate; as shipped, commented, a fresh scaffold reports `host_steps: 0`. Symmetrically, the files carrying real content — `workspace.yml`, `defaults.yml`, `service.yml` — are active by design, and every other mirror ships inert.
  - **Template storage** (`templates.go`): `//go:embed templates` mirrors the output tree. Walker rule — files ending `.tmpl` render through `text/template` with **custom `[[ ]]` delimiters** (`Delims("[[", "]]")`), then the suffix is stripped; every other file is copied **verbatim**. The `[[ ]]` choice is deliberate: the inert `info.yml`/`docker.yml` references contain literal `{{ .Project.Name }}` examples that must survive copy unrendered. Dotfiles are stored with a `dot-` path-segment prefix (`dot-editorconfig` → `.editorconfig`, `dot-dwe/config` → `.dwe/config`) because `go:embed` silently skips `.`/`_`-prefixed names — **not** `all:` (which would also pull in editor junk). `renderPlanFS(fs.FS, opts)` is FS-injectable for tests.
  - **Inert overrides** — `deploy.yml`/`lifecycle.yml`/`info.yml`/`docker.yml` and the per-service `workspace/services/<name>/deploy.yml` ship **fully commented**. DWE pipeline composition is full-replacement (an active `deploy.yml` replaces the whole built-in pipeline, and a half-edit silently drops phases), so these stay inert and the built-in default stays active until the user uncomments. There is no divergence test — the only drift guard is the source pointer in each file's header (`Default*Config` constructor / `docs/reference/config/<x>.md`). A fully-commented YAML doc decodes to `io.EOF`, which the strict `KnownFields(true)` pipeline loaders in `project/config/workspace.go` now tolerate (empty doc == absent file → built-in default applies); see the Task 9 note in the plan.
  - **`styles.yml` shape** — branding renders the **nested** schema (`header.lines: [Title]`, `header.tagline: Tagline`, `colors.accent: Accent`); there is no top-level `title:` key, and `LoadStylesConfig` is lenient `yaml.Unmarshal` so a flat key would silently vanish. Empty branding falls back to commented placeholders via `[[- if ]]` guards.
  - **`.gitignore` is not a template** — the DWE block is the Go const `dweGitignoreBlock` in `gitignore.go` (single source of truth), applied via a separate `applyGitignore` append-merge path (create when absent; append only missing lines under a `# dwe` marker when present; idempotent). A template would make `renderPlan` double-emit `.gitignore`.
  - **`CLAUDE.md` symlink** — `linkClaudeMd(dir) (fallback bool, err error)` tries `os.Symlink("AGENTS.md", dir/CLAUDE.md)`; on error or pre-existing target it writes a verbatim copy and returns `fallback=true` (surfaced as `Result.SymlinkFallback`). The symlink call is a func var for testability.
  - **Starter service** — `applyServicePlan` rewrites the embedded `workspace/services/app/` segment to the chosen `Service` name, or drops the folder **plus every path in `serviceScopedOutputs`** (the ai render pack, `workspace/tests/smoke.yml`) when `Service == ""`. `service.yml` ships class-1 defaults active — `type`, `container`, `dir`, `dir_internal`, `work_dir_internal`, `icon`, `info.title` (a fully-commented `service.yml` is invalid under strict decode) — with the rest commented.
  - **Nested-project detection** — `detectNestedProject` walks ancestors (not the target itself) for a `workspace.yml`; a hit sets `Result.NestedWarning` (the cli decides how to present it) and never blocks.
- `internal/core/workflow/snapshot/` — snapshot subsystem (synthetic-command builder for workflow execution, create/restore/remove/list/exec operations, workspace-files capture, services diff, observer). Organised as a three-tier strict layering: `meta/` (leaf: descriptor types + path coordinates + `ScanArtifacts` + `WriteFileAtomic`; no internal subpkg imports) → `archive/` (tar I/O: `Pack`, `Unpack`, `VerifyExtractedArtifacts`, `ReadManifestFromTar`; imports `meta/` only) → root (business workflows: imports both). The cycle-break that pinned `scan.go` into `meta/` is intentional — `archive.VerifyExtractedArtifacts` re-scans extracted artifacts via `meta.ScanArtifacts`, and if `scan.go` lived at the root the import would go archive→root while root already imports archive. Same reason `WriteFileAtomic` is exported in `meta/atomic.go` (was private `writeFileAtomic`) — both `workspace_files.go` and `restore.go` at the root call it. Helper files split out from the create/restore flows: `workspace_files.go` (`captureDWEFiles` / `restoreDWEFiles` with `preserve_keys`-aware strip + merge over `*yaml.Node`, plus the 1 MiB `localYMLMaxBytes` cap on untrusted archive content; returns/accepts `meta.DWEFiles`) and `services_diff.go` (`DiffServices` + exported `FormatServicesDiff` shared by `snapshot inspect`, the restore prompt, and the `snapshot.<name>.services_diff` validator). User-visible schema: `docs/reference/config/snapshot.md`. Load-bearing invariants:
  - **Atomic IO contract** — `meta/manifest.go` / `meta/current.go` write a temp file **in the same directory** as the final file (cross-filesystem `rename` is not atomic), `chmod 0o644`, then `os.Rename`. Mirrors the `journal/state.go` pattern. The shared helper is `meta.WriteFileAtomic` (`meta/atomic.go`). The pre-restore backup at `.dwe/snapshots/.pre-restore-backup/` follows the same contract and overwrites the previous backup on each restore.
  - **No `schema_version` field** in `Manifest`. The manifest has no version gate, no migration path; rename / restructure freely as needed.
  - **Streaming sha256, int64 size, no symlinks** — `meta/scan.go` walks the snapshot directory excluding `manifest.yml` and `workspace/`, computes per-file sha256 via `io.Copy(hasher, f)` (never `io.ReadAll`) so multi-GB dumps don't OOM; rejects any entry with `os.ModeSymlink` set with a clear error — workflows are not allowed to produce symlinks in the snapshot dir.
  - **Render-context propagation point** — snapshot vars (`${snapshot.name|path|description|variant|created_at}`) are threaded from `runtime.RunContext.Render.Snapshot` + `.SnapshotScope` into every sub-step `RenderContext` in `runners/workflow/step.go` (`renderCtx.Snapshot` / `renderCtx.SnapshotScope` assignment block) and at the two leaf-context constructions in `build_context.go` (the `BuildRunContext` and `BuildSnapshotRunContext` constructors). Without this propagation, snapshot vars in `with:` values of leaf user commands resolve empty. Scope visibility is gated by the `tpl.SnapshotScope` enum (`SnapshotScopeNone` → compile error; `SnapshotScopeCreate` → all keys except `created_at`; `SnapshotScopeRestoreOrRemove` → all keys). The scope check runs in `tpl.RenderCommand` via `validateSnapshotScope` **before** `CompileVarSyntax` so `CompileVarSyntax` stays pure-syntactic with no error path.
  - **Workflow executor reuse** — `exec.go` builds a synthetic `*model.CommandDef{Type: "workflow", Steps: workflow.Steps}` and dispatches via `usercommands.RunCommand`. Snapshot workflows are user-command workflows executed at runtime from a different source file; there is **no** parallel pipeline executor, no shadow scheduler. `BuildSnapshotRunContext` mirrors `BuildRunContext` but injects `Snapshot` + `SnapshotScope` into `Render`.
  - **Live-view observer wiring** — `ExecParams.StepObserverFactory func([]model.WorkflowStep) StepObserverCloser` is invoked **after** `SelectWorkflow` and **after** the pre-workflow confirmation prompt, immediately before dispatching to the runner; this defers any TUI footer until all `huh` prompts are done. `StepObserverCloser` is a local interface embedding `runtime.WorkflowStepObserver` plus `Close()` — the snapshot package owns the `defer obs.Close()`; the CLI never holds the observer past factory construction. The CLI implementation lives in `internal/cli/snapshot/liveui.go` (`newSnapshotLiveObserver`); it returns `nil` for non-TTY or `--no-live` so the runner's nil-observer path produces plain output. A depth-counted pause/resume helper on the observer bridges `widgets.SetHuhHooks` and `StepIOSuspender` against the same `liveui.LiveLine` so nested suspends from in-step `ConfirmCommand` prompts compose with the step-level suspend window — `live.Pause` / `live.Resume` each fire exactly once per command step.
  - **Archive-safety contract** (`archive/unpack.go`, with the rejection types + size caps in `archive/archive.go`):
    1. Path validation runs pre-checks on the raw `tar.Header.Name` (`filepath.IsAbs`, empty / `.` / `..` rejection, `filepath.IsLocal` Go 1.20+), then joins against the staging root, then `pathsafe.ContainedRel` as belt-and-braces. **Never** rely on `filepath.Join` + `ContainedRel` alone — `Join` normalises `..` away before `ContainedRel` ever sees the original header.
    2. Type allowlist: `tar.TypeReg` and `tar.TypeDir`. Go 1.11+ normalises `TypeRegA` ('\x00') to `TypeReg` in `tar.Reader.Next()`, so the allowlist does not need a separate case for it. `TypeSymlink` / `TypeLink` / `TypeChar` / `TypeBlock` / `TypeFifo` / `TypeXGlobalHeader` (and any other typeflag) are rejected with an error naming the entry.
    3. Resource caps: package-level constants `maxUnpackBytes = 50 << 30` (50 GiB) and `maxUnpackFiles = 100_000`; enforced via `io.LimitReader` wrapping the decompressor. Constants, not config — promote to config later if needed.
    4. No follow on existing files: `O_CREATE|O_WRONLY|O_EXCL` refuses to overwrite anything inside the staging tree.
    5. Atomic install with backup rollback: extract into `./snapshots/.unpack-<random>/` (suffix from `mkdirRandom` via `crypto/rand` — blocks TOCTOU symlink pre-creation). If the final `<name>/` already exists, rename it aside as `.backup-<random>/`, rename staging into place, then remove the backup; on second-rename failure restore the backup so the final directory is never left half-installed. Any error during extraction triggers `os.RemoveAll` of the staging dir.
    6. Manifest-driven artifact verification (`VerifyExtractedArtifacts`) runs **after** extraction and **before** the rename-into-place step. Each `manifest.yml` artifact path is validated against the staging root with `filepath.IsLocal` + `pathsafe.ContainedRel` **before** `os.Open` — a maliciously crafted manifest cannot make the verifier read sibling-snapshot or system files; path-safety failure is a hard error, not a warn. Per-artifact sha256 is recomputed via `io.Copy(sha256.New(), f)` (never `io.ReadAll`). Three diagnostic groups (`Missing` / `HashMismatch` / `Extra`) are written to `cmd.ErrOrStderr()` (never `os.Stderr` directly); `Missing` and `HashMismatch` share a single grouped `continue? [y/N]` prompt — declined → cleanup + `UnpackVerifyDeclinedError`. `--no-verify` bypasses verification but always prints the bypass warning to stderr for CI visibility. The CLI summary line is driven by the typed `UnpackResult.Verification` (`VerificationSkipped` / `Clean` / `Warned`) — stderr is never parsed to reconstruct it. Pack writes only `<name>.tar.gz` — no `.sha256` sidecar.
  - **Lock acquisition** — every mutating subcommand (`create`, `restore`, `rollback`, `remove`, `pack`, `unpack`) wraps its `RunE` body with the CLI lock helper `cmdctx.AcquireProjectLocksOrReport(baseDir, w)` + deferred release — **never** `lock.AcquireProjectLocks` directly. The helper wraps `lock.AcquireProjectLocks`, prints `*lock.ProjectLockHeldError` via the writer (exit code 2 preserved, error returned unchanged), and wraps any other error as `acquiring project locks: %w`. **`pack` is included**: without the lock, packing a snapshot mid-`remove` produces a corrupt or truncated archive.
  - **Synthetic command ID** — internally constructed `CommandDef` uses `ID: "<internal>.snapshot.<scope>"`; suppressed from notifications via `SkipNotify: true` at sub-step level (`runners/workflow/step.go`, in `runCommandStep`'s `subCtx` literal). Snapshot subcommands emit their own notifications at the command boundary using `notify.OpCommand` with operation labels `"snapshot:create"` / `"snapshot:restore"` / `"snapshot:rollback"`.
- `internal/core/workflow/setup/` — interactive setup wizard for fresh projects (`workspace/setup.yml` schema + executor). **Model**: `SetupConfig{Questions []Question}`, `Question{ID, Type, Title, Description string; Required bool; Writes string; Options []Option; Validate *ValidateSpec}`, `Option{Value, Label string}`, `ValidateSpec{Preset, Regex string}`. **Loader**: `LoadSetupYAML(path)` (strict-decode, missing file → `(nil, nil)`, parse errors wrapped); semantics-permissive (unknown `Type` / duplicate IDs / invalid `writes:` caught by validators, not loader). **Merge logic**: `BuildOverlay(questions, answers) map[string]any` (dot-path → nested map), `BuildPortOverlay(overrides) map[string]any` (port key → `services.<name>.ports.<port_name>`). The wizard now applies these overlays via the comment-preserving `local.ApplyOverlayToNode` + `local.WriteLocalYAMLNode` path (sequentially, last wins) — the old map-based `MergeIntoLocal` was removed; the node-aware mergeable guard in `local/local_node.go` reproduces `validateMergeable`'s map-over-scalar rejection AND the single legacy bare-int-port-leaf → `{port:N}` exception. **Validation**: `ValidateAndCoerce(q, raw) (any, error)` — processes `input`-type answers through preset validators (`port` → `int`; `hostname`, `path`, `non-empty` → `string`) or regex checks, returning typed values; other question types bypass coercion (huh already produces typed output). **Wizard executor**: `Run(ctx, WizardDeps)` orchestrates port-conflict prompts + setup questions (answers → overlay → merge → atomic write); huh-bound asker functions (`AskQuestions`, `AskPortOverrides`) are dependency-injected for testability. **Huh bindings**: `NewHuhAsker(out)` returns the production asker functions; `coerceInputAnswers`, `coercePortOverrides` are pure helpers extracted for unit testability. Sentinel error `ErrWizardCanceled` indicates user abort (returns without writing). Belt-and-suspenders type/semantic enforcement at wizard runtime: type assertions per question type, required-answer checks (zero-value rejection), re-validation of `input` presets against answer map values, `BuildPortOverlay` range check (1..65535).

## Core — User Commands (`internal/core/usercommands/`)

- `internal/core/usercommands/` — declarative command system facade (re-exports subpackages):
  - `model/` — pure types: `CommandDef`, `CommandFile`, `ParamDef` (carries `Widget` enum and `Options` field), `ContextDef`, `FileSpec`, `WorkflowStep` (with optional `Name` for pipeline-side `sub_step_overrides` targeting; `StepName()` returns `Name` when set else `Command`) + `WorkflowParallel`, enums. `ParamWidget` enum: `WidgetInput` / `WidgetSelect` / `WidgetMultiselect` / `WidgetConfirm`; zero value inferred from `Type` and presence of `Options`. `ParamOptions` (union: static `[]OptionItem` or dynamic `From` dot-path; unmarshals from YAML scalars `${...}` or sequences, rejecting mixed types); `OptionItem{Value, Label, Description}`. `ParamDef.EffectiveWidget()` helper returns the defaulted widget per the type-driven inference. `CommandType` payload field is `Cmd` (legacy `Run` / `type: command` are strict-decode errors). **`type: daemon`**: declarative sugar for long-running named containers — one block declares `service` / `argv` / `params` plus a `daemon:` sub-block (`container_template`, `on_already_running` ∈ `{error, noop}`, `auto_remove`, `stop_timeout`); the source command is consumed by the registry expander and **dropped** from `byID`, replaced by four virtual `CommandDef`s `<base>.start` / `.logs` / `.stop` / `.restart`. `.start/.logs/.stop` are `type: builtin` targeting the daemon-runtime builtins; `.restart` is `type: workflow` composing `.stop` + `.start`. Each synthetic carries `DerivedFromDaemon = <base>` plus `SourceDaemon *DaemonSpec` (yaml:"-", expansion-time metadata for inspect — never validated, never loaded from YAML). Sentinel errors `ErrDaemonServiceRequired` / `ErrDaemonServiceNotLiteral` / `ErrDaemonContainerTemplateRequired` / `ErrDaemonStopTimeoutInvalid` are joined via `errors.Join` by model validation and matched per-field by `validate/commands/daemon.go` to suppress double-emission. Container naming + standard labels (`dwe.project` / `dwe.daemon.id` / `dwe.daemon.params`) live in `internal/shared/daemon/` (`ResolveContainerName`, `StandardLabels`, `FilterArgsByLabels`); the regex `^[a-zA-Z0-9_][a-zA-Z0-9_.-]*$` is the authoritative runtime gate on argv → `docker --name`. **`service:` is literal-only in v1** — no `${...}` / `{{...}}` substitution allowed (label stability).
  - `loader/` — YAML discovery and parsing: `DiscoverCommandFiles`, `LoadCommandFile` (parse + validate; runtime callers), `ParseCommandFile` (parse only; validators only — never use at runtime, it skips semantic validation), `ComputeGroup`, `ComputeCommandID`.
  - `registry/` — `Registry`, `LoadRegistry`, `GroupNode`, cross-ref validation; diagnostic API (`BuildRegistryFromParsed`, `ValidationIssue`, `Diagnostics`); `WalkWorkflowSteps` recursive walker (path: `step[i]` at root, `step[i].parallel.steps[j]` inside groups). **Bridge visibility (container command surface)**: `ApplyVisibility` additionally runs `applyBridgeVisibility` — outside a bridged invocation it resets every `CommandDef.BridgeHidden` to false (idempotent re-apply); inside one (`bridgeclient.InContainer()`) it DFS-collects the effective `model.BridgeDef` per group node (field-wise `MergeBridge` down the tree — deeper file `group:` blocks override shallower ones), merges each command's own block over its owning group's effective value, and sets `BridgeHidden = !AllowedFromChain(callerExtendsChain(cfg, bridgeclient.CallingService()))` — the caller chain is the calling service plus its service-level `extends:` ancestors (children inherit the parent's command rights; nil cfg or unknown caller degrades to exact-name matching, and a visited set caps malformed cycles). Opt-in default: no block anywhere → hidden in containers. `registry.list` filters `BridgeHidden` UNCONDITIONALLY (unlike `Hidden`, even `ListAllIncludingHidden` excludes it — the inspect escape hatch is a host-side debug aid), while `Get` still resolves it (workflow steps must execute non-bridged sub-commands; the CLI-side `bridgeGuard` rejects direct invocation instead). Daemon expansion copies `src.Bridge` onto every synthetic; `addCommandFile` merges `group.bridge` last-wins-per-field like Title/Description/Hide. `model.BridgeDef` carries exactly two fields: `enabled` (a `*bool`, so "absent → inherit" stays distinguishable from an explicit `false` — that is what makes the group/command field-wise merge meaningful) and an optional `services` list of workspace service keys (empty/absent → reachable from every bridge-enabled service). **Single-block → multi-command sugar** is a registry-level pattern, currently instantiated once by `type: daemon` and intended as the shape any future declarative multi-command type follows: `LoadRegistry` runs the expander at **load** time, the source `CommandDef` is consumed (never inserted into `byID`, so `Get("<base>")` is not-found by design), a synthetic group node for the base ID is `ensureGroup`'d — inheriting the source's `LocalName`/`Description` as `GroupMeta` and its `Hide` expression, so `ApplyVisibility` evaluates the expression once for the group instead of N times and no phantom group header leaks into `dwe commands list --all` — and each synthetic carries a `DerivedFrom*` back-reference plus a `Source*Spec` pointer (`yaml:"-"`) for inspect; a synthetic ID colliding with an explicit command is a hard error naming both. **Expansion is config-blind**: `LoadRegistry(baseDir)` has no `*config.DweConfig`, so nothing config-derived (project full name, dot-path resolutions, compose files, docker binary) may be baked into a synthetic's `with:` — it is resolved at builtin runtime from `ectx.Config` — and anything param-derived is emitted as a `${param.<name>}` template literal that `renderBuiltinWith` resolves at dispatch. Packing resolved `ParamDef` structs into `with:` instead would serialise defaults / patterns / descriptions into the daemon label — wrong shape and lossy on read-back.
  - `resolve/` — `Params`, `ParamDefaults` (raw-string prefill for the parameter form: merges `provided ∪ DefaultFrom ∪ Default`; empty-as-missing parity with `Params`; no coercion, no required-check, no pattern check — those stay in `Params` at run time), `Options` (resolves dynamic `${...}` dot-paths in `ParamDef.Options` to a flat `[]OptionItem` list, normalizing scalar lists, maps, and `[]{value, label}` objects), `Context`, `BuildEnv`.
  - `runtime/` — runners and execution. **Package layout**: a leaf `spec/` subpackage (cycle-breaker — `Runner` interface, `RunContext`, `FileProbeResult`, `StepStatus`/`StepResult`/`WorkflowStepObserver`/`StepIOSuspender`, `ErrConfirmInsideParallel` + `ErrWorkflowNestedParallel` sentinels) + `internal/runio/` (shared I/O helpers — `StdoutOf` / `StderrOf` / `StdinOrOS` / `RenderArgvAppendFrom` / `AppendArgvFrom` / `ParallelChildIO` / `WireChildIO` (the `defer runio.WireChildIO(rc, c)()` child-IO wiring shared by all runner subpackages — PTY in parallel, full PTY on a bridged-interactive run via `bridgedTTYChildIO`, ctx-default stdout/stderr/stdin otherwise) / `BuildRenderedEnv` / `IsNonInteractive` / `BindCancel` / `ColorForceEnv`; placed under `internal/` so only runtime-subpackages can import). **Child color/TTY contract**: `ColorForceEnv(rc)` returns the CLICOLOR_FORCE/FORCE_COLOR/COLORTERM trio when the child writes into a capture (parallel LineTee) OR when dwe itself runs color-forced on a pipe (`ColorForced()` = CLICOLOR_FORCE truthy + NO_COLOR absent — the host-bridge shape; `stdoutIsTerminal` is the test seam); on a real terminal it returns nil (children inherit the tty). `bridgedTTYChildIO` gives a SEQUENTIAL child a full PTY (stdin+stdout+stderr) only when `ColorForced() && !stdoutIsTerminal() && DWE_BRIDGE_STDIN_TTY=1` — the stdin condition is load-bearing: `docker compose exec` refuses a TTY session over a non-terminal stdin, and a PTY wired onto piped stdin data would never deliver EOF to the child. Cleanup must NEVER wait unconditionally on the stdin pump: it reaps the pump via `os.File` read deadlines only when the fd actually supports them — in the daemon-forked shape fd 0 is a plain blocking pipe (`exec.Cmd` StdinPipe, not pollable, `SetReadDeadline` fails), and waiting there deadlocked every bridged command the moment its child exited (frozen output, dead Ctrl+C); on deadline-incapable stdin the cleanup closes the master and abandons the pump goroutine instead (accepted cost: a keystroke arriving in that window feeds the dying pump, not the next step). + five per-runner subpackages under `runners/`: `host/` (`host.Runner`, `host.DWERunner`), `service/` (`service.ExecRunner`, `service.RunRunner`), `script/` (`script.Runner`), `builtin/` (`builtin.Runner` — imports the engine builtin package as alias `engbuiltin` to avoid same-package collision), `workflow/` (`workflow.Runner` plus step / parallel / log / helpers / observer-fire files; PTY allocation for parallel child IO lives in `internal/runio.ParallelChildIO`, not in `workflow/`). Root `runtime/` holds the `NewRunner` factory (switch on `Cmd.Type` dispatching to `&host.Runner{}` / `&host.DWERunner{}` / `&service.ExecRunner{}` / `&service.RunRunner{}` / `&script.Runner{}` / `&workflow.Runner{}` / `&builtin.Runner{}`), `BuildRunContext` / `BuildSnapshotRunContext`, `ComputeFilePathsProbe` + `ComputeFilePaths` + `PrepareFileEffects` (split between `files_probe.go` and `files_resolve.go`), `ConfirmCommand`, `RunCommand`, notify helpers, and a comprehensive set of type aliases that re-export every spec contract type (`type Runner = spec.Runner`, `type RunContext = spec.RunContext`, etc.) plus every concrete runner type (`type HostRunner = host.Runner`, `type WorkflowRunner = workflow.Runner`, etc.) so external callers and the `internal/core/usercommands/usercommands.go` re-export chain compile unchanged. `runners/workflow/` aliases the filesgate `spec` package as `fgspec` to avoid collision with the runtime `spec`. **Cycle-break for `workflow → RunCommand`**: the workflow runner needs `runtime.RunCommand`, `runtime.BuildRunContext`, and `runtime.ComputeFilePathsProbe`, but importing the root package from a subpackage would form a cycle (root imports `runners/workflow` for the factory). Resolution: `runners/workflow/workflow.go` declares three function-variable seams (`RunCommandFn`, `BuildRunContextFn`, `ComputeFilePathsProbeFn`) wired by root `runtime`'s `init()` — set once at process start, called from workflow internals. A workflow-package external test file (`wire_test.go`, `package workflow_test`) blank-imports root runtime so the workflow test binary picks up the same wiring without forming a build cycle. Script env contract: `DWE_BIN`, `DWE_FILES_JSON`, `DWE_NONINTERACTIVE`. **Shell env contract** (`hostContractEnv` in `runners/host/host.go`): `DWE_BIN` + `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME` + `COMPOSE_FILE` (colon-joined, absolutised against `ProjectRoot`) — appended after `env:` so contract wins on duplicate keys. **Service-runner template fields**: `resolveServiceFields` renders `service`, `workdir`, `workdir_from` (and their `runner:` overrides) through `tpl.RenderCommand` before resolution — same contract as `argv`/`cmd`/`compose_args`. This unblocks `service: app-${param.service}` + `workdir_from: services.${param.service}.work_dir_internal` generic commands. `BuildRunContext` + `ComputeFilePathsProbe` are reused by the executor's files_gate evaluation. **`BuildPreRenderedRunContext`** is the pipeline-only sibling of `BuildRunContext`: identical except that `with:` values are stringified but NOT run through `tpl.RenderCommand`. The pipeline renders every step's `cmd`/`with`/`check`/`files_gate` leaves once at resolve time (`pipeline.renderStepFields`), and that render is **not idempotent** — a second pass would parse a value that resolved to text containing a literal `{{` as a Go template and fail the step, and would expand a value that resolved to another `${vars.*}` reference twice. Both pipeline consumers of a resolved `with:` (`execCommandAction`, the `evalFilesGate` probe) take this entry point; every non-pipeline caller (`dwe cmd`, workflow sub-steps via `BuildRunContextFn`, `validate/checks`) keeps `BuildRunContext` because their `with:` is still raw. **`RunContext.UnderParallel`** is set on every sub-step of a parallel group (pipeline or workflow); guards reject nested parallel (`ErrWorkflowNestedParallel`) and transitive interactive confirmations (`ErrConfirmInsideParallel`). **`RunContext.WorkflowSubStepOverrides`** carries pipeline-side per-sub-step `files_gate` directives forwarded from `DeployStep.SubStepOverrides` via `ActionContext.SubStepOverrides`; the workflow runner applies the gate before each matching sub-step in both sequential and parallel paths via `evalSubStepOverrideGate`. Overrides are intentionally not propagated to inner workflows. **`WorkflowStepObserver`** (2 methods: `OnStepStart` / `OnStepEnd(idx, step, StepResult{Status, Duration, Err, SkipReason})`) is an optional opt-in event sink for top-level sequential workflow steps; set via `RunContext.StepObserver` (nil → current plain behavior unchanged). Parallel groups are reported as a single step at the observer surface — the inner per-sub-step rendering keeps its existing `liveui.LiveLine` block path. Every early-`continue` branch (`when:` false, `files_gate` skip, `continue_on_error` failure) emits `OnStepEnd` before continuing so live rows can finalize. Companion optional capability **`StepIOSuspender`** (`SuspendForExec` / `ResumeAfterExec`, detected via comma-ok type assertion) is invoked iteration-scoped around every sequential command step so a surrounding live footer can pause for the child's stdout without stacking across iterations. The workflow's per-block `liveui.NewLiveLine` does NOT call `widgets.SetHuhHooks` — interactive prompts are forbidden inside parallel, and overwriting hooks set by a surrounding `PlainReporter` would corrupt its pause/resume dance. Parallel block rendering: the per-sub-step `lineTee` callback refreshes the live row on **every** frame (final and non-final) so `\r`-progress bars are visible, but only commits newline-terminated frames to the per-sub-step buffer and log; the post-Wait emit suppresses per-sub-step status lines in TTY mode (already shown in the frozen block rows) and prints a single `writeParallelSummary` footer (`✓ [<elapsed>] parallel: <workflow-id>`), while non-TTY mode keeps the per-sub-step status lines plus a plain-text footer. Failure-output dumps print in BOTH modes. **`argv_append_from` (`internal/runio/args.go`)**: `AppendArgvFrom(ctx, rc, argv)` runs the command's host shell expression via `config.ShellBin` with `cmd.Dir = rc.ProjectRoot` (NOT `cmd.Workdir` — for a `service_exec` command that names a path inside the container; the project root is the rule `condition.EvalCmd` already follows), captures stdout only (stderr streams to the user, stdin stays unwired), and appends ONE argv element per output line **byte-for-byte** — never re-parsed as shell, trailing newline ignored, blank lines dropped (a stray `""` silently changes what a tool does).
