Appended items come AFTER the declared `argv` with `${args}` already spliced in place. Rendering goes through the exported `RenderArgvAppendFrom` wrapping the UNEXPORTED `withoutArgs(rc)` — keep `withoutArgs` unexported: the point is one safe entry point both `runners/service` and `runners/host` share, not a second primitive each can wire up its own way (`${args}` is rewritten to `"$@"` and passed as positional parameters everywhere else, so leaving `Args` visible in this one field would make it interpolate caller bytes into program text; a literal `${args}` is additionally rejected at load time). Empty output returns **`spec.ErrArgvAppendEmpty`**, whose full skip contract is written on the sentinel and translated in exactly one place — `runtime.RunCommand`: stderr note + nil error (exit 0), `messages.success` suppressed, desktop notification suppressed (the declined-confirmation precedent), file effects rolled back as on the error path. A pipeline `type: command` step therefore **Finishes** rather than Skipping and journals as success — such a step needs a `files_gate`/`check:`. Load-time placement rules (argv-only, `type: daemon` rejected) live in `model/types.go` `validateArgvAppendFrom` + the per-type `allowedFieldsFor` allowlist. The `BuildPreRenderedRunContext` hazard is that `BuildRunContext`'s per-value `tpl.RenderCommand` loop is **ungated** — it renders every `with:` value unconditionally, unlike the pipeline's `hasKnownVarRef`-gated pass — so the literal-`{{` failure is not hypothetical: a var holding a `docker inspect -f "{{.State.Status}}"` format string resolves to text the second pass then parses as a Go template. The `${args}` invisibility in `argv_append_from` is a **consistency rule, not a security boundary** *for that field*, and the plan that introduced it says so outright (`docs/plans/completed/20260804-pipeline-primitives.md` constraint 3a): dwe runs on the developer's own machine and a workspace author who can write `argv_append_from` can already run anything via `type: shell`, so there is no boundary left to defend at that level. Its output becomes argv elements, never program text, and the value is never sanitized or quoted on this basis; the rule exists so no single field becomes the one that interpolates caller arguments into program text while every other field does not. The **shared** `withoutArgs` helper is a different matter and must stay unexported: in `RenderShellCommand` it is what stops the raw Go-template form `{{ index .Args 0 }}` from bypassing the `"$@"` positional transport, which `args.go`'s own comment calls "the whole security property" and records as verified exploitable before the change (this is also why `RenderArgvAppendFrom` is th and `RenderArgvAppendFrom` is the only entry point; it is belt-and-braces to the load-time literal-`${args}` rejection, since it also covers the raw Go-template form `{{ index .Args 0 }}` that a textual check cannot see). The two placement rules whose reasons are non-obvious: `cmd:` is rejected because appending to a shell string would splice the computed values back into program text — exactly the surface the `${args}` → `"$@"` transport exists to avoid — while `type: daemon` is rejected *even though it accepts argv*, because the expansion packs that argv into the synthetic `.start` command, so the documented "empty output → skip" semantics would read as "silently fail to start the daemon". `validateArgvAppendFrom` additionally requires a non-empty `argv:` that declares the program: an `argv:` of nothing but `${args}` splices to an EMPTY vector when the command runs without pass-through arguments, which would make the first computed line `argv[0]` and execute it relative to the project root. The sentinel contract also leaves `--output json` unaffected — command execution streams the child's output and has no JSON envelope to carry a skip state.

## Service Workdir & Exec Mode Contract

Cross-cutting: `internal/core/project/config/` + `internal/core/usercommands/runtime/runners/service/` + `internal/core/execution/builtin/containers/`. It answers the two questions a container command used to leave to whoever wrote the image — "which directory does it land in?" and "what happens when the container is not running?" — and answers them the same way for `service_exec`, `service_run` and `type: daemon`. User-facing shape: `docs/reference/config/commands/types.md` § Workdir resolution and § Mode resolution.

- **`config.ServiceByContainer(cfg, container) (ServiceConfig, bool)`** — the single service lookup for runtime callers. **Matches on the `Container` field, NOT on the `cfg.Services` map key**: the folder `workspace/services/main/` may declare `container: app-main`, and every runtime speaker (a command's `service:`, `docker compose exec <name>`, the daemon builtin's `service`) uses the compose service name, so a key-based lookup silently misses. It iterates `slices.Sorted(maps.Keys(...))` because two folders may legally declare the same `container:` and `cfg.Services` is a map — without the sort, first-match-wins would depend on randomized map order and the same project would resolve differently between runs. Nil config or an empty container name returns `(zero, false)`, never a panic.
- **`config.ContainerWorkdirFallback(cfg, container) string`** — `cli.workdir` → `work_dir_internal` → `dir_internal`, first non-empty, `""` when the container is unknown or none is set. This is the same order `dwe shell` applies, and matching it is the whole point: a command and a shell session into one service now land in the same directory. **`dwe shell` is not yet a caller** — `internal/cli/shell/exec.go`'s `resolveShellOptions` still inlines its own copy of the three rungs (below a `--workdir` flag rung it alone has) and resolves the service by the `cfg.Services` map key rather than by `container:`. That is a known outstanding drift, not an exemption from the rule below; converting it is the natural next step, and until then a `container:` differing from its folder name resolves differently in the two paths. Both helpers live in **`project/config`** rather than beside the runner because `runio` sits under `runtime/internal/` and the daemon builtin cannot import it; that placement is the reason they are exported at all.
- **The seven-rung workdir chain** — `resolveServiceFields` (`runners/service/exec.go`) and `resolveDaemonWorkdirUser` (`builtin/containers/daemon_start.go`) implement one chain, first non-empty wins: (1) `workdir: internal` → no flag, fallback skipped, stop; (2) `runner.workdir_from` → `workdir_from`; (3) `runner.workdir` → `workdir`; (4) `cli.workdir`; (5) `work_dir_internal`; (6) `dir_internal`; (7) no `--workdir` flag, the image's own `WORKDIR` applies. Rungs 4–6 are `ContainerWorkdirFallback`, looked up **after** the `runner.service` redirect, exactly like the `cli.user` fallback. A second implementation of any rung is a drift bug — extend the helper, not the caller.
- **The `internal` sentinel outranks `workdir_from`** — for that one value the published "`workdir_from` beats a literal `workdir`" rule **inverts**, because `internal` is not a path but an explicit "no `--workdir`, and do not guess one either", and there is no other way to express it. Deliberate symmetry with `model.UserModeInternal` on `user:`, which likewise emits no flag and skips the `cli.user` fallback. The consequence is that a command whose container workdir is literally the relative path `internal` changed meaning — the one place this work moved an already-explicit declaration.
- **Daemon symmetry (`resolveDaemonWorkdirUser`)** — pure (config in, two strings out), so the resolution is testable apart from `DaemonStart.Run`, which only calls it. Three externally visible daemon behaviours changed when it adopted the shared chain: `workdir_from` now beats a literal `workdir` (the daemon did the inverse, contradicting the documented service-runner precedence); **a dot-path resolving to nil now falls through to the next rung instead of aborting `.start`** with `path not found in config`; and the user chain **gained** its second rung. That first pair trades a hard error for a success in a different directory, which is the accepted cost of one chain; a **non-string** value at the dot-path is still a hard error, and that distinction is the only thing left catching a typo'd path. The user chain is now two rungs (`user:` → `cli.user` → no flag), where the daemon previously read `user:` alone and emitted no flag without it — a daemon whose service sets `cli.user` therefore changes the uid it runs as, and with it the ownership of everything it writes. That is why task 1 of the plan gated the whole half on an inventory: the corpus holds exactly one daemon and it declares `user:` explicitly, so nothing moved. `internal` is non-empty, so it stops at rung one and `buildStartExtraArgs` emits nothing.
- **`model.DefaultExecMode` is `ExecModeExecOrRun`** — a `service_exec` command with no `mode:` falls back to `docker compose run --rm` (warning on stderr) when the container is stopped, instead of refusing. `ExecModeExecOrFail` is now the **opt-in** for commands that must never create a container, and the only mode that pre-probes purely to produce that error. The flip is observable in exactly one state — the probe succeeds and reports the container stopped; **both** modes already select `exec` when the probe *errors*, so an unreachable daemon behaves identically before and after and must not be documented as changed (pinned by a test).
- **The default's reach into `check:`** — a pipeline `type: command` action pointing at a mode-less `service_exec` command becomes **container-creating**: a postcondition that used to fail cleanly on a stopped stack now starts an ephemeral container to answer itself. Such checks should declare `mode: exec-or-fail`. Pinned by an executor-level test in package `pipeline` (`executor_test.go`) rather than in `runners/service` — `service_test.go` cannot import `pipeline` without a cycle, and the probe seam is unexported — so the stub-docker harness with a `compose ps` printing nothing is what makes "stopped" reachable from there.
- **`containerRunningFn`** — the probe seam (`var containerRunningFn = isContainerRunning`) exists because the real probe shells out to `docker compose ps`. Every test that needs to reach a probing mode replaces it and restores it with `t.Cleanup`; before the seam, no test could exercise the default at all, which is why the old default went unexamined. Tests here assert the exec-vs-run **branch** and the warning text, never a full argv slice — an argv assertion would hard-code whatever the container-TTY decision emits into a test about exec modes and couple two independent contracts.

## Core — Validation (`internal/core/validate/`)

- `internal/core/validate/` — static project validation framework: `Validator` interface, `GroupValidator` interface (a `Validator` that owns child validators sharing its `Domain()`; `Children() []Validator` + `RunGroup(ctx, children) []Diagnostic` — `Registry.Run` expands children during scope matching so `[<domain>]` and `[<domain>, <child-id>]` both work, then delegates execution to `RunGroup` so the group can choose its own scheduling. Used by `validate/linters/` and `validate/i18n/` for parallel fan-out or grouped validation; the group's `Run` is unused), `Diagnostic` (severity / domain / target / file / line / message / hint), `Context` (carries `CommandRegistry any` — nil-tolerant: validators self-skip gate checks; also carries `ValidateCfg *config.ValidateConfig` + `ValidateCfgWarnings []Diagnostic` + `ValidateCfgLoadErr error` populated by the caller's single `LoadValidateConfig` parse so validators never re-parse the file), `Registry` (scope-dispatch), `Summary`, `ExitCode` (severity-gated). `Severity` and `Diagnostic` are type aliases for the leaf `internal/core/validate/diag` package (avoids an import cycle with `internal/core/project/config`, which references `diag.Severity` from `CheckEntry`). Subpackages: `config/` (per-file YAML validators + `deploy_files_gate` aggregator + `parallel_groups` walker matching the pipeline sentinels via `errors.Is`; `config.validate` validator reads `ValidateCfgLoadErr` / `ValidateCfgWarnings` from `Context` and is the SOLE emitter of validate.yml load-error diagnostics — `os.ErrNotExist` is silent, every other load error becomes one error diagnostic; the `generated` validator (`generated.go`) checks each `generated.<field>` — `pattern` required and compiles as a regex with **≥1 capture group** else `SeverityError`, `file` is a pathsafe contained-relative path, field name a valid `${generated.<name>}` identifier — plus `render.config.template` pin resolution (warn if a pinned pack does not resolve) and a cross-check that `generated-missing <svc> <field>` predicates reference a declared `generated:` field via the shared `condition.ParseGeneratedMissing` parser; the `deprecations` validator (`deprecations.go`) emits `SeverityWarning` with `File:Line` (via a yaml-node walk) for `configs:`/`mountpoint` in `service.yml` and `service_configs_copy`/`service_configs_check` in `deploy.yml`/`reset.yml` — warnings do not change exit code outside `--strict`), `templates/` (per-kind manifest + override validators; explicit `enabled: false` opt-out is silent — only policy and actionable skips emit info), `commands/` (uses `loader.ParseCommandFile` for categorised diagnostics), `env/` and `checks/` (see below), `i18n/` (see below). **Domain assembly convention**: every domain exposes an `All(...)`-shaped constructor returning `[]Validator` (`config.All()`, `env.All(cfg)`, `checks.AllForStage(s)`, `commands`/`templates` loops, `setup.All(...)`, `linters.All(...)`, `i18n.All(...)`, `snapshot.All(...)`, `bridge.All()`, `tests.All()`), and `internal/cli/validate/validate.go::buildRegistry` is the single registration site — adding a domain is one `All()` plus one `Register` loop, never a change to `Registry`. **Validators are per-file and mutually independent by design**: `config/` alone registers one validator per config file plus the cross-file aggregators, `Registry.Run` invokes each matching `Run` with no shared error path or early return, and `dwe validate` deliberately proceeds on a failed merged-config load (`loadForValidate` returns the `errPartialLoad` sentinel with a nil `*DweConfig`; domain validators nil-guard `ctx.Cfg`) — so one unparseable file yields its own diagnostic instead of blinding the run to every other file's. **Preflight participation is narrow and opt-in**: `preflight.Run` assembles `env.All` + `checks.AllForStages` + the single `config.validate` validator and nothing else; every other domain (`config/`'s remaining validators, `commands/`, `templates/`, `setup/`, `linters/`, `i18n/`, `snapshot/`, `bridge/`, `tests/`) is `dwe validate`-only, because preflight answers "can we run?" and a content mistake in an unrelated domain must never block a lifecycle command. Registering a new domain in `preflight.Run` is therefore a deliberate decision, not the default. The `config/` subpackage also owns `services_folder.go` — validator ID `services-folder`, the per-service-folder shape check that pins the folder symmetry: a folder without `service.yml` is a `SeverityError` (`service folder %q has no service.yml`, hint `every service folder must contain a service.yml file`) and suppresses the folder-level `SeverityOK` diagnostic, while any regular file outside the `knownServiceFiles` allowlist (`service.yml`, `deploy.yml`, `reset.yml`) is a `SeverityWarning` naming the expected set; subdirectories and a missing `workspace/services/` directory are ignored. **Adding a new per-service file type therefore requires adding it to `knownServiceFiles`** — otherwise every project that adopts the new file gets an `unknown file in service folder` warning from `dwe validate`. And `template_refs.go` owns the `config.template_refs` check, the `dwe validate` complement to `tpl`'s known-head whitelist: a `${head.path}` whose head IS a known root key but whose remaining path does not resolve in `cfg.Raw` is a `SeverityWarning` — almost always a `vars:` typo, which otherwise renders to a literal `${vars.opechatka}` with no error until the step runs. It enumerates references with `varsusage.EnumerateAllUsages(ctx.ProjectRoot)`, drops non-references via `tpl.IsVarNamespaceRef` (a head-only `${state}` / `${vars}` is a shell variable, not a reference), and gates on `config.IsAllowedRootKey` — which deliberately excludes `param` / `context` / `files` / `host` / `snapshot` / `args` (never in `Raw`) and `generated` (lenient-by-contract and empty on first deploy), so the validator does not become its own noise source. The gate is the root-key ALLOWLIST, not a "head present in `cfg.Raw`" probe: the strict root makes `Raw`'s keys a subset of `allowedRootKeys`, so a presence probe would go silent on the case that matters most — a project with no `vars:` block at all, where every `${vars.*}` reference is unresolvable.
- `internal/core/validate/env/` — seven hardcoded environment probes: `env.docker_bin`, `env.docker_daemon`, `env.docker_compose` (accepts any plugin version — the `docker compose` subcommand resolving at all is itself proof of the plugin; legacy v1 was a separate `docker-compose` binary), `env.git_bin`, `env.shell_bin`, `env.project_perms` (verifies `.dwe/` is writable via create-temp-file), `env.ports_free` (collects host ports from `cfg.Services[*].Ports` for enabled services; queries `docker ps --format=json` once and classifies each declared port: containers labelled `com.docker.compose.project=<our project>` are tolerated, foreign-compose / unlabelled docker containers trigger a conflict naming the foreign container, ports outside Docker fall back to `net.Listen` to detect non-Docker processes). Probes are config-blind beyond the nil-safe binary accessors `config.DockerBin` / `config.GitBin` / `config.ShellBin` (a nil cfg yields the defaults). Docker daemon / compose / ports probes use a 5s timeout and self-suppress when the docker binary is missing (avoid double-reporting alongside `env.docker_bin`). `env.ports_free` also self-skips on `Context.Stage == "stop"` (port conflicts irrelevant when stopping); load failures for `docker.yml` (no compose project name available) fall through to a docker-blind run that still catches conflicts but cannot distinguish "ours" from foreign. `All(cfg)` returns the seven probes in fixed order. Hints follow the project's concise format; long remediation text uses `\n` splits. **Test seams**: `dockerPSOutFn` (canned `docker ps` NDJSON) and `portListenFn` (simulated busy/free port) live as package-level vars in `ports.go` for swap-in-tests.
- `internal/core/validate/checks/` — declarative project checks from `workspace/validate.yml`. `AllForStage(cfg, loadErr, baseDir, cmdRegistry, stage, services, skipServicesGate)` and its multi-stage form `AllForStages(... stages ...)` produce one synthetic `Validator` per `CheckEntry` (Domain=`checks`, ID=entry.ID). Both signatures are nil-tolerant — a nil `*config.ValidateConfig` yields an empty slice; a nil `*registry.Registry` yields unknown-command diagnostics for any `type: command` entry. Both return `[]Validator` only — never an `error`. Load-time problems (unknown builtin, unknown user command, `with:` validation failure against `builtin.Validate`, target user command type outside the `{shell, script}` whitelist) become a pre-baked failing validator that emits a single cached `Diagnostic` from `Run`. Runtime `type: command` dispatch goes through `runtime.BuildRunContext(cfg, cmdRegistry, def, entry.With, projectRoot)` (the `with:` block IS the user-command params payload) and is locked down: `SkipConfirm=true`, `NonInteractive=true`, `SkipNotify=true`, `Stdout=io.Discard`, `Stderr=&bytes.Buffer{}` (captured for inclusion in the failure diagnostic), `Stdin=nil`. `MatchStage(entry, stage)` and `MatchAnyStage(entry, stages)` are the stage-filter primitives (empty stage / empty slice = match all). `AllForStage` delegates to the multi-stage `AllForStages`, which `preflight.Run` calls via `stagesForPreflight(stage)` — passing `{deploy, post-setup}` for the deploy final preflight so `post-setup` checks (skipped at the interactive pre-wizard gate, which queries only `deploy`) run there. Stage is metadata on `CheckEntry`, not on `Validator` — `validate.Registry.Run` only filters via `MatchScope` (domain / domain+id), so stage filtering happens at *assembly time* one level up. `MatchServices(entry, cfg.Services)` is the second assembly-time filter: a check with `services: [a, b]` only assembles when at least one of `a` / `b` is enabled in the merged config (OR semantics; empty/missing list = run unconditionally). `AllForStage` takes a `skipServicesGate bool` to bypass this filter when the caller is `dwe validate checks <id>` (explicit-by-ID escape hatch — currently used by `internal/cli/validate/` when `scope = ["checks", "<id>"]`). Unknown service names are NOT detected here — the loader has no view of `cfg.Services`. Detection lives in the `config.validate` validator (`internal/core/validate/config/validate_yml.go::validateUnknownServiceRefs`) so typos surface as load-time error diagnostics in the single `dwe validate` pass. User-visible schema: `docs/reference/config/validate.md`.
- `internal/core/validate/setup/` — setup wizard validators from `workspace/setup.yml`. `All(setupCfg, setupErr, setupPath)` (function-arg form, no `validate.Context` extension to avoid import cycles) produces one `setup.parse` validator + per-question validators. Assembly mirrors the snapshot-domain pattern: the SOLE emitter of `setup.parse` load-error diagnostics, called once by both `runValidate` and the menu RunE. Validators include `setup.type_known` / `setup.id_required` / `setup.writes_required` / `setup.id_unique` / `setup.writes_unique` / `setup.writes_syntax` / `setup.writes_scope` (three enforcement layers: forbidden top-level namespaces, service-overlay leaf shapes, non-service paths) / `setup.options_valid` / `setup.validate_exclusive` / `setup.validate_only_on_input` / `setup.validate_preset_known` / `setup.validate_regex_compiles` / `setup.type_writes_consistent` / `setup.required_consistent`. Reuses `config.ValidIdentifierKey` for identifier validation so rules can't drift. Reuses `config.overlayAllowedKeys` for service-overlay scope. User-visible schema: `docs/reference/config/setup.md`.
- `internal/core/validate/linters/` — external linter domain (`linters.*`). Built-in adapters (`shellcheck`, `hadolint`) plus a `type: generic` exec-and-wrap adapter; `Adapter` interface (`DefaultBin` / `DefaultPaths` / `DefaultExtensions` / `DefaultFilenames` / `ReservedFlags` / `BuildArgs` / `ParseOutput`); `walk.collectFiles(baseDir, paths, exts, filenames, pathsAreDefaults)` (recursive `filepath.WalkDir` rooted under `baseDir`; symlinks and `.git/` always skipped; `pathsafe.ContainedRel` containment with a short-circuit for `paths: ["."]` since `ContainedRel` rejects `.` by design; missing **default** paths are silently dropped, missing **user-configured** paths surface a Warning); `runtime.linterValidator` (per-linter `validate.Validator` — autodetect via `exec.LookPath`, default-bin-missing silent / explicit-bin-missing Warning, `exec.CommandContext` with `context.WithTimeout(parent, DefaultLinterTimeout)` where parent falls back to `context.Background()` when `ctx.Ctx` is nil, output bounded by `MaxLinterOutputBytes` via `boundedWriter`; two diagnostic buckets — `operationalDiags` never clamped, `findings` clamped from above by `entry.Severity` if set — so `severity: info` cannot silence a timeout Error or panic); `lintersGroup` implementing `GroupValidator` (`sync.WaitGroup` + buffered-channel semaphore — **NOT** `errgroup.WithContext` because one linter's failure must not cancel siblings; per-goroutine `recover()` converts panics to Error; concurrency capped at `MaxLinterConcurrency = runtime.NumCPU()`; pre-allocated `results[i]` slot per child — no shared slice, no mutex); `All(validateCfg, validateLoadErr, baseDir, userCfg)` (corrupt-config short-circuit returns zero validators; `validateCfg == nil` falls through to per-adapter autodetect; reserved-flag rejection via `validateUserFlags(adapter, entry.Flags)` produces a synthetic error validator instead of registering the linter, so a bad config short-circuits before any subprocess runs). Package-level vars `DefaultLinterTimeout` / `MaxLinterOutputBytes` / `MaxLinterConcurrency` are exported for test override (test helpers swap + `t.Cleanup`-restore). Built-in adapters lock output format via `ReservedFlags` (parser-load-bearing) — reservation match policy covers exact, `--long=value`, `["--long", "value"]`, and **short-attached `-fgcc`** forms. Real-binary smoke tests for shellcheck/hadolint are gated by `exec.LookPath` + `testing.Short()`. User-visible schema: `docs/reference/config/validate.md`.
- `internal/core/validate/i18n/` — translation-file domain (`i18n.*`). Validates project-layer translation files (`workspace/i18n/*.yml`) via three validators: `parse_error` (strict-decode failures and directory-level read errors), `orphan` (warnings for `commands.<id>` / `groups.<id>` entries whose ID is not in the user-command registry; per-locale disambiguation via `ID = pf.Locale+"/"+id`), `unknown_ui_key` (warnings for `ui.*` keys not in the whitelist maintained by `internal/shared/i18n.KnownUIKeys`). `All(projectFiles []i18n.ProjectFile, reg *registry.Registry) []validate.Validator` aggregates the three; per-file read/parse errors surface via the `parse_error` validator (ID `_load_error`), and the CLI call site discards the loader error. Loader: `i18n.LoadProjectBundles(projectRoot)` returns per-file parse metadata for the validator; silent on absent directory, non-fatal on per-file errors. Does NOT run in preflight (preflight answers "can we run?", not "is the content clean?"); only in `dwe validate`. User-visible schema: `docs/reference/config/i18n.md`.
- `internal/core/validate/snapshot/` — static validators for `workspace/snapshot.yml` and on-disk snapshot directories. `All(cfg, snapCfg, snapCfgErr, baseDir, reg, verifyChecksums) []validate.Validator` is the single assembly seam, registered from `internal/cli/validate/validate.go` (`buildRegistry`) alongside `env.All` and `checks.AllForStage`. Validators emit `snapshot.*` diagnostics: `config_loadable` (silent on `os.ErrNotExist`), `create_defined` / `restore_defined` (info), `variant_pairing` / `rollback_target_exists` (warn), `<name>.manifest_valid` / `<name>.artifacts_exist` (error), `<name>.checksums` (warn, runs only with `--verify`), `<name>.last_create_failed` (info), and `template_scope` (error — catches `${snapshot.created_at}` in `create:` blocks earlier than the runtime compile check). `dwe validate snapshot [<name>] [--verify]` is the dedicated subcommand (mirrors `validate env` / `validate checks`); `<name>` scoping filters via `MatchScope` to a single snapshot.
- `internal/core/validate/bridge/` — host-bridge domain (`bridge.*`): a single `servicesValidator` (ID `services`) over every service's `bridge:` block. Errors: unknown `on_unreachable` value (closed enum `fail`|`warn` — an unknown value is inert at runtime, behaving as `fail`, which is exactly how a typo silently flips the shim's exit-code policy, so it fires **regardless of the enabled toggle**) and relative `shim_path` (container path → `path.IsAbs`, NOT `filepath.IsAbs` — a Windows host must not reject `/usr/local/bin/dwe`). Warnings (only when bridge is effectively enabled): no container target (unreachable on loaded configs — `container:` defaults to the folder name — kept for literal-built configs, mirroring the `BuildOverlaySpec` skip), and the reachable hazard: no `dir`/`dir_internal` workspace mapping → the overlay omits `DWE_HOST_WORKSPACE`/`DWE_CONTAINER_WORKSPACE`, shim cwd translation is unavailable, and the daemon containment check rejects every in-container invocation (type-aware hint: apps declare the pair; tool/infra cannot declare `dir` under strict decode → opt out or mount at an identical path). Registered in `internal/cli/validate/validate.go` via `valbridge.All()` alongside the other domain loops, with a `dwe validate bridge` scoped subcommand. Participates in `dwe validate` ONLY — never preflight (preflight consumes only `env.All`, `checks.AllForStages` and the single cherry-picked `config.validate` validator; a bridge config mistake must not block unrelated lifecycle).
- `internal/core/validate/tests/` (integration-tests stage 3a) — `workspace/tests/` scenario domain (`tests.*`). `All()` → a single `scenariosValidator` (`Domain()=="tests"`, `ID()=="scenarios"`), guarded on `ctx.Cfg != nil` throughout (mirrors the nil-cfg guard other domains use for the `errPartialLoad` path); resolves `workspace/tests/` and iterates `*.yml` in filename order. Per file: loads via `envtest.LoadScenario` (a load error is one `SeverityError` diagnostic and — for free — covers **name normalisation**, since `LoadScenario` already runs `ValidateScenarioName`), parses `Timeout` via `time.ParseDuration`, and checks every `env.services` enable/disable entry against `ctx.Cfg.Services`. Steps are validated as **one whole phase, exactly like runtime**: all steps' `with:`/`cmd:` are rendered, wrapped in a single synthetic `config.DeployPhase{Name: "tests"}`, and resolved via **one** `pipeline.ResolvePhaseSteps` call — matching `envtest`'s own `runSteps`, so it catches whole-phase invariants (notably `checkUniqueStepNames`) that a per-step validation pass would miss. The render context is `ctx.Cfg.Raw` overlaid with the scenario's own `env.vars`, with any `env.vars` entry whose value is the literal `auto` substituted by a fixed placeholder host port — `auto` is the only magic value in the schema (ports only), so a `${vars.db.port}` param renders to a valid int and passes strict-typed builtin validation, while a genuinely bad **concrete** param elsewhere still fails as `SeverityError` — deliberately NOT a blanket `${...}`-in-raw downgrade heuristic (that would mask concrete errors alongside templated ones). Documented residual limitation: a var populated only post-deploy (e.g. `${generated.*}`) is absent at validate time and can yield a spurious diagnostic unless given a project-level default. `type: command` step refs resolve via `reg, ok := ctx.CommandRegistry.(*registry.Registry)` (re-implemented locally, 3 lines — `registryFrom` is unexported in `internal/core/validate/config`, not shared); nil/absent registry skips the command-ref check entirely. Emits `config.ScanComposeIsolation(ctx.Cfg, ctx.ProjectRoot)` findings once per file-set (outside the per-file loop), always as `SeverityWarning` — the runner, not this validator, is the tier that treats a `Blocking` finding as fatal. **Never registered in `preflight.Run`** — validate-only by design, so a scenario file typo never blocks an unrelated `dwe deploy run`. `dwe validate tests` is the dedicated CLI scope (registered in `buildRegistry` alongside the other domains).
- `internal/core/validate/secrets/` — encrypted-secrets domain (`secrets.*`), the smallest domain after `bridge/`. Two validators split along the content/readiness line: **`secrets.recipient`** (content, `dwe validate` only) fires when markers or `.age` sources exist but `secrets.recipient` is missing / not an `age1…`, and on a damaged marker payload (`secrets.CheckMarker` — identity-free, which is why `corrupt` belongs here rather than under "unresolved": a keyless developer must not be sent hunting for a key that would not have helped). It raw-loads the layers itself when `ctx.Cfg == nil`, so the scoped `dwe validate secrets` run that is supposed to *explain* a failed config load is not blinded by it. **`secrets.unresolved`** (readiness) fires on a non-empty `cfg.SecretsState.Unresolved` or on an `.age` source in a resolvable config pack that **actually fails to decrypt** with the loaded identity (a file encrypted to another recipient or truncated is caught here, not mid-deploy); its `.age` scan mirrors `renderConfigsForRun`'s `config.DeployOrder(cfg, ["app"])` iteration, so a disabled service or an unresolvable pack is invisible to it exactly as it is at render time. Diagnostics are grouped **by reason** (one row per reason listing sorted paths) because a keyless developer has every marker unresolved for one cause and one row per marker would bury the single actionable fix. `UnresolvedValidator()` is the cherry-pick constructor used by `preflight.Run` and `runPreWizardPreflight` — **not** a filter of `All()` by ID, so adding a content validator to the domain can never silently start blocking lifecycle commands. **Both validators emit a `SeverityOK` row on a healthy project** (`render.FormatSummary` prints an all-zero counter set as `validation skipped (no files found)`, which made a healthy state indistinguishable from a domain that never ran — on the one command a developer runs to check themselves after onboarding). `secrets.recipient`'s row is tied to the recipient ALONE (set, valid, `diags` empty), so a project that ran `secrets init` and encrypted nothing yet still gets it; its `Message` is empty, the target is the statement. `unresolvedValidator.Run` is ordered inventory-FIRST for this — a `markers == 0 && recipient == ""` fast path FIRST of all (this validator runs in preflight, so a project with no `secrets:` block must not pay for `collectEncryptedSources`' per-service `ResolveTemplatePack` + `LoadManifest` on every lifecycle command; an `.age` source without a recipient is `secrets.recipient`'s content error, so nothing is hidden), then markers + `collectEncryptedSources`, return nil on an empty inventory (a project with no secrets stays silent), marker diags, identity source from `SecretsState.IdentitySource` or the `LoadIdentity` `Source` KEPT for an `.age`-only project, `.age` checks, and the OK row only when nothing was emitted; the old early return skipped marker-only projects before any source was known. The counter message is its own string (`"%d encrypted value(s) and %d config-pack source(s) readable via %s"`) — `inventoryPhrase` is the ERROR helper with `(e.g. …)` examples and is not reused — and the source goes through `sourcePhrase`, the source WORD only, so a positive row can never widen what `dwe validate --output json` reveals about the machine. `sortDiagnostics` orders severity DESCENDING, so on a mixed project the error stays `Diagnostics[0]` and the OK row lands last; preflight and the pre-wizard gate filter `SeverityOK` and print nothing extra. Registered in `internal/cli/validate/validate.go` (`buildRegistry`, a `dwe validate secrets` leaf, and the scope-description case).

## Core — Docs (`internal/core/docs/`)

- `internal/core/docs/` — embedded documentation browser: `embed.go` exposes `BuiltinFS fs.FS` via `//go:embed embedded` (synced tree committed to `internal/core/docs/embedded/`, mirrored from `docs/reference`, `docs/internals`, `docs/i18n` plus the repo-root `README.md` copied flat to `embedded/README.md` at build time); `source.go` defines `DocRoot{Name, FS, ProjectPath}` and `Sources(projectRoot)` returning built-in + optional project roots; `tree.go` walks the trees to build a file-node hierarchy; `topic.go` implements topic parsing (`ParseTopic` splits `path#anchor`) and resolution (exact match → fuzzy substring match across roots); `lang.go` implements per-file language fallback with content-hash staleness detection; `content_hashes_gen.go` is a committed generated file (regenerated by `make build`) containing `var ContentHashes map[string]string` — the manifest of `sha256(file bytes)[:12]` per en-language file for translation staleness checking. The README is keyed by the flat `"README.md"` relpath (not docs-prefixed); translation walkers and resolvers must accept this top-level relpath alongside `reference/...` paths. Every markdown-*content* call site resolves `locale` through `i18n.ResolveLocale(flagLang, cfgLang, sysLang)` **directly** rather than using `rflags.Locale` (clamped to the YAML store, wrong namespace for markdown); `dwe docs generate` is the deliberate counter-example — it renders YAML-store command strings, so `rflags.Locale` is the right source there. Docs subsystem is **read-only** — never calls `lock.AcquireProjectLocks` and never runs preflight; works without `workspace.yml` (project DocRoot simply omitted). The `//go:generate` directive in `embed.go` uses the relative path `../../../scripts/sync-embedded-docs.sh` (three segments up due to the deeper `internal/core/docs/` location). **Anchor derivation is single-sourced through `parseHeadingSlugLabel` (`headings.go`)** — it returns `(level, slug, label)` where the **slug comes from the RAW heading text and the label from the markdown-stripped one**, and every producer (`ParseHeadingSlugs` → `docs show --anchors`/`--toc`; `ParseDoc` → `Heading.Slug`, consumed by `docstui`'s link jumps; `search.go`'s section attribution → the `#anchor` in every `docs search` row) plus the consumer (`SliceByAnchor` → `topic#anchor`) goes through it. The asymmetry is the whole point: `stripInlineMarkdown` ends in `stripEmphasis`, and slugging its output dropped the `_` in every builtin name and snake_case key, so three of the four surfaces advertised `servicedirsensure` while the resolver answered only to `service_dirs_ensure` — the tool handed out anchors it then rejected. Never call `Slugify(stripInlineMarkdown(x))`, and never recompute a slug from `Heading.Text` (it is the lossy display label; read `Heading.Slug`). `stripEmphasis` itself follows CommonMark's intra-word rule for `_` (a run flanked by alphanumerics on both sides is literal), so labels read `env_file`, not `envfile`; asterisks are still dropped unconditionally. Guarded by `TestAdvertisedAnchorsResolve`, which asserts over the **real embedded tree** that every advertised anchor resolves — the defect was invisible to per-side unit tests because the two sides were never compared. **`docs show` long-document hint**: when a whole document ≥ `longDocMinLines` (120) with ≥ `longDocMinSections` (4) is emitted to a **non-TTY** stdout and no anchor was requested, `emitLongDocHint` (`internal/cli/docs/show.go`) writes one **stderr** line naming `--toc` and the `topic#anchor` form. stderr is load-bearing — the case it addresses is `docs show <topic> | head`, which truncates stdout only. It exists because documenting the flags was measurably insufficient: an observed agent session read the `llms-txt` briefing line telling it to use `--toc`/`--anchors` "instead of piping through `head`/`sed`" and then piped 17 of 17 `docs show` calls through `head`/`sed`. **Translation freshness gate**: every file under `docs/i18n/<lang>/` MUST begin with `> Translated from: <relPath> @ <hash>` where `<relPath>` is the en-source path relative to `docs/` (or bare `README.md`) and `<hash>` is the first 12 hex chars of `sha256(en-source)` recorded in `ContentHashes`; `lang_test.go::TestRussianTranslationsAreFresh` walks every `i18n/ru/**.md` in `BuiltinFS` and fails CI on missing headers, unknown relpaths, or stale hashes. Adding a new language requires a sibling `TestXxxTranslationsAreFresh` (current test is hard-coded to `i18n/ru`). User-visible schema: `docs/reference/docs/index.md` and `docs/reference/config/i18n.md` (long-form section). **`search.go`** (agent-discoverability milestone) — `Search(roots, query, locale, SearchOptions) []SearchHit`. Tokenized: `strings.Fields` (never `Split(q, " ")` — an empty token would zero every result), AND across tokens, ranked by **min** per-token count (sum lets a section repeating the commonest word outrank the one about the pair). Two tiers: section-level hits, plus per-document hits for a doc whose tokens all appear but never inside one section (the tier is per document — a global "nothing matched anywhere" tier would never fire). Both tiers are sorted together by `count desc → tier asc → path asc → section asc`: the tier is a **tie-break below the count**, so a doc-level hit matching four times outranks a section hit matching once (ordering the tiers outright buried the strongest answer below every weak one, invisible at a narrowed `--limit`). The tier lives on an unexported `rankedHit` for the duration of the sort and deliberately never reaches `SearchHit`, which is the `--output json` payload. Matching is case-insensitive **substring** by design (`uid` matches `guide`; `depends_on:` depends on it) — do not "fix" it to word boundaries. `SearchOptions.Literal` treats the whole query as one needle and, like the tokenized path, trims first so an all-whitespace query finds nothing. `SearchHit.Snippet` is the densest source line, whitespace-collapsed and capped at `snippetMaxLen` bytes **including** the ellipsis on a rune-then-word boundary, so the append-only 4th TSV column can never give a row a fifth field. The measurement behind the tie-break ordering: with the tiers ordered outright, a doc-level hit matching four times landed below twelve section hits — nine of them matching once — on the built-in tree for the query `UID GID env`. The tier is deliberately kept off `SearchHit` so it never reaches the JSON payload. `SearchOptions.Literal` is a flag rather than a quoting convention because `dwe docs search` takes exactly one argument and the shell strips quotes, so `"a b"` and `a b` arrive identical; matching stays case-insensitive **substring** by design (`uid` matches `guide`, and `depends_on:` depends on it) — do not "fix" it to word boundaries. Snippet sanitization is **two** passes and both are load-bearing: non-printable runes are DROPPED first — the snippet is the only channel through which document content reaches stdout and a doc tree can be untrusted (`--source project` inside a cloned repo), so an embedded ESC/BEL/OSC sequence would otherwise clear the screen, recolor it, or set the window title — and then `strings.Fields`+join collapses every whitespace run (tabs and newlines included, since markdown tables carry both) to a single space, which is what keeps the snippet from breaking a tab-separated row. The comparator carries a fifth key, `Source asc`, purely to make the order total. On the long-document hint: `--anchors` / `--toc` are a third silence gate — they short-circuit `runDocsShow` and return **before** `emitLongDocHint` is reached, so asking for structure never also triggers the hint; do not move the call earlier. The thresholds are deliberately high for the same reason the gates exist: the note must read as a property of *this* document being large, not as a banner on every read.
- `internal/core/docs/llmstxt/` — pure-function generator for the agent-targeted `llms.txt` index. `Generate(Opts) (string, error)` takes `ProjectRoot`, `IncludeIntern`, `DocTopics` ([]docs.TopicEntry), and exported summary types (`CommandSummary`, `ServiceSummary`, `InfoSummary`) populated by collectors in `internal/cli/docs/llmstxt_collectors.go`. No disk I/O, no config import — keeps the package golden-testable. Empty `DocTopics` (fresh checkout without `make build`) renders an empty Documentation section silently. CLI surface: `dwe docs llms-txt` (read-only, no lock, no preflight). `Opts` also carries `Builtins []BuiltinSummary`, `Conditions []ConditionSummary` and `ReservedEnvNames []string` (agent-discoverability milestone) feeding four static briefing sections; the layering rule is what forces them through `Opts` rather than an import. The **project-agnostic** (`--no-project`) output is capped at 12 KB by `TestDocsLlmsTxtCommand_SizeBudget` — the cap is on `--no-project` only, since a project-aware cap would test someone else's workspace size. That number is duplicated in the `Long` text, both locales of `docs/reference/docs/commands.md` and `AGENTS.md.tmpl`; change all four together. The file-output flag is **`--out PATH`**, deliberately not `--output`: the root command owns a persistent `--output` (format) with the `-o` shorthand, and a local long-name collision left cobra unable to resolve the shorthand at all (`Unknown shorthand flag: 'o'`). `--out` matches `dwe docs generate`; after the rename `-o json` resolves to the root format flag and is simply ignored here, exactly as on `dwe docs show` — the document IS the payload. There is no deprecated `--output` alias, because re-registering the long name would reintroduce the shadowing. The four briefing sections exist because an agent otherwise reverse-engineers this knowledge from other repositories; the whole milestone's goal is that it finds what already exists in dwe instead — which is also why the sections are identical inside and outside a project (they describe dwe, not the project).
- `internal/core/docs/render/` — glamour rendering: `Render(content, opts) (RenderResult, error)` where `RenderResult{Output []byte, Diagrams []DiagramRef}`. **Text-level mermaid preprocess** before glamour invocation: `mermaid_preprocess.go` scans fenced mermaid blocks BEFORE rendering and emits placeholder text (`<📊 [...]>`), capturing source into the `Diagrams` side-channel. Reason: glamour exposes no `goldmark.Extender` hook, so a parallel AST parser would double-parse; text-level preprocess is the canonical design. Placeholder variants depend on renderer status (disabled → `[diagrams disabled]`; unavailable → `[mmdc not installed — Y to copy]`; otherwise → `[view schema]`). Raw markdown path (non-TTY / `--raw`) skips `Render` entirely and emits verbatim bytes. Tests use golden fixtures and preprocess edge cases (nested fences, unclosed blocks at EOF).
- `internal/core/docs/mermaid/` — mermaid rendering chain: `Renderer` interface with `Render(ctx, src, theme, width)` where width is a cache-key component (callers vary per render). `Chain(...Renderer)` composes renderers in order; `Disabled{}` short-circuits to `ErrRenderingDisabled`. `FileCache{ cacheDir, capBytes, versionFunc }` wraps a renderer with XDG cache (fallback to tempdir), LRU eviction by mtime (atime unreliable), atomic write-temp+rename, and singleflight dedup of concurrent same-key misses. `MmdcRenderer{ bin, versionFunc }` invokes mmdc via `exec.CommandContext` with 10s timeout and theme/width args; per-platform process-group kill on Unix via `Setpgid` + `Kill(-pgid, SIGKILL)`, default kill on Windows; `ErrMmdcNotAvailable` on `exec.ErrNotFound`. `term.go` capability detection: `CanInline()` → false in tmux (passthrough unreliable), true on kitty-capable via rasterm, false elsewhere (best-effort hint); `OpenSystem(path)` → `open` (darwin) / `xdg-open` (linux) / `cmd /c start ""` (windows — empty title arg mandatory for space safety). `CacheDir()` → XDG / UserCacheDir / TempDir. Defaults: `auto` mode tries mmdc silently; `mmdc` mode requires it (error placeholder on missing); `off` mode never invokes (disabled placeholder). Tests use fake-mmdc.sh fixture and injection seams for call-count assertions.
- `internal/core/ui/docstui/` — interactive documentation browser. **As of TUI Stage 4 this is a `tui.Plugin` (`plugin.go`, `var _ tui.Plugin = (*browser)(nil)`) on the shared `tui.Frame` — NOT a standalone `tea.Model`** (old `renderTwoPanel`/`View`/title-bar/help-footer/`keys.go` deleted; frame owns geometry, borders, focus, status line, mouse, alt-screen, teardown). Public entrypoint: `docstui.Run(ctx context.Context, opts Options) error` (replaces the old caller-owned `tea.NewProgram` + `NewModel`). `Options` fields: `Roots`, `Renderer`, `ProjectRoot`, `MermaidTheme`, `Title`, `Locale`, `Translator`, `MmdcMissingNotice` (mmdc-install guidance surfaced in the diagram render-error overlay when `E` is pressed on a "rendering disabled" diagram — replaces the old global banner that `loadTopic` prepended to every topic; `openErrorOverlay`/`currentDiagramError` route the prefetch-captured "render failed" text and this notice through the same overlay). `StatusContext()` returns the middle zone: current path + 📊 N/M diagram progress + `[lang]`. `docstui.Run` maps `tui.ErrTooNarrow` to a clean user-facing error (`terminal too small for the docs browser`) rather than degrading to a flat UI — unlike cmdbrowser's huh-selector `runFallback` and statustui's plain-text `renderDefaultStatus`, docs has no non-TUI rendering path worth reusing; `ErrNotTTY`, `ErrCancelled`, and wrapped panics pass through unchanged (`TestRunMapsErrTooNarrow`).
  - **Plugin shape**: two static panels `Panels() = {tree:Weight 1, viewport:Weight 5}`. `ViewPanel(id, inner Region)` renders each panel body into the frame-computed INNER region (no panel border drawn by the plugin). Panel consts `panelTree`/`panelViewport`.
  - **Actions** (`actions.go`): `RegisterStandard(reg, NavUp, NavDown, PageUp, PageDown, Top, Bottom, Select, Filter, Reload)` — reload binds `ctrl+r` only (pre-migration `r` alias dropped). Plugin-local actions: `diagram.prev` (`[`), `diagram.next` (`]`), `diagram.open` (`o`), `diagram.copy` (`y`) in a **Diagrams** help section; `locale.cycle` (`L`), `locale.english` (`e`) in a **Locales** section; `tree.collapse` (`h`/`←`), `tree.expand` (`l`/`→`) — `nav.left`/`nav.right` stdlib NOT registered (these plugin-local IDs own the keys instead). **Intentional Stage 4 change**: `h`/`l` previously toggled expansion on both keys; now directional — `h`/`←` collapse (step to parent if already collapsed), `l`/`→` expand (step into first child if already expanded) — locked in `actions_test.go`. `tab`/`shift+tab`/`?`/`q`/`esc`/`ctrl+c` stay framework built-ins.
  - **Inline filter** (`/`, `ActionFilter`) = plugin-internal capture mode: `CapturingInput()` returns true while filtering; raw keys edit the query (printable/backspace/enter/esc), registry bypassed; query row "/ query (N)" renders in the tree panel; `enter` commits (expand ancestors, keep selection), `esc` cancels (restore snapshot), `FocusRequestMsg` returns focus to tree — mirrors cmdbrowser filter.
  - **Async structure + plugin-owned context**: plugin holds a `context.Context`+`cancel` derived from the `ctx` passed into `docstui.Run`. `Init() tea.Cmd` batches watcher subscription + prefetch-progress subscription. **First `loadTopic` fires from `Update(tea.WindowSizeMsg)` (only command-capable hook; `Resize` is void), guarded by `firstLoadDone`**, at the computed viewport-panel inner width (cached body region + `{1,5}` weights — `layoutPanels` formula replicated in the plugin). `WindowSizeMsg` updates `ContentWidth` for subsequent loads without re-rendering (no reload storm, no `YOffset` reset — matches old `WindowSizeMsg` handler behavior). `FileChangedMsg` and `ProgressMsg` flow via the Frame's non-key forwarding to `plugin.Update`; generation filtering + per-topic cancel preserved exactly. **`ProgressMsg` (one per rendered background diagram) used to re-inline the WHOLE document via `SetContent` on every tick — that per-completion storm hitched any in-progress scroll on diagram-heavy docs (background rendering "touching" the foreground render). It now only updates progress/StatusBar synchronously and coalesces the placeholder refresh onto a trailing tick** (`scheduleDiagramRefresh`/`applyDiagramRefresh`/`diagramRefreshMsg`/`diagramRefreshDelay=150ms`): the first ProgressMsg of a run arms the tick, later ones ride it (`diagramRefreshTickInFlight`), and when it fires it re-inlines once — BUT **re-arms instead (deferring) while a wheel burst is still in flight (`wheel.tickInFlight`)**, so background diagram rendering never rewrites the viewport mid-scroll. `Close() error` → `cancel()` + `Watcher.Close()` + `Prefetch.Close()` (idempotent/nil-safe). Do **not** add a construction-time or `Init`-time topic load: the only command-capable hook that knows the real panel width is `Update(tea.WindowSizeMsg)` (`Resize` is void and returns no `tea.Cmd`, which is why the first load is `firstLoadDone`-guarded there), so a load started earlier renders at a wrong — or zero — width.
  - **Mouse**: `Update` handles `tui.PanelClickMsg` (tree click → `focusRow(Y)`; viewport click on the scrollbar column → proportional jump, see below) and `tui.FocusChangedMsg` (tracks active panel `b.active` for nav/scroll routing); wheel arrives as `tui.WheelMsg{Panel,Delta}` via `Update` (pointer-routed by hit-zone, dispatched directly by the frame — not through `HandleAction`/`nav.*`). **Both wheel paths keep per-notch work O(1) and defer the EXPENSIVE follow-up to a single debounced flush** (revdiff pattern, `wheelState`/`wheelDebounceMsg`/`wheelLoadDelay=60ms` in `plugin.go`) — without this a buffered wheel flood ran the heavy work once per notch, stalling the bubbletea event loop so the backlog drained (and interrupts were reached) only long after the user stopped scrolling. **`WheelMsg.Delta` is the coalesced notch count** (the framework's WithFilter batches a wheel flood — see § `internal/core/ui/tui/`): the viewport scrolls `ScrollBy(Delta*wheelViewportStep)`, the tree moves `abs(Delta)` rows. **Tree panel**: moves the cursor + `EnsureFocusVisible` immediately and arms a deferred topic load (`loadPending` → `selectCursor` → glamour render at settle). **Viewport panel**: `ScrollBy` (only the offset moves — O(1)) stays immediate, but `syncActiveDiagram` (which re-inlines the WHOLE document via `SetContent` on a diagram-boundary crossing) is deferred (`syncPending`, flushed once at settle) — that per-notch `SetContent` was an event-loop stall on diagram-heavy docs. Coalescing: every notch bumps `wheel.gen` + sets its pending flag; only the first notch of a burst schedules a `tea.Tick` (`tickInFlight` gate via `armWheelTick`), later notches ride it; the tick re-arms while `gen` keeps advancing (mid-burst) and `flushWheel` runs the armed work once `gen` matches (sync first, then load). **Interrupt**: `HandleAction` (any bound key) and `handlePanelClick` call `cancelWheelLoad()` (drops `loadPending` only) so the new command's own load takes over immediately. `wheelViewportStep=2` (deliberately small — finer control, screen keeps pace). **Scrollbar click-to-scroll**: `scrollViewportToClick(x,y)` maps a click on the last inner column (`viewportInner.Width-1`, where `applyInnerScrollbar` draws the bar) to a proportional `ScrollToLine` (`y*maxOffset/(h-1)`, clamped); off-column clicks and short (no-scrollbar) documents are ignored. **Keyboard scroll is the primary path** (the wheel is a bonus): on top of `j/k`+arrows, `PgUp/PgDn`, `g/G`+`Home/End`, the docs browser adds vim **half-page `ctrl+d`/`ctrl+u`** (`actionHalfPageDown`/`actionHalfPageUp` → `navHalfPage`: viewport `ScrollBy(±VisibleHeight/2)`, tree jumps `treeInner.Height/2` rows).
  - **Mermaid theme**: constructor calls `resolveMermaidTheme(opts.MermaidTheme)` and stores the resolved theme (NOT `opts.MermaidTheme` raw) — probes `lipgloss.HasDarkBackground` for `auto`; golden tests must force a concrete resolved theme via the `mermaidThemeForTest` seam.
  - **Tree surface (Frame-facing)**: **As of TUI Stage 4 (Plan 2) `TreeWidget` is a thin wrapper over `*tree.Engine[*TreeNode]`** (`internal/core/ui/tui/tree`) — the prior "mirror cmdbrowser exactly" duplicated `topIdx`/`ensureFocusVisible`/`focusRow`/`clipToViewport` core is now engine-owned. `MoveUp/MoveDown/MoveStart/MoveEnd/Collapse/Expand/Toggle/Cursor/SetCursor/VisibleNodes` delegate to the engine. The **`Expanded bool` field on `TreeNode` was DELETED**; every read routes through `e.IsExpanded(node)` and every write through `e.SetExpanded(node, b)`/`e.Toggle()`. Adapter: `Children → node.Children`, `Key` is **kind-prefixed** (`group\x00<root>` / `node\x00<root>\x00<path>` / `heading\x00<root>\x00<path>\x00<idx>`), `Expandable → true for any non-heading directory PLUS files carrying heading rows` (Codex #4 — NOT "dir-with-children"; empty/index-only dirs stay Enter/Toggle-able), false for heading rows. **Expansion + cursor now survive a locale `Rebuild`** — the engine's `expanded` map is keyed by stable `Key`, so `Rebuild` rebuilds the node graph then calls `e.SetRoots(newRoots)` (the manual `findByPath` transfer is gone) with a **two-tier cursor fallback** (`SetCursorByKey(prevHeadingKey)` → on false `SetCursorByKey(prevParentFileKey)` → on false `ParkCursorIfHidden()`; a vanished heading falls back to its parent file, NOT first-visible). This is the **intended Stage-4 bugfix** (docs previously lost expansion on locale change). Multi-root group nodes are seeded default-expanded in `NewTreeWidget` only (after `SetRoots`, before the first `RebuildVisible(nil)`), so user collapse/expand persists across `Rebuild`. The filter (`tree_filter.go`/`ApplyFilter`) drives the engine's `keep` predicate — `e.RebuildVisible(nil)` when nil/inactive/**empty-query** (Codex #3: `Matches("")` is true, so a naive keep would emit the whole tree), `e.RebuildVisible(matchPredicate)` only for a non-empty active query — each followed by `e.ParkCursorIfHidden()`. `TreeFilter`/`/`-UX/ancestor-expand-on-commit are untouched.
  - **`index.md` folding** (TUI-only, in `tree_widget.go::addNodeAsChild`, NOT in `docs.BuildTree`): when a directory contains an `index.md`, the file is folded into the directory node — the directory borrows the index file's H1 as its tree label (`nodeLabel`), selecting the directory renders the index file's content (`contentNodeFor` → `loadTopic`), and the `index.md` itself is dropped as a separate row. A directory without an `index.md` keeps the prior behavior (blank viewport on select). The fold lives in the TUI layer so non-TUI consumers (`docs export`, `docs list`) still see `index.md` as an ordinary file.
  - **Diagram placeholders** (`diagram_inline.go`): each mermaid block renders as an inline text line substituted *after* glamour; the currently selected diagram (the one `o` opens) is styled in the accent color + bold with a `▸` marker, the rest muted, so the active one is obvious without reading the counter. **Cursor-driven diagram focus**: `diagramLineIndices` records each placeholder's rendered line per load; `syncActiveDiagram` (called after every cursor move / scroll and after the initial load / heading jump in `applyTopicLoaded`) makes `activeDiagramForCursor` the active one — the last diagram at or above the reading cursor, but preferring the topmost diagram *visible in the window* when that one has scrolled off above (so `o`/`y`/`E` act on what's on screen after a mouse-wheel scroll, which pins the cursor to the window's top edge via `pinCursorToWindow`). This is distinct from `jumpToDiagram` — the `[`/`]` manual switch, which moves the cursor onto the chosen diagram's line and scrolls it into view; `syncActiveDiagram` never scrolls. The `E` error-log hint is advertised only on the *active* failed diagram (it opens the overlay for `DiagramState.Current`, so a hint on a non-current diagram would be a no-op).
  - **Scrollbar** (`plugin.go::applyInnerScrollbar`): the **last inner content column** is carved into a full-height scrollbar — a muted shaded track (`░`) with a solid accent thumb (`█`) at the current position; rows at or over the inner width are `ansi.Truncate`d to make room. It operates on **border-free inner content** — the Frame owns the border, so nothing overdraws a border rune (that technique survives only in `tui/overlay_scrollbar.go::OverlayScrollbar`, which does own its box). Returns the content unchanged when the whole document fits, or when the inner width is under 2. Thumb size/position derive from `Viewport.TotalLines()` / `YOffset()` and the height the Frame passes in — **not** `VisibleHeight()`.
  - **Render-error overlay + selection mode** (`errorlog.go`, `plugin.go::updateErrorOverlay`) — the `E` overlay is a `CapturesInput` viewport modal (rounded box + `tui.OverlayScrollbar`) that takes raw keys: `c` copies the whole raw error text to the clipboard (`tea.SetClipboard`) plus a status flash, `s` toggles **selection mode**, every other key scrolls the viewport, and `esc` never reaches here (the Frame closes it). Selection mode republishes the overlay with `ReleaseMouse: true` **and** `FullScreen: true` and resizes the block to the whole terminal via `applyErrorOverlayDims` (`b.TermWidth × b.TermHeight−1`, the `−1` reserving the hint row); `errorState.selectionOverlay` then renders a border-free opaque block plus that hint row, so every on-screen cell is error text or blank padding and a released-mouse drag-select can only grab the error — no border columns or status line bleed in (the terminal owns native selection and cannot be clipped to a sub-rectangle, so the fix is to remove everything else from the screen). While selection mode is on the framework gets no mouse, so click-outside-close and overlay wheel-scroll are off by design. The hint is `MaxWidth`-bounded to the block so a narrow terminal cannot push the overlay past the edge and get truncated by `fullScreenOverlayView`'s clamp. Leaving selection mode clears both flags and `applyErrorOverlayDims` shrinks back to the content-fit box. The `c` confirmation and the diagram-copy `y` share one flash channel — `StatusBarWidget.SetFlash`/`ClearFlash` driven by `setStatusFlash`, **generation-gated** via `statusFlashClearMsg{gen}` against `b.flashGen` so a stale clear tick from an earlier flash never wipes a newer one (this is the pattern cmdbrowser later copied).
  - `watcher.go` uses `fsnotify` for hot reload of project-docs only (embed is static); goroutine exits cleanly on context cancellation. `prefetch.go` spawns a bounded worker pool (2 workers) to render diagrams asynchronously; `Queue` takes **one topic's** items at a time, ordered by diagram index within the document — there is no cross-topic priority, because navigating away *cancels* the previous topic's renders via `topicCancel` rather than deprioritising them; individual render failures logged but not cancelling; producer-consumer channel pattern with `ok` check after receive (mandatory to avoid spin on closed channel). Language fallback banners prepended when translation missing/stale; per-file resolution via `ResolveContent` with `stale` flag (hash mismatch). Tests cover tree state, action dispatch, filter transitions, diagram tracking, watcher+prefetch goroutine cleanup via `goleak.VerifyNone(t)`; golden frame tests at width buckets 60/79/80/99/100 via `tui.RenderFrame`/`tui.BuildHelp`.
- `internal/core/docs/export/` — tree export to disk: `Tree(dst, roots, opts) error` where `Opts{Lang, IncludeProject, IncludeInternals, Force}`. Refuses non-empty target without `--force`. Per-file fallback: missing translation appends en with a banner `> **Note:** This file is not translated to <lang>. Original English version below.` Mermaid blocks preserved as-is (IDE-friendly). Creates `<dst>/<root.Name>/<relPath>` hierarchy with deep markdown structure intact.

## Core — UI (`internal/core/ui/`)

Domain-aware renderers. Intended to be a sink — only `internal/cli/**` should import these. (The `ui-is-sink` rule is one of three layering contracts whose `depguard` activation is deferred; see [Dependency Rules](#dependency-rules) above.)

The root `internal/core/ui/` directory holds **zero `.go` files** — all output, styling, and prompt code lives in seven sibling subpackages. The historical flat package was split into three domain-aligned subpkgs (`styles/`, `widgets/`, `render/`) plus four pre-existing siblings (`ask/`, `cmdbrowser/`, `statusview/`, `statustui/`). One-way dep: `render, widgets → styles`. `styles/` is the only subpkg with a third-party `charm.land/huh/v2` import (used to construct `HuhTheme`); that does not make it a non-leaf for internal layering purposes.

- `internal/core/ui/styles/` — palette + icon + huh-theming. **3 files**: `styles.go`, `icon.go`, `icon_replacements.go`. Owns:
  - **Palette resolution**: `ApplyStyles(cfg *config.StylesConfig)` resolves light/dark hex pairs once at apply time via `lipgloss.HasDarkBackground()`; user overrides apply to both modes. Empty `text:` resolves through `lipgloss.NoColor{}` (terminal default) rather than `lipgloss.Color("")`. String-typed accessors `ColorAccent`, `ColorSuccess`, `ColorWarning`, `ColorDanger`, `ColorMuted`, `ColorBorder`, `ColorText` (**7 semantic tokens**) are the only sanctioned bridge between v1 lipgloss here and v2 lipgloss in `cmdbrowser/` (and Fang's `ColorScheme`).
  - **Style accessors**: lowercase `lipgloss.Style` vars (`styleAccent`, `styleMuted`, `styleSuccess`, `styleWarning`, `styleDanger`, `styleBorder`, `styleText`) remain unexported to preserve the `ApplyStyles`-mutation invariant. Cross-pkg readers go through 1-line accessor functions: `AccentStyle()`, `MutedStyle()`, `SuccessStyle()`, `WarningStyle()`, `DangerStyle()`, `BorderStyle()`, `TextStyle()`. Render-pkg files call `styles.AccentStyle().Render(text)`. String-render helpers `StyleKey`, `StyleMuted`, `StyleWarning`, `StyleInfo`, `StyleGroup`, `StyleSectionTitle`, `StyleSubheader`, `StyleFailed`, `StyleService*`, `StyleOption*`, `RenderEnabled`, `RenderPartial`, `RenderStopped` are the recommended path for external callers; also exports `DefSep` (used by `render/info.go` + `render/summary.go`) and `ServiceTypeStyle(serviceType) lipgloss.Style` (consumed by topology renderer).
  - **huh theming**: `HuhTheme`, `ApplyFormGlyphs`, `BuildPaletteApplier`, `Theme()` — moved here from the old `huh.go` because they construct themes from palette colors (semantically styling, not widget runtime).
  - **Icons**: `SafeIcon`, `IconPrefix`, `IsAmbiguousWidthIcon` (in `icon.go`); `SuggestSafeIcons` plus internal `stripTrailingVS16` (in `icon_replacements.go` — tightly coupled, must stay together).
  - **Logomark helpers**: `LogoMark()` / `LogoMarkPlain()` were intentionally moved to `render/logo.go`, NOT `styles/`, because they are output composites (icon + accent style wrapping), not palette primitives.
  - **`TermWidthOrZero(f *os.File) int`** (`styles.go`) is the width probe behind `render/`'s responsive-table budget (see `render/`'s Responsive tables entry): it returns 0 — not a fallback width — when `f` is not a terminal or its size is unknown. This is the opposite contract from the pre-existing `TermWidth()`, which has no `*os.File` parameter (always probes `os.Stdout`) and falls back to 80 columns on the same non-TTY/unknown-size condition; `TermWidth`'s 80-column fallback exists for callers that need *some* width unconditionally, while `TermWidthOrZero`'s 0 exists so a piped/non-TTY renderer can distinguish "no terminal, don't shrink" from "an 80-column terminal, maybe shrink" — conflating the two would silently push every piped run (and the entire golden test suite) into narrow-mode wrapping. Two test seams, `termWidthIsTerminalFn` / `termWidthGetSizeFn`, let tests simulate TTY/non-TTY and specific sizes without a real terminal.
- `internal/core/ui/widgets/` — interactive huh-based forms. **6 files**: `confirm.go`, `multiselect.go`, `selector.go`, `huh.go`, `interactive.go`, `filter_quit.go`. Imports `styles/` for theme + glyph application; no other internal subpkg deps.
  - `RunSelector`, `RunMultiSelect`, `RunConfirm`, `ConfirmRun` (caller pre-renders title; renders a sorted `key = value` summary above the prompt). `SelectorItem`, `MultiSelectItem`, `MultiSelectResult` types.
  - `ErrCancelled` (used by 20+ call sites — defined in `selector.go`).
  - **`RunHuhForm(ctx, form) error`** (TUI Stage 6, `huh.go`) is the single canonical executor for every huh form in dwe: wraps `RunWithPromptHooks`, calls `form.RunWithContext(ctx)`, translates `huh.ErrUserAborted` → `ErrCancelled`. `defaultRunConfirmForm` / `defaultRunConfirmRunForm` / `defaultRunSelectForm` / `defaultRunMultiSelectForm` (the default `run*FormFn` seams) and `internal/core/ui/ask`'s `Form.Run` all route through it; the per-wrapper hook-snapshot blocks that used to live in `RunConfirm`/`RunSelector`/`RunMultiSelect` were removed in the same change (**hooks-fire-once invariant**: each wrapper keeps only a defensive `ErrUserAborted → ErrCancelled` mapping so seam-swapped tests returning raw huh errors keep their contract — the hook wrap itself must not double-fire).
  - **`SetHuhHooks` / `ClearHuhHooks`** — package-level hooks fired around every `widgets.Run*` call (and, transitively, every `RunHuhForm` call) so a `LiveLine` footer can pause/resume cleanly. Any new prompt-like UI must go through `widgets.Run*`/`RunHuhForm`, never bypass. **`RunWithPromptHooks(fn)`** — production helper that snapshots the hooks, runs `before`, defers `after`, and invokes `fn`; used by full-screen UI (cmdbrowser, statustui) that cannot go through `Run*`, and internally by `RunHuhForm`. `SnapshotHuhHooks` remains test-only.
  - `IsInteractiveTTY` (and similar env helpers in `interactive.go`).
  - Intra-pkg lowercase symbols stay lowercase (`snapshotHuhHooks`, `multiSelectMinHeight`).
- `internal/core/ui/render/` — DTO → string section renderers. **20 implementation files** + 22 test files: `info.go`, `info_auto_hosts.go`, `info_auto_urls.go`, `summary.go`, `deploy_info.go`, `pending.go`, `topology.go`, `gitworkspace.go`, `diagnostics_table.go`, `table.go`, `brand_header.go`, `logo.go`, `selector_header.go`, `vars.go`, `secrets.go`, plus the responsive-table engine — `table_wrap.go`, `table_fit.go`, `table_view.go`, `table_record.go`, `table_budget.go` (test count runs ahead of the impl count for the file-less `test_helpers_test.go`, which pins the `TestMain` non-TTY seam, `golden_test.go` and `vars_secrets_test.go`) — and a `testdata/` golden tree (one `.golden` file per table renderer, byte-compared, `UPDATE_GOLDEN=1` to regenerate). Imports `styles/` (and `statusview/` for view-model DTOs). **Exported names dropped the `Render` prefix per revive** — e.g. `RenderInfo` → `Info`, `RenderBrandHeader` → `BrandHeader`, `RenderSummary` → `Summary`, `RenderServicesTable` → `ServicesTable`, `RenderPendingBanner` → `PendingBanner`, `RenderTopology` → `Topology`, `RenderDiagnosticsTable` → `DiagnosticsTable`, `RenderDeployStatus` → `DeployStatus`, `RenderDeployInfo` → `DeployInfo`, `RenderGitWorkspace` → `GitWorkspace`, `RenderDaemonTable` → `DaemonTable`, `RenderSectionTitle` → `SectionTitle`, `RenderSubheader` → `Subheader`, `RenderDefinition` → `Definition`, `RenderDefinitionAt` → `DefinitionAt`. The pre-existing `BrandHeader` *type* was renamed to `Brand` to free the function name. **Signature contract**: renderers return strings — never take an `io.Writer` — so that `internal/cli/` stays the single writer to stdout/stderr and every table stays golden-file comparable; the lone sanctioned exception is the thin `PrintSelectorHeader(w, …)` helper below. The same rule binds `internal/core/project/stack/`'s collectors and section renderers (whose own grandfathered exception is `RenderServiceDeployDetail`). Every sink-probing table entry point ships an explicit-width sibling (`ServicesTableAt` / `DaemonTableAt` / `DeployStatusAt` / `GitWorkspaceAt` / `SectionTitleAt` / `SecretsStatusAt`) for TUI panels that must bypass the probe — ship both halves when adding a table. **Encrypted-secret masking lives here, not in the caller**: `MaskSecretValue(v) (any, bool)` + `EncryptedPlaceholder` (`<encrypted>`) are the single primitive, and `VarsList` / `layerLine` call it themselves rather than trusting a flag their caller passed — the renderer is the last stop before the terminal, so a future caller that forgets still cannot print ciphertext; it copies rather than rewriting in place, because the merged config is shared with every other consumer. `VarLayerBadge(layer, encrypted)` is the one place the ` (encrypted)` badge suffix is spelled (shared by the vars list table and the TUI browser's type badge, which is why `styleLayerBadge` accent-matches on the `local` PREFIX) and the suffix is **text-only** — the JSON `layer` field stays `local` / `default` and `encrypted` is the machine answer. In `secrets.go`, `SecretsStatus` knows nothing of the state vocabulary (rows carry `State` / `Reason` verbatim plus an `OK bool`, the only thing styling reads, so a new state string needs no renderer change), and its STATE column is `Wrap` but deliberately **not** `Flex`: both path columns use the hard-splitting `wrapPath`, so their floors collapse to the header width and a flexible STATE would let the table "fit" at 20 columns by shredding LAYER/PATH instead of falling back to records. The two header lines (`Recipient` / `Identity`) render unwrapped and are excluded from the overflow assertion — an age recipient and a keyfile path are single unbreakable tokens the user copies.
  - **Responsive tables** (`table_wrap.go`, `table_fit.go`, `table_view.go`, `table_record.go`, `table_budget.go`) — every table renderer (`Table`, `DaemonTable`, `DeployStatus`, `GitWorkspace`, `ServicesTable`, `DiagnosticsTable`/`DiagnosticsByDomain`) is built on a shared `tableView{Headers, Rows, Cols []columnSpec, Style, Padding, BorderRow, Zebra, Center}` and degrades across three stages at `Render(budget int)`: fits → render as today; does not fit → shrink flexible columns and wrap once at the final width (`fitRows`); does not fit even at column floors → `fitRows` returns `ok=false` and `Render` falls back to `renderRecords`, a labeled-record layout (no ellipsis truncation anywhere — an unbreakable long token, e.g. a URL, floors its column instead of being cut). The budget is derived from the renderer's **sink**, not a hardcoded stream: `stdoutBudget()`/`stderrBudget()` (`table_budget.go`) call `styles.TermWidthOrZero(f *os.File)` on `os.Stdout` for every renderer except `DiagnosticsTable`, which uses `os.Stderr` (its three call sites — preflight, deploy menu ×2 — all write diagnostics to stderr). **Its sibling `DiagnosticsByDomain` is NOT one of them**: same rows, different sink — its only call site (`dwe validate`, `cli/validate/validate.go`) writes to `cmd.OutOrStdout()`, so it probes stdout. Getting this backwards is the concrete failure mode the rule exists to prevent: probing stderr there would shrink `dwe validate > report.txt` to the terminal width and leave `dwe validate 2>/dev/null` unbounded on a narrow terminal. **A non-TTY sink means budget 0, and budget 0 means unbounded, byte-identical legacy output** — `fitRows` with `budget == 0` still applies `Max` column caps and still wraps at them, it just never shrinks a flex column below its natural width; this is why every Task-1 golden stays byte-identical across all of Tasks 2–13. The goldens hold regardless of how the suite is launched because `TestMain` (`test_helpers_test.go`) pins the package-level `termWidthFn` seam to the non-TTY default (0) for the whole run — narrow-mode tests opt in explicitly via `withTermWidth(t, w)`. Explicit width wins over the sink probe: every sink-probing entry point has an explicit-width sibling — `ServicesTableAt`, `DaemonTableAt`, `DeployStatusAt`, `GitWorkspaceAt` (all `width int`, 0 = unbounded) — used by TUI panels that already know their own width. A new table should ship both halves. **A `Max` cap never overrides an unbreakable token**: `naturalWidths` clamps cell content to `Max`, but `fitRows` then raises each natural width back to the column's `columnFloors` value, because the wrap helpers refuse to split a URL or a wide rune and Lipgloss lays the column out at the token's width regardless. Skipping that step makes `distributeDeficit` see negative headroom and *widen* the column, so the table overflows the budget while still reporting `ok=true`. Cells must be plain text — the wrap helpers are not ANSI-aware, so semantic color is applied by `tableView.Style` *after* wrapping, never baked into a cell before it. The table/record mode decision is made once per `Render` call (per renderer, per call site); `DiagnosticsByDomain` instead calls `tableView.fit(budget)` per domain (it never calls `Render` at all) to force one shared mode across every domain it emits (see below) — reusing each domain's already-fitted rows — so `dwe validate` never mixes table and record layout mid-output — this is a deliberate exception to "each renderer decides independently" and the only place `dwe status`'s cross-table mode mismatch (Services vs. Deploy vs. Git each deciding on their own row/column shape) is accepted rather than unified. A new table therefore inherits shrink/wrap/record degradation for free simply by being built as a `tableView` — that is the extension point, not a per-renderer layout pass. Two clarifications on the sentences above. First, budget 0 skips the **record fallback** as well as the shrink pass: with `budget == 0` `fitRows` runs neither deficit distribution nor the floor check, so `ok` is never `false` and `Render` can never reach `renderRecords` — "unbounded" means unbounded in both directions, and wrapping at `Max` column caps still happens. Because `TestMain` (`test_helpers_test.go`) pins the package-level `termWidthFn` seam to 0 for the whole suite (narrow-mode tests opt in via `withTermWidth(t, w)`), the goldens do not depend on `go test` happening to pipe stdout — so a golden mismatch is almost never a budget bug: check that seam before touching budget logic. Second, `DiagnosticsByDomain` is the one renderer that never calls `tableView.Render` at all — it calls `fit(budget)` for every domain up front and then drives the shared `renderTable` / `renderRecords` helpers directly with that single verdict, because a table sitting immediately above a record block reads as a bug. The `DiagnosticsTable`/`DiagnosticsByDomain` split generalizes: audit a new renderer's actual call sites to pick `stdoutBudget()` vs `stderrBudget()`, never copy the neighbouring renderer's budget func — backwards, it shrinks `dwe validate > report.txt` to the terminal width and leaves `dwe validate 2>/dev/null` unbounded on a narrow terminal. Equally, a `width` argument of 0 means unbounded, never "probe the sink": a wrapper that wants the probe must call the sink-probing entry point (`render.DaemonTable`), not `…At(rows, 0)`. On the floor-raise: skipping it is not a cosmetic overflow but the terminal hard-wrap the whole feature exists to prevent — a table that reports `ok=true` and then wraps in the terminal is strictly worse than the record fallback it declined. And record mode is the one place over-long output is legitimate: it has no lower width bound and never truncates, so an unbreakable token wider than the budget overflows by design there.
  - `Summary`, `Info`, `PendingBanner(p *journal.PendingApply) string` (renders one warning line per pending op; empty string when `p == nil`; used by `dwe status` and its subcommands).
  - `ServicesTable(rows, extraCols, withDirCol bool)` is the single table renderer used by all three type-keyed status sections — apps pass `withDirCol=true`, tool/infra pass `false`; built-in columns NAME [+DIR] CONTAINER **HOSTS PORTS** STATE RUNNING. `Hosts` and `Ports` from `ServiceTableRow` are always-visible columns formatted by `formatHostsCell` / `formatPortsCell` (single entry → bare value, multi-entry → sorted `name=value` pairs, empty → `—`); dynamic per-row `Extras map[string]string` filled in by `stack.RenderCustomCells`; missing key → `—`.
  - `Topology`, `DiagnosticsTable` (paired with `FormatDiagnostics` and `FormatSummary`), `DeployStatus`, `GitWorkspace`, `DaemonTable(rows)`. **Two diagnostics layouts**: `DiagnosticsTable(rows)` renders the 6-column table **with** a DOMAIN column (used by preflight + the deploy menu, where rows span only env+checks); `DiagnosticsByDomain(rows)` renders **one titled table per domain** with the DOMAIN column dropped (used by `dwe validate`). Domains order by `domainDisplayOrder` (unknowns appended alphabetically); each title is colored by the worst severity in its group. Both share the column-agnostic `diagnosticsTable(rows, showDomain)` core.
