The FILE column wraps on `/` via `wrapPath` so long relative paths don't widen the table — linter findings are relativized to the project root by `internal/core/validate/linters` (`relToBase` in `runtime.go`), not here.
  - **Logomark helpers**: `LogoMark()` (accent-colored `{▪}` for v1 contexts that own no outer color envelope) and `LogoMarkPlain()` (unstyled `{▪}` for v2 contexts and outer-colored envelopes that would otherwise nest escapes). **Logomark exclusion list** (do NOT prefix): notification titles, tab-completion descriptions, pipeline phase headers, template/PR/commit footers, generated doc artifact bodies.
  - **Selector / TUI title helpers** (`selector_header.go`): `SelectorTitle(projectName, base)` composes `DWE · <project> · <base>` (middot-joined, project omitted when empty); `BrandedSelectorTitle(projectName, base)` prefixes the plain logomark → `{▪} DWE · <project> · <base>`. **`BrandedTitleForConfig(cfg *config.DweConfig, base string) string`** is the **single shared source** the three full-screen TUIs use for their status-line brand (`cli/status` via `statustui`, `cli/docs` via `docstui`, `cli/command` via `cmdbrowser`): it takes the **config** and reads `cfg.Project.Name` (nil-safe), so the *compose* project name (`dwe_tbm`) can never leak into a title where the *display* name (`tbm`) belongs — the bug that made status drift from docs/commands. `PrintSelectorHeader(w, projectName, base)` writes the styled subheader for non-TUI selectors.
  - **vars renderers** (`vars.go`, consumed by `cli/vars/`): `VarValue(value) (string, error)` (scalar or YAML subtree for `get`), `VarsList(items, namespace) string` (flat leaves — path + effective value + layer badge, namespace-filterable), `VarInspectView(in, width) string` (per-layer `Default`/`Local`/`Current` block + origin file + grouped usages with `file:line` + line text + the "dynamic paths not tracked" caveat). All return strings (no `io.Writer`) per the section-renderer contract; names follow the no-`Render`-prefix convention.
  - **info.yml auto-blocks (render-time expansion)**: `type: auto-urls` and `type: auto-hosts` items in `workspace/info.yml` expand at render time (when `dwe info` is executed), not at YAML load time. The `InfoItem` struct carries a `Source*Spec` pointer (yaml:"-") populated by `InfoItem.UnmarshalYAML` at load time, dispatching on the `type:` field to unmarshal into the concrete `AutoURLsSpec` or `AutoHostsSpec` (standard Go `type alias InfoItem` shadow technique avoids infinite recursion). `LoadInfoConfig` is config-blind — it never computes topology or iterates services. At render time, `renderInfoItem` dispatches to `renderAutoURLs` and `renderAutoHosts` (pure string-returning functions in `info_auto_urls.go` / `info_auto_hosts.go`, both intra-pkg lowercase since same package) which call `config.DeployOrder(cfg, types)` directly to iterate services in deterministic deploy order (never `range cfg.Services`, whose iteration is randomized). `autoDetectPortVia(cfg)` helper searches for a single enabled `type: infra` service with `ports.http == 80` or `ports.https == 443` — zero or >1 candidates return nil; the `port_via:` field overrides auto-detection when explicitly set. When `workspace/info.yml` is absent, `LoadInfoConfig` returns `DefaultInfoConfig()` (synthesized with `auto-urls` and `auto-hosts` sections whose `Source*Spec` pointers are populated directly in Go code, not via UnmarshalYAML). Service display fields `icon`, `info.title`, `info.primary_host` (formerly `host_key`), `info.primary_port` (formerly `port_key`), and `info.paths` are read-only display fields consumed only by info renderers (and status/cmdbrowser for `icon`). The randomized-map-order rule is enforceable rather than stylistic because every one of these renderers is byte-compared against a `testdata/*.golden` file: iterating `cfg.Services` directly makes those tests flaky, not merely non-deterministic in production output. User-visible schema and examples: `docs/reference/config/info.md`.
- `internal/core/ui/ask/` — shared huh-based form-runner for generic `[]Field` shapes, and (as of TUI Stage 6) the single declarative-keymap construction layer for every huh form in dwe. **Purpose**: unified form asker used by the setup wizard's question loop, user-command param forms, the deploy menu's two interactive selects, and the setup wizard's port-override/service-toggle prompts; previous two parallel implementations merged into one, and Stage 6 folded in the three remaining raw-`huh.NewForm` sites. `FieldKind` enum: `FieldInput` / `FieldSelect` / `FieldMultiselect` / `FieldConfirm` / `FieldPassword` (zero `FieldUnknown` is a sentinel error rejected at `Build`). `FieldPassword` (the hidden `dwe secrets set` prompt) is **appended after `FieldConfirm`** so no existing kind's iota value moves; it shares one `case FieldInput, FieldPassword:` branch in `buildHuhField` (adding only `EchoMode(huh.EchoModePassword)`, so the quit-hint suggestion trick and `Required`/`Validate` are identical) and `detectKinds` classifies it as an input kind, which is what makes the submit-help line and `RunOptions.Quit` behave exactly as for `FieldInput`. `Field` struct (Key, Title, Description, Kind, Required, Default/Defaults for pre-fill, Options, per-field Validate callback, `Height int` for select/multiselect viewport height, `Filterable *bool` — **multiselect only**, huh/v2 `Select` has no `Filterable` method, and `Affirmative`/`Negative` — **`FieldConfirm` only**: empty strings keep huh's own defaults, which is what makes every pre-existing confirm site byte-identical, while `secretsprompt.ConfirmImport` labels its buttons after the two outcomes (`Enter key` / `Abort`) because declining aborts the command). `Option` struct (Value, Label, Description). `Result` struct carrying a map of resolved values typed by kind (string for scalar, `[]string` for multiselect, bool for confirm); typed accessors `String(key)` / `Strings(key)` / `Bool(key)` prevent untyped access.
- `internal/core/ui/secretsprompt/` — the two interactive halves of the age-identity onboarding gate: `PromptIdentity` (one `ask.FieldPassword`, in-form `Validate` = `secrets.ParseIdentity` + recipient match, so a typo or a foreign key is corrected without losing the prompt; Esc → `widgets.ErrCancelled`) and `ConfirmImport` (an `ask.FieldConfirm` labelled `Enter key` / `Abort` — the two outcomes, since the decline aborts the command). Both take the caller's streams through `ask.RunOptions{Input, Output}`; that is why the confirmation is a form here rather than a `widgets.RunConfirm` call, which drives `os.Stdin`/`os.Stdout` itself and takes no writer. **Importable only from `cli/*`** (sink rule): `core/workflow/keygate` owns the decision and never imports this package — the cli layer wraps these two functions into the `keygate.PromptFunc` / `ConfirmFunc` closures over the cobra streams. Every message is DWE-authored and fixed (`parseMessage`; `mismatchMessage` names BOTH recipients, which is the whole diagnosis — the key is valid, it just belongs to another project): the `age` parser echoes input characters, which here are private-key bytes, so its error text never reaches a surface. `PromptIdentity` re-parses and re-checks the recipient AFTER the form returns, because the `runAsk` seam and huh's accessible mode can both hand back a value the field validator never saw. Consumers: `cli/secrets` (`promptIdentityFn`), `cli/deploy`, `cli/lifecycle`.
  - **Build/Run split** (Stage 6, the seam Stage 7's in-TUI form overlay needs): `Build(title, fields, opts) (*Form, error)` constructs a `huh.Form` (one group dispatching each field to `huh.NewInput` / `NewSelect` / `NewMultiSelect` / `NewConfirm`, `styles.Theme()` applied, keymap assembled per `QuitSpec`/`SubmitHelp`) without running it; `(*Form).Run(ctx) (Result, error)` executes via `widgets.RunHuhForm` (hooks + `RunWithContext` + abort translation) and harvests the result; `(*Form).Huh() *huh.Form` exposes the underlying form for Stage 7 to embed as a capturing-overlay child model (drives `Update`/`View` directly, never calls `Run`); `(*Form).Result() Result` harvests bound values independent of how the form was driven. Package-level `Run(ctx, title, fields, opts)` is exactly `Build` + `Form.Run` (unchanged call signature from pre-Stage-6). An empty `fields` slice short-circuits at `Build` (`Form.empty`) without invoking huh.
  - **Cancel contract**: `Run`/`Form.Run` return `widgets.ErrCancelled` on user abort (Stage 6 changed this from raw `huh.ErrUserAborted` — every caller checks `errors.Is(err, widgets.ErrCancelled)` now).
  - **Declarative keymap overrides** (Stage 6, `RunOptions`): `Quit *QuitSpec{Keys []string, Help string}` (nil = huh defaults; empty `Keys` treated as nil) sets the form-level Quit binding and, per-field-kind, hijacks a help slot so the quit hint renders in huh's field help line (huh hides the form-level Quit binding from field help): select/multiselect → `Filter` slot (rebinds the Filter *activation* key to quit, which as a side effect makes `/`-filtering unreachable — intended for the sites that use it); input → `AcceptSuggestion` slot plus a fake single-blank `SuggestionsFunc` (huh only exposes that binding when suggestions are enabled). For a `Filterable: false` multiselect the hijacked Filter slot never renders at all (huh's `WithKeyMap` disables and hides Filter binds when not filterable) — quit still works via the form-level binding, only the help hint is absent; this is a known, documented limitation, not a bug. `SubmitHelp string` cosmetically relabels the submit help verb (e.g. "select"). `ShowHelp *bool` (nil = leave huh's default). No `filterAwareQuit` wiring in `ask` — the Filter-slot hijack and live filtering are mutually incompatible on the same field, and no `ask` consumer needs both; add it only when one does. A site that needs a non-default quit binding declares a `QuitSpec`; hand-rolling a raw `huh.NewForm` + keymap at the call site is not an available escape hatch (that carve-out is what Stage 6 closed — see **Migrated call sites** below). `docs/internals/tui-keymap.md` § 7 ("Forms guidance") is the cross-surface companion to this bullet: it records that registry wiring was evaluated and **declined** (a huh form runs standalone via `ask.Run`/`(*ask.Form).Run` → `widgets.RunHuhForm`, not as a `tui.Plugin` driven by `Frame.Update`, so there is no registry dispatch point to wire through), and that consequently only the **quit** binding and its help-line presentation are unified across sites — form-internal navigation stays on huh's own key routing.
  - **Migrated call sites** (Stage 6 closed both carve-outs the ask package previously documented): `internal/cli/deploy/menu.go`'s `selectMenuItemInteractive`/`selectDeployServiceInteractive` and `internal/core/workflow/setup/huh.go`'s `askPortOverrides`/`askServiceToggles` are now plain `ask.Run`/`ask.Build` calls with `QuitSpec` overrides — no more raw `huh.NewForm` outside `internal/core/ui/ask` and `internal/core/ui/widgets` in production code (`setup/help_runtime_test.go` keeps a raw form as a huh-behaviour canary; test-only, exempt). `internal/core/ui/widgets/selector.go`, `confirm.go`, `multiselect.go` remain separate pre-existing primitives (command tree / cmdbrowser callers), unaffected by this stage — the rule is still "use `ask` when `[]Field` suffices", not "no raw huh anywhere". `internal/core/ui/tui/formoverlay_test.go` likewise builds raw `huh.Form`s as Stage 7 overlay fixtures — also test-only, also exempt; the "grep for `huh.NewForm` should come back empty" invariant is scoped to production code, not to the whole tree.
- `internal/core/ui/cmdbrowser/` — two-panel interactive command browser used by `dwe commands` when no exact ID is given (no-ID with `--inspect` is a hard error, not a browser launch). **As of TUI Stage 3 this is a `tui.Plugin` (`plugin.go`, `var _ tui.Plugin = (*browser)(nil)`), the framework's first real consumer — NOT a standalone `tea.Model`** (`model.go` / `keymap.go` deleted; geometry, borders, focus, status line, alt-screen, mouse mode, and teardown now belong to `tui.Frame`/`tui.Run`). Public surface is **frozen and unchanged**: `Run(title, items, opts) (Result, error)`, `Item` (carries precomputed `Inspect` string — package is decoupled from `usercommands.CommandDef`), `Options` (incl. `Edit *EditSpec` and `RunForm *RunFormSpec`), `DefaultOptions()`, `Result { Idx, Action, SkipConfirm, ForceParamForm, Values }` (`Values` = params harvested from the in-TUI RunForm overlay, nil on every non-browser and no-form-needed path), `Action` / `Mode` enums (each with `*Unknown` at iota 0). Builds its tree internally (`treeModel`/`treeNode`) by splitting `Item.ID` on `.` — no dependency on `usercommands.GroupNode`. **As of TUI Stage 4 (Plan 2) `treeModel` is a thin wrapper over `*tree.Engine[*treeNode]`** (`internal/core/ui/tui/tree`): nav/scroll/expansion/directional-collapse-expand are engine-owned (the local `moveUp/moveDown/onLeft/onRight/toggleFocused/ensureFocusVisible/focusRow/rebuildVisible/clipToViewport/indexOfFocused` copies were deleted). `treeNode` stays as payload (counts/leaves); `build`/`ensureGroup`/`recomputeCounts`/`setIncludePrivate`/`itemsForFocus`/`nearestVisibleAncestor` stay. The adapter is `Children → n.children`, `Key → n.id`, `Expandable → len(n.children) > 0`. Cursor mapping is golden-critical: `focusedNode()` → `e.Cursor()` (nil ⇒ root), `focusedID` reads → `e.Cursor().id` or `""`, all `focusedID = …` writes → `e.SetCursorByKey(id)` (incl. `""`/root + `nearestVisibleAncestor`/`nearestRestoredAncestor`); `nodesByID`/`root` are kept only for `breadcrumb()` "(root)" and `itemsForFocus()`. The filter (`filter.go`) keeps its dim+counts logic but routes expansion storage through `e.ExpandedSnapshot()`/`e.SetExpandedByKey()`/`e.RestoreExpanded()`; the `exitFilter`/`commitFilter` `if !focusVisible() { … nearestVisibleAncestor }` sequence relies on `RebuildVisible` not re-parking the cursor. Palette is wired through `cmdbrowser/palette.go`, which constructs `charm.land/lipgloss/v2` styles from `styles.Color*()` strings and applies them to `list` / `viewport`. `ActionEdit` and `ModeEdit` are declared in `run.go`, appended at the END of their iota blocks — that is what makes them additive: every pre-existing `Action`/`Mode` constant keeps its numeric value.
  - **Plugin shape**: two static panels `Panels() = {tree:Weight 2, list:Weight 7}` (≈ the old `leftWidth ≈ 2·w/9` split). `ViewPanel(id, inner Region)` renders each panel body into the frame-computed INNER region (no panel border drawn by the plugin). **Framework padding delta**: `Frame.renderBody` adds `Padding(0,1)` inside the border, so plugin inner width = **outer − 4** (vs the old cmdbrowser inner = frame − 2); every width threshold was recomputed against framework inner widths — `treeCountsMinWidth=18` (tree counts, `tree_render.go`) and `listBadgesMinWidth=74` (list badges/param counts, `plugin.go`), the inner widths at terminal 99–100. The `−4` delta is a **framework-wide** rule, not a cmdbrowser detail: any new plugin must recompute its width buckets against the frame's INNER width (see the Geometry bullet under `internal/core/ui/tui/`), because sizing against terminal/outer width overshoots by exactly the 2 border + 2 padding columns.
  - **Actions are per-mode** (`actions.go`, `browser.Actions(reg)` / `HandleAction`): always `nav.*`/`select`/`filter` (`/`)/`inspect` (`i`); in `ModeRun` ALSO `cmd.skip-confirm` (`y`, toggles `skipConfirm`) and `cmd.force-form` (`e`, sets `ForceParamForm` + selects) — registered in NEITHER `ModeEdit`/`ModeInspect`, so they vanish from the `?`-modal help automatically. Tab/?/q/esc stay framework built-ins. List-panel `nav.left`/`nav.right` are **no-ops** (panel switching is Tab/click-only; the legacy left-arrow "return to tree" affordance is dropped). Each `Binding.Section` (`Navigation`/`Panels`/`Actions`/`General`) groups the help modal.
  - **Inline filter** = plugin-internal capture mode (Decision 3, `filter.go` + `*browser` filter methods in `plugin.go`): `CapturingInput()` returns true iff `b.filter != nil`; `Frame` then forwards raw keys (printables extend the query, action-letters are TYPED not dispatched), the query line renders in the tree panel, the tree filters live (`applyAutoCollapse` + match counts); `esc` restores the snapshot, `enter` commits + focuses the nearest visible ancestor. No dimming overlay.
  - **Inspect** = `CapturesInput` overlay (Decision 4, `inspect.go` + `*browser` inspect methods): `inspect` action builds the viewport via `Item.Inspect(width)`, `PendingOverlay()` returns `Overlay{Content, Width, Height, CapturesInput:true}` once per open (`inspectPending` blocks double-push); navigation scrolls the viewport, `enter` selects (`Result{Idx, Action}` + `tea.Quit`), `esc` closes (Frame-side via `routeWhileCapturing`) — the Frame then forwards a `tui.OverlayClosedMsg` which `Update` handles by clearing `b.inspect`/`inspectPending`, so a later unmatched raw key (forwarded raw to `Update` in normal mode) cannot re-mark it pending and resurrect the closed modal. A proportional scrollbar is overdrawn onto the box's right border when the body overflows the viewport (`inspectState.applyScrollbar`, mirroring the docs-browser bar). Filter (no-overlay branch) and inspect (modal-open branch) are mutually exclusive. The bar is the shared framework helper `tui.OverlayScrollbar(box, yOffset, totalLines)` (glyphs `tui.OverlayScrollbarThumbGlyph`/`OverlayScrollbarTrackGlyph`) — docstui's error overlay calls the same function, so this is one implementation, not two mirrored copies; do not re-roll a per-package variant.
  - **Edit** = in-TUI form overlay (TUI Stage 7, `edit.go` + `Options.Edit *EditSpec`, mirrors `inspectState`): opt-in per Options — `Edit == nil` keeps ModeEdit's exit-and-return commit-and-quit **byte-identical** (goldens unchanged). With `Edit != nil`, `onSelect` (Enter / double-click) in ModeEdit runs `openEdit(idx)` → `EditSpec.BuildForm(idx) (*ask.Form, error)` → `tui.NewFormOverlay(form.Huh(), b.body, {Hint: "enter save · esc cancel"})`, stores `editState{fo, form, idx}`, sets `editPending`, returns `fo.Init()`; a BuildForm error (or nil form) shows an error flash and opens nothing. This is cmdbrowser's `ask` coupling (`edit.go` plus the `EditSpec`/`CommitOutcome` types in `run.go`, whose `BuildForm`/`Commit` signatures reference `*ask.Form`/`ask.Result`) — deliberately accepted so the plugin harvests results via `ask.Result` (the framework `FormOverlay` stays `ask`-free, taking `*huh.Form`). `updateEdit` forwards EVERY message (typed keys AND async blink/next-group ticks) to `fo.Update`, re-marks `editPending`, then polls `fo.State()` — **completion is async** (the Enter Update leaves `StateNormal`; a follow-up `nextGroupMsg` sets `StateCompleted`, so State is polled after every forwarded msg): `StateCompleted` → `commitEdit` runs `EditSpec.Commit(idx, form.Result()) (CommitOutcome, error)` synchronously, replaces `items[idx]` in place + `refreshList()` on success (a var edit never adds/removes leaves — tree shape + cursor untouched), sets the flash, and returns `tea.Batch(requestCloseOverlay(token), flashClearTick)`; a Commit error flashes `✗ …` and still closes; `StateAborted` (ctrl+c never reaches the form — defensive) = cancel. `OverlayClosedMsg` (esc / click-outside) clears `edit`/`editPending` so a later raw key cannot resurrect the closed form. `requestCloseOverlay(token)` returns a `tui.CloseOverlayMsg{Token}` cmd (plugin-initiated pop, no `OverlayClosedMsg` echo; the token — minted per open in `editState.token` and carried on the overlay via `FormOverlayOptions.CloseToken` — makes the Frame ignore a stale close after the overlay was already dismissed). Golden full-frames with the overlay open live in `testdata/frame_editform_{80,99,100}.golden`.
  - **RunForm** = in-TUI param-form overlay (commands-param follow-up, `runform.go` + `Options.RunForm *RunFormSpec`, mirrors `edit.go`) — the **ModeRun** sibling of Edit. Opt-in per Options: `RunForm == nil` keeps ModeRun's exit-then-form behaviour **byte-identical** (existing goldens unchanged; every caller that does not supply one — `DefaultOptions`, tests, the vars browser — is untouched). The terminal action differs fundamentally from Edit: EditSpec is **write-and-stay** (Commit persists `local.yml`, browser stays open, row refreshes); RunFormSpec is **harvest-and-quit** (collect the param values, close the overlay, quit the browser — the command executes AFTER alt-screen teardown because it streams docker/pipeline output to the plain terminal). The overlay-driving plumbing (pending/token handling via `runFormPending`/`editTokenSeq`, `FormOverlay` forwarding, `setStatusFlash`/`flashError`, `requestCloseOverlay`) is shared with the edit machine; only the terminal step differs. `RunFormSpec{BuildForm func(idx int, force bool) (*ask.Form, error); Harvest func(idx int, res ask.Result) map[string]string}` (in `run.go`) — `force` is true when the user pressed the force-form key (`e`), false for plain Enter, so the CLI can auto-skip the form when all required params are satisfied. Three entry points route through `openRunForm(idx, force)` when `RunForm != nil`: `onSelect` (Enter on a list item, `force=false`), `onForceForm` (`e`, `force=true`), and `updateInspect`'s Enter (`force=false`, from inside the inspect overlay — `ActionInspect` is registered unconditionally so inspect is reachable in ModeRun; the inspect state is retired only when a form actually opened or immediate-run was chosen, a BuildForm error keeps inspect valid). `openRunForm` calls `RunForm.BuildForm`: a BuildForm error → `setStatusFlash(flashError(...))`, opens nothing; a **nil form OR an empty `ask.Form` whose `Huh()` is nil** (`ask.Build` returns `&Form{empty:true}` with a nil huh form for zero fields — hosting it would be an inert cancel-only overlay that never completes, trapping the user) → commits `Result{Action: ActionRun, ForceParamForm: force}` with NO `Values` and `tea.Quit`, so the orchestrator recomputes the identical prefilled from the same `--set` and runs exactly as the exit-and-run path (byte-identical); else wraps in `tui.NewFormOverlay(form.Huh(), b.body, FormOverlayOptions{Hint: "enter run · esc cancel", CloseToken: token, MaxHeight: b.body.Height})` (MaxHeight bounds multi-field forms to scroll inside huh), stores `runFormState{fo, form, idx, force, token}`, sets `runFormPending`, returns `fo.Init()`. `updateRunForm` mirrors `updateEdit` — `OverlayClosedMsg` (esc / click-outside) clears run-form state so a later raw key cannot resurrect the form; else forward to `fo.Update`, re-mark pending, poll `fo.State()`: `StateCompleted` → `finishRunForm` harvests `Result.Values = RunForm.Harvest(idx, form.Result())`, sets `Result{Idx, Action: ActionRun, SkipConfirm, ForceParamForm, Values}`, returns `tea.Batch(requestCloseOverlay(token), tea.Quit)`; `StateAborted` (ctrl+c never reaches the form — defensive) = cancel. `Update` routing AND `PendingOverlay` give `runForm != nil` **priority over `inspect`** (mirrors the `edit != nil` precedence) so an open run-form and a reachable inspect overlay never cross-route. Golden full-frames with the param-form overlay open (single- AND multi-field, to exercise the height bound) live at 80/99/100 × 24.
  - **Status flash** (TUI Stage 7, docstui pattern, `edit.go`): `setStatusFlash(text)` sets `b.flash`, bumps `b.flashGen`, and returns a `statusFlashDuration=2s` tick emitting `statusFlashClearMsg{gen}`; `StatusContext()` returns the flash text while set (overriding the breadcrumb), and a stale-gen clear tick is ignored so a newer flash is never wiped. `✓ …` (success, built by the caller's Commit closure so it carries the value) / `✗ …` (error, `flashError`), single-line.
  - **Mouse** (Decision 7): `Update` handles `tui.PanelClickMsg` (single click moves tree cursor / list selection, no run/toggle), `tui.FocusChangedMsg` (tracks the active panel `b.active` for nav/scroll routing); wheel arrives as `tui.WheelMsg{Panel,Delta}` via `Update` (pointer-routed by hit-zone, dispatched directly by the frame — not through `HandleAction`/`nav.*`); double-click → `select` (toggle group / run list item).
  - **Status line** middle = breadcrumb of the focused tree group + `[--yes ON]` when `skipConfirm` (Decision 6, `StatusContext()` called every render). The breadcrumb noun (`command`/`commands` vs `var`/`vars`, `itemNoun`) stays **hardcoded English** — not translated this pilot. **Additive `ActionEdit`/`ModeEdit`** power the `dwe vars` browser: in `ModeEdit` Enter returns `ActionEdit` and the noun is "var"; the ModeRun-only `e`/`ForceParamForm` binding is byte-identical and untouched (regression-pinned). `cli/vars/browser.go` maps vars → `Item` (`ID = vars.<path>`, `Description = effective value`, `Type = layer-source badge`, `Inspect` closure). There is no key-hint footer to adapt: post-Stage-3 the `dwe commands` chrome is exactly the framework `?`-modal help plus this status line, so a mode-specific binding is advertised by its registration (or absence) in `Actions(reg)`, never by a rendered footer row.
  - **Run rewiring** (`run.go`): `applyDefaults` → empty-items / non-TTY guard → read size → `width < minBrowserWidth(80)` or `height < minBrowserHeight(15)` → `runFallback` (huh selector; the old in-TUI 60–79 single-panel layout is GONE — Variant A) → else `out, err := runTUI(newBrowser(...), tui.RunOptions{Brand, Project, Mouse:true, Translator, Locale})`, `res, _ := out.(Result)`. `runTUI` is a package-local seam (`var runTUI = tui.Run`, swapped in tests). The second `widgets.RunWithPromptHooks` wrap is **dropped** — `tui.Run` owns it now; `tui.Run`'s `widgets.ErrCancelled` maps straight through, and the `ActionUnknown` → `ErrCancelled` guard via `res.Action` stays. **Translator/locale** thread in via new `Options.Translator`/`Options.Locale` (nil-safe → `i18n.NopTranslator`/`"en"`), populated by `cli/command/list.go` + `cli/vars/browser.go`, mapped onto `tui.RunOptions`. Test seams `isTerminalFn` / `terminalSizeFn` / `runSelectorFn` / `runTUI` are package-local function vars. Golden full-frame renders live in `testdata/frame_{run,edit}_{80,99,100}.golden` + `help_{run,edit}.golden` via the exported `tui.RenderFrame`/`tui.BuildHelp` harness.
- `internal/core/ui/statusview/` — view-model types passed from the command layer to renderers (ui must never load YAML or compute hashes): `DeployStatusView`, `DeployStatusRow`, `DeploySummary`, `ConfigDelta` enum (`ok` / `changed` / `missing`), `GitWorkspaceRow`, `DaemonRow`.
- `internal/core/ui/statustui/` — interactive read-only status TUI. **As of TUI Stage 5b this is a `tui.Plugin` (`plugin.go`, `var _ tui.Plugin = (*plugin)(nil)`) on the shared `tui.Frame` — NOT a standalone `tea.Model`** (the legacy `*model`'s `View()`/`Update(tea.Msg)(tea.Model,tea.Cmd)`, `renderTitleBar`, the status-bar chrome, and `keys.go` were deleted in the cutover; the Frame now owns geometry, borders, the status line, `?` help, mouse, alt-screen, and teardown). `Deps` mirrors the fields from `statusContext` that the TUI needs (Cfg, State, Tracked, SvcDeploys, DockerCfg, Topo, TopoStatus, IsRunning, ProjectRoot) plus **`Translator i18n.Translator` and `Locale string`** (Stage 5b addition, threaded into `tui.RunOptions`; nil Translator falls back to `i18n.NopTranslator{}`, empty Locale to `tui.Run`'s default) — a decoupled bundle to avoid an import cycle. `Run(ctx, Deps) error` owns a child context (`runCtx, cancel`), builds a `*model` sized `(0,0)` (the Frame owns geometry; real dimensions arrive via `Plugin.Resize`/`ViewPanel`) and wraps it in `newPlugin(m, cancel)`, then calls the package seam `var runStatusTUI = tui.Run` with `tui.RunOptions{Brand: render.BrandedTitleForConfig(d.Cfg, "Status"), Mouse: true, Translator, Locale}` (`Project` left empty — the whole branded title is the `Brand`); `Close()` (called by `tui.Run` on every exit path) invokes `cancel`, replacing the old `defer cancel()`. Returns `nil` on clean quit, `tui.ErrTooNarrow` unchanged (caller falls back to plain text), and passes through `tui.ErrNotTTY`/panic errors otherwise — `mapRunError` and the old `isTerminalFn`/`terminalSizeFn` seams are gone (`tui.Run` owns the TTY + size gates).
  - **Single panel, tabs as body content** — `Panels()` returns one panel (`panelMain`, Weight 1); tabs are stacked body content (tab strip + shared viewport), not side-by-side Frame panels. `ViewPanel` renders a centered spinner while `m.loading`, else `renderBody`: sizes the viewport to `inner` minus `panelChromeRows=2` (tab-strip + divider rows) via `viewport.SetWidth`/`SetHeight`, then joins tab strip + a muted divider line + `viewport.View()`. Five tabs unchanged: Services (apps+tools+infra), Deploy, Topology, Git, Daemons.
  - **`buildTabs` returns a data snapshot, not rendered strings** — `buildTabs(ctx, d) (tabSnapshot, string)` (`load.go`) runs every Docker-/git-backed probe (`stack.CollectApps`/`CollectTools`/`CollectInfra`, `CollectDeployStatus`, `RenderTopology` — no per-service width, so it stays pre-rendered here — `collectGitWorkspaceFn`, `collectDaemonsFn`) exactly once and packs the results into a `tabSnapshot` struct; it does **not** call any table renderer. `tabsLoadedMsg` carries the `tabSnapshot` (not `anchors` — those are now width-dependent and recomputed at render time). Composing a tab's body — including the per-tab title/warning-prefix/empty-placeholder text that `buildTabs` used to produce — happens in `renderTab(snap tabSnapshot, index, width int) (body string, anchors []int)`, a pure function with no Docker/git probe, called from `renderBody` (`plugin.go`) at the panel's real inner width via `stack.RenderAppsRows`/`RenderToolsRows`/`RenderInfraRows`/`RenderDeployStatusRows` and `render.GitWorkspaceAt`/`stack.RenderDaemonsAt` (the width-parameterized Task 9 render halves — see the `render/` and `stack` entries). This is why the same snapshot can be re-rendered at a new width (a resize) without re-probing Docker or git. `renderActiveTab` (`plugin.go`) memoises the `renderTab` result on the key tuple `(m.loadGen, m.active, width)` in `m.renderCache*`, so repeated `View()` calls with none of the three changed reuse the previous render — a tab switch changes `active` and a terminal resize changes `width`. **The key tuple alone does NOT cover a reload**: `ActionReload` bumps `loadGen` when the reload *starts*, and the spinner keeps ticking during the load, so at least one frame renders the pre-reload snapshot and caches it under the already-bumped gen; `Update`'s `tabsLoadedMsg` branch therefore clears `renderCacheValid` explicitly when the new snapshot actually lands. Without that the reloaded body never reaches the screen until a tab switch or a resize.
  - **Reload/`YOffset` preserved verbatim** — `Plugin.Update`'s `tabsLoadedMsg` handling (stale-`gen` drop, **snapshot** assign + `renderCacheValid = false`, `loadedAt`/`healthIndicator`, `YOffset` restore when `reloadGen==gen && reloadActive==active` else `GotoTop`) and `spinner.TickMsg` are byte-identical to the pre-migration `model.Update`, per the migration plan's single most important invariant. The plugin's OWN `WindowSizeMsg` case is a no-op (`return nil`) — unlike the deleted legacy handler, sizing is owned entirely by `Resize`/`ViewPanel`, never recomputed from raw terminal dimensions. Unmatched messages delegate to `viewport.Update` for scroll.
  - **Actions** (`actions.go`) — plugin-local **Tabs** section: `tab.prev` (`shift+tab`/`left`/`h`), `tab.next` (`tab`/`right`/`l`), `tab.1`..`tab.5` (jump); a **Navigation** section: `section.next` (`]`), `section.prev` (`[`); plus stdlib `tui.ActionReload` (`ctrl+r`, registered first). **`tab`/`shift+tab` are rebound to switch tabs, NOT the framework focus built-ins**: because this is a single-panel surface the Frame calls `Registry.DisableFocusNav()` before the plugin's `Actions` hook (see `internal/core/ui/tui`), stripping the dead `focus.next`/`focus.prev` built-ins (they also vanish from the `?`-modal). Within-tab table jumping lives on `]`/`[`. `HandleAction` operates on the held `m *model`: prev/next wrap via `m.setActiveTab((active±1+n)%n)` where `n` is the fixed `len(tabTitles)` (5, never zero — the count is static, so there is no modulo-by-zero to guard); the `!m.loaded` guard instead blocks navigation before the first `tabsLoadedMsg` has delivered any data; jumps call `m.setActiveTab(k)` directly; `section.next`/`section.prev` call `m.jumpSection(±1)`, which scrolls `viewport` to the next/previous sub-table anchor of the active tab (clamped at the ends, no wrap; no-op for a tab with `<2` anchors). Anchors are per-tab 0-based line offsets held in `model.sectionAnchors` (aligned with `tabTitles`), computed by `renderTab` (via `joinSectionsWithAnchors`, specifically `renderServicesTab`) at render time — width-dependent since a shrink can push a sub-table into record mode and change its line count — only **Services** stacks multiple sub-tables (Apps/Tools/Infra) today; the rest are single-section (nil). Reload re-runs the existing `loadGen++`/`reloadActive`/`reloadYOffset`/`reloadGen`/`reloading` sequence + `buildTabsCmd`.
  - **Branding** (`run.go`) — the status-line left zone is composed exactly like the other TUIs, through the **single shared helper** `render.BrandedTitleForConfig(d.Cfg, "Status")` (`{▪} DWE · <project> · Status`) passed whole as `RunOptions.Brand`, with `Project` left empty. The helper takes the **config** (not a name string), so it always uses the bare display name `cfg.Project.Name` — the earlier bug fed `sc.ProjectName` (the *compose* project name `dwe_tbm`) into the brand while docs/commands used `tbm`; routing all three TUIs through `BrandedTitleForConfig` makes that drift impossible. `statustui.Deps` therefore has **no `ProjectName` field** — the brand derives from `Deps.Cfg`.
  - **Mouse** (`mouse.go`) — `handlePanelClick` acts only on `Y==0` (tab-strip row); `tabAt(x)` maps a panel-local X column to a tab index via `tabHitZones()`, which mirrors `renderTabStrip`'s layout exactly (1-col leading pad, `▌ ▐` decorations on the active tab, 3-space inter-tab gaps) so click hit-zones match what is drawn — clicks past the last tab or on the leading pad return `(-1, false)` (no-op); `Y>0` clicks (viewport body) are no-ops (status rows are not selectable). `handleWheel` scrolls `viewport` by `Delta * wheelViewportStep(2)` lines via `ScrollUp`/`ScrollDown`; a wheel turn never changes focus.
  - **StatusContext** returns the middle status-line segment (health indicator + "loaded X ago", or a loading/reloading state) — the old `renderStatusBar` `leftParts` minus the help text, which the Frame now supplies itself on the right. Recomputed every render.
  - The dispatcher in `internal/cli/status/status.go` uses `shouldUseTUI(noTUI, noFlags)` which short-circuits to plain when `--no-tui`, any `--no-<section>`, `TERM=dumb`, or non-TTY; on `errors.Is(err, tui.ErrTooNarrow)` from `runStatusTUIFn` it now also falls back to `renderDefaultStatus` (plain text), mirroring cmdbrowser's narrow-terminal fallback. Tests mutating package-level seams MUST NOT call `t.Parallel()`. `ViewPanel` must remain a pure render function — mutation belongs in `Update` handlers. That fallback is deliberately a **third** narrow-terminal shape rather than a copy of cmdbrowser's: statustui already owned a complete non-TUI text rendering path (`renderDefaultStatus`), so falling back to it costs nothing, whereas cmdbrowser must synthesise a huh selector and docstui — having no text path — can only error.
- `internal/core/ui/tui/` — **shared full-screen TUI framework (registry/keymap/overlay-input surface locked in Stage 1; mouse layer wired in Stage 2; first real consumer + two `Plugin`/Frame revisions landed in Stage 3).** The reusable chrome/layout/input/overlay layer the three full-screen TUIs (cmdbrowser, docs browser, statustui) migrated onto across Stages 3–5b. **First production importer (Stage 3): `internal/core/ui/cmdbrowser/`** (the command/vars browser pilot) — see its entry above; docs browser migrated in Stage 4 (`internal/core/ui/docstui/` — see its entry above); statustui migrated in Stage 5b (`internal/core/ui/statustui/` — see its entry above), completing all three consumers. It depends on `core/ui/styles`, `core/ui/widgets` (for the `RunWithPromptHooks`/`ErrCancelled` exit wrapper around the full-screen program) + the charm.land v2 stack (`bubbletea/v2`, `bubbles/v2`, `lipgloss/v2` — **never** v1 `github.com/charmbracelet/lipgloss`; the v1 that shows in `go list -deps` is transitive via `core/ui/styles`, which exposes raw color-string accessors). **Locked surface** (Stage 1): `Binding`/`Action`/`Registry`, stdlib shared actions (`RegisterStandard`), `Aliases` semantics (dispatch + hidden from help), `CapturesInput` overlay contract, and the i18n namespace (`tui.help.section.<id>`, `tui.help.action.<id>`, `tui.help.title`) — **now in live use as of Stage 3**: real YAML keys ship in the built-in en bundle (`internal/shared/i18n/translations/en.yml`, allowlisted in `KnownUIKeys`) and the help modal renders localized section/action strings. **Mouse layer** (Stage 2, all in `frame.go` / `hittest.go` / `registry.go` / `actions.go`): (1) **CellMotion enable gate** — `View` sets `tea.MouseModeCellMotion` when `f.opts.mouse && mouseCapable()` (`TERM != "dumb"`), else `tea.MouseModeNone`; per-program opt-in via `RunOptions.Mouse`; (2) **`Registry.MatchMouse(event string) (Action, bool)`** — scans registered bindings for `Binding.Mouse == event`; after the Stage 4 wheel overhaul `standardBindings` populate `Mouse` only for `ActionSelect` (`"double-click"`); `ActionNavUp`/`ActionNavDown` no longer carry a `Mouse` field (`"wheel-up"`/`"wheel-down"` are no longer valid registrable events); `"click"` is intentionally never registered (frame-owned); (3) **`classifyHit` Region-math hit-test** — pure `classifyHit(geo Geometry, panels []panelRect, helpHint Region, ov *Overlay, x, y int) (hitZone, PanelID)` over `Geometry` + panel outer regions + help-hint region + active overlay's centred bounds, returning a `hitZone` (`zoneNone`/`zonePanel`/`zoneHelpHint`/`zoneModal`/`zoneOutsideModal`); uses Region math, NOT `lipgloss.Compositor.Hit` (the compositor cannot classify panels or the help-hint zone; Region math is sufficient and already needed); (4) **Pointer-routed `WheelMsg`** (Stage 4 routing; the Stage-4b coalescer described under Frame below re-introduced the accumulator, so this paragraph's *routing* claims hold but its "synchronous, one message per raw notch" claim does not) — `handleMouse` is now only the fallback dispatch path for a `tea.MouseWheelMsg` that the input filter let through. Routing: when the top overlay `CapturesInput` the raw `tea.MouseWheelMsg` is forwarded to `plugin.Update` and `refreshCapturingOverlay` swaps in the re-rendered snapshot (mirrors the captured-key path); a non-capturing overlay or an active inline filter (`plugin.CapturingInput()`) swallows the wheel; otherwise `classifyHit` classifies the pointer position — a `zonePanel` hit forwards `WheelMsg{Panel: id, Delta: ±1}` (`Delta` −1 = up, +1 = down) to `plugin.Update` **without touching focus**; `zoneHelpHint`/`zoneNone`/horizontal wheel are swallowed. The plugin owns the per-panel scroll amount (`WheelMsg.Delta` is the signed notch count ±1; viewport panels multiply by their own step; tree/list panels move one cursor row per notch). `double-click` remains the only event dispatched via `MatchMouse`; (5) **Double-click via injected `frameClock`** — `frameClock interface{ now() time.Time }` with production `realClock{time.Now}`; `Frame.clock` defaulted in `newFrame`; `lastClick struct{ id PanelID; x, y int; t time.Time }` scopes the double-click to the same panel + same cell within `doubleClickWindow=400ms`; `!lastClick.t.IsZero()` sentinel (zero `time.Time` is never a valid prior click); cleared in full after the Select fires (triple-click → exactly one Select). **Stage 3 kept `doubleClickWindow=400ms`** as the chosen value (on-device feel sign-off is a Post-Completion item); (6) **Click routing** — `handleMouse` classifies via `classifyHit` first, then acts by zone: `zoneHelpHint` → `ActionHelp`; `zonePanel` → `focus.Set` + double-click test; `zoneNone`/status → swallow; overlay open → `zoneModal` (inside) swallowed (body never acts behind it) while `zoneOutsideModal` **dismisses the overlay** via `dismissTopOverlay` (click-away-to-close, mirroring `esc`; a capturing overlay is also notified via `OverlayClosedMsg`); `tea.MouseReleaseMsg`/`MouseMotionMsg` ignored; `panelLocal(outer Region, x, y int) (lx, ly int)` helper (pure, unit-tested) translates absolute click to panel-inner-local coordinates. **Superseded by the Stage-4b wheel coalescer (see Frame below):** item (4)'s "immediate / synchronous / one message per raw notch" description no longer holds — `tea.WithFilter(wheelFilter)` drops raw notches and `flushWheelAccum` emits ONE `WheelMsg` per panel per trailing tick, so `WheelMsg.Delta` is the NET coalesced notch count (±N), never guaranteed ±1, and the `wheelAccum`/`wheelTickArmed`/`wheelArmMsg`/`wheelFlushMsg` machinery is back (in `filterWheel`, not `handleMouse`). Only the routing claims of item (4) — pointer-by-hit-zone, focus untouched, plugin owns the step, `MatchMouse` uninvolved — survive unchanged.
  - **Stage 3 revisions (two, sanctioned by spec § 7 "one revision back")** — (1) **Unified input-capture**: the new `Plugin.CapturingInput() bool` method (see Plugin below) lets a plugin take raw input *without* an overlay — `Frame.handleKey` adds a capture branch BEFORE the registry match (`f.overlay.Empty() && f.plugin.CapturingInput()` → forward the key to `plugin.Update`, only `ctrl+c` → `tea.Quit`), so the cmdbrowser inline filter takes printables live with no dimming overlay; for `CapturesInput` overlays the modal-open branch now routes via `routeWhileCapturing` (swallow→`plugin.Update`, hardQuit→`tea.Quit`, close→`overlay.Pop()`), the Stage-1 helper finally wired in. (2) **Translator/locale wiring**: `RunOptions` gained `Translator i18n.Translator` + `Locale string`, mapped by `Run` to new `withTranslator`/`withLocale` frameOptions (nil/zero → `NopTranslator`/fixed locale), so the `?`-modal help renders localized strings instead of Stage 0's hard-coded `NopTranslator`. Also new in Stage 3: exported framework messages **`PanelClickMsg{Panel PanelID; X, Y int}`** (forwarded to `plugin.Update` on a single content-area panel click — border clicks set focus but emit NO message, avoiding negative local coords) and **`FocusChangedMsg{Panel PanelID}`** (forwarded on EVERY focus change — Tab/Shift+Tab built-ins AND click — since `handleBuiltin` focus branches never otherwise reach the plugin; mandatory so the plugin can route nav to the right widget). **Cross-package test harness**: `testsupport.go` exports `RenderFrame(p Plugin, opts RunOptions, w, h int) (string, error)` + `BuildHelp(p Plugin, tr i18n.Translator, locale string, w, h int) (Overlay, error)` for consumers (e.g. cmdbrowser goldens) that cannot reach the package-private `*_test.go` helpers. **Stage 4** migrated the docs browser (`docstui` — see its entry above); **Stage 5b** migrated statustui (see its entry above), completing all three `Plugin` consumers. The `Plugin` interface is **frozen** post-`CapturingInput` (the one deliberate Stage-3 contract addition). See `docs/internals/tui-keymap.md` for the full action taxonomy, default bindings, mouse vocabulary, and i18n namespace.
  - **Frame** (`frame.go`) — the bubbletea `tea.Model`, constructed via unexported `newFrame(Plugin, ...frameOption) (*Frame, error)`. Holds the plugin, registry, focus manager, overlay stack, geometry, and brand/project strings. `newFrame` **validates before launch** and errors on a duplicate action/key (`plugin.Actions(registry)`) **or** invalid panel layout (`Panels()` empty / any non-positive `Weight`) — these never fail at `View`. `Update`: `tea.WindowSizeMsg` → recompute geometry + `plugin.Resize(body)`; **modal-input policy** — while an overlay is open, key msgs are framework-handled only (help-close/quit) and plugin action keys are **swallowed** (no acting behind the modal); with no overlay, keys → `Registry.Match` → built-in (help/focus/quit) handled by the framework else `plugin.HandleAction`, and if unhandled the raw msg forwards to `plugin.Update`. **All non-key msgs always forward to `plugin.Update`** (async preservation, even with help open); `PendingOverlay()` is drained after both a `HandleAction` and a `plugin.Update`; the plugin's returned command is forwarded directly (the framework has no command of its own to batch this stage). **Stage 3 input-capture branches** in `handleKey`: (a) **no-overlay capture** — when `f.overlay.Empty() && f.plugin.CapturingInput()`, the registry is bypassed and the raw key forwards to `plugin.Update` (only `ctrl+c` → `tea.Quit`); placed BEFORE the registry-match block so a capturing plugin (cmdbrowser inline filter) takes printables/action-letters raw; (b) **capturing overlay** — when `Top().CapturesInput`, route via the now-wired `routeWhileCapturing(msg) captureDecision` (small enum: `captureSwallowToPlugin`→`plugin.Update`, `captureHardQuit`→`tea.Quit`, `captureClose`→`overlay.Pop()`+reset `lastClick`+forward `OverlayClosedMsg{}` to `plugin.Update` so the plugin clears the state that produced the overlay — see Plugin); non-capturing overlays keep the esc/?/q-only policy. `View() tea.View` owns the terminal envelope: sets `AltScreen = true` (a `tea.View` field in v2, **not** a program option) and sets `MouseMode` to `tea.MouseModeCellMotion` when `f.opts.mouse && f.mouseCapable()` (CellMotion = click + wheel, no motion spam), else `tea.MouseModeNone`. Plugins return panel **strings** only — never `tea.View`. The status line (brand+project · plugin `StatusContext()` · `? help`, width-aware truncation) is composed **outside** `Composite` so it is structurally never dimmed. **Wheel-coalescing input filter** (`tea.WithFilter(wheelFilter)` installed by `Run` via `buildProgramOptions`; `wheelCoalesceInterval=time.Second/60`): the load-bearing flood-control. bubbletea v2 reads input FIFO from an UNBUFFERED `p.msgs` channel and calls `model.View()` **once per message** (`tea.go` eventLoop), so admitting every raw wheel notch as its own message means a terminal/trackpad flood (macOS momentum scroll emits notches for ~1-2s after the fingers lift) drains FIFO before any later key/click is even seen — the "freeze after stopping" + "can't interrupt", **independent of diagrams**. A WithFilter runs in the event loop BEFORE Update/View: `Frame.filterWheel` accumulates vertical panel wheels into `wheelAccum[PanelID]` and **drops the raw notch (`return nil` → NO Update, NO View)** — a **trailing debounce**: the first notch of an idle burst returns a `wheelArmMsg` so `Update` schedules ONE `wheelFlushMsg` tick (`wheelTickArmed`-gated) `wheelCoalesceInterval` out; when it fires `flushWheelAccum` drains the **net** accumulated delta into one `WheelMsg{Panel, Delta}` per panel. The backlog drains at filter speed (µs each) and an interrupting key/click is reached immediately. **Trailing, not leading**: a leading-edge throttle left a stale partial delta that a later opposite-direction notch combined with and scrolled the WRONG way (codex review) — the net-delta flush keeps direction correct and never drops the tail. Any `tea.KeyPressMsg`/`tea.MouseClickMsg` first calls `clearWheelAccum()` (drop pending scroll so the interrupt wins) then passes through. Non-coalescable wheels — horizontal (`wheelButtonDelta==0`), outside a panel (`classifyHit != zonePanel`), or while a NON-capturing overlay is open / the inline filter is capturing — pass through unchanged (a capturing overlay's vertical wheel IS coalesced, under the `OverlayWheelPanel` sentinel) so `handleMouse` still forwards the raw `tea.MouseWheelMsg` to a capturing overlay's embedded viewport. **`WheelMsg.Delta` is now the coalesced notch count (±N)**: the plugin applies the magnitude (`ScrollBy(Delta*step)`; tree moves `abs(Delta)` rows). This **replaced an earlier per-`View()` wheel-burst render throttle** — that throttle made `View()` cheap but could not stop the FIFO input backlog (codex's diagnosis), so `View()` is once again plain (rebuilds every call); the filter caps wheel-driven Updates to ≈60/s, which is fine. `handleMouse`'s own `tea.MouseWheelMsg` panel-dispatch branch remains as a fallback (e.g. tests calling `Update` directly bypass the program-level filter). **Stage 3 click/focus delivery**: `handleClick` on a single content-area `zonePanel` click always `focus.Set(id)`, then forwards a `PanelClickMsg{Panel, X, Y}` (panel-local coords via `panelLocal`) to `plugin.Update` — but ONLY when the click is inside the inner content region (border clicks set focus, emit no message — no negative coords); `handleBuiltin` focus branches AND click `focus.Set` both emit `FocusChangedMsg{Panel}` to `plugin.Update` (the plugin cannot learn Tab-driven focus otherwise). Double-click continues via `MatchMouse("double-click")` → `HandleAction(ActionSelect)` AFTER the single-click cursor move. **Capturing overlays coalesce too.** The "while an overlay is open … pass through unchanged" clause above is accurate only for a **NON-capturing** overlay (the help modal, which swallows the wheel): when `Top().CapturesInput`, `filterWheel` accumulates the vertical notch under the synthetic sentinel `OverlayWheelPanel` (`const OverlayWheelPanel PanelID = "\x00overlay-wheel"`, `plugin.go`) and the flush delivers `WheelMsg{Panel: OverlayWheelPanel, Delta}` to the plugin, which scrolls its embedded viewport by `Delta` notches — the same freeze fix as body panels, since a momentum flood backing up the FIFO froze the modal exactly like it froze the body. Only **horizontal** (`Delta==0`) overlay wheels still reach `handleMouse` as a raw `tea.MouseWheelMsg`. Consumers do not hand-roll the scroll: `docstui.scrollErrorOverlay` and `cmdbrowser.scrollInspect` are both thin wrappers over `tui.ScrollOverlayViewport(*viewport.Model, delta)` + `tui.OverlayWheelStep`. On flush the two bucket kinds are mutually exclusive (the filter routes to one or the other), so the top overlay's kind selects the follow-up: a capturing top scrolled itself in place → `refreshCapturingOverlay` (`ReplaceTop`, never `Push`); otherwise a panel wheel may have opened a new plugin overlay → `drainOverlay`.
  - **Geometry** (`geometry.go`) — single source of truth for **outer-vs-inner sizing**, border ownership, and the overlay coordinate space, computed once per resize. `Geometry{Term, Outer, Inner, Status, Overlay Region}`; `Region{X, Y, Width, Height}`. **Border ownership rule: the FRAME draws every border; plugins receive INNER dimensions only** (content area inside border + padding) and must never draw the frame border. `Outer` = terminal minus the bottom status line; `Inner` = `Outer` minus border (`borderSize=1`) + padding (`hPadding=1`, `vPadding=0`) per side; `Overlay` = the inner body region, so modals **never** cover the status line. `tooNarrow(w, h)` (below `minWidth=40` / `minHeight=10`) drives the launch fallback. `layoutPanels(body, weights) []Region` splits the body horizontally by weight with a **deterministic remainder policy** — widths sum **exactly** to the body width (last panel absorbs the remainder, mirroring cmdbrowser's `right = total − left`; never naive `w*weight/sum`, which leaks a column at odd widths like 79/99). It is pure with **no error path**: positive-non-empty weights are a documented programmer-error precondition enforced by `newFrame`'s validation, keeping `View`'s hot path branch-free. All chrome constants are provisional. **Consequence for plugin authors** — because `renderBody` adds `Padding(0,1)` inside the border, the region a plugin receives is `outer − 4` columns, not `outer − 2`; every width-bucket threshold inside a plugin must be recomputed against this INNER width (cmdbrowser's `treeCountsMinWidth=18` / `listBadgesMinWidth=74` are the worked example — see its entry above).
  - **Registry** (`registry.go`) — locked (Stage 1) `action → binding + description + section` keymap. `Action` (string id), `Binding{Keys, Desc, Section, Aliases []string, Rebindable bool, Mouse string}` where: **`Aliases`** dispatch (wired into `Register`/`Match` via the same key map, pre-commit collision guard covers aliases too — an alias colliding with any existing key/alias or the binding's own canonical keys is a hard error, no partial mutation) but are **hidden from help** (`buildHelpOverlay` renders `Keys` only — muscle-memory compatibility without cluttering the modal); **`Rebindable`** is metadata-only (no config loader built; future keymap config is sketched in `docs/internals/tui-keymap.md`); **`Mouse`** vocabulary after Stage 4 wheel overhaul: only `"double-click"` is a registrable value (`validMouseEvent` accepts it alone); `"wheel-up"`/`"wheel-down"` are no longer registrable (`validMouseEvent` rejects them — wheel events are dispatched as `WheelMsg`, not through `MatchMouse`); `"click"` is intentionally never registered (frame-owned, so `validMouseEvent` rejects it too); resolved via `Registry.MatchMouse` (wired in Stage 2). `NewRegistry()`, `Register(Action, Binding) error` (pre-commit validation guards duplicate action, duplicate key, and alias collision — no partial mutation), `Match(key) (Action, bool)`, `Binding(Action)`, `Sections() []Section` (ordered, for help generation). **Built-in actions** (auto-registered by `NewRegistry`): `ActionHelp` (`?`), `ActionQuit` (`q`/`ctrl+c` — `esc` is intentionally NOT a quit key/alias: it only ever closes the visible overlay (frame modal-input policy, consulted before the registry) and is a no-op in normal mode, so `esc` never exits the TUI), `ActionFocusNext` (`tab`), `ActionFocusPrev` (`shift+tab`). **`DisableFocusNav()`** removes the two focus built-ins (and their `Navigation` section, via the private `unregister`): `newFrame` and `BuildHelp` call it automatically when the plugin declares **≤1 panel** — focus can never move there, so `tab`/`shift+tab` would be dead keys advertised in help. It runs BEFORE the plugin's `Actions` hook so the freed keys can be rebound (statustui claims them for its within-tab table jump); direct-registry tests must mirror this ordering. **Stdlib shared actions** (opt-in, plugin-handled): `RegisterStandard(reg *Registry, actions ...Action) error` in `actions.go` lets plugins register framework-supplied default bindings for `ActionNavUp`/`ActionNavDown`/`ActionNavLeft`/`ActionNavRight`/`ActionSelect`/`ActionFilter`/`ActionInspect`/`ActionReload`/`ActionTop`/`ActionBottom`/`ActionPageUp`/`ActionPageDown`; `ActionTop`/`ActionBottom` carry dual binds (`["g","home"]`/`["G","end"]`) unifying cmdbrowser-vs-docs divergence with no muscle-memory loss. Stdlib actions are NOT auto-registered; the unexported `standardBinding(Action) (Binding, bool)` accessor is promoted to `StandardBinding` only if a Stage 3 pilot needs the fetch-then-customize path.
  - **Plugin** (`plugin.go`) — the contract, **frozen as of Stage 3** after the one deliberate addition below, proven by a test stub (`stub_test.go`). Methods: lifecycle `Init() tea.Cmd` / `Close() error`; `Resize(body Region)`; `Update(tea.Msg) tea.Cmd`; **per-panel** `ViewPanel(id PanelID, inner Region) string` (replaces a single `View() string` so frame-owned multi-panel layout is expressible — a single-region plugin just declares one panel); `Panels() []Panel` where `Panel{ID PanelID, Title string, Weight int}` (horizontal split weight); `StatusContext() string`; `Actions(*Registry) error` (registration hook, surfaces the duplicate error so `newFrame`/`Run` fail **before** launch) + `HandleAction(Action) (tea.Cmd, bool)` (bool reports whether the plugin handled it; built-in help/focus/quit never reach the plugin); `PendingOverlay() (Overlay, bool)`; `Result() any` (typed plugin result, returned unchanged by `Run` — no wrapper type). **`CapturingInput() bool` (Stage 3 addition)** — reports whether the plugin is currently taking raw input WITHOUT an overlay (the cmdbrowser inline filter); while true, `Frame` suspends registry dispatch and forwards keys to `Update`, reserving only `ctrl+c`. This is the one revision the Stage-3 pilot fed back before the interface froze; every concrete plugin and the test stubs implement it (default `false`). Shared value types `PanelID`, `Panel`, `Overlay{Content string, Width, Height int, CapturesInput, ReleaseMouse, FullScreen bool, CloseToken int}` are defined **here** (Go has no forward declarations); `CapturesInput bool` on `Overlay` signals that raw input (incl. printables) routes to the plugin, the registry is bypassed, and only `ctrl+c` (hard-quit) + `esc` (close overlay) remain live — `?` does not open help while a capturing overlay is `Top()`. The exported framework messages `PanelClickMsg{Panel PanelID; X, Y int}`, `FocusChangedMsg{Panel PanelID}`, `OverlayClosedMsg{}`, and **`WheelMsg{Panel PanelID; Delta int}`** (forwarded to `Update` — see Frame) also live here. `WheelMsg` is pointer-routed by hit-zone, NOT by focus, and the plugin decides the per-panel scroll amount; post-Stage-4b it is emitted by the coalescer's trailing flush rather than per raw notch, so `Delta` is the NET notch count (±N), not ±1; it is never dispatched through `MatchMouse`. `OverlayClosedMsg` fires when a `CapturesInput` overlay is dismissed via esc (the `captureClose` path) **or** by a click outside the modal — both funnel through the single emitter `dismissTopOverlay` — those overlays are always plugin-pushed, so it never false-fires on the framework-owned help modal; it is the ONLY notification a plugin gets that its overlay was popped (the cmdbrowser clears `b.inspect`/`inspectPending` on it, preventing a later unmatched raw key from resurrecting the closed inspect modal). The overlay manager adds methods over this type, never a second definition. **Two completions to the message contract.** (a) These four exported messages are the **only** channel by which mouse, focus, overlay-close, and wheel activity reach a plugin — a plugin never receives raw `tea.MouseClickMsg`/focus events for them, and adding `WheelMsg` did **not** extend the frozen interface (it is a `tea.Msg` value routed through the existing `Update`, not a new method). (b) Post-Stage-4b, `WheelMsg` is emitted by the coalescer's trailing flush rather than per raw notch, and `Delta` is the NET coalesced count (±N) — the `±1` above is the pre-coalescer contract; a plugin that ignores the magnitude under-scrolls every burst (viewport panels apply `ScrollBy(Delta*step)`, tree panels move `abs(Delta)` rows). The plugin side of the routing rule is an obligation, not just a description: a plugin **must not** change focus in response to a `WheelMsg` — pointer-routed scroll must never steal keyboard focus.
  - **Overlay manager** (`overlay.go`) — `overlayStack` over the `Overlay` type with `Push`/`Pop`/`Top`/`Empty` enforcing **mutual exclusivity** (one visible modal). `Composite(base, ov, body Region) string` centres the overlay over the body via lipgloss v2's `Compositor` (base layer + centred overlay layer — adopted over hand-rolled ANSI cell math per the decision recorded in `overlay.go`). **Dimming applies to the body region only** (re-rendered through a muted style before the overlay layer is placed); `Composite` receives only the body string, so it **structurally cannot touch the status row**. The click-outside-modal policy (a click outside dismisses the overlay, mirroring `esc`; a click inside is swallowed) is a documented constant (`overlayClickOutsideDismisses`) + a single hit-test seam — no bespoke zone classifier.
  - **`Overlay.ReleaseMouse` + `Overlay.FullScreen`** (native-selection support, `plugin.go` + `frame.go`) — two orthogonal opt-in flags a top overlay may set. **`ReleaseMouse`** forces `Frame.View` to emit `tea.MouseModeNone` regardless of `opts.mouse`, handing the mouse back to the terminal so the user can natively drag-select and copy the overlay's text; it reverts automatically when the overlay pops. The tradeoff is deliberate and must not be "fixed": with the mouse released the framework receives no click/wheel at all, so click-outside-to-close and overlay wheel-scroll stop working for as long as it is set (keyboard scroll and `esc` still work). It composes with `CapturesInput` — keys still route to the plugin, only mouse reporting flips — and a plugin toggles it by republishing the overlay (`ReplaceTop`) with the flag flipped. **`FullScreen`** makes `View` short-circuit to `fullScreenOverlayView`, rendering the overlay as the ENTIRE terminal (no body panels, no borders, no status line, content padded/clamped to `geo.Term`) instead of compositing it centred over the inner body. It exists because **the terminal owns native selection and cannot be clipped to a sub-rectangle**: with `ReleaseMouse` alone a drag-select would sweep in the frame's border columns and status row, so the only way to make the selection contain exactly the overlay is to leave nothing else on screen. The overlay MUST size its own `Content` to the full terminal (the Frame's pad/clamp is a safety net, not a layout engine). The two flags are orthogonal, but docstui's render-error overlay — the sole consumer — sets both in selection mode.
  - **`OverlayScrollbar` / `ScrollOverlayViewport`** (`overlay_scrollbar.go`) — the shared chrome for capturing viewport overlays, so cmdbrowser inspect and docstui's error modal are one implementation rather than two mirrored copies: `OverlayScrollbar(box, yOffset, totalLines) string` overdraws a proportional thumb/track onto the box's right rounded border (glyphs `OverlayScrollbarThumbGlyph` `█` / `OverlayScrollbarTrackGlyph` `░`), and `ScrollOverlayViewport(vp *viewport.Model, delta int)` scrolls by `delta` **coalesced** notches at `OverlayWheelStep=3` lines each (no-op at `delta==0`). Do not re-roll a per-package copy of either.
  - **Capturing-aware `drainOverlay`** (TUI Stage 7, `frame.go`) — the load-bearing async-overlay fix: `drainOverlay` now checks the top overlay before applying a plugin-pending overlay — when `Top().CapturesInput` it `ReplaceTop`s in place (never `Push`), so an async republish (a huh cursor-blink tick, a resize while a form overlay is open) refreshes the snapshot without stacking a duplicate layer that esc would then pop one at a time; otherwise (no overlay / non-capturing top) it `Push`es as before. The check lives in the **function body**, not the ~11 call sites — the only ones reachable while a capturing overlay is Top() are the async branches (default, `WindowSizeMsg`, `FocusRequestMsg`), the rest run with no overlay open so the fold is behaviour-preserving. `refreshCapturingOverlay` stays a distinct entry point for the explicit key/wheel refresh paths (identical behaviour under a capturing top; it must NOT reset the double-click record).
  - **`CloseOverlayMsg{Token}`** (TUI Stage 7, `plugin.go` + `frame.go` `Update` case) — the plugin→framework close primitive (mirror of `FocusRequestMsg`): a plugin returns it as the msg of a `tea.Cmd` and the Frame pops the top overlay + resets the double-click record but does **NOT** emit `OverlayClosedMsg` (plugin-initiated close — the plugin already cleared its state; the `OverlayClosedMsg` echo is only for esc / click-outside dismissals it did not initiate). Needed so a form overlay can close itself on successful submit (Enter); an empty stack is a harmless no-op. **Stale-close guard:** the msg carries the `Overlay.CloseToken` it targets (the plugin stamps a unique non-zero token on the overlay via `FormOverlayOptions.CloseToken` and echoes it here). Because the close travels as a deferred `tea.Cmd`, the user can dismiss the overlay (esc / click-outside) and open a different one (help, inspect) before it is processed; the Frame therefore pops **only** when the current top's `CloseToken` equals `Token`, so a stale request is ignored instead of popping the newer modal. The match is a plain `top.CloseToken == m.Token` with no special case for zero, so an **untargeted** `CloseOverlayMsg{}` (token 0) still pops any top that also carries 0 — which is every framework-owned overlay and every overlay pushed without a token. That is the pre-token behaviour preserved for single-overlay plugins, NOT a no-op and NOT protected against a stale close. cmdbrowser mints tokens from `editTokenSeq` (pre-incremented, first token 1) and passes them through `requestCloseOverlay(token)`. Every framework-owned overlay (the `?`-help modal, and any overlay pushed without a token) leaves `CloseToken` 0, so an untargeted close pops one of those and nothing else — safe only while a plugin can have at most one overlay open. **Any plugin that can open more than one overlay in a session MUST mint a distinct non-zero token per overlay** and thread the same value through BOTH `FormOverlayOptions.CloseToken` and the `CloseOverlayMsg` it later returns; leaving the token 0 reintroduces exactly the stale-close race the guard exists to close.
  - **`FormOverlay`** (TUI Stage 7, `formoverlay.go`) — the reusable embeddable huh-form host (the milestone's second form-host mode, "in-TUI overlay, edit-and-stay"); the framework stays `ask`-free by taking a `*huh.Form` (`NewFormOverlay(form, body Region, FormOverlayOptions{MaxWidth, MaxHeight, Hint, CloseToken})`). Sizing is **host-owned**: `applyWidth` sets the form width to `min(body.Width − formOverlayHChrome(4), MaxWidth (0 ⇒ formOverlayMaxWidth=72))` floored at `formOverlayMinWidth=10` via `form.WithWidth`, re-applied by `Resize`. **`MaxHeight` optional bounded height (commands-param follow-up)** — `MaxHeight == 0` keeps the content-driven behaviour (design decision 1 — a taller-than-body form is truncated lossily by `clampOverlay`; this is the vars single-field path, byte-identical). `MaxHeight > 0` is a content **CAP, not a fixed height** — huh's `Group.WithHeight` sets an EXACT viewport height and the bubbles viewport PADS short content, so a naive `form.WithHeight(body.Height)` would balloon a one-field form into a tall blank modal; the wrapper instead captures the uncapped `naturalHeight = lipgloss.Height(form.View())` ONCE at construction (after `WithWidth`, BEFORE any `WithHeight`; `Form.View` works pre-`Init`) and `applyHeight` clamps from it against `budget = MaxHeight − formOverlayVBorder(2) − hintRows(1 iff Hint != "")`: `budget < natural` → `WithHeight(budget)` (huh scrolls its group viewport to the focused field, `clamped=true`); `budget ≥ natural` AND previously `clamped` → `WithHeight(natural)` (un-clamp — huh's `WithHeight` is one-way, `WithHeight(≤0)` a no-op not a reset, so the restore must be explicit); `budget ≥ natural` never-clamped → **leave the form untouched** (renders content-driven, byte-identical to `MaxHeight == 0`; calling `WithHeight(natural)` here pads a row via huh's group footer). The capped view is NEVER re-measured — every clamp derives from the stored natural height, so a shrink-then-grow `Resize` (via `applySize` = `applyWidth` + `applyHeight`) un-clamps correctly. cmdbrowser's run-form passes `MaxHeight: b.body.Height` (multi-field command forms scroll); vars `openEdit` passes 0. `Init`/`Update` forward to the form (re-asserting `huh.Model` → `*huh.Form`), `Update` **swallows `tea.WindowSizeMsg`** (huh would otherwise auto-size its group height from terminal dims while its own height is 0) and is a no-op post-completion. **Embedding contract** (verified against huh/v2 v2.0.3): `SubmitCmd`/`CancelCmd` are nil when embedded, so completion is detected ONLY by polling `State() huh.FormState` after every forwarded Update; completion is **asynchronous** (the Enter Update leaves `StateNormal` + returns a follow-up cmd → `nextGroupMsg` → a later Update sets `StateCompleted`, so the host must return huh's cmds to bubbletea and re-poll — tests pump the returned cmds before asserting); the bubbles/v2 virtual cursor renders inline in `View()` (no `tea.View.Cursor` plumbing), and its blink arrives as async msgs (why the capturing-aware `drainOverlay` above is load-bearing). `Overlay()` renders the form (plus an optional muted footer hint row) in a rounded-border `Padding(0,1)` box (mirroring inspect), measuring Width/Height from the rendered box, and returns `Overlay{CapturesInput: true}`. A nil form yields an inert wrapper (State=`StateNormal`, empty Overlay) so an upstream BuildForm error never panics the Frame. The follow-up cmd that produces `nextGroupMsg` is huh's own `NextField`. **Consumers build the embedded form with `ask.RunOptions{ShowHelp:false}`** (`cli/vars/browser.go`, `cli/command/list.go`) — note that the flag is set by the CONSUMER at `ask.Build` time, not by `FormOverlay`: huh's built-in help line advertises `ctrl+c = quit the form`, which is wrong under the Frame (there `ctrl+c` hard-quits the whole TUI and never reaches the form at all, so `StateAborted` is defensive only), so it is suppressed and the `Hint` footer row becomes the **single authoritative key hint** for the overlay (the rendered rows are `enter save · esc cancel` for edit and `enter run · esc cancel` for the run form; hardcoded English, per Stage 6's "form chrome i18n is out of scope" decision). Consequently a consumer whose form can exceed the body region must opt into `MaxHeight` **deliberately**: with `MaxHeight == 0` there is no scrolling at all, only lossy `clampOverlay` truncation.
  - **Focus manager** (`focus.go`) + **palette** (`palette.go`) — `focusManager` tracks the active `PanelID` across the plugin's declared panels: `Next()`/`Prev()` (wrap; no-op on zero/one panel), `Set(PanelID) bool` (false on unknown id), `Active() PanelID`, `BorderFor(PanelID) lipgloss.Style` (focused style only for the active panel). `palette.go` is the single styling bridge — `focusedBorder()`/`unfocusedBorder()` build v2 `lipgloss.Style` from `styles.ColorBorder()`/`styles.ColorAccent()` accessors (no v1 styles).
  - **Help** (`help.go`) — `buildHelpOverlay(reg *Registry, tr i18n.Translator, locale string, width, height int) Overlay` renders the registry's sections/bindings into the `?`-modal body (section label · keys · description). The modal is clamped on **both axes** (`MaxWidth`/`MaxHeight`) to the body region passed in — load-bearing because `Composite` does not clip, so an unclamped modal taller than a small-but-permitted terminal (`tooNarrow` only floors height at `minHeight`) would grow the composited frame past the screen and corrupt the alt-screen. `locale` is required because `Translator.T(locale, uiKey, fallback)` takes it; Stage 0 passed `i18n.TranslatorOrNop(nil)` + a fixed locale and deferred real wiring — **Stage 3 wires a real translator/locale** through `RunOptions` → `withTranslator`/`withLocale` so the modal localizes. The framework i18n key namespace (`tui.help.section.<id>`, `tui.help.action.<id>`, `tui.help.title`) is **now in live use as of Stage 3**: real keys ship in the built-in en bundle (`internal/shared/i18n/translations/en.yml`, stored without the `ui.` prefix) and are allowlisted in `KnownUIKeys` (`internal/shared/i18n/known_keys.go`) — kept in exact sync with en.yml (`TestBuiltinCoverage`). en-only in the built-in bundle; ru comes from a workspace overlay or an injected `Store` (the i18n test injects a ru bundle). `select` is deliberately NOT keyed — its label is mode-dependent ("Select"/"Edit") and a single key would flatten the vars-browser relabel. Aliases are intentionally excluded from the rendered help body (`buildHelpOverlay` renders `Binding.Keys` only).
  - **Run** (`run.go`) — the **sole exported entry point**. `RunOptions{Brand, Project string, Mouse bool, Translator i18n.Translator, Locale string (Stage 3), + unexported test seams input/output/isTTY/size}`; `Run(p Plugin, opts RunOptions) (any, error)` returns `Plugin.Result()` **unchanged**. `Translator`/`Locale` map to `withTranslator`/`withLocale` frameOptions (nil/zero → `NopTranslator`/fixed locale); `newGeometry` is unaffected. Builds the frame via `newFrame` and **returns its error before any program start** (duplicate action/key surfaces here, not at runtime). `tea.WithInput`/`tea.WithOutput` are appended **only when each seam is non-nil** — a zero-value `RunOptions` must fall through to default stdin/stdout, because in bubbletea/v2 `WithInput(nil)` *disables* input. **Capability gate before launch**: non-TTY → typed `ErrNotTTY` (no program start, no plugin `Init`); `tooNarrow` → `ErrTooNarrow` fallback sentinel (caller drops to a plain selector; the fallback UI is a later stage). **Teardown**: a named-return surfaces the `Close()` error **only when the program returned no error**, else `_ = plugin.Close()` (errcheck-safe; documented close-error precedence). The actual run routes through the package-private `runProgram` seam (default `(*tea.Program).Run`) so the zero-value-input, mouse-flag, and close-error tests are deterministic without a real event loop. Alt-screen and mouse are **not** program options in v2 — they live on `Frame.View`, fed via `frameOptions` mapped from `RunOptions`.
- `internal/core/ui/tui/tree/` — **generic tree behavioral engine (TUI Stage 4, Plan 2)**, a pure-generic leaf depending on nobody in the project (`maps`/`slices`/`strings` only), consumed by `cmdbrowser` + `docstui`. It replaces the prior "mirror cmdbrowser exactly" duplication (the byte-identical `topIdx`/`ensureFocusVisible`/`focusRow`/`clipToViewport` core + structurally-identical visible-set rebuild and directional collapse/expand) with a **compiler-guaranteed** shared engine. `Engine[N comparable]` owns the *behavior* — visible-row set, cursor, vertical scroll (`topIdx`), a `parent` map, expansion state, directional collapse/expand, navigation, and scroll/clip math; each consumer keeps its own node graph, row rendering, payload, and filter UX. **Rendering is deliberately NOT in the engine (Decision 7, the load-bearing boundary)**: the engine returns `VisibleNodes()` + `Cursor()` + `Clip(full, height)`; consumers render each row themselves because glyphs, counts, truncation, and styles genuinely differ. The engine has **zero filter/query/count concepts** — `RebuildVisible(keep func(N) bool)` takes an optional per-node `keep` predicate (Decision 6: `nil` → all expanded nodes; non-nil → include a node if `keep(node)` OR any descendant is kept, the ancestor-inclusion mechanic) and knows nothing about *why* a node is kept; docs drives its reduce-set filter through `keep`, cmdbrowser passes `nil` and dims/counts externally.
  - **Adapter contract** — a 3-method interface (not closures, for a discoverable/testable contract): `Children(N) []N` (ordered children), `Key(N) string` (**stable, unique id surviving a `SetRoots` rebuild** — expansion + cursor identity are keyed by it), `Expandable(N) bool` (false for leaves / heading rows). cmdbrowser → `N = *treeNode`, `Key = n.id` (dot-path); docs → `N = *TreeNode`, `Key` is **kind-prefixed** to dodge collisions (group → `"group\x00"+root`, file/dir → `"node\x00"+root+"\x00"+path`, heading → `"heading\x00"+root+"\x00"+path+"\x00"+idx`, idx = heading sibling position so duplicate/locale-translated headings stay unique-and-stable).
  - **Map-keying discipline (Decision 8)** — `expanded map[string]bool` is keyed by **`Key` (string)** so it survives a node-graph rebuild; `parent map[N]N` and `byKey map[string]N` are rebuilt per generation in `SetRoots` (pointers valid within one generation, `byKey` gives O(1) cursor re-resolution); `cursor` is stored as **`N`** and re-resolved by `Key` on rebuild. The zero value of `N` (`nil` for a pointer) is the **root/none sentinel** (maps to cmdbrowser's `focusedID == ""`); every nav/collapse/expand/`indexOf`/`EnsureFocusVisible` path is guarded against a nil cursor (behaves as "no focus", no panic).
  - **Expansion keyed by stable `Key` is a bugfix (Decision 3)** — `SetRoots(roots)` re-resolves the cursor by its prior `Key` and, because `expanded` is `Key`-keyed, **preserves expansion across a node-graph rebuild automatically**; if the prior cursor's `Key` is gone it sets the cursor to zero and does **NOT** auto-park (the consumer decides the fallback). This fixes docs losing expansion on locale change.
  - **Cursor re-park is a separate call, never folded into `RebuildVisible` (Decision 4, golden-critical)** — `RebuildVisible` does NOT touch the cursor. `ParkCursorIfHidden()` (docs-only; moves the cursor to `visible[0]` when it falls out of a non-empty visible set) is invoked by docs after every rebuild; cmdbrowser **never** calls it (it tolerates an off-screen cursor and resolves it later via `nearestVisibleAncestor`). Folding park into `RebuildVisible` would flip a cmdbrowser golden. `SetCursorByKey(key) bool` resolves `""`/unknown → zero-value cursor (returns false), known key → that node (returns true).
  - **Bulk/key expansion accessors** — `ExpandedSnapshot()` (copy), `RestoreExpanded(snapshot)`, `SetExpandedByKey(key, b)` exist specifically to back cmdbrowser's filter machinery (`newFilterState`/`applyAutoCollapse`/`restoreExpansion` now route their storage through the engine while the filter logic stays in cmdbrowser); node-based `IsExpanded(n)` / `SetExpanded(n, b)` (docs ancestor-expand). `Clip(full, height)` / `EnsureFocusVisible(height)` / `FocusRow(row)` (click-past-last = no-op) are ported verbatim from the old cmdbrowser logic. Tested against a **fake node type** (`tree_test.go`) so the engine is validated with zero consumer coupling.

## Core — Notify (`internal/core/notify/`)

- `internal/core/notify/` — desktop notifications. `New(cfg) *Notifier` (concrete struct, nil-safe `Notify`) dispatches via an unexported `backend` interface to `noopBackend` or `nativeBackend`. Factory short-circuits to noop on `cfg == nil`, master switch off, empty channels, CI / `DWE_NONINTERACTIVE` / non-TTY (test seam: `isInteractiveForNotify`). `nativeBackend` wraps `osNotify` (test seam, signature `func(title, body string, icon any) error` — `any` mirrors beeep.Notify, lets the embedded `[]byte` icon flow through without temp-file conversion) in a goroutine + non-blocking semaphore + 2s timeout — slot release lives in the inner goroutine so a hung OS notifier can't leak more than one goroutine per process. **Platform split**: `native_other.go` (build `!darwin`) binds `osNotify = beeep.Notify` and sets `beeep.AppName = "DWE"` in `init()`; `native_darwin.go` (build `darwin`) binds `osNotify = darwinNotify`, which dumps the embedded PNG to a temp file and execs `terminal-notifier -title T -message M -group DWE -appIcon <png> -contentImage <png>`, falling back to `osascript` (no icon) on missing/failed `terminal-notifier`. Why not beeep on darwin: modern macOS silently ignores `-appIcon` for terminal-notifier, so beeep's bare `-appIcon <icns>` shows terminal-notifier's bundle icon; we add `-contentImage` so the DWE logo at least renders as a thumbnail in the notification body. `assets/icon.png` is embedded via `//go:embed` (`notificationIcon []byte`) and passed to every notify call. Title format: `✓/✗ DWE · <project>: <op> succeeded/failed` (project segment dropped when empty); body carries duration plus optional truncated error. `Op` (`OpDeploy` / `OpRun` / `OpCommand`) and `Outcome` (`OutcomeSuccess` / `OutcomeFailure`) enums with `*Unknown` at iota 0. `OutcomeFromErr(err)` is shared by all hookpoints. Errors always logged at debug, never returned. Consumers each declare a local `notifier` interface (one method) for testability — they never depend on a public interface.
