- **Notifier hookpoints**: `internal/cli/deploy/deploy.go` (deployRunCmd), `internal/core/workflow/lifecycle/RunRun`, and `internal/core/usercommands/runtime/RunCommand`. All three follow the same shape: `start := time.Now()`, `var projectName string`, `userpkg.Load` (parser error → `slog.Warn` + nil cfg, never blocks), `newNotifier(cfg)` (consumer-local seam), defer firing `Notify(context.Background(), ...)` from a named-return `err`. The defer is installed **before** the main config load so a malformed `workspace.yml` still triggers a failure notification. **`SkipNotify` invariants**: `lifecycle.RunContext.SkipNotify` is set by `RunRestart` so the inner `RunRun` does not fire (restart is not a notify event). `runtime.RunContext.SkipNotify` is set transitively at **every** internal invocation site (`runners/workflow/step.go` sequential + `runners/workflow/parallel.go` parallel paths, `pipeline/executor.go` action dispatch) — only the top-level `runCommandByID` orchestrator leaves it at zero-value false. The runtime gate is `rc.Cmd.Notify && !rc.SkipNotify`; the cheap-path check installs the defer before any I/O so pre-run failures still notify. Validator emits an info diagnostic for the static-detectable direct-parallel-sub-step case (`internal/core/validate/commands/notify.go`); the runtime guard handles transitive cases the validator cannot see. `notify: true` on `type: daemon` is a validator error.

## Core — Bridge (`internal/core/bridge/`)

The host-side half of the dev-container bridge (the in-container half is `internal/shared/bridgeclient` + `cmd/dwe-shim`). User-visible behavior: `docs/reference/concepts/bridge.md`. Design references (D1–D12) live in the implementation plan; the load-bearing invariants are restated here.

- `internal/core/bridge/` — daemon + lifecycle + overlay generation. **The daemon is a stateless forwarder** (design D5): one connection = one command, fork `dwe <argv>` per connection, no in-memory cache, no project model — never add business logic here. Files: `daemon.go` (Config + env contract consts `EnvInvokedFrom` / `InvokedFromContainer` / `EnvNonInteractive` / `BindOverrideEnv`; `DefaultBridgeDir(projectRoot)` = `.dwe/bridge`), `listeners.go` (unix `host.sock` 0660 in dir 0700 + TCP `127.0.0.1:0` always; best-effort extra bind on the docker gateway IP via injectable resolver — bind failure on Mac is silent and expected; `port` written AFTER bind; `token` 0600 written once and kept across restarts — stable project identity; `DWE_BRIDGE_BIND` override is comma- or whitespace-separated; **never `0.0.0.0`**), `session.go` (per-accept: auth — constant-time token compare on TCP / peercred on unix — then HELLO validation: protocol version, realpath cwd containment inside `--project-root`, `tty:true` → `tty_unsupported`; 10 s HELLO deadline so half-open connections cannot pile up; SIGNAL allowlist = SIGINT/SIGTERM only; conn close → SIGTERM to the process group, 5 s grace, SIGKILL; `daemon_shutting_down` ERROR for accepts during Close), `exec.go` + `exec_unix.go` / `exec_windows.go` (the injectable `LaunchSpec`/`Process`/`LaunchFunc` seam; production `launchOS` resolves `os.Executable()` **lazily** so an injected launcher never touches it — calling it from a test re-executes the test binary, the documented recursion hazard; Setpgid + group signaling; signal deaths map to 128+sig; launch failure → STDERR + EXIT 127, protocol ERROR frames stay reserved for the D4 code set). **Env override contract (design D7)**: the daemon re-filters the client-sent env through `bridgeclient.StripEnv` (single strip-set source — `DWE_BRIDGE_DIR` / `DWE_BRIDGE_PROJECT` / `DWE_BRIDGE_UNREACHABLE`, `DWE_HOST_WORKSPACE`, `DWE_CONTAINER_WORKSPACE`, any `DWE_PROJECT_ROOT*`), then **also drops execution-hijacking variables** (`subprocessEnv` — `dangerousEnvNames` = `PATH`/`BASH_ENV`/`ENV`/`SHELLOPTS`/`BASHOPTS`/`IFS`, `dangerousEnvPrefixes` = the dynamic-loader families `LD_*` + `DYLD_*`), **replaces the host-identity set with the daemon's own values** (`hostIdentityEnvNames` = `HOME`/`USER`/`LOGNAME`/`TMPDIR`/`SSH_AUTH_SOCK`, `hostIdentityEnvPrefixes` = `DOCKER_*`/`COMPOSE_*`/`XDG_*`; `daemonEnviron` is the injectable seam — a container `HOME` silently sends the host docker CLI to `unix:///var/run/docker.sock`, absent on Docker Desktop / OrbStack macs, because contexts resolve via `$HOME/.docker/config.json`), and **force-sets** `PATH` to the daemon's own host PATH plus `DWE_INVOKED_FROM=container` + `DWE_NONINTERACTIVE=1`. The two `DWE_*` are host-controlled only; client-sent values are dropped, which is what makes the CLI container policy unspoofable. The PATH/loader strip is the security counterpart: the forked host `dwe` resolves `docker`/`git`/`sh` by bare name, so a container-controlled `PATH`/`LD_PRELOAD` would otherwise be host code execution — the command allowlist limits WHICH commands run, this hardening keeps allowlisted ones off attacker binaries. `DWE_INVOKED_FROM` must NOT affect `--output json` shape. `subprocessEnv` and both sets (`dangerousEnvNames`/`dangerousEnvPrefixes`, `hostIdentityEnvNames`/`hostIdentityEnvPrefixes`) live in `session.go` alongside the accept path, and `daemonEnviron` is the test seam. Before exempting any variable a bridged command needs forwarded, confirm it cannot influence process spawning — the strip set is the only thing between a container-writable env and the host `dwe`'s bare-name resolution of `docker`/`git`/`sh`, so an exemption granted for convenience reopens exactly the trust-boundary hole the set exists to close. **Age identity env is daemon-owned**: `DWE_AGE_KEY` / `DWE_AGE_KEY_FILE` join `hostIdentityEnvNames`, so `subprocessEnv` drops the client's values and appends the daemon's own — a container can never choose which host file the forked host `dwe` parses as an identity, while a host running with an env-only identity keeps working over the bridge. The same two names are also in `bridgeclient.strippedEnvNames` (shim-side strip); they are string literals there rather than `secrets.EnvKey` / `EnvKeyFile` references so the shim-facing leaf stays free of the `filippo.io/age` dependency. Container **reads** are deliberately NOT gated: `dwe vars get` / `dwe render config` from a bridged container already reach the host `dwe`, which holds the identity, and return plaintext — the same exposure the container already has through its own rendered `.env` and config files (documented as accepted in `docs/reference/config/secrets.md`).
- Process lifecycle: `ensure.go` (`Ensure`/`Cycle`/`StopDaemon`/`ProbeDaemon` over a **bridge-private flock** on `.dwe/bridge/daemon.pid` via `shared/lock` — NEVER `lock.AcquireProjectLocks`, this is deliberately not a project lock; Ensure releases the probe flock before spawning — the daemon re-acquires at startup and a spawn-race loser exits cleanly; pidfile mtime is the uptime source, written exactly once by the daemon's own startup acquire), `spawn_unix.go`/`spawn_windows.go` (detached spawn: Setsid + released handle, child stdout/stderr → `daemon.log` so panics land there), `autostop.go` (`RunAutoStop` with injectable `Subscribe`/`CountRunning`: docker events filtered on `com.docker.compose.project=<resolved name>` — the label every other dwe probe matches, via `config.ResolveComposeProjectName`; 60 s `docker ps` poll fallback; 10 s startup grace; zero containers → graceful shutdown removes `host.sock`+`port`, **keeps `token`**; count errors are logged and retried, never fatal; deliberately NO connection-idle timeout — a dead daemon cannot be revived from inside a container).
- `composegen.go` + `prepare.go` — **overlay regeneration model (design D8)**: `.dwe/compose.bridge.yml` is dwe-owned machine state (same model as `.env` and rendered configs), regenerated atomically (same-dir temp + rename, write-if-changed) by the prepare hook on EVERY compose-up command and **DELETED when no enabled service has bridge enabled** — the overlay step ALWAYS runs even when bridge is fully off, so toggles can never leave a stale fragment in the `-f` chain. `RenderOverlay(OverlaySpec)` stays a pure renderer (golden tests); `BuildOverlaySpec` iterates via `config.DeployOrder` (never `range cfg.Services`). Two deliberate deviations from the original design sketch: mount sources are ABSOLUTE host paths (relative compose sources resolve against the FIRST `-f` file's dir, which shifts with `compose.base`), and `DWE_HOST_WORKSPACE`/`DWE_CONTAINER_WORKSPACE` are the per-service hub mapping (`<root>/<svc.dir>` ↔ `svc.dir_internal`) — both omitted when the pair is missing, and the daemon containment check then rejects untranslated cwds (the `validate/bridge` warning). Shim arch per service via `DockerArchResolver` (container inspect `{{.Image}}` → image inspect `{{.Architecture}}`, injectable `CommandOutput` seam; fallback = host arch with a warning — except the expected `No such container` case (first deploy / after reset), which falls back SILENTLY with only a `trace.Decision` `-v` line, since the host arch is correct for native images and re-resolves at the next start — never hardcoded amd64). **Container names for the arch probe are derived with `config.ResolveComposeProjectName(baseDir, cfg)`** — docker.yml's `project_name` when set, NOT `Project.FullName()` (the standing compose-bypass rule); `spec.Project` / `DWE_BRIDGE_PROJECT` stays `FullName()` — it is diagnostic identity, not a container prefix. The overlay also injects `DWE_BRIDGE_SERVICE: <OverlayService.Key>` per service — the workspace services map key (NOT `.Name`, the compose container name), because per-command `bridge.services` lists match workspace keys. Chain position: `composeFiles()` inserts `config.BridgeOverlayRelPath` after app overlays, BEFORE local.yml overlays (local keeps the last word); existence is stat'd at CALL time, not load time — the prepare hook regenerates/deletes the file after `LoadConfig` and before compose args are built. `Prepare(PrepareOptions)` = overlay regenerate-or-delete (always) + shim materialize + daemon Ensure-or-Cycle (`CycleDaemon: true` for `deploy run` and whole-stack restart — cycle REPLACES ensure, never runs in addition); call sites: `lifecycle.RunRun` (after the deploy gate AND post-pull reload, before phases) and `deploy.RunHelper` (after locks). `AnyBridgeEnabled(cfg)` gates daemon-touching steps and the best-effort ensure in top-level `dwe status`. **The bridge is strictly opt-in**: `BridgeEnabledExplicit` (in `project/config/`) defaults to OFF for every service type — it deliberately does NOT share the render toggles' app-default-on shape (`renderEnabledExplicit`), because the bridge mounts a host-controlled binary into the container and opens a container→host command channel.
- `internal/core/bridge/shimassets/` — embedded shim binaries + materialization. `//go:embed all:bin` with a COMMITTED `bin/.gitkeep` placeholder: the shims themselves are gitignored build artifacts (`make shims` cross-compiles `cmd/dwe-shim` for linux amd64/arm64; a prerequisite of `build` and all `test*` targets), and `//go:embed` against an empty dir is a hard compile error for the whole module. `Materialize(baseDir)` writes `.dwe/bridge/shim-linux-*` 0755 atomically (same-dir temp + rename — a half-written shim must never be observable at the bind-mount source of a running container), write-if-changed with chmod-only mode repair; `Status(baseDir)` reports current/stale/missing per shim; `FileName(arch)` is the single shim-name source (composegen references names, not bytes). The package is a leaf — no import of parent `core/bridge`.

## Shared — Leaf Infrastructure (`internal/shared/`)

- `internal/shared/version/` — `Version` / `Commit` / `Date` / `BuiltBy` vars + `Info()`; injected via `-ldflags -X`. Makefile LDFLAGS target `github.com/semsemyonoff/dwe/internal/shared/version.<Field>`.
- `internal/shared/prompt/` — shell-prompt segment renderer for starship / similar integrations. Public entry point: `Run(stdout io.Writer, args []string) int`; internal testable form: `runFromDir(stdout, args, cwd string, useColor bool) int` (explicit cwd so tests use `t.TempDir()` without touching process state). **Lipgloss is deliberately not imported**: lipgloss auto-downgrades to no-color when stdout is piped, which it always is when starship invokes `dwe prompt`. Local `findRoot(start)` stat-walks up to `workspace.yml` — intentionally duplicates `project.Locate` logic to avoid `filepath.EvalSymlinks` cost and the cwd-injection issue on the `Locate` path. Four stub structs (`workspaceStub` / `stylesStub` / `stateStub` / `promptCacheStub`) decode only the fields the prompt needs via lenient `yaml.Unmarshal`. Color is emitted as TrueColor ANSI (`\x1b[38;2;R;G;Bm…\x1b[39m`) using `strings.Builder` + `strconv.AppendUint` — never `fmt.Sprintf` to keep the render path allocation-free. `NO_COLOR` env: suppressed when the variable exists at all (`os.LookupEnv`, value irrelevant). Any error (yaml parse, IO, missing project) → silent exit 1. **Output shape**: `{▪} <project> [<service>] <deploy-icon> <stack-icon>` — each tail segment is independently optional; only the `{▪} <project>` prefix is a stability guarantee. **Service tag**: `detectService(cwd, root)` enumerates `workspace/services/*/`, reads each `service.yml`'s `dir:` and `extends:` fields via lenient `yaml.Unmarshal` into `serviceDirStub`, then a two-pass loop: first builds a `stubs` map, then per-service walks the `extends:` chain via `resolveDir` (cycle-safe via a per-walk `seen` map, no fixed depth cap) until a non-empty `dir:` is found. The resolved path is `filepath.Clean(filepath.Join(rootClean, dir))` for relative dirs, or `filepath.Clean(dir)` when `filepath.IsAbs(dir)`. Match: `cwdClean == resolved` or `strings.HasPrefix(cwdClean, resolved+sep)`. Resolved paths equal to root (`dir: .`) or escaping root (`dir: ..`, absolute paths outside root) are rejected via a `HasPrefix(resolved, rootPrefix)` containment check — otherwise they would tag every cwd in (or above) the project. The deepest match wins by `len(resolved)`. Per-file size cap of 64 KB on the hot path: `os.Stat` first, skip if oversized (defense against generated/anchor-bomb YAML on a per-keystroke render path). `cwd` is `filepath.Clean`-ed at the top to tolerate trailing separators or `./` segments from `$PWD`-honoring shells. Sanitized via `sanitizeName` at render time. Does NOT resolve symlinks (mirrors `findRoot`). Cost: one `os.ReadDir` plus N stat+read+parse per prompt invocation (sub-millisecond on typical projects). **Stack icon** (`●` running / `◐` partial / `○` stopped, colored from `palette.success` / `warning` / `muted` — `muted` default `#6B7280`, overridable via `styles.yml` `colors.muted`): backed by `.dwe/prompt-cache.yml` (TTL 2 min). `readStack(root, composeProject, now)` returns the cached state when fresh; on stale or missing cache it invokes `refreshStack` (shells out to `docker ps -q --filter label=com.docker.compose.project=<project>` via the test-injectable `dockerPsFunc(ctx, project, env)` with a 150 ms hard timeout). The probe env comes from `readDockerProcessEnv(root)`: `process_env` overrides from `workspace/docker.yml` + `workspace/docker.local.yml` (per-key, local wins — mirrors `LoadDockerConfig`'s deepMerge; lenient — bad YAML and non-string values silently skipped; values literal, never template-resolved, matching the strict loader) applied over the process env via `docker.MergeEnv`, so the probe targets the same daemon (`DOCKER_HOST` / `DOCKER_CONTEXT`) as lifecycle commands; nil (inherit) when no overrides. Read only on the refresh path — fresh-cache renders pay no extra file IO. **No-downgrade rule**: `writeCache` is invoked ONLY when `refreshStack` returns `ok=true` AND state is `stackRunning` (count > 0). On `stackStopped` (zero rows) the cache is NOT written — the stale cache value is returned while it is younger than `staleTrustCap` (5× TTL = 10 min); past the cap the confirmed zero-probe **renders** as `stackStopped` (still no write), so a stack stopped outside dwe converges to `○` instead of showing `●` indefinitely. An unconfirmed zero (`refreshStack` `ok=false`) never downgrades at any age, and with no cache at all the result is `stackNone` (icon omitted). Rationale: a zero-result from `docker ps` is indistinguishable between a genuinely stopped stack and a wrong label filter (templated `docker.yml.project_name` cannot be resolved by prompt); writing `stopped` could downgrade a correct `running` left by an authoritative writer. `stopped` writes are reserved exclusively for authoritative writers (lifecycle commands + `dwe status`). The compose project name in prompt comes from `readComposeProjectName`, mirroring `ResolveComposeProjectName` precedence with literal values only: `project_name` from `workspace/docker.local.yml` then `workspace/docker.yml` (local wins; template values fall back), else `prefix-name`, else `project.name`. Projects with templated `project_name` see prompt refresh as a persistent zero-result while lifecycle/status writes keep the cache accurate — past `staleTrustCap` that zero renders `○` until the next authoritative write. The `docker` binary name is hardcoded — `binaries.docker: podman` bypasses prompt-driven refresh (lifecycle writes still work); `process_env` IS honored (see above), only the binary name is fixed. Extending that precedence list: **every branch of `readComposeProjectName` lowercases its result**, hand-repeating `config.normalizeComposeProjectName`. This is the single deliberate exception to the one-resolver rule in `internal/core/project/config/docker.go` — the prompt hot path must not load the full config — and the repeat is mandatory, not stylistic: without it the `com.docker.compose.project` label filter misses every container of a project whose `project.name` / `project.prefix` / `docker.yml project_name` carries uppercase, and the prompt would render a permanently-stopped stack.
- `internal/shared/promptcache/` — leaf package: best-effort writer + invalidation API for `.dwe/prompt-cache.yml`. **Zero `internal/core/**` imports** (the leaf invariant — `core/project/stack.HealthState` does the `Health → state-string` mapping in `core/` so callers compose `promptcache.Write(root, stack.HealthState(h))`). Public surface: string constants `StateRunning` / `StatePartial` / `StateStopped`; `Write(root, state string) error` validates `state` is one of the three constants (returns error otherwise without writing), ensures `<root>/.dwe/` exists, encodes `{updated_at: time.Now().UTC().Format(time.RFC3339), state: <state>}` to YAML, atomic write via `os.CreateTemp(<root>/.dwe/, "prompt-cache-*.tmp")` + `os.Rename`; `Remove(root string) error` removes the cache file, idempotent on `os.ErrNotExist`. Callers (lifecycle / deploy / service / snapshot / status CLI packages) invoke directly — `_ = promptcache.Write(...)` or `_ = promptcache.Remove(...)`; errors are deliberately ignored at every call site (best-effort: the cache is observability, not correctness). **Writer matrix**: see `docs/guides/starship.md` § Writer map. The rule that matrix encodes: a site that ends with the WHOLE stack in a known state **writes** it — `dwe run`, `dwe restart` with no service arg, and `dwe services enable|disable --apply` (after the entire toggle plan including after-hooks) write `running`; `dwe stop` with no service arg and project-wide `dwe reset run` write `stopped` (reset writes before journal cleanup, so a later `journal.Remove` failure cannot leave a stale `running`); top-level `dwe status` writes the exact aggregate via `stack.HealthState(stack.HealthFromStatusInput(...))`. Every site whose effect is SCOPED or whose outcome is unknown **invalidates instead of guessing** via `promptcache.Remove`: each `<service>`/`--service` variant of `stop` / `restart` / `reset run`, `dwe deploy run` (it can no-op via "already up-to-date"), and `dwe snapshot restore` / `rollback` / **`create`** (the create workflow may stop or restart containers, and on failure may have partially mutated container state). `dwe status <subcommand>` neither writes nor invalidates — only the top level performs the full aggregation. `dwe prompt`'s own refresh only ever writes `running`, **never** `stopped`: a zero-result `docker ps` is indistinguishable from a wrong-label filter, so the no-downgrade rule bounds writes, not rendering — a confirmed zero-result is still *rendered* as stopped once the stale value is older than `staleTrustCap` (5× TTL = 10 min).
- `internal/shared/generatedstore/` — leaf store for service-minted "generated-once" secrets (Laravel `APP_KEY`, Magento `crypt.key`, …), mirroring `promptcache`'s leaf-IO shape. **Zero `internal/core/**` imports.** Backs `.dwe/generated.yml` (`const DefaultRelPath`, no `schema_version`); on-disk schema is `services: {<svc>: {<field>: <value>}}` (string values, block scalars for multi-line). `Load(path)` returns an empty store on a missing file but **surfaces** a corrupt file (NOT swallowed); `Save(path, s)` is atomic (temp + rename, mirrors `journal.Save`). Accessors: `Has(svc, field)`, `Get(svc, field)`, `Service(svc) map[string]string`, `SetIfAbsent(svc, field, val) bool` (write-if-absent — the harvest contract: a stored value is never overwritten), `ClearService(svc)`, `ClearAll()`, `IsEmpty()`. The store is intentionally separate from `.dwe/deploy/state.yml` so snapshot restore (which overwrites the journal) leaves harvested secrets untouched. `.dwe/` is fully gitignored, so secrets stay out of git. Consumed by `templates/config` (`RenderConfigs`/`HarvestGenerated`), the `generated-missing` predicate, `renderConfigsForRun`, and `reset --clear-generated`.
- `internal/shared/secrets/` — leaf crypto package for encrypted-at-rest config values. **The only package in the tree that may import `filippo.io/age`** (justification in the package doc: standard audited format, `age`-CLI-readable output, X25519 gives encrypt-with-public-key-only for free). **Zero `internal/core/**` imports.** Three files: `secrets.go` (the `ENC[age:…]` scalar marker — `MarkerPrefix`, strict full-string `IsMarker` (`^ENC\[age:[A-Za-z0-9+/=]+\]$` — the WHOLE scalar or nothing, so a string merely *containing* `ENC[` stays data), `ContainsMarker` for the output guards, `CheckMarker` (identity-free damage check: marker shape + base64 + age file header — without it a keyless machine cannot tell `no_identity` from `corrupt`), `Encrypt`/`Decrypt` over a base64-wrapped age file, `EncryptBytes`/`DecryptBytes` for native `.age` files); `identity.go` (the `Identity` wrapper — `age.X25519Identity` implements `fmt.Stringer` **with the secret key**, so the wrapper owns `String()` ("age identity for age1…") and exposes the key only through `Export()`; `Keygen`, `ParseIdentity` (the FIRST `AGE-SECRET-KEY-1…` token **anywhere** in the text wins — a bare key line, a whole `age` CLI keyfile, CRLF and a keyfile whose lines a paste joined into one all parse; later tokens are ignored; no token or a token `age` refuses is `ErrInvalidIdentity` with DWE-authored text, never the `age` error, whose message echoes the input's private-key bytes), `ParseRecipient`, `KeyfilePath`, `KeysDir`, `WriteKeyfile` (`MkdirAll` + explicit `chmod 0700` so a pre-existing 0755 dir is tightened, then `O_CREATE|O_EXCL` 0600 — a true no-clobber write), `LoadIdentity(recipient) (Identity, Source, error)` and `LoadAnyIdentity(exclude)` for rekey recovery); `redact.go` (`Redactor` — `NewRedactor` drops values under `MinRedactRunes` (4) and sorts longest-first so a value containing another is handled once; `Redact` replaces with `RedactPlaceholder` (`***`)). **`LoadIdentity` order is `DWE_AGE_KEY` (text) → `DWE_AGE_KEY_FILE` (path) → keyfile, first-present-wins with NO fall-through**: the first present source must match the recipient or it is `ErrWrongIdentity` naming the source — silently ignoring an explicitly-set env identity is how a CI job reports "no key" when the real problem is the wrong key. Keyfile `ErrNotExist` and an empty recipient are `ErrNoIdentity` (the latter without touching the filesystem); any other read error passes through unwrapped so a permissions problem is never hidden. The returned `Source` is the **consulted** source on failure as well (`SourceNone` only for an empty recipient), and `layers.go` records it in `SecretsState.IdentitySource` unconditionally — that is what lets `dwe secrets status` word `invalid (…)` / `wrong recipient (…)` per source instead of collapsing every failure into `none`; a recipient mismatch is the typed `*WrongIdentityError` (unwraps to `ErrWrongIdentity`) carrying `Have`/`Want` structurally, so a display surface never re-parses the sentence, and `SourceLabel` is the single place a source is named for display (`$DWE_AGE_KEY`, `$DWE_AGE_KEY_FILE <path>`, `keyfile <path>` — locations, never content). Sentinels `ErrNoIdentity` / `ErrWrongIdentity` / `ErrInvalidIdentity` / `ErrCorrupt` are what `config`'s `Reason*` strings are derived from — through **two** mappers, which is load-bearing: `unresolvedReason` handles a per-marker `Decrypt` failure and may default to `ReasonCorrupt` (only the three decrypt sentinels reach it), while `identityReason` handles an identity-LOAD failure and defaults to `ReasonNoIdentity`, because that path deliberately returns a keyfile permission error unwrapped — running it through the first mapper reports "the encrypted payload is damaged" for every marker in the project and sends the developer to `dwe secrets rekey` over a local key problem. `identityReason`'s one non-default arm besides `ReasonWrongIdentity` is `ErrInvalidIdentity` → `ReasonInvalidIdentity`: a source that WAS present but holds no key is repaired, not obtained, so it must not read as `no_identity`. `keygate.IdentityReason` (behind `IdentitySet.Reason()`, formerly `cli/secrets`'s `identitySet.reason()`) is the same rule on the display side; the two must agree, and a new sentinel arm lands in both. `validate/secrets.reasonPhrase` words `invalid_identity` per source (`$DWE_AGE_KEY is set but holds no age identity`) from fixed strings only. Symmetrically, `keygate.IdentitySet`'s `classifyMarker` / `Decrypt` must not collapse a post-`CheckMarker` `ErrCorrupt` (header and base64 valid, body damaged) into `wrong_identity` — that would contradict the loader on the same value. `IdentityHint(recipient)` and `DisplayKeyfilePath(recipient)` live here rather than in their two former unexported copies (`validate/secrets`, `cli/secrets`), so the validator, the gate and the CLI print one fix sentence without a cross-sibling import. `ListKeyfiles()` is the sorted-by-filename scan behind `dwe secrets key list`, returning `KeyfileInfo{Path, Recipient, State}` over the fixed `KeyfileState` vocabulary (`ok` / `unreadable` / `unparsable` / `misnamed`, the last one reported under the PARSED recipient) — **no error text and no file content is carried out**, because both echo key material; a missing directory is an empty slice and a nil error. A test pins that `%v` / `%s` / `%+v` on an `Identity` — or on a struct embedding one — never contains `AGE-SECRET-KEY-`.
- `internal/shared/docker/` — `Compose` for building/executing `docker compose` with policy args; `NewCompose(cfg, dockerCfg, baseDir)` / `NewComposeAll(cfg, dockerCfg, baseDir)`; always read the binary via `Compose.BinName()` (nil/empty-safe). **`Compose.BaseDir` is the project root and is applied as `cmd.Dir` on every docker subprocess** (`Exec`, internal `output`, `RunningServices`) so that relative `-f compose.yaml` paths in `cfg.ComposeFiles()` resolve correctly even when dwe is invoked from a project subdirectory. Callers constructing raw `exec.Command` against `compose.BinName()` (e.g. `internal/core/project/stack/topology.go`, `stack/daemons.go`, `internal/core/usercommands/runtime/runners/service/exec.go`, `internal/cli/command/daemonset.go`, `internal/cli/compose/compose.go` raw passthrough) MUST also set `cmd.Dir = compose.BaseDir` (or the equivalent `baseDir` parameter for the stack helpers). Empty `BaseDir` is only safe inside tests that never exec. Health helpers: `WaitContainersHealthy`, `HealthStatus`, `ContainerIDsFor`. `StopContainer(ctx, dockerBin, name string, timeoutSec int) error` for direct container stop (no compose); idempotent on "No such container". `RemoveContainer(ctx, dockerBin, name string) error` is the symmetric `docker rm -f` helper, also idempotent on "No such container"; the pair backs both `dwe stop <name>` (stop only) and the per-service-reset synthetic `docker_stop_remove_container` builtin (stop+rm). `RestartContainer(ctx, dockerBin, name string, timeoutSec int) error` issues `docker restart -t <timeoutSec>`; unlike `StopContainer`, it is **NOT idempotent** — returns `ErrNoSuchContainer` when the container does not exist, so callers can differentiate "never deployed" from "stopped". `DefaultStopTimeoutSec` exported constant shared with `internal/core/execution/builtin/containers/daemon_stop.go`. **Build-base prepull** (`prepull.go` + `dockerfilerefs.go`, backing `build.prepull_bases`): `func (c *Compose) DeriveBuildBases(services []string) ([]BaseRef, error)` runs `c.output(c.BuildInternalArgs("config", "--format", "json"))` on the SAME `Compose` instance the caller will `Exec` with — never `BuildArgs`, since user `args.global` (e.g. `--ansi always`) would corrupt the machine-readable JSON. It decodes `services.<name>.{platform,build.{context,dockerfile,dockerfile_inline,args,platforms}}`, resolves `dockerfile` relative to the (already-absolute) `context` unless it is itself absolute, and feeds each Dockerfile's bytes plus the service's `build.args` and its resolved **target platform** into the hand-rolled, pure (no I/O) parser in `dockerfilerefs.go` — handles line continuations, `# escape=`, pre-`FROM` `ARG` defaults overridden by `build.args`, `FROM [--platform=...] ref [AS stage]` with stage-name/`scratch` exclusion, and `${VAR}`/`${VAR:-def}`/`$VAR` substitution (an unresolvable var, e.g. buildkit's `$BUILDPLATFORM`, is skipped via a `trace` warning, never an error). Each result is a `BaseRef{Ref, Platform}` — the `Platform` is the FROM's effective build platform: a `FROM --platform=<p>` literal/ARG-resolvable value; the service's **target platform** (`build.platforms` wins, else `services.<name>.platform`) for a bare `FROM` **or** a `--platform=$TARGETPLATFORM` builtin (Compose already knows the target platform, so a base with no explicit platform builds for it); or `""` for `--platform=$BUILDPLATFORM` (the native build host → daemon default) / an unresolvable var / no service target platform. **The platform is load-bearing, not cosmetic**: on a host whose default platform differs from the pinned/target one (e.g. `platform: linux/amd64` on an arm64 mac), prepulling/probing the default variant would leave buildkit to fetch the target variant from the LAN registry — the exact fetch this feature avoids. A multi-entry `build.platforms` yields one `BaseRef` per target platform. Dedup + sort key is `(Ref, Platform)`, so the same ref pinned to two platforms yields two entries. Returns sorted, deduped external refs; an empty `services` slice means "derive for all". `ImageExists(ref, platform string) bool` / `PullImage(ref, platform string) error` mirror the `volumes.go` raw-`exec.Command(bin, ...)` pattern but — DELIBERATELY unlike `volumeExists` — set `c.BuildEnv()`/`c.BaseDir` so `DOCKER_HOST`/context overrides from `process_env` apply; both append `--platform <p>` when `platform != ""` (an `image inspect` that predates `--platform` support just errors → treated as "missing" → a safe pull, per the advisory contract); `PullImage` streams stdout/stderr straight to the terminal (base pulls can take minutes) with a `trace.Command` echo first, matching `Compose.Exec`. All three are pure mechanics + errors — `internal/shared/docker` never prints user-facing text; the caller (`internal/cli/docker`) owns warnings and the advisory degrade-to-warning contract.
- `internal/shared/daemon/` — cross-cutting daemon domain utilities: `ResolveContainerName(projectFullName, renderedTemplate)` (prepends `<projectFullName>-`, validates against `^[a-zA-Z0-9_][a-zA-Z0-9_.-]*$` — the authoritative runtime gate on container names), `StandardLabels(projectFullName, daemonID, params)` (emits `--label key=value` argv pairs for the three standard labels), `FilterArgsByLabels(projectFullName, daemonID)` (`--filter label=…` argv for `docker ps`); sentinel errors `ErrContainerNameInvalid` / `ErrDaemonAlreadyRunning` / `ErrDaemonNotRunning`; label-key constants `LabelProject` / `LabelDaemonID` / `LabelDaemonParams`. Consumed by `internal/core/execution/builtin`, `internal/core/project/stack`, `internal/core/validate/commands`, and `internal/cli`.
- `internal/shared/bridgeproto/` — host-bridge wire protocol leaf (note: `shared/daemon/` above is TAKEN by user-command daemon utilities — hence the `bridge*` naming). Length-prefixed binary frames identical on both transports: `[4-byte BE uint32 payload length][1-byte type][payload]`, types 0x01–0x09 (HELLO / STDIN / STDOUT / STDERR / STDIN_CLOSE / SIGNAL / RESIZE-reserved / EXIT / ERROR), `MaxPayload` guard bounds memory. `ProtocolVersion` const + ERROR code constants (`auth_failed`, `version_mismatch`, `cwd_outside_project`, `tty_unsupported`, `daemon_shutting_down`). Deliberately NO channel-id in the header — one connection = one command; multiplexing would be a new `protocol_version`. `WriteFrame` emits header+payload in a **single `Write`** so concurrent frame writers on a `net.Conn` cannot interleave. Token helpers: generate (crypto/rand 32 bytes hex), read/write file 0600, constant-time compare — `TokenEqual` **rejects empty tokens on both sides** so a missing token file can never authenticate. Peercred: `PeerUID`/`PeerIsSameUser` behind build tags (`SO_PEERCRED` linux / `LOCAL_PEERCRED` darwin / fail-closed stub elsewhere, mirroring the `shared/lock` fallback shape).
- `internal/shared/bridgeclient/` — the in-container shim core; **leaf** (no `core/`/`cli/` imports), no cobra, no lipgloss (mirrors `shared/prompt` — `cmd/dwe-shim` is a thin main over it). Transport selection per design D2: stat + dial `$DWE_BRIDGE_DIR/host.sock` (300 ms timeout — a dead socket inode on Docker Desktop refuses instantly, by design; never platform-detect) → fall back to TCP `host.docker.internal:<port>` reading the `port`+`token` files (2×100 ms retry on missing — daemon-restart window). Token is sent only on TCP; unix authenticates via peercred. HELLO always carries `tty: false` (even on a real terminal — design D11). Color: `OptionsFromEnv` probes the shim's own stdout (`stdoutIsTerminal` seam) and on a real terminal appends `CLICOLOR_FORCE=1` plus `COLORTERM=truecolor` via `ForceColorEnv` (container `NO_COLOR` / explicit `CLICOLOR_FORCE` / explicit `COLORTERM` win unchanged) — the host-side dwe writes into a pipe and would otherwise downgrade to no-color and quantize the palette to the container's bare `TERM=xterm`. `SetStdinTTYEnv` force-owns `DWE_BRIDGE_STDIN_TTY` (spoofed values dropped, appended only when the shim's stdin IS a terminal) — the signal that lets host runners give user-command children a full PTY (see § runio). `StripEnv` is the **single strip-set source** (the daemon re-filters through it) — `DWE_BRIDGE_SERVICE` (`EnvBridgeService`) is deliberately NOT in it: the overlay injects it per service (value = the workspace service KEY, not the compose container name) and it must reach the host-side dwe, whose `registry.applyBridgeVisibility` consumes it via the `InContainer()` / `CallingService()` helpers here; container-reported, hence advisory (UX boundary, not security). The canonical `EnvInvokedFrom`/`InvokedFromContainer`/`EnvNonInteractive` constants also live here (leaf — importable from `core/` registry code); `core/bridge` keeps aliases for daemon-side call sites. `TranslateCwd` is path-boundary-aware prefix rewrite (`/workspace` does not match `/workspaces`), untranslatable cwds pass through as-is — the daemon (`resolveCwd` in `core/bridge/session.go`) FALLS BACK to its own project root for any cwd it cannot place inside the project (hooks routinely `cd` out of the container mount and send `/`), logging a note instead of rejecting; `ErrCodeCwdOutsideProject` is no longer emitted (kept for wire compat). Unreachable policy (design D10): default error + exit 1 (a hook must block the commit), `DWE_BRIDGE_UNREACHABLE=warn` → warning + exit 0; a connection dying before delivering ANY frame is mapped to unreachable too (Desktop's host proxy can accept the connect and only then discover nothing listens); `version_mismatch` renders the fixed "shim outdated, re-run `dwe deploy` to refresh shim binaries" remedy. Tests run the real client against in-process fake servers over loopback TCP + tmpdir unix sockets — no Docker.
- `internal/shared/lock/` — project-level file lock with stale-PID detection (Unix-only build tag): `Acquire(path)` uses `flock(LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB)`; on `EWOULDBLOCK` reads the holder PID and calls `Kill(pid, 0)` to reclaim stale locks. Lock files: `.dwe/deploy/deploy.lock` and `.dwe/snapshots/snapshot.lock`. Lock files are intentionally left on disk after `Release` with the last writer's PID; **never** delete a conflict lock file (inode-race invariant, `lock.go:120-125`). `project.go` exposes `AcquireProjectLocks(baseDir) (release func(), err error)` — the **single seam** for project-mutating commands: acquires `deploy.lock` first, `snapshot.lock` second (alphabetical), releases in reverse; on second-lock failure the first is released before returning the error; error message is `"<deploy|snapshot> operation in progress: pid N"` (wraps `lock.HeldError`). Every snapshot mutating subcommand (`create` / `restore` / `rollback` / `remove` / `pack` / `unpack`) and every deploy lifecycle command (`deploy run` / `run` / `stop` / `restart` / `reset`) calls this helper — never `lock.Acquire` directly on the deploy or snapshot lock path. Lifecycle commands acquire the project locks **after** `preflightRun` succeeds. Behavior detail: `docs/reference/config/state/index.md` § Lock file and `docs/reference/config/snapshot.md` § Lock interaction.
- `internal/shared/git/` — git update probe: `Probe`, `PullFFOnly`, `Decide`. **Mode is binary: `ModeOn` / `ModeOff`** (`UpdateMode` string type) — the former `prompt`/`auto`/`check` triad was collapsed (YAGNI) and the dead `ActionPullAuto` constant removed. Safety matrix: not-a-repo / `ModeOff` → skip; dirty / no-upstream / fetch-failed / diverged → warn; up-to-date → skip; behind + `ModeOn` → `ActionPullPrompt` on a TTY (ask, then `pull --ff-only`), `ActionWarn` ("behind … skipping in non-interactive") otherwise. The consumer (`workflow/lifecycle/run.go`) maps the resolved `on`/`off` string to `git.UpdateMode` before calling `Decide`. `Runner` interface for tests.
- `internal/shared/envfile/` — `.env` generation: `BuildContent(cfg)`, `Regenerate(configPath)`. No cobra / UI deps. Host-id resolution (`HostUID` / `HostGID`) delegates to `shared/hostid`. **Marker guard + explicit chmod.** `BuildContent` refuses any emitted value that is still an `ENC[age:…]` marker — the three system values (`PROJECT` from `cfg.Project.Name`) **and** every export rule, naming the export and the source path (the *rule default* when the value came from `default:` rather than `from:`, so the message never points at a path the value did not come from) and pointing at `dwe secrets status`. The guard lives here rather than in preflight because **none** of the four `.env` write paths run preflight: `dwe render env`, the compose auto-regeneration in `cli/docker`, the `dwe services enable/disable` regeneration, and `renderAndSourceDotEnv` in `lifecycle/run.go` — which fires BEFORE `PreflightFunc`, making it the first thing a keyless `dwe run` reports. The guard tests `secrets.ContainsMarker`, NOT `IsMarker`: a rule whose `from:` resolves to a map or a sequence is emitted through `FormatValue`'s `%v`, so the marker arrives embedded in `map[password:ENC[age:…]]` and an exact-match test writes the ciphertext into the one plaintext sink the container reads. `Write` additionally `os.Chmod`s the target to 0600, so a pre-existing permissive `.env` is tightened rather than left as-is — the chmod runs **before** the `os.WriteFile`, since `WriteFile` keeps an existing file's mode and tightening afterwards leaves the plaintext world-readable for the length of the write (permanently if the process dies in between). The same write-order rule applies to the two sibling sites, `execution/templates/config`'s `.age`-sourced outputs and `cli/secrets`' `writeOutputFile`; a `fs.ErrNotExist` from the pre-chmod is ignored because `WriteFile` then creates the file at the tight mode itself.
- `internal/shared/hostid/` — leaf owner of the platform host-id logic shared by `envfile` and `tpl`: `Current() Info` does a single `user.Current()` read and returns `{UID, GID}` (`UID()` / `GID()` are convenience wrappers). `envfile.HostUID` / `HostGID` delegate here; `tpl.CurrentHostInfo` returns `HostInfo(hostid.Current())` (an identical-field struct conversion, so existing `HostInfo` literals in tests stay valid). Centralizes what was duplicated across `envfile/render.go` and `tpl/render_command.go`.
- `internal/shared/tpl/` — Go template engine: `Render`, `RenderCommand`, `EvalCondition`, `EvalCommandCondition` (render-first). `FuncMap()` returns a per-call shallow clone of a sprout-backed map (`std`, `strings`, `numeric`, `slices`, `maps`, `regexp`, `conversion`, `time`, `filesystem`, `semver`) plus domain helper `appURL`. Hermetic (no env, FS, network, crypto). `commandFuncMap` adds `resolve` / `resolveMap` / `resolveFile` (command scope only). **Snapshot template scope gate**: `${snapshot.*}` resolves only inside snapshot workflow blocks. The gate is enforced by `validateSnapshotScope(expr, scope)` in `render_command.go`, called from `RenderCommand` **before** `CompileVarSyntax`. `CompileVarSyntax` has no error path and must stay pure-syntactic — never add scope logic there. Three scope values: `SnapshotScopeNone` (zero value, default — compile error on any `${snapshot.*}`), `SnapshotScopeCreate` (all keys except `created_at`), `SnapshotScopeRestoreOrRemove` (all keys). Use `meta.BuildSnapshotVars(...)` (from `internal/core/workflow/snapshot/meta`) to build the map; inject via `RunContext.Render.Snapshot` + `.SnapshotScope`. **`generated` namespace** (config render): `RenderContext.Generated map[string]string` holds the current service's harvested values; `CompileVarSyntax` routes `case "generated"` → `{{ resolveGenerated .Generated "<name>" }}`. Like every other `${...}` resolver, `resolveGenerated` is **lenient** (absent key → `""`) — there is no strict-vs-lenient distinction. Config templates reference per-service config fields via `${services.<name>...}` (the curated `injectServicesIntoRaw` subset only) and free-form values via `${vars.<path>}`; a singular current-service `${service....}` binding does NOT exist. **Unknown-head whitelist**: `CompileVarSyntax`'s default branch (bare dot-path → `{{ resolve .Raw "X" }}`) fires only when `X`'s head is in the exported `KnownVarHeads` — the union of `config.allowedRootKeys` (duplicated here since `tpl` must not import `internal/core/project/config`; a cross-check test in `config` asserts `allowedRootKeys ⊆ tpl.KnownVarHeads`) and the special namespaces (`files`, `host`, `param`, `context`, `snapshot`, `generated`, `args`). An unrecognized head (`${HOME}`, a typo, a stale pre-strict-root bare dot-path like `${databases.x}`) is left as a literal `${...}` instead of collapsing to `""` — this is a correctness control, not a security boundary. **The head must also carry a tail**: `IsVarNamespaceRef(inner)` (= known head AND (`hasTail` OR head == `args`)) is the exported rule every caller asks — the compiler's own default branch, `ValidateRawScope`, `pipeline.hasKnownVarRef`, `pipeline.UnresolvedTemplateRefs` — so a head-only `${host}` / `${files}` / `${services}` stays literal instead of resolving against `Raw` (erasing to `""`, or interpolating a whole sub-map as `map[...]` text) — those are lowercase shell variables that happen to collide with namespace names: a `${vars.*}` value reaching a host `sh -c` program is ordinary substitution, not a trust boundary, so the whitelist never sanitizes or quotes. `__configPath` is deliberately excluded from `KnownVarHeads` — it is a loader-injected internal key, not part of the authoring contract. **Raw/Host-only scope gate**: `ValidateRawScope(expr)` is the sibling of `validateSnapshotScope` for the five heads that resolve from a caller-populated `RenderContext` field rather than `Raw`/`Host` (`param`, `context`, `files`, `generated`, `args` — `snapshot` stays with `validateSnapshotScope`, which has the better message). The one render path whose context is structurally Raw + Host only — `pipeline.renderContextFor`, which scenario steps share since `envtest` has no renderer of its own — calls it before `RenderCommand` and fails on error, so a reference to a namespace with no source there is an error naming the namespace instead of a silent `""` from the lenient resolvers. Do NOT wire it into `usercommands.buildRunContext` or workflow sub-steps: those render `with:` against the parent command's context, where `${param.*}` is legitimate. **`VarPattern`** is the exported `${...}` matcher (`= varPattern`, `\$\{([a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_.]*)\}`) reused by `varsusage`'s static scanner so it keys off the renderer's OWN pattern semantics (no internal whitespace, no leading digit) rather than a divergent grep. The tail rule is not arbitrary: every namespace form in the authoring contract is dotted (`${vars.db.host}`, `${project.name}`, `${host.uid}`, `${files.id.path}`), while head-only `${host}` / `${files}` / `${services}` tokens are ordinary shell variables and are rife in pipeline `cmd:` strings — which only began rendering when resolve-time rendering landed, which is what forced the rule. Both mis-rewrites it prevents (erasure to `""`, or a `map[...]` dump of a whole sub-map) were **silent**, and that silence is the exact failure mode the known-head whitelist exists to stop. `${args}` is the ONE bare form the contract defines, hence the explicit `head == "args"` escape — and it is still rejected by `ValidateRawScope` on the pipeline path, where the namespace has no source at all. `IsKnownVarHead(head)` is the exported head-alone entry point over the unexported `knownVarHeadSet`; outside callers must ask through it (or `IsVarNamespaceRef` for a full inner path) rather than re-indexing the exported slice, so there is exactly one membership index and it cannot drift from the rewrite. **One documented exception to the whitelist**: `docker.yml`'s `project_name` goes through `config.resolveVarTemplate` (`internal/core/project/config/docker.go`) instead — see there.
- `internal/shared/pathsafe/` — filesystem safety: `CheckNoSymlinks`, `ContainedRel` (rejects `..` / `.` escaping), `EnsureRealUnder` (callers must `filepath.EvalSymlinks` before passing). Used by template renderers to prevent escape attacks.
- `internal/shared/render/` — `Writer` with ANSI output methods (Success / Error / Warning / Info / Definition / TableHeader / ASCII art); plain passthrough for logs; `Writer.Confirm` is the non-TTY Y/n fallback. `Writer.ASCII(lines []string, font string) error` emits plain figlet text — callers wrap output in lipgloss style externally (typically via `accent`). No built-in color path; `validFigureColors` and `NewColorFigure` were dropped as part of the branding refactor.
- `internal/shared/liveui/` — shared live-output infrastructure: `LiveLine` + `LiveBlock` (sticky footer + multi-row block via `bubbles/v2/spinner` + private ticker) and output helpers (`LineTee`, `ANSIOnlyStripper`, `LogSanitizer`, `JoinWriters`). Consumed by `internal/core/execution/pipeline/plain.go` and by workflow parallel groups in `internal/core/usercommands/runtime/runners/workflow/parallel.go`. **Nine non-negotiable invariants** for the live view are enumerated in the package doc comment at the top of `liveline.go`, which is also what the in-code `Invariant #N` citations refer to — read them before touching anything here. Two of the nine (#6 `\r` is data, #7 prompt handoff through `widgets.SetHuhHooks` + `SuspendForExec`/`ResumeAfterExec`) are enforced in `internal/core/execution/pipeline`, not here; the numbering is shared because the design is. **Block-mode pending state (integration-tests stage 3b)** — `SetBlockRowPending(idx, label)` sets a block row's label WITHOUT starting its stopwatch (a queued scenario must not accrue elapsed time while it waits for a worker slot), keyed on an **explicit `pending bool` field on `blockRow`** — set only by `SetBlockRowPending`, cleared by `SetBlockRowRunning`/`SetBlockRowFinal`. `renderBlockRowLocked` renders a `pending` row with a gray `IconRunning` dot instead of the blue spinner and omits the elapsed bracket; render keys on the flag ONLY, **never** inferred from `startTime.IsZero()` — the workflow parallel runner (`runners/workflow/parallel.go`) keeps queued rows never-started for real durations (`SetBlockRowRunning` fires inside each worker goroutine after an errgroup slot is granted), so inferring pending from a zero start time would silently change that consumer's queued-row rendering. Existing consumers (which never call `SetBlockRowPending`) render byte-identically by construction. Guards mirror `SetBlockRowRunning` (out-of-range/disabled → no-op); `SetBlockRowRunning` after `SetBlockRowPending` starts the stopwatch then (first-call `startTime.IsZero()` branch). Consumed by `internal/cli/test/`'s aggregated `dwe test run --parallel` display. **Rendering model — hand-driven models, never a bubbletea program.** `LiveLine` updates and renders its `bubbles/v2` models directly (`spinner.Model.Update(spinner.TickMsg{…})` on each tick of the private 10 Hz ticker, then `spinner.View()` composed into an ANSI frame written to `termOut`); it must NEVER call `tea.NewProgram`, `term.MakeRaw`, or issue terminal capability queries. The footer is painted into normal scrollback next to the pipeline's own stdout/stderr streaming — not an alt-screen program — so an event loop or raw mode here would take ownership of the terminal the pipeline output is still writing to. The only terminal syscall in the package is the width probe `term.GetSize(os.Stdout.Fd())`, itself overridable through the `liveLineTestHooks.widthFn` seam.
- `internal/shared/trace/` — leaf diagnostic sink for `-v`/`--debug` output. **Zero `internal/core/**` imports** (the leaf invariant); imported by `docker` / `git` / the execution engine / `preflight` for emit, but only `cli/` calls `Configure` + installs the slog handler. **All output is stderr-only** so stdout (incl. `--output json`) stays a clean machine-readable contract. Public surface: `Level` enum (`LevelOff` / `LevelVerbose` / `LevelDebug`, `int32`-backed via `atomic.Int32`), `Configure(w io.Writer, lvl Level)` (called once from root; `w` may be nil), `Enabled(l Level) bool`, `Command(ctx, name, args...)` (Verbose+; `$`-prefixed copy-pasteable line), `Decision(ctx, format, …)` (Verbose+; step run/skip + reason, `when:`/condition + preflight results), `Debugf(ctx, format, …)` (Debug-only; timings/exit codes/env/config internals), `LinePrinter` interface (`PrintLine(string)`, must be concurrency-safe), `SetPrinter(p) (restore func())`, `WithLinePrinter(ctx, p)`, `FormatCommand(args) string`, and `NewSlogHandler()` (`slog.go`). **Three-level emit precedence** (`emit`): (1) ctx-carried printer (`printerFrom` / `WithLinePrinter`) — set per parallel sub-step for correct attribution; beats (2) global active printer (last remaining entry of the `SetPrinter` stack) — set by `PlainReporter.StartPipeline` to wrap `r.live.PrintlnDiag`, which frames the line above the footer via `termOut` but writes the line **text to the diagnostics writer = `os.Stderr`** (set via `LiveLine.SetDiagWriter` in `NewPlainReporter`), NOT `r.live.Println` (screen/stdout) — so in-pipeline `-v`/`--debug` echoes stay off stdout and `dwe run --debug 2>debug.log` captures them; beats (3) the `Configure` fallback writer (`os.Stderr`, used outside any pipeline + in JSON mode). `LevelOff` is a zero-alloc early return after one atomic load → no overhead when no flag is set. **Concurrency contract**: the global printer stack is mutated both by sequential nested-pipeline callers and — since parallel `dwe test run --parallel N>1` scenarios each run their own in-process steps pipeline — by concurrent siblings under `mu`. `SetPrinter` stamps each entry with a monotonic id and its restore removes **exactly its own entry by id** (not an index truncation of everything above it) under `sync.Once`, so an out-of-order restore never drops a still-active sibling's printer (which would leak its `-v`/`--debug` diagnostics to the fallback `os.Stderr` and garble the aggregated live display); the active printer is the last remaining entry. Parallel sub-steps within one pipeline attribute via the ctx printer and never touch the global slot. **slog handler** (`slog.go`): installed via `slog.SetDefault` **only** at `LevelDebug` (`-v` never touches slog); `Enabled` is always true (gating happens at install time), records route through the same ctx→global→fallback precedence, and `WithAttrs`/`WithGroup` are supported. `installSlogHandler` captures Go's pristine default logger once and **restores it** when a later same-process `Execute` runs below Debug — so a prior `--debug` does not leak the always-emit handler into a subsequent non-debug run (matters for embedded/repeated `Execute`; the one-shot CLI never revisits). **Level precedence** (`levelFrom` / `debugEnvTruthy` in `cli/root.go`): `--debug` OR truthy `DWE_DEBUG` → Debug; else `--verbose` → Verbose; **flag beats env** (`DWE_DEBUG` in `{"","0","false","no","off"}`, case-insensitive + trimmed, is off). **`FormatCommand`/`quoteArg` is the single quoting source** — lifted byte-identically from the old `docker/compose.go` `formatCommand`/`quoteArg`, so compose error-wrap goldens must stay byte-identical to it. **Tier contents**: Verbose carries user-facing lifecycle command echoes (`Compose.Exec`) plus pipeline decisions; Debug additionally echoes **read-only docker probes** — `Compose.output` and `Compose.RunningServices` each wrap their `trace.Command` call in an explicit `if trace.Enabled(trace.LevelDebug)` guard **to keep `dwe status -v` quiet**, since a single status render fans out into a burst of `ps`/`inspect` probes that would bury the lifecycle echoes a user asked `-v` for, so a new read-only probe must copy that guard while a user-facing lifecycle command echoes unguarded at Verbose+ — alongside the per-subprocess `cwd=` / `env=` lines, the exit code + duration line, and `slog.Debug` records. `trace` is also the **single** diagnostic sink: a domain package must never hand-roll a `fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr)` diagnostic, which would bypass the three-level precedence and land un-framed mid-live-frame on raw stderr instead of above the footer. User-facing details: `docs/guides/troubleshooting.md` § Verbose & debug output. **Secret redaction** (`RegisterRedaction(values []string)` + `ResetRedaction()` for tests): a process-global `secrets.Redactor` behind the same `mu`. Registration is a **union, never a replacement**, so nested and concurrent config loads — `dwe test --parallel` runs several pipelines with stacked `SetPrinter` entries and per-context printers — all converge on one consistent set, and no restore-ordering problem exists because nothing is ever removed. **The single installer is `config.LoadConfig`** (60 call sites load config and the root `PersistentPreRunE` does not), which registers every decrypted plaintext after assembly; `config.LoadConfigSanitized` registers nothing. Redaction runs at **two** points: `trace.Command` redacts each argument *before* `FormatCommand` (`quoteArg` rewrites `'` to `'\''`, so a secret containing an apostrophe would otherwise no longer be a substring of the formatted line), and `emit` redacts the finished line before every printer path — which is what covers the `.dwe/logs` mirrors of parallel-step traces. `FormatCommand` itself is untouched, so its goldens do not move. **`Redact(s string) string` is the exported third entry point** — the same process-global redactor, identity when none is installed — for display code that builds a line without going through `trace.Command`/`emit`; its consumers are `pipeline.StepCommand` / `FormatAction` / `FormatCondition`, which is what makes every plan and dry-run surface redacted (see Core — Execution). Values under `secrets.MinRedactRunes` (4) are skipped (redacting `"1"` would shred every line) and child-process output is deliberately not touched — both documented in `docs/reference/config/secrets.md`.
- `internal/shared/i18n/` — localization system: embedded + project-layer translation file loading, locale resolution, and typed lookup. **Schema**: `Bundle{UI map[string]string, Commands map[string]CommandStrings, Groups map[string]GroupStrings}` (CommandStrings and GroupStrings carry description + params/title respectively). **File format**: YAML files under `internal/shared/i18n/translations/` (built-in) and `<projectRoot>/workspace/i18n/` (project overlay, optional); strict `KnownFields(true)` decode. **Loader**: `Load(projectRoot)` reads embedded files + project overrides (project-wins deep merge per locale); returns a `*Store` with methods `T(locale, uiKey, fallback)` (generic lookup for `ui.*` namespace), plus typed helpers `CommandDescription` / `CommandConfirmationText` / `ParamDescription` / `GroupTitle` / `GroupDescription`. `LoadProjectBundles(projectRoot)` returns per-file parse metadata for the validator (source path, locale, parse error if any); missing directory → `(nil, nil)`, OS error → sentinel `ProjectFile{Locale: "", ParseErr}`, parse error → non-fatal (skipped, file appears in result slice). **Resolver**: `ResolveLocale(flagLang, configLang, sysLang)` precedence chain: flag → config → system `$LANG` parse → `"en"` default. **Scope v1**: user-command `description`/`confirmation_text`/`param.description`, command-group `title`/`description`, and doc-generator `ui.*` strings. DWE's own Cobra strings stay English; `${snapshot.*}` variables stay English. **Coverage test**: `coverage_test.go` ensures every `ui.*` key in built-in `en.yml` exists in every other built-in locale. **Two disjoint i18n namespaces exist and must never be conflated.** This package owns namespace one: `workspace/i18n/<lang>.yml` — YAML command/UI strings, strict-decoded into `Bundle`, project-wins deep-merged per locale, validated by the `internal/core/validate/i18n/` domain. Namespace two is `docs/i18n/<lang>/**` (today `reference/`, `guides/`, and a flat `README.md` — there is no `docs/i18n/<lang>/internals/` tree; `docs/internals/` is excluded from the mirror) — markdown long-form documentation resolved by `internal/core/docs/lang.go`'s `ResolveContent`, which does whole-file per-file fallback to the English source with **no merge at all** and has **no validator** (freshness is guarded by `lang_test.go::TestRussianTranslationsAreFresh` in CI, not by `dwe validate`). Different file formats, different loaders, no shared code path — a key never crosses between them, and a missing translation degrades differently on each side (YAML: per-key fallback to `en`; markdown: whole-document fallback). User-visible details: `docs/reference/config/i18n.md`.

## Container TTY Contract

Cross-cutting: `internal/cli/command/` + `internal/core/execution/pipeline/` + `internal/core/usercommands/runtime/` + `internal/shared/bridgeclient/`. The question it answers is "may this service command hand the container a terminal?", and the answer is assembled from a `RunContext` field, a process-global env marker, and a compose-flag classifier. User-facing shape: `docs/reference/config/commands/types.md` § Container TTY.

- **`spec.RunContext.UserInvoked`** — marks an invocation the user launched themselves, as opposed to one runtime invoking another. **It gates container TTY allocation and nothing else.** Exactly one site sets it true: `runCommandByID` (`internal/cli/command/runbyid.go`), as `!bridgeclient.NestedRuntime()` read **before** the same function writes the marker — the read-then-write order is what keeps a top-level invocation from marking itself nested. `runCommandByID` is the single execution path for `dwe commands <id>`, the TUI run flow **and** the host bridge (`core/bridge/exec.go` re-execs `dwe <argv…>` as a plain subprocess), so that one assignment covers every entry point. The only other write is derivation, not decision: the workflow runner's `runCommandStep` (`runtime/runners/workflow/step.go`) passes `rc.UserInvoked && !rc.UnderParallel` down to a sub-step, so a sequential sub-step inherits and a `parallel:` sub-step — whose I/O is fanned into per-step writers — never asks for a TTY. Derived there rather than stamped a second time in `parallel.go`, so there is one place to read and one to reason about.
- **Why the zero value is load-bearing** — `false` suppresses the TTY, so a new entry point that forgets the field can only be conservative (pipe-shaped output, no terminal in the container). `pipeline.execCommandAction` deliberately never sets it: a `type: command` step must not hand a container a terminal, and the omission *is* the behaviour, which is why `TestExecCommandAction_LeavesUserInvokedFalse` pins it next to its `SkipNotify` twin. **Do not reuse `SkipNotify`** — it answers the same "is this top-level?" question today, but notifications and terminal allocation are unrelated concerns, and the next caller that flips `SkipNotify` for a notification reason must not silently move the container TTY with it.
- **`bridgeclient.EnvNestedRuntime` (`DWE_NESTED_RUNTIME`)** — the marker that makes the provenance survive a process boundary. Set **process-globally** via `MarkNestedRuntime` (`os.Setenv`), never per-spawn: `pipeline.execShellAction` never assigns `cmd.Env` at all and the set of spawn mechanisms is not reliably enumerable, so every descendant inherits it however it was spawned — a `type: dwe` re-exec, an `sh -c` snippet calling back through the exported `DWE_BIN`, a project script. Read as `os.Getenv(name) != ""`, **not** `os.LookupEnv`: tests clear it with `t.Setenv(name, "")`, which `LookupEnv` still reports as set. Once marked, a process stays marked for the rest of its life.
- **Where the mark is written** — `pipeline.ExecAction` (`executor.go`), unconditionally on entry. Not the deprecated `Run` wrapper, where it would be a silent no-op, and not only `RunWithOptions`, which `dwe reset step` bypasses by calling `ExecAction` directly (`cli/lifecycle/reset.go`). One place covers `type: shell` / `dwe` / `host` / `script` steps on both routes. The second write site is `runCommandByID`, after its own read.
- **Strip-set membership is mandatory** — `EnvNestedRuntime` is in `bridgeclient.strippedEnvNames`, so the daemon never forwards a container-set value to the host. A bridged `dwe cmd` **is** a user invocation (it is the reason `bridgedTTYChildIO` exists); a marker leaking across the trust boundary would classify every bridged command as nested and kill its TTY. Pinned by a row in `TestStripEnv`. The bridge path itself never sets the marker.
- **Deliberate gap** — `files_gate` commands and shell `when:` predicates evaluated **before the first `ExecAction` of the process** run unmarked, so a `dwe cmd` re-entry from one of those is classified user-invoked. Later ones *are* marked (the marker is process-global and never cleared, and `evalFilesGate` runs per step), so **do not pin a test on "gates are always unmarked"**. Accepted because the failure mode is today's behaviour — no `-T` — i.e. conservative.
- **`runio.WantContainerTTY(rc)`** — `rc.UserInvoked && (bridgedTTYActive(rc) || isTerminal(StdoutOf(rc)) && isTerminal(StdinOrOS(rc)))`. The bridge arm short-circuits the stream probe on purpose: over the bridge dwe's own streams are pipes and the child's PTY is fabricated by `bridgedTTYChildIO` inside `WireChildIO`, i.e. **after** `BuildCommand` has fixed the argv, so probing at argv-build time would emit `-T` for a session about to get a real terminal. Conversely this is not a plain terminal probe, because `pipeline.childIO` fabricates a PTY for a sequential step whenever dwe's stdout is a terminal and hands it over as `rc.Stdout` — a bare probe answers "yes" in exactly the `type: command`-step case the predicate exists to catch. Streams resolve through `StdoutOf` / `StdinOrOS` because the fields are nil-able and a raw read would answer "not a terminal" for the top-level `dwe cmd` the predicate exists for. `isTerminal` is a **second** seam beside `stdoutIsTerminal`, not a generalisation of it: `stdoutIsTerminal` answers for the process's own `os.Stdout`, which is what `colorForceActive` and `bridgedTTYActive` mean.
- **`-T` injection and the flag classifier** — `buildDockerComposeCmd` (`runtime/runners/service/exec.go`) appends `-T` right after `composeArgs` when `!WantContainerTTY(rc)` and `hasTTYFlag` finds none. The classifier runs over the **effective** vector — `compose.CommandArgs["exec"|"run"]` (from `docker.yml`'s `args:`) concatenated with the rendered `compose_args` — because a flag declared in `docker.yml` is otherwise invisible at this point. It matches `-T`, `-T=<value>`, `--no-tty` with or without a value, and `T` inside a short-flag **bundle** (`-dT`, `-Td`, `-iT`). Only the long form is case-insensitive: compose spells it lowercase and pflag is case-sensitive, so an uppercase-only matcher would recognise nothing a project can have written, while a case-insensitive one still catches `--no-TTY`. The short `T` stays case-sensitive because compose has no `-t`. Bundle decomposition (`shortFlagLetters`) stops at the first value-taking shorthand and refuses on an unknown letter, because shape alone cannot tell `-dT` from `-uTest` — refusing falls back to the old whole-token behaviour, which is the safe direction. The same helper backs `hasDetachFlag`, and that is what it was added for: an unrecognised `-dT` computed `detached=false` while suppressing the TTY, which forced colour into a detached container and baked ANSI into its Docker logs permanently. Any occurrence hands the decision to the author **regardless of its value**, which makes `compose_args: ["--no-tty=false"]` the deliberate force-a-TTY escape hatch. Only TTY flags suppress the auto-detect — `-d`, `--name`, `--rm` stay orthogonal on purpose.
- **Paired colour forcing** — suppressing the TTY would grey out a child whose output still reaches the user, so `ColorForceEnv(rc, forceOnSuppressedTTY bool)` takes a third disjunct in `colorForceActive`: `forceOnSuppressedTTY && isTerminal(rc.Stdout)`. The flag is computed by the caller, not derived in `runio`, because only the service runner can see the effective compose flags; it passes `ttySuppressed && !detached` and the three host-side runners (`host.go`, `dwe.go`, `script.go`) pass `false` — a host child has no container TTY to suppress and nobody should derive a value for them. **The `!detached` half is load-bearing**: `-d` is valid on both `exec` and `run`, a detached child's output never reaches `rc.Stdout`, so forcing colour there writes ANSI escapes into the Docker logs permanently (`hasDetachFlag` therefore counts any `-d` / `--detach` occurrence, `-d=false` included — a false positive costs one uncoloured run, a false negative is permanent).
- **Raw `rc.Stdout` vs `StdoutOf` asymmetry** — the colour disjunct probes the **raw** field while `WantContainerTTY` resolves through `StdoutOf` / `StdinOrOS`. Deliberate and load-bearing: an internal caller that leaves `Stdout` nil (or points it at `io.Discard`, as `validate/checks/loader.go` does) **parses** the child's output and must never receive forced colour merely because the process's own `os.Stdout` is a terminal. A "consistency" cleanup unifying the two would inject ANSI into parsed output.

## Entrypoint (`cmd/dwe/`)

- `cmd/dwe-shim/main.go` — the standalone in-container shim binary: a thin main over `internal/shared/bridgeclient` (signal.Notify for SIGINT/SIGTERM, `bridgeclient.OptionsFromEnv`, `os.Exit(bridgeclient.Run(...))`). No cobra, no lipgloss — the whole point is a ~3 MB static `CGO_ENABLED=0` linux build (`make shims` → `internal/core/bridge/shimassets/bin/`, embedded into the host dwe and materialized to `.dwe/bridge/` at prepare time).
- `cmd/dwe/main.go` — entry point (uses `fang.Execute` for styled help/errors). Four non-obvious startup behaviors: (1) **Prompt hot-path bypass** (`isPromptInvocation`): if `os.Args` is exactly `["…", "prompt"]` or `["…", "prompt", "--check"]`, `prompt.Run(os.Stdout, os.Args[2:])` is called and `os.Exit` fires immediately — before cobra, fang, pflag pre-parse, and `loadHelpColorScheme`. Any other `prompt` shape (e.g. `--help`, unknown flags, `--check=true`) falls through to cobra so help/errors render normally. (2) **Pre-Cobra pflag pre-parse** (`configPathFromArgs`): extracts `--config/-c` from `os.Args` using pflag with `UnknownFlags=true` *before* Cobra runs, so the project root can be located without triggering Cobra flag parsing. (3) **Early styles injection** (`loadHelpColorScheme`): calls `project.Locate` + `config.LoadStylesConfig` + `styles.ApplyStyles` before `fang.Execute`, then passes a `fang.WithColorSchemeFunc` so `--help` output uses the project's `accent`/`muted` tokens. Silent on all failures — Fang's default colors apply when styles cannot be loaded. The scheme func is ALWAYS installed (even without styles.yml) because it also pins the palette mode: fang's own `mustColorscheme` resolves isDark=false whenever stdout is not a terminal, which would render the LIGHT palette into a pipe — invisible dark-on-dark text over the host bridge. Both lipgloss v1 and v2 resolve DARK on pipes, so the wrapper forces `lipgloss.LightDark(true)` when `!term.IsTerminal(stdout)` and every dwe surface agrees. `styles.ApplyStyles` runs twice on a normal invocation (once here before Cobra, once from root `PersistentPreRunE`); the second call is a no-op when styles haven't changed. (4) **Custom error handler**: suppresses output for `ErrSilent` (command already printed its own error) and for `ExitCode`-bearing errors (diagnostics already printed via `fang.WithErrorHandler`); delegates all others to `fang.DefaultErrorHandler`. Exit-code errors also produce a non-zero `os.Exit(ec.ExitCode())` after `fang.Execute` returns.
